- **The harness cannot produce a refusal honestly** - one means moving a real body, which is the failsafe, which the self-test turns off - so the rule is pinned by the pair: 320 ticks of correction with refusals still flags (`anticheat.constant-correction-is-itself-a-finding`), and 400 ticks of a boss's shove with no refusals flags nothing (`anticheat.a-shove-is-not-a-constant-correction`).

### 🌊 Water stays where the maze put it
- **A flooded chamber is a chamber until the first tick.** Water finds the one air cell it is allowed to spread into and leaves, and a room authored as a flooded hall becomes a dry hall with a wet corner in the first seconds of a run. Barriers under the pool - which the flooded condition already placed - fix the floor and nothing else: the surface still has air over it, and a doorway, a broken block or an ore stud is all it takes.
- **Structure void in the shell of every water cell**, which is what the block was made for: invisible, no collision, no light taken, and not air, so water cannot spread into it while the room looks and walks exactly the same as it did. One cell in every air cell that touches water turns every pool in the maze into a fish tank.
- The scan covers the room plus one cell in every direction, because water is often in a floor or a wall and its shell is then outside the room's own bounds. **Only air is ever written over**: a chest, a spawner, a lantern and the water itself are left exactly as they were, and that half is asserted separately.
- Pinned by `expedition.water-is-sealed-where-it-stands`.

### 🔱 The Sunken Temple is drowned, not decorative
- **The elder guardian is out** - of the hall roster, of the sentinel, and of the reward table. Its mining-fatigue aura is an area effect on a *timer* rather than a fight mechanic, so what it actually contributed to a hall was a player's pickaxe quietly stopping, arriving from a body they may not even have seen yet. The drowning, the guards and the prismarine were always the temple; the elder guardian was the tide telling you to leave.
- **And no more sea pickles in the temple's water.** One on the surface is the block that makes a drowned hall read as a garden pond. The temple's own basins lost theirs, and so does any flooded room in the temple; every other dungeon's flooded rooms keep them, where they are the one sign the water has been there long enough to grow something.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-tide-keeps-its-own-monsters`.

### 🟢 The Slime Launcher is a right click
- **It used to take the left hand, and what it took away was the swing.** It also made the same click do two different things depending on how long you had been holding the button. It is one click on the right now - the whole rack at once, **2 at the bottom tier, 3 at tier II, 5 at tier III** - and the left hand is left alone, so a launcher is something you carry and use rather than something you switch to. The reload, and the budget behind it, are unchanged; only the button moved. The item's own description said tap-for-one, hold-for-five, and it has been rewritten to match.
- Pinned by `bosses.the-launcher-is-a-right-click`.

## 1.9.32

### 🌬️ The Gale Warden's wind is a ring, not a seed storm
- **The hurricane was 1,920 gust seeds a second, for eleven seconds.** A `gust_emitter` is not a particle - it is a *seed* that lives a second or more and spends that life spawning gusts of its own, so one of them costs a client dozens of particles and a long-lived entry in its particle engine. The wind boss drew its big rings with ninety of them, and the hurricane drew two such rings **every tick**. That is the lag, and it is a different bug from "too many particles" with a different fix: the count that matters is the count of seeds.
- **The big rings are plain gust puffs now, at a third of the sites.** A ring is a shape, so what it needs is many things in a circle and not many of anything expensive - a `GUST` is one short-lived particle that draws instantly and is gone. Thirty of them read exactly like ninety seeds did, and the seeds are kept where a handful of them *is* the look: the tornado's own column and the burst on release.
- **And a hard ceiling on seeds anyway**, in the single funnel every boss effect already draws through: **48 gust emitters per player per tick**, spent from the shape that asks first. It is set by the largest legitimate use in the mod - the ender dragon's clusters of two to eight through the same funnel - so nothing else notices it exists, and a shape asking for fifty or ninety is cut evenly instead of being chopped in half.
- Pinned by `vfx.the-wind-rings-are-puffs-and-the-seeds-are-budgeted`.

### 🧨 Wind charges and explosions are not flight
- **A wind charge is a launch, not a nudge**: eight or ten blocks up, a second and a half of climb, then a fall that does not follow the arc a jump would have taken - and every one of those ticks is the shape the anticheat's flight check exists to catch.
- It arrived with **no attacker to measure a knockback direction from**, so the damage hook skipped it entirely and the movement that followed was judged as the player's own. A creeper has no attacker either, and a wind charge fired at your own feet has *you* as its causing entity - read as "the victim shoved themselves". Explosions now stand every movement check down for **three seconds** (up from the two a plain shove gets), which is long enough to cover the throw, the whole arc and the landing rather than expiring mid-descent.
- **The launch that dealt no damage is caught too**: a blast whose shove reaches a body standing outside its injury radius never triggers a damage event at all, so the outbound motion packet is the only evidence it happened - and a shove that points sharply upward now reads as a launch there. Nobody is knocked *up* by a hit.
- One trap worth writing down, because it is where the first attempt went wrong: a wind charge is **not in vanilla's explosion tag**, and its `message_id` is `"mob"` - the same one every bow shot shares - so a check written against the message id recognises every arrow in the game and no wind charge at all. It is read by damage-type registry key instead.
- Pinned by `anticheat.an-explosion-launch-is-not-flight`.

### ❄️ The Frozen Crypt is a sepulcher now
- **Icicles out of the roof**, in tapering columns with a blue-ice tip - the one shape that says the ceiling is *frozen* rather than merely lit, and no other dungeon in the maze hangs anything from its roof, so it is also how the crypt reads as itself from the doorway.
- **Frozen pools** break the floor into ice and blue ice instead of one even sheet, drift gathers along the walls where snow would actually gather, and spikes stand out of the floor. Then the **graves**: real coffins in facing rows down both aisles, each a slab of bone with a lid of packed ice and a soul lantern at the head of every other one.
- **Six set pieces, one per kind of chamber**, so two chambers of this dungeon are never dressed alike: the **frozen gate** at the entrance, the **sepulcher hoard** in every vault (a plinth with the takings iced into it), the **ice altar** in the shrine and the chapel, the **thawed font** in the water rooms, the **ice colonnade** in an ordinary hall, and the **thawed camp** - a fire burning in a ring of ice - in all three rest rooms, because a camp dressed as a tomb is a camp a hurt explorer walks past.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-crypt-is-a-sepulcher-and-not-a-stone-room` (every chamber gets a piece, all six are reachable, the vaults and the three camps are dressed as what they are).

### 💼 Jobs are a standing, not an errand
- **A worker's own rank**, from the jobs they have actually finished: Hand, Working Hand, Journeyman, Foreman, Master, Guildsman. Six promotions, the first three jobs in so a new player sees the number move in one evening and the last a season's work.
- **It pays, permanently** - up to 2.5× on every future job, on top of the skill scaling that was already there, plus a **flat 6× on the templates themselves**: those were written as pocket money when the board was the only income in the game, and five errands have to be worth an evening against what the same evening of expeditions pays.
- **And the board now pays tokens and pushes the contract board**: every claim pays gems, and a finished job advances any live dynamic contract of the same shape by a quarter of the job's size. The two boards were a loop that did not close - a contract is a day's work and a job is an errand, and finishing one gave you no reason to look at the other.
- Contract cards on the job board are now a **shortcut to the delivery window** instead of a picture of one, and a delivered contract pushes a live job of the same shape back the other way.
- Pinned by `jobs.a-worker-has-a-standing-of-their-own`.

### 📈 The trade window is an exchange
- **Live supply and demand, measured rather than invented**: every item and every dollar that moves through a completed trade is tallied, so the window can say what this server's players have actually paid this week - busiest first - alongside a line for everything in the offer in front of you, including the honest answer "never traded here".
- **Your own trade history**, newest first: partner, both sides on the server's own valuation, and which way the deal went for you. A trader who cannot remember what they paid last time is a trader making it up.
- Both are persisted, aged out of the window after a week, and read from one place, so the number on the tile and the number the market is carrying cannot disagree.
- Pinned by `trade.the-exchange-remembers-what-moved`.

## 1.9.31

### 🌱 Auto-Replant actually replants
- **The perk never fired once - not rarely, never.** It read `player.blockPosition()`, which is the cell a standing body occupies, and then refused to put anything down because *that cell is never air*. The rules were fine; the position was the whole bug. It now uses the position handed down by the break event: **the block that was actually harvested**, which is the one cell that is guaranteed free a tick after the crop leaves it.
- It also got the checks it was missing rather than regrowing crops into dirt: only a **fully grown** crop replants (an immature one was trampled, not harvested), the ground under it must still be **farmland**, and the spot must be free - so a crop is replaced where it stood and nothing else is ever overwritten. Plus the villager sparkle, so a player can see the free crop land.
- Pinned by `skills.the-replant-finds-the-crop-and-not-the-feet`.

### 📉 The market can go up again
- **"Stocks only ever go down" was not the clock and not the shelves.** The quotes come off a mean-reverting walk, and a listing's band is wide enough that most of the board never even touches it - what was one-way was the **level underneath them**: the per-update cap on the recalibration was a fifth of *the level itself*, so a board that had fallen to the floor could only climb back at a fifth of that floor, while the fall that put it there ran at a fifth of something much bigger. Fast down, glacial up.
- The cap is now **a fifth of the level on the way down** - where the damping is doing real work, so a windfall still gets priced in over an afternoon instead of between two clicks - and **a fifth of whichever of the level and the benchmark is larger on the way up**. Recovery from the floor is now at least as quick as the fall that made it necessary, and never slower.
- Pinned by `market.a-fallen-board-can-climb-again`, which drives one equal-sized gap in both directions and requires the same size of move, then re-checks that a level above its target still steps down inside the old cap.

### 🎟️ The lottery pays what the server is worth
- **The jackpot is no longer tickets alone.** 5% of the server's own spread - the tenth-percentile wallet against the ninetieth - is added by the house, scaled by how much of the server actually bought in, so four buyers get the whole of it and one buyer gets none of it. A pot fed only by ticket sales was the same size on every world; this one is worth what the place it runs in is worth.
- Built from **percentiles, not the peak**: one dupe or one lucky gravestone is not an economy, and paying the lottery off the richest wallet would make it one. A server with a single player has no spread and therefore no bonus, which is deliberate - the house pays for a shared game.
- **Every jackpot in the game's history was announced as coming "from 0 players"**: the crowd line was built from `tickets.size()` immediately *after* the board was wiped. Fixed, and the line now also says how much of the prize was the crowd and how much was the spread.
- Pinned by `lottery.a-jackpot-is-read-off-the-server` (the counter's estimate is the draw's own arithmetic, a one-wallet server pays no spread bonus, a full house is worth at least a single buyer, and the crowd line carries a real count).

### 📦 The Deliveries hopper can't be pocketed any more
- **The hopper in the intake was a picture of a hopper and a real, takeable item.** It could be clicked out of the slot, shift-clicked into a pack, or handed over by the same code that returns your delivered items when the window closed - so the window paid out free hoppers. It is marked now, and every path that touches the slot reads the sign as empty: it is never delivered, never returned and never taken, and a genuine hopper dropped in still works normally.
- **And a `SWEEP YOUR PACK` button**, at last: one click pulls everything in your inventory that an active contract is asking for into the intake. The window used to tell you what it wanted and then make you hunt for it across thirty-six slots.
- Pinned by `deliveries.the-intake-sign-is-not-cargo`.

### ⛏️ A descent is bought, not walked into
- **Expeditions now cost expedition EXP to unlock.** EXP only ever bought the six lines, which meant a run was as free as the last one once a tree was paid for and the only thing between a player and the richest site in the mod was the walk. Sites are priced off what they pay - Sunken Temple and Frozen Crypt 45, Crystal Cavern 70, Monster Cave and Void 120, Magma Forge 180 - and that price competes with the six lines for the same points, which is the whole tension of the mode in one number.
- **The Deep Mine is deliberately free**, because it is where the first EXP comes from and a price on it would lock a new explorer out of the only thing that pays. Every site's price is printed on its tile in the Expedition menu, with what you have banked beside it.
- The fee is **charged last**: the site is taken and purged first, and if anything at all falls over before the maze is carved, nothing has been spent. An expedition that cannot start must not have cost anything.
- Pinned by `expedition.a-descent-has-a-price`.

### 🪨 The collapse warns you before it takes you
- **Being caught by the fall used to end the run in the same tick the rock started moving.** The roof giving way over the chamber you were standing in was the warning *and* the sentence, so the last chamber of a collapse was not a decision - it was a dice roll on which doorway you happened to be behind. The fall now **catches you and gives you eight seconds**: no loot banked yet, a pad that still works, a countdown on the action bar, and the site coming down around you for every one of those seconds.
- **It is deliberately eight seconds and not more** - long enough to turn around, read a compass and run for a pad, far too short to loot the chamber on the way out. Anything you secure in that window counts, so the last eight seconds of a lost run are still a run. The same window applies to the other way the fall ends a run: being inside the site when the last chamber comes down.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-fall-warns-before-it-takes-you`, which drives all three states of the window - including the one that must never again be the same instant as the first.

### 🔌 Leaving the server ends the run
- **A run does not outlive the body in it.** Logging out mid-expedition used to leave the site held against your name with its clock still running, so you came back to a maze that had been falling for however long you were gone - a free escape on a good day and a stolen run on a bad one. It now ejects, the same way being caught does: **no loot banked, your ordinary inventory untouched**, and the site's own items - the exit compass and the loot backpack - taken back, because they are the site's and not yours. The walk back to the pad is the run; there is no version of leaving that performs it for you.
- Wrapped so a failure mid-ejection still drops the run: leaving must never be held up by bookkeeping.

### 🧭 The chunk anchor holds its ground
- **It used to be that the one machine whose whole job is keeping its chunks loaded was skipped by the very check for whether its chunks were loaded.** The machine tick loop ignored any machine standing in an unloaded chunk - so an anchor whose ticket had been dropped by something else sat dark until the once-a-minute re-assert came round. An anchor is now **re-asserted the moment it is found dark**, before anything is asked about its ground, and the ticket sweep behind it runs every twenty seconds instead of every minute.
- Worth stating plainly, because it is the whole promise of the item: an anchor that has stopped holding its chunks is now a thing that repairs itself rather than a thing you find a week later with a stalled farm sitting on it.

## 1.9.30

### 🪨 The gravel actually falls now
- **Rubble used to simply appear on the floor of a doomed chamber** - a few blocks a tick arriving at ground level - which read as a room being *filled* rather than as a room coming down, because nothing in it ever fell. Part of every shed is now **real falling rock**: each block is spawned at the chamber's own roof and left to the game's own physics, so it drops, lands where it lands, stays there as the rubble, and hurts whatever it comes down on.
- **The share of it that falls grows as the site comes apart** (three blocks a tick at the first chamber, six at the last, never more than the chamber's own shed rate), so the last few chambers of a fall are visibly heavier than the first ones.
- **The roof crumbles in place rather than being torn open.** Panels over a shedding chamber turn to loose gravel a few at a time - lamp, beam and ceiling alike - which is the crack spreading, and it is *also* why a doomed chamber gets gradually darker: its own light is in that ceiling. It is done this way deliberately, because there is no rock above a site (the expedition dimension is flat and empty, and every wall, floor and ceiling in the maze was written by the mod) - a hole in a roof is a hole into the void, and a chamber that opens upward during a collapse is a chamber you can leave through the ceiling.
- **The moment a roof gives way is a burst of it**, up to twenty-eight blocks of the chamber's own ceiling coming down at once - capped on purpose, because a hall is six hundred cells and dropping all of them as entities in one tick is a tick spike rather than a spectacle. The written rubble is still what buries the floor; this is the part you watch.
- **And it never stops being audible**: a chamber coming down crunches under its own falling rock for every second of the fall, not only at the end.
- Falling rock is a **knock** rather than a full falling block (bounded at a heart and a half per block, against the five hearts a ten-block fall does in vanilla), because the collapse is meant to be a room you leave rather than a room that kills you the moment it starts. The burst at the moment of the breach lands heavier, because that one *is* the roof giving way.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-roof-comes-down-as-rock` (a chamber really does drop its base share of roof, the share only grows with the fall, it never exceeds the chamber's own shed rate, and the giving-way burst is bounded).

## 1.9.29

### 🍯 A stillroom is a well and not a shelf
- **The draughts thin as the run drinks them.** The Alchemy Hall is the one room in the maze that hands out healing rather than paying for it, and a hall that heals full every time is a hall a run stops leaving: two of them in one run used to be **six full draughts and six bottles**, which is more health than an expedition's worth of camps. The first draught of a run is now worth exactly what the cauldron says, and every one after it pays **half of the one before**, down to a floor that is still worth having - so the first hall is a real reason to walk into a hurt room and the second is a fight with a patch-up at the end of it.
- **The bottle is capped instead, because falloff cannot price it.** A potion drunk three chambers later is worth exactly what it was worth when it was handed over, so the run gets **one**: the coven had one bottle left, the first cauldron in the run gives it, and every cauldron after that is a mend and nothing to carry.
- Pinned by `expedition.a-stillroom-is-a-well-and-not-a-shelf`, which reads the whole ladder: the first is full, every one after it is smaller, the floor is real and reachable, the six cauldrons a two-hall run can find are worth well under three full ones, and the bottle cap holds.

### 🧙 The coven drinks its own cures
- **The cures cut both ways.** Every few seconds, the **worst-off witch in the hall drains one of the cauldrons** - the pot is gone (with the sound, a burst of hearts at it and a line on the action bar counting what is left) and she is put back on her feet. The pack is already the biggest in the site, and a slow fight is a fight against a pack that is patching itself up between blows.
- **Beaten to it, and that is the room.** The witches take a moment to reach the pots, so a player who bursts in swinging takes the first cauldron; **drinking the hall dry yourself is the greedy play**, because every cauldron they take is one you do not get. The room says so once on the way in - the cauldrons are standing in the middle of the hall, and both sides can see them.
- Three rules keep it a race rather than a grind: she only drinks when she is properly hurt (a fight the player is winning fast never pays for her), she only drinks **while the explorer is standing in the hall** (a cauldron drained behind somebody's back is a cure they never had a chance at), and the room's cauldrons run out.

## 1.9.28

### 🧪 The Alchemy Hall: the one room worth walking into hurt
- **A witches' stillroom, and the biggest fight in the maze.** Almost the whole pack is witches - three in four bodies, with an evoker in the coven's colours as the fourth - and the hall wakes with three more bodies than depth alone would ask for, which makes it the largest single pack a run has to fight. That is the price of what is standing in it.
- **Three cauldrons left on the boil, and they answer a right-click.** A **Healing Draught** is an instant mend on the spot - a few hearts, not a full heal - and a real **Potion of Healing** to carry out of the room, which is the one thing a run can bring home from a site that is still worth something on the walk back. Each cauldron answers exactly once and stays standing when it is empty, and the fourth pot in the ring is plain water, because a stillroom whose cauldrons are all magic reads as a vending machine rather than as a room somebody works in.
- **The work is still standing.** Brewing stands down both sides with barrels of stores beside them, nether wart growing in all four corners on soul sand, barrels and shelves along the walls, glowstone burning under the ceiling over the benches, and the coven's takings in a rich chest at the near end. The draughts get the same rising halo the supply crates have, for the same reason - an unopened cauldron in a hall full of cauldrons is the one thing a hurt explorer is looking for.
- **Gated at depth two, and heavier in the dungeons it belongs to** (a crystal cavern and a sunken temple roll it three times as often), so the cures are a reason to have gone somewhere rather than a reason to never need the camps.

### 🏚️ The collapse now tells you how far along it is
- **The ring.** A circle of dust is drawn around the explorer at a radius that **closes with every chamber that falls** - thirty-four blocks out when the first chamber goes, nine when the last one is under the fall. It is the countdown, and it is the one piece of the collapse a running player can read without looking away from the way out.
- **The groans.** A rumble paced faster and pitched lower as the site comes apart, coming from the nearest chamber that is actually coming down rather than from the sky, so the sound has somewhere to look. Past the last third the ground starts booming under it, and the last chamber gets the boom plus the site's own chime.
- **The shafts.** Every chamber mid-fall stands under a column of sparks that grows with the fall, so a site coming down chamber by chamber draws itself across the maze and the parts still standing are the parts with no light over them. A chamber leaving the world gets its own shock ring and dust column, both scaled by how far along the site is.
- All three read off one number - the share of the site already under the fall - so they cannot disagree with each other, and the whole thing draws through the shared particle budget the bosses use rather than around it.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-collapse-has-a-read` (the pressure is clamped and hits both ends, the groans only get faster and lower, the ring only closes, and neither end of it is drawn inside the player) and `expedition.the-alchemy-hall-is-a-stillroom-and-not-a-cave` (the room is rollable but gated, the pack is the biggest in the site, most of it is witches, the draught is a heal worth having and not a full one, and the cauldron is a real interactable).

## 1.9.27

### 🏚️ The collapse is a collapse, chamber after chamber
- **“The room collapse only works once.”** Three separate ways the fall could stop being a fall, and all three are closed.
- **The clock starts a collapse and no longer ends it.** A run that buys time - the mini-boss sentinel is worth a minute, a floor guardian three, a Time Shard its seconds - pushed the elapsed clock back under the run's own duration, and the whole collapse block with it: the chamber that was mid-shed froze where it stood with every chamber behind it still standing, and the site simply stopped coming down. The gate is the collapse's own flag now, the clock's only job is to start it, and a site that is already falling cannot be sold another minute at all (the shard says so, the sentinel says so, the guardian just pays its bounty).
- **A chamber that was cleared still has a roof over it.** The shed step skipped any chamber already marked cleared - and cleared is what every chamber the run *fought through* is, plus every camp the moment you walk into it. On a run that had gone anywhere, the fall walked past most of the maze and took down the one room nobody had cleared, which is exactly what one chamber coming down looks like. Only two things take a chamber out of the queue now: it is already shedding, or its roof has already come down.
- **And a chamber that cannot be buried comes down anyway.** The gate on the roof was the rubble count, and rubble only lands in a cell that is empty - so a room filled to its own ceiling could never reach the count and would shed for the rest of the run while the rest of the site waited on it. Every shed now has a deadline of its own (`COLLAPSE_MAX_SHED_TICKS`), inside four collapse steps, after which the roof gives way on the clock instead.
- Pinned by `expedition.the-collapse-cannot-be-stopped-or-bought-off`, which drives the four rules directly: the fall runs on its own flag past the clock, a time bonus is refused once the site is committed, a cleared chamber is still in the queue, and a shed ends on its window even with nothing in the room to bury.

### ⛺ The camps are a health budget, not the whole deck
- **All four camp faces are quieter.** Three camps sat on the same heavy line at **18** apiece - two chambers in nine, which is what happens when you turn the healing up and leave the weights alone - and the maze stopped reading as a maze. They sit at **9** now, the Moonlit Chapel at **5**, and the quiet rooms that thin out as a run goes deeper now name the waystation as well as the shrine and the reading room.
- **A resting place can no longer be a hazard.** The condition roll skipped the sanctuary and nothing else, so a reading room, a waystation or the new camp could be handed a shedding roof or a floor under water - a camp whose whole promise is safety. The rule is the rest-room rule now, for all of them.

### 🌿 The Calm Campplace: the main way to heal
- **A room that is only ever a camp.** The other three are rooms built for something else and found quiet - a shrine's stone, a library nobody is reading in, a caravan that stopped. This one is a glade: grass underfoot in a mossed border, a fire on a hearth of dressed stone with the stores stacked inside the reach of its light, an oak grove at the four corners, and no pack, no trap and no puzzle anywhere in it.
- **It mends the most of anything in the site** - **twice** what a bare sanctuary's floor pays a beat - it hands you the full heal on arrival like every camp, and it is the one quiet room depth does not thin out, so the camp that survives a deep run is the camp with nothing in it but the healing.
- **Rare-medium, and never out of reach.** The heaviest of the four camps and no heavier: it can be rolled from the first chamber past the threshold on, in **every** dungeon, and it is deliberately not rare enough to be a lucky roll - the walk to it is the price.
- Its payout is the mend rather than a second purse: one ordinary chest, no rich one.

## 1.9.26

### 🏦 The Expedition Shop, and Expedition EXP to spend in it
- **A permanent shop, open whether or not you are standing in a site.** Every counter in this mod was inside something - the supplier's window only exists during a run, the descent menu only matters at the moment you decide to go in. This one is different, because what it sells is not for a run: it is what the **runs have added up to**.
- **Runs pay money. Runs also pay EXP, and EXP is permanent.** One point per **$5,000** of secured loot - so the two-hundred-thousand run pays about **40**, deliberately: one good run buys one level of one line, and the whole tree is something like fifty runs of work. A currency a good night maxes out is a currency with nothing left to say.
- **Six lines, and they are the requests read back.** *Windstep* is speed while you are inside a site, *Ironblood* is strength, *Second Wind* is **one fatal blow a run survived**, *Longer Rope* is **a minute more on the clock per level**, *Deep Pockets* is **more room in the pack the entrance hands you**, and *The Broker* is **the chance an Expedition Broker is standing in a chamber**. Costs climb a rung per level, the ladder is the whole price, and everything is read through one named number per line so the run and the shelf cannot disagree.
- **Opened three ways, and it is the same window all three times.** `/expedition shop`, a slot in the descent window next to the compass, and the **Expedition Broker** - an ordinary villager who rolls into a chamber once you have bought the line. A shop with two inventories is a shop that can disagree with itself, so there is one.
- **The upgrades are felt, not just stored.** Speed and strength are re-applied every second for the length of a run (an effect handed over at the threshold and drunk away is a purchase you cannot rely on); the rope is added to the clock before a second of it is earned; Deep Pockets changes the pack at the moment the entrance gives it to you; and Second Wind spends itself on a real fatal blow, standing you back up at half health with a totem burst and letting the run go on.

### 💧 Flooded rooms stay flooded
- **The water goes in the floor instead of on top of it, which is the whole of the fix.** A layer laid over a walkable floor has a five-wide channel of air beside it - the dry cross the archways run off - and four open doorways at the end of it, so it ran out of the room in the first seconds of a run and left a dry hall with wet patches. Sunk a block, the pool's only neighbours at its own level are the chamber's own floor blocks, the wall band and the bedrock underfoot. You step down into it and wade.
- **And an invisible barrier under anything the pool cannot trust**, because barriers are the one block in the game that is watertight without being visible - so a hole left behind by an ore stud or a chest can no longer drain half a chamber into the rock while nobody is watching.

### 🚪 "Sometimes it just kicks me out of the expedition"
- **The deep exit pad has to be stood on now.** The entrance pad is announced from the first second of a run and you are told to walk back to it, so stepping onto that one is already deliberate. The deep pad is the opposite kind of thing: a 2x2 square of glowstone rolled into a random chamber at depth six and beyond, which used to extract on a **four-block-radius brush** - so a player crossing a chamber they had never seen could be paid out and thrown home mid-run with no idea what had happened to them. It now takes **two seconds of standing on it**, it says so when you find it, and the run goes on if you walk off.

### ⛺ Three camps, and a room that is just beautiful
- **Camps are the site's commonest rooms now, not a lucky roll.** "Find a camp" is the plan a run is built around, and a camp the maze rolls one time in twenty is a plan nobody can make - so the two existing camps are among the heaviest faces in the deck, and there is a **third**: *Wayfarer's Waystation*, a caravan stopped dead in the middle of the chamber with its fire still burning, hay underfoot, barrels still full, lamps on posts at the corners and a rug through the middle. It mends like a sanctuary, a little better.
- **And a new room that is here to be looked at: the Moonlit Chapel.** A quartz nave between two rows of pillars, candles down both aisles, a ceiling punched through with its own **star field** and a moon over the altar, and an altar at the end of it holding the chamber's best chest. Nothing about it is novel mechanically - it is a walk and a payout - and that is the point: a dungeon whose every room is a decision is a dungeon nobody looks up in.

### 🧾 Six new checks
- The EXP rate (a two-hundred-thousand run is worth tens, never hundreds, and EXP is proportional to the payout), the shelf (six lines, each with a climbing cost ladder whose stated total is its own rungs, and each worth something different at its last level than at none), a whole level bought-kept-and-felt (charged once, on the ledger, and worth the one minute it is sold as), **every line read back through the exact seams the run uses** and every one of them moving, the deep pad's hold, and the three camps being rollable and at least a fifth of the deck two chambers in.

## 1.9.25

### 🧪 The Potion Belt is a brewing stand you carry
- **Filling a flask is brewing now, not shopping.** The belt used to sell refills for money, which made it a vending machine wearing a brewing stand's icon: the flasks were a purchase rather than a craft, so carrying the right ingredients into a fight and knowing the recipes was not part of it at all. The belt's own window now holds a **brewing bay** - a bottle, an ingredient and blaze powder to burn - and it brews through the game's own recipe book, so **water and nether wart become an awkward potion** and awkward plus sugar becomes Swiftness, exactly as they do at a stand.
- **The moment a finished potion of one of the four kinds is sitting in the bottle slot, the belt drinks it and that flask is full again.** Four flasks, four potions: Speed (Swiftness, long and strong), Fire Resistance, Night Vision and Strength - and the long and strong brews of each count. A **splash or lingering** potion does not: a thrown potion is a different item for a different job, and converting one into a draught would be a quiet upgrade rather than a recipe.
- **The bay says what to brew.** The window's middle slot is a live recipe card that reads what is in the bottle, what it becomes, and a progress bar while it brews; a flask with nothing in it now tells you to go and brew one instead of quoting you a price.
- **A brew takes twenty seconds, the same as the block it copies** - a belt that brews quicker than a brewing stand is a stand nobody would ever place. And the belt is on the **non-placeable** list by type, so a brewing stand you carry can never become a block in the world.

### ⛏️ The expedition is a trip with a health budget
- **Saturation healing inside a site is a fifth of what it is outside one.** Vanilla saturation regen is the largest free healing in the game - up to six health a second while a body is full - and it arrived in a dungeon whether or not the explorer did anything to earn it. A meal still feeds a run and no longer carries it: **food eaten in a site heals 80% less**, and the number is a seam the build can assert rather than a constant buried in an injector.
- **Supply crates mend you now, and they glow.** Opening one gives back **four hearts** on top of what it always gave, with a heart-and-totem burst at the block - and every crate within sight now carries a **rising halo** for as long as it is standing, so the thing you are walking toward is visible from across the chamber before you know what it is.

### 🧭 Ruins do not stack, and nothing spawns on the roof
- **"I could not advance because the new expedition did not delete the old ones."** A site is permanent and a run's blocks are never removed, so the site has to be: it is handed back when a run ends, but the **ground is burnt**. A site that has ever held a run is never handed out again, and the next explorer of that dungeon is given a lane slot nobody has ever built in - instead of a maze still standing from somebody else's run, with their cage bars in the archways and their chests already emptied.
- **The ledger is saved with the codex**, and a single block read at the middle of where that site's threshold would be is the answer for the ground a rolled-back save has forgotten.
- **Vanilla's own spawner stands down inside the expedition realm, and only there.** What it placed up on the roofs was a body no room owned - no pack, no glow, and a chamber that could never be cleared. Every other dimension keeps spawning exactly as it did.
- **Packs land where a body can actually stand.** A room's spawn point is rolled against two questions now - a cell with floor under it and air for a head, then a fallback to any cell with nothing solid in the way - and a guardian's adds use the same search, so a pack that used to fail to place (or place itself into the ceiling) has somewhere to arrive. **Any enemy standing in a chamber that the room did not spawn is marked for the glow sweep**, so nothing hostile in a room you are clearing can hide from you - and a stray body can never make a chamber unclearable.

### 🧭 The Expedition Compass, and a wait that is a number
- **A fatal blow costs eight minutes**, in the middle of the five-to-ten window: long enough that a run is a decision rather than a retry button, short enough that "when can I go back" is a number and not an evening. It used to be five.
- **The wait has exactly one way out: the new Expedition Compass.** Right-click it and the site takes you back early, with an end-rod and enchant burst and the clock named in the message. It is bought at the descent menu for **$25,000** - where the wait is announced, because an item that answers a problem has to be where the problem is - and the compass opens its own line in the descent window.
- **It is spent only when there is a wait to erase.** A click with nothing to skip says so rather than eating the item, because a wait nothing can shorten is a tax on the player who just lost a run, and an item that can be wasted by somebody who does not need it yet is a worse item than one that cannot.

### 🛡️ A hovering operator is not an invalid move
- **"It says invalid move in creative while I am flying and not moving."** A hovering operator is a body the **server** keeps repositioning - every scripted carry, every ability that writes a position, every correction the module itself issues - so it reads as a run of teleports, which is the exact shape a client abusing stand-downs makes. The administrative stand-down is now the line between the two: a **flying flag, creative mode or spectator** is the world's own state and not a claim, so the correction rule stays quiet on it.
- **Test mode still takes the excuse away**, exactly as every other stand-down in the module is lifted there: an operator who asked to be judged is judged. And a plain survival body is not excused at all, so the finding is untouched where it was doing its job.

### 🧾 Nine new checks, and they are the requests read back
- The site's regen scale (a fifth, and the seam stays a function of where the body is), the **realm answering for its own monsters** (and no other level), the **ground ledger** taken-remembered-skipped with the release that must *not* forget the ground, the fatal wait asserted against **both** ends of the five-to-ten window, and the compass refused on an empty clock, spent on a real one and billed at exactly its own price.
- On the belt: that it is unplaceable by type and not declared placeable, that Swiftness fills the Speed flask while a **Long Night Vision and a Strong Strength** still fill theirs, that a **splash and an awkward** potion fill nothing, and that a draught spends a charge, lands its effect, and is refused a second flask inside the shared cooldown while a brew tops the flask back to full.
- On the anticheat: a survival body is not excused, **null is not excused**, and an ability-flying body, a creative one and a spectator all are - with test mode lifting it and leaving it restored.

## 1.9.24

### 🏛️ The Auction House is a market now
- **It was one paged list of everything, which is a fine market for twenty listings and an unusable one for two hundred.** A player hunting a stack of iron and a player hunting a sword read the same page, and the only instrument either of them had was the next-page arrow. The window has **three shelves' worth of instruments** now instead of one list and one arrow.
- **Three views, and the one that was genuinely missing is the third.** *Whole market* is what was there before; *my listings* is what you have for sale; and *my bids* is the view the house never had at all - a player who bid and walked away had no way whatsoever to see what their money was committed to, which listings were waiting on them and which had quietly been taken back off them. The view button walks all three, and right-clicking walks it backwards.
- **Shelves, and a search that composes with them.** Tools & Gear, Blocks, Food & Farming, Materials, Machines & Mods and Misc, plus a search that matches an item's name, **a seller's name**, or a listing number someone pasted into chat as `#42`. Searching "iron" on the Materials shelf is the one query a player actually makes, so a search and a shelf are two filters that multiply rather than two buttons that fight.
- **Five orderings instead of newest-first.** Cheapest, dearest, **ending soon** and most contested join the list. Most contested is the one the old list could not express at all: what is worth a look is what other people are already fighting over.
- **Every row now says which of them is about to go, and what it is to you.** A **ten-cell time bar** coloured by how much is left, how many people are in the bidding and who is leading, the seller's name - and the line that matters most, which reads **YOURS**, **YOU ARE WINNING** or **OUTBID** depending entirely on who is reading it, because the same row is read by the seller, by the player winning it and by everybody else at once. Glint is spent on three things rather than everything: what you are winning, what has been taken off you, and what is closing.
- **Bidding is three buttons where it was one.** Left-click **BID** takes the minimum, left-click the **+10%** button beside it jumps the queue one step, and **right-click BID** opens a prompt to type an amount of your own - the house quotes the minimum, your balance and whether you already hold the top bid *before* it takes the money. A seller's own listing turns those two buttons into **cancel** and **reprice**, and reprice is correctly refused the moment somebody has bid.
- **A bidder can see their own money.** The window's balance line now reads what is **committed to live bids** alongside cash and pending `/claim` items, and says outright that it comes back in full if someone outbids them.
- **The seller's side got a panel, and the house got a help page.** *Sell held item* prices and lists what is in your hand from inside the window; an empty shelf, an empty search and an empty bids list each say **what to do about it** rather than showing a blank grid; and the help button spells out selling, bidding, buying, payouts and every command, so the window explains itself.
- **`/auction search <text>`, `/auction mine` and `/auction bids`.** The window can already open on a search and on a view, so the commands that advertise themselves in the help page now actually exist instead of being a promise.
- **The numbers underneath are pinned by tests.** Six new checks cover the view and sort cycles, the shelf each kind of item files onto (sword, armour, ingot, coal, block, food, wheat, misc, and a stamped mod machine), that "what does it cost" has one answer from the opening price through the increment to the zero-increment clamp, that **the ending-soon flag is a real minority of a listing's life** - it used to fire at fifty minutes, which lit a sixth of every half-hour listing - the three ways a search matches, and the four readings of one status line.

## 1.9.23

### 🗡️ The guardian fight is fought at the arena's size
- **The arena's extra distance is now the fight's, not just the room's.** Eighty-four blocks of floor is a longer fight, not a bigger one, and the old moveset treated it as a bigger one: a ring eight blocks out, a rush of a fixed twenty-two ticks and a rockfall aimed at where you were standing. The arena runs a **four-move cycle** now - one move more than a chamber, and the fourth is the arena's own - and it exercises the room rather than being scaled to it.
- **The slam is a shockwave.** Rock erupts at his feet and then a wall of rubble **runs outward across the floor** for about a second until it has crossed the room, drawn where it actually is each tick. It does not climb: the terrace three blocks above the pit is the answer, so the room's height is now part of its length and *"get out of the way"* is a decision about which way.
- **The rush is measured against the distance, and a miss costs him.** A charge that always ended after its own fixed twenty-two ticks could not cross the room it was built for, so the run is longer the further away you are - and a rush that ends with nothing in front of it leaves **him jammed against the wall he ran into, staggered and open**, which is the window the whole dodge is worth.
- **The rockfall lands where you are going.** The line of ceiling is thrown ahead of your own movement, so walking out of it without thinking no longer works, and the **thrown pillar** - a column torn out of his own colonnade, hurled down the lane at where you will be, and landing as a debris ring a doorway wide - is the one move in the mod that answers distance instead of position. A boss whose only ranged answer was a slow rockfall could be kited across his own hall forever.
- **And the wide arena is built by a test now, not by a comment.** A new self-test carves a real three-by-three arena into a real level through the same builder the game calls, then reads the blocks back: eighty-four across, **nine cells and no wall standing on any of the seams between them**, all nine cell middles somewhere a body can stand, and every opening cut through the **outer ring** with exits on more than one side. A rework that quietly grew a wall through the middle of the boss room fails the build.

### ⛺ Camps matter, and can be found
- **Healing inside a site is worth half of what it was, and comes half as often.** An expedition is a trip with a health budget, and mending in it was cheap enough that a careful player never once had to think about a rest - which made every quiet chamber scenery. Chamber mends and supply crates give `50%`, the regen a resting place gives is `50%`, and it ticks every **eighty** ticks instead of forty. A camp's arrival mend is the one heal left whole: the camp is what you walk to, not what you live in.
- **The camps are findable.** A rest site you cannot find is not a rest site, so a sanctuary is now the **commonest of the quiet rooms** rather than one face among thirty, every camp within a hundred and ten blocks burns a **column of light** from its floor to its roof, and walking into any chamber with one a chamber or two away tells you **which way it is and how far**.
- **Reader's Rest: the new reading room, and the site's second camp.** Bookshelf walls from floor to ceiling, a floor of planks with a carpet running through it and rugs at the corners, a reading dais of shelves in a ring around an **enchanting table**, lanterns over it, and **barrels of enchanted books** - real books off the world's own enchantment registry, clamped to each enchantment's own maximum level. Ripe at depth two and rarer the deeper a run goes, and it mends you completely when you walk in.

### 🖥️ The machines have windows
- **The Chunk Anchor opens a window**: which nine chunks it is holding, how many of them are loaded *right now*, how many anchors you hold and what the next one costs, a button that draws the boundary again, and a button that **buys the next anchor on the spot**.
- **The Item Sorter opens its filter, as the items themselves.** Every filtered item is drawn in its own slot; clicking one in the window takes it out, clicking one in your pack puts it in, and a button cycles what it may read from and another empties the list. Nothing in the window is consumed - it is a list, not a chest.
- **The three farm machines share one window**: how far each reaches, **how many chests it has found and how much room they have** (the reason a harvester "does not work" is almost always that it has found nothing to put the drops in), a button that **draws the square it works** in the world, and a button that runs one second of it immediately.

### 🔗 The Chunk Anchor keeps its promise, and up to three of them
- **It loads what it says it holds.** Placing one forces the nine chunks *and asks for them there and then*, so a player who puts one down and walks away cannot catch the ground unloading first; every ticket is re-asserted on server start and once a minute after that.
- **Three per player, each dearer than the last.** One is a farm, three is a base, and the price ladder (`250,000`, then `600,000`, then `950,000`) is read from the anchor's own constant by the shop, the window and the placement rule alike. Picking one up makes the next one cheaper again, because the rule is about ground being held rather than money being spent.

### 🌱 The farm machines actually farm
- **The sprinkler was a fountain that did nothing.** Both of its writes went through freshly built property objects instead of the ones the blocks carry - and a rebuilt `moisture` or `age` property is not the same object as the block's own, so `hasProperty` answered false and every write went nowhere. Farmland now gets the moisture vanilla reads, and crops advance on **their own age property**, one stage at a time, never skipping one. That is why a sprinkler neither kept a field watered nor made it grow.
- **Bigger fields and chests that are actually found.** The planter reaches `7` blocks instead of four and the harvester and sprinkler `9` and `10`; and where all three used to look only at the five positions touching them - a chest you must build *against* the block - they now search a cube of three blocks around themselves, **nearest container first**, so the double chest beside the field is the one that gets the seed and the harvest.
- **A sprinkler cannot be broken by the weather.** It is a cauldron, so rain and flowing water filled it harmlessly - but lava poured in or snow landing on it turned it into a **different block** and the machine stopped being a machine. A filled cauldron of any kind now still counts as the sprinkler standing there, and the sprinkler **empties itself back out** on its next tick rather than being a casualty of the weather.

### 🍖 Cooking out of the backpack
- **The Portable Furnace and the Portable Campfire fuse into a backpack at the Item Forge.** Both were machines you had to put down, which is a strange thing for a *portable* furnace to be. Fused in, they cook out of the pack's own storage: the ore you just mined and the beef you just killed cook where they lie and sit back down cooked, and the pack's kitchen button cooks what it can right now and tells you which of the two things is missing when it cannot.
- **The two are different machines, and stay different.** A fused **furnace** smelts anything and **burns fuel out of the pack** - coal is eight smelts, a stack of coal is sixty-four, it takes the fuel as it needs it and banks the rest. A fused **campfire** is always lit, wants nothing at all, cooks **food only** and takes half again as long per item. One kitchen per pack, and a backpack with both machines in the shop is a backpack that gets to choose.

## 1.9.22

### 🏛️ The guardian's arena is three chambers wide
- **Eighty-four blocks across, and the maze is not allowed in it.** The arena was a single cell with a second storey on top of it, which made it a tall room rather than a big one: a guardian who can cross it in two seconds is a guardian fought in a cupboard, and the slam, the rush and the warp all had nowhere to go. It is a **three-by-three block of cells** now - the middle one is the room, the **eight around it are reserved** the moment the arena is rolled, so the maze never grows a wall through the middle of the fight - and the whole block is one floor from the pit to the underside of an eighty-four-wide coffered vault.
- **The doors moved to the edge.** The arena's middle has no wall in it to cut an archway through, so every way in and out is held by one of the eight rim cells and sits on the **outer ring**: an explorer who does not want the guardian can cross the terrace from edge to edge without ever stepping down into the pit, and the archways close only on the one who chose to drop in. Clearing the arena opens its exits **on three different sides of the block**.
- **Everything else scaled with it.** The pit is eighteen blocks each way from the middle instead of six, the terrace is a proper ring to fight on, the colonnade spreads to fill the room, the guardian's reach and his slam are written against the arena's own span rather than the cell it used to be, the collapse treats the whole block as one chamber (the rim cells come down as part of it), and the site's camera-facing rules follow you across all nine cells rather than only the middle one.

### 🧹 The site cleans up after itself
- **Every body an expedition spawns wears a tag, and the site's own tag is the only thing the sweep reads.** A pack is spawned persistence-required, so nothing natural ever took one away, a site is a permanent place, and a run ends without anybody walking back into the maze - which together added up to the next explorer of that ground starting in the last one's leftover pack. Every monster now carries `ff_expedition_mob` (written at the one factory they all come out of, so no spawn site can forget it), and **a run that ends - extracted, died, abandoned, logged off - takes its own bodies out of the world**. Farms are untouched by construction: no cow, villager, pet, or another dungeon's pack has ever been tagged, so the sweep has nothing to match.
- **A new expedition clears the ground first.** Anything still standing in the new site wearing a glow it cannot account for - a body from a run whose state was lost, or one from a build before the tag existed - is removed before the first block is carved, so **"why is there a glowing zombie in my brand new site"** has no way to happen again.

### 🌊 Falling under the site no longer throws you out of it
- **The abyss guard used to hand you to the exit pad, and the exit pad is an extraction.** A body that fell under the dungeon floor was teleported onto the entrance glowstone, and the very next tick read a body standing on the pad and **finished the run** - so the flooded chambers were a trap that could not be read as one: dive under the water in a flooded hall, drop into a pool, slip off the wreckage of a broken crossing, and the site threw you out of itself mid-run for going swimming. A rescue now puts you back on the floor of **the chamber you were already in** (your own column where it has a floor, the chamber's middle where it does not), and only falls back to the pad when the site itself is gone - and when it does, it disarms the pad for five seconds first, because a rescue is a landing and not an exit.

### ⛏️ The collapse falls at a speed you can run from
- **Chambers come down nearly twice as fast.** A chamber per `25` ticks was slow enough that the outer rooms falling was background noise while the explorer walked the maze; it is `14` now, and each doomed chamber is rubble for `10` ticks instead of `18` while laying that rubble `5` blocks a tick instead of `3`. The middle of the fall is shorter but no less visible - it is the same roof coming down harder, not a room that was deleted.

### 🛒 The shops stock what they say they stock
- **The eight late-game machines are on the Exclusive shelf.** They were defined, placeable, documented and functional, and not in the catalogue - which from the other side of the screen reads exactly like they were never added. The Chunk Anchor is priced from its own constant so the window and the machine cannot disagree, and a new self-test asks the shelf itself, so a machine that is minted without being priced fails the build.
- **The machines place.** Every one of them was missing from `ModItems.PLACEABLE_TYPES`, which is read fail-closed: the client showed the block for a tick, the server refused it, and the item stayed in the hand, which in play is *"the thing I just bought unpacks itself"*. All eight are declared placeable now, and the self-test asks each machine's own constructor rather than a list kept somewhere else.
- **The netherite ingot is off the expedition supplier.** A run's payout is the only thing that counter trades in, and an ingot was the one line that turned secured loot straight back into raw progression - the same armour a mine would have given, bought with a site nobody had to survive. The deep end sells what a run cannot dig for itself.
- **The supplier sells the Pack Upgrader, one per purchase.** The patch that adds a row to the Loot Backpack was a rich-chest find and nothing else, so a run that wanted one had to be lucky; it is on the counter now at `9,000`, priced against the Time Shard so carrying room and time stay a real decision, and the counter hands over exactly one every time.
- **Finding one in a chest is more likely too.** A rich chest held a patch `18%` of the time at best; it is `34%`, climbing `7%` per ten chambers and capped at `55%`. Still a rich chest or nothing, still a coin flip at the very deepest point of the maze - but a run that goes looking for one now has a fair chance of coming home with it.

### 📦 The Chunk Anchor finally shows you what it is holding
- **The outline of the nine chunks is drawn in the world**, at the anchor's own level, with the four corners and the anchor's column picked out brighter. It is the one machine whose entire job is invisible - it does something where you are not - so the thing it owes you is a way to see which farm is inside its ground and which one is one block outside it and quietly not growing. Drawn only when a player is within `72` blocks, so an anchor in a corner nobody is standing in costs one distance check.

## 1.9.21

### 🏚️ A chamber comes down before it is gone
- **The collapse has a middle, and the middle is made of the same stuff the end is.** A doomed chamber used to vanish in the same tick it was named - a wall of gravel where a room had been - so the only thing that told anybody the site was coming apart was a chat line three rooms back. Now the chamber **sheds**: real rubble lands on its floor a few blocks at a tick, gravel dust and smoke come off the roof over it, the odd lump of ceiling drops on whoever is still under it, and the chamber says so on the action bar **only when it is the room you are standing in**. The room tells you it is dying because it looks like it is dying.
- **The roof gives way when enough of it has already fallen in.** The gate is the rubble count rather than the clock: past `COLLAPSE_RUBBLE_TARGET` blocks and its own moment, the chamber **breaches** - a real blast with sound and a shockwave that throws whatever is standing in the hall off its feet, the pack under the roof dying with it - and then **every archway that led into it is choked with rubble**, eight blocks deep through both halves of the wall and into both halls, so the corridor beyond ends in a wall of gravel. A dead chamber with an open door in it was a shortcut through a collapse, and a chamber whose doorway was spotless never looked like it had fallen at all.
- The doorway lanes are kept clear while a chamber sheds. The roof can have the room; it does not get the door somebody is running through.

### 🗺️ Every run gets a site of its own
- **A site is handed out instead of assumed.** It was a constant per dungeon type, so two explorers who started the same dungeon were given the same coordinates and carved one maze between them: same rooms, same chests, same pack, same escape pad, and whichever of them cleared a chamber cleared it for both. Each run now takes the first slot in its dungeon's own lane that no other live run is standing in, and hands every slot back when the run ends - escape, death, the site taking them, or a body that logs off mid-run. The lanes themselves never move: the dungeons were always three thousand blocks apart and still are.
- **Descents take a slot too.** A ladder used to assume the next site north was free; it asks now, so a descent can no longer land on somebody else's maze (or on its own).

### 🏛️ The guardian's arena is a giant room, and it is a different one per dungeon
- **One space, not two.** The arena's own roof comes off and a second storey is built on top of it - **22 blocks** from the pit to the underside of a coffered vault, with the lower hall's ceiling left open into it. It is the only chamber in the maze that is a single room from floor to roof.
- **A pit, a terrace, and stairs between them.** The middle twelve blocks stay at the dungeon's floor level and the whole rim around it is raised three. The guardian's **Slam erupts along the floor he is standing on**, so three blocks of terrace is three blocks the blast does not climb: the high ground is where the slam cannot reach you, and both the **Rush** and the **Warp** are written to answer it (the warp now arrives at *your* height rather than at the pit's).
- **A colonnade that breaks sightlines**, in a different arrangement and material in every one of the seven dungeons, and a floor that does something per dungeon too: the temple's pit floods (and slows every step in it), the forge's rim is on fire, the crypt's is snow over ice, the cavern's corners are amethyst. The arena's ore studs are gone with it - a boss room is not a seam, and the arena already pays a bounty.

### 🧰 Eight late-game machines
- **Chunk Anchor** - holds the **3x3 chunks** around itself loaded whether or not anybody is standing there, so crops grow and furnaces burn offline. The most expensive thing in the shop and limited to **one per player**, refused at the block rather than at the till. It gives its chunks back the moment it is picked up or uprooted, and re-asserts them on every restart.
- **Repair Station** - the anvil's job, priced instead of enchanted. Put a damaged tool in the middle slot and pay in **money**: no levels, no XP, no materials. The price is live and follows the tool (its own maximum durability, how far gone it is, and **every enchantment on it**), and it **rises with every repair that player has already bought**, up to double - a service, not a shortcut.
- **Item Sorter** - a hopper with a filter. Right-click it with an item to keep that item in or out, with an empty hand to see what it reads and move on to the next mode (**hoppers only / any container / both**), and with a **Backpack, a bundle or a shulker** to take the whole filter list off the bag in one click. It pulls matching items out of the containers around it and passes them on; everything else it leaves exactly where it was - and an **unconfigured sorter moves nothing at all**.
- **Portable Furnace** and **Portable Campfire** - a blast furnace and a smoker that fold up into your pack and come out wherever you are: **twice the speed of a plain furnace**, real vanilla screens, all the ore and food recipes they should take. Picking one up hands its contents back first, so a furnace with a stack of ore in it cannot eat the ore.
- **Potion Belt** - four draughts on your hip (**Speed, Fire Resistance, Night Vision, Strength**), drunk instantly from the belt rather than sipped from the hand. Each flask has its own cooldown and the belt itself settles between draughts, so four flasks is not four buffs in a row. Flasks are **brewed back full with money**, not ingredients.
- **Auto Planter** - stands beside farmland and keeps the ground sown, pulling seed out of the chest or hopper next to it: wheat, carrots, potatoes, beetroot, and nether wart on soul sand.
- **Auto Harvester** - cuts every **mature** crop in its radius and sweeps the yield into the container beside it, which is what makes it pair with the Auto-Sell Hopper instead of with your hands. Nothing immature is ever touched.
- **Irrigation Sprinkler** - keeps every farmland block in its radius at full moisture with no channel to dig, and the water it throws is a shove on whatever is growing: crops in its radius come in sooner. It rains on its own clock, so a field of them is not a fountain.

## 1.9.20

### 👻 Vanish hides what you are holding, for real
- **The held item is taken out of the hand, not just hidden in the packets.** Emptying the slots in the packets was enough for everybody except the one body that decides how it looks: your own client draws your own held item from your own inventory and never reads the packet, so a vanished moderator looked at themselves and saw the sword still there. The hand is genuinely emptied now - every tick, so an item picked up mid-vanish goes the way of the armour.
- **The moderation kit is the one stack that stays.** It is handed over by `/ff anticheat spectate` and a kit item parked in the wardrobe cannot be right-clicked, so the exemption is asked of the kit's own predicate rather than a list kept beside it. The kit is now handed over **before** the vanish rather than a line after it, so the exemption applies to it and the vanish never parks it on the way past.

### 🗺️ The sidebar stops covering the screen
- **`You are in [room]` is now just `[room]`.** A sidebar is exactly as wide as its longest line, so eleven characters of nothing were paid for by every line on the screen being eleven characters wider. The chamber's name alone says where you are, and the condition rides beside it because a flooded hall is a different fight and not a footnote.
- The other widest lines are trimmed to match: `Next guardian in 10 chambers` is `Guardian in 10 halls`, `THE GUARDIAN HOLDS THE DESCENT` is `THE GUARDIAN HOLDS`, and `Return Compass points to the pad` is `Compass points at the pad`.

### 🛤️ The rails are on the floor of the Deep Mine, not in it
- The haul road was written into the **floor cell**, so every rail in the mine was swallowed by the rock it was laid on. It runs one block up now - the cell a cart would actually roll through - and only into empty space, so it can no longer overwrite the room it is built in.

### 🪨 A site will not hand you its own ceiling
- **Gravel drops nothing inside a site until the site is coming down.** The maze's gravel is the cracked roof of an unstable chamber and the rubble that roof sheds; mining the hazard out for a stack of blocks was income the run's ledger never priced. The rule is the ore rule's twin - one guard on the funnel every break goes through - and it lifts during the collapse, because that is the site's last act and the rubble is what it leaves.
- The unstable roof's falling lump no longer becomes an item either. It shatters where it lands.

### 👁️ The pack's glow is borrowed, and released properly
- **The 1.9.19 fix released half a mark.** A mark is two mechanisms - the glowing tag that draws the outline and the team membership that colours it - and only the team was ever released. A body with the tag and no team does not stop glowing; it glows **white**. Since a marked pack is persistent by design and a site is a fixed coordinate per dungeon type, every marked monster that outlived its run stayed standing exactly where the next run is built, wearing a bright outline nobody had given it: read as "passively spawned mobs still glow".
- **Both halves are released now, on every way a body can leave** - the death handler, the end of a run, and a sweep that runs every second and drops the ledger entries for bodies that are gone or dead, which is what catches a monster that was discarded or unloaded rather than killed. Nothing glows in a site unless a run that is running right now says so.

### 🗿 The floor guardians fight back
- **Stronger: forty times the body, inside a band.** A guardian is given 500-1,400 health, 20 armour, 2.5x attack, 1.15x speed, 1.5x knockback and **0.85 knockback resistance** - the band is there because the six bodies he can be dressed in span sixteen to eighty health and forty times a spider is not forty times an elder guardian. He can no longer be juggled with a wind charge, and he cannot be killed by walking past him.
- **Four moves, three per dungeon, no two dungeons the same three.** A **Slam** erupts the floor of the arena in a ring of rock and throws everything inside it off its feet. A **Rush** puts his shoulder down and crosses the hall at sprint-and-a-half - faster than any explorer, through whatever is in the line. A **Rockfall** opens the ceiling in a line between him and you, so the ground you are about to stand on is what moves. A **Warp** puts him through the rock and out behind your back, arriving with the swing already going.
- **Nothing is announced.** A slam is an explosion of rock at his feet, a rush is a shoulder coming down the hall, a rockfall is the ceiling opening above you and a warp is a portal flash behind your back - the only warning is the one the world gives. Past a third of his health he **stops holding back** (speed, strength and resistance, with no speech about it).

## 1.9.19

### ⛏️ An expedition ore pays the run, not the pocket
- **Ores in a site drop nothing and pay money instead.** Instant diamonds made every chest in the dungeon a formality - the item in your pocket was worth more than the ledger credited and it came home whether the run did or not. The drop itself is cancelled at the one funnel every break goes through, keyed on the breaker, so the ore still breaks and still pays `ExpeditionManager.onBlockBroken`'s value onto the run and simply leaves nothing behind. The mining tree's own perks (Fortune, Gemsmith, Excavator, Auto-Smelt) do not run inside a site either, because every one of them hands over a stack and a stack is what an expedition ore may not become.
- **Killing is the small income now: every body pays about a quarter of what it did.** A shallow run used to be a fight with some looting attached - ten ordinary bodies outpaid every chest in the chamber. Zombie/husk/drowned $150 -> **$38**, skeleton/stray $175 -> $45, creeper $300 -> $75, witch $350 -> $90, vindicator $500 -> $140, wither skeleton $550 -> $150, ravager $1,500 -> **$400**. The chests, the ores and clearing the chamber are the money; a pinned self-test fails if ten bodies ever outpay the shallowest chamber bounty.

### 🌊 A chamber is also in a state
- **New chamber conditions, rolled once when the room is carved and readable from the room itself:** **Flooded** (water across the floor with a dry cross at the archways, so it slows a fight without walling it in), **Burning** (braziers and magma burning through the netherrack), **Unlit** (every lamp in the room is out - the pack's black outline becomes the only thing you see of them), **Overgrown** (moss beds, hanging roots, leaf cover) and **Unstable** (rubble on the floor, and a body-sized lump of the roof coming down on whoever is standing under it every two seconds). A little over half of all chambers are left ordinary on purpose.
- The condition is announced once on the way in, carried on the sidebar beside the room's name, and never rolls on the threshold, a sanctuary or the guardian's arena.
- **The sidebar says where you are again, in its own bold line**: `You are in [room name] [condition]`, above the clock and the ledger. A chamber is built out of the same rock as every other one and the only wall that differs is the one you never see, so the room's name is the first thing worth knowing.

### 🔥 The Magma Forge's floor is netherrack with magma through it
- The forge floor was blackstone with one trench of lava in it. It is netherrack now with **magma crust across a third of it** - the site costs something to walk across rather than being a flat floor with a hazard drawn on it - and the braziers, anvils on chains and quench cauldrons all still stand.

### ⏳ The clock is a countdown, not a fuse
- **Low time summons rather than nudges.** From three-quarters of the clock the site starts sending packs at you: small ones at first, then **a swarm every eight seconds in the last tenth**, scaling with how deep you got, with the action bar saying how many just woke.
- **At zero, the site comes down chamber by chamber, outermost first, and the threshold is last.** Each chamber is buried under rubble with its pack crushed inside it, and **if the roof finds you, that is the run** - a fatal ejection: the loot is written off, your items are safe, and you are put back where you came in. A pad still works during all of it, so a player who planned their route can still sprint home. All the ways of being thrown out of a run - the fatal blow and the roof - now go through one method and read the same to the player.
- **Fixed: a descent ladder could end the run instead of starting a new site.** A body that arrives ON a pad by teleport and a body that walked back to it are the same reading one tick later, so the pads could read a landing as an extraction. The pads now do not answer for the first five seconds at a site, and a body that reads as somewhere else is pulled back to its pad for a full second before the run counts as left - which is the teleport, not an exit.

### 🛡️ The anticheat says what it caught
- **Every check is named in words and removed with a line of its own.** `ore-vision` is now **X-Ray (seeing ore through stone)**; `flight` is **Flight**, `killaura` is **Kill aura**, `autoclicker` is **Auto-clicker**, `scaffold` is **Scaffold** and so on - and each carries its own kick line instead of one shared sentence: *"Legit, you were too obvious with that one."* / *"Really? Flying? That's just pathetic."* / *"Your arm is not that long, and I measured it twice."* / *"That block had a health bar, not a light switch."* The name and the line go to the player on the kick screen, into the strike warning, onto the staff alert and into the ledger, so nobody is left reading a key of the week.
- **Ore vision no longer fires on coal.** The three-buried-ores rule counted *everything* - three unlucky coals is a hillside with a cave in it - so the bar is on the **valuable** ores now (diamond, emerald, gold, debris) and the session still has to have cut **40 blocks of real rock** before the claim means anything.

### 👻 A glow mark is given back when its body dies
- The pack's black outline is a scoreboard team keyed by a body's name, and a name outlives the body that held it: a marked zombie dies, the next naturally spawned zombie is handed the same name **and walks around wearing a pack's outline it was never part of**. Every marked body now gives its name back the moment it stops existing, whoever killed it and whether or not anybody did.

## 1.9.18

### 🏛️ Six new chambers, and a roll that reads the dungeon
- **New faces: Beast Menagerie, Star Observatory, Steam Baths, Clockwork Hall, Ossuary, Gilded Vault** - thirty faces now, each with its own build, its own props and its own pack. The gilded vault pays three chests; the menagerie is a ring of open pens you can fight behind; the baths are three sunken pools over soul sand that lift a body out of one.
- **The roll is a weighted pool, not a band ladder.** Thirty faces do not fit in a hundred-wide ladder of three-point slices, so every face carries a weight, **depth gates the rooms worth the walk down** (gilded vault past chamber eight, observatory/ossuary/clockwork past five), and **the dungeon's character multiplies what is left** - a crystal cavern has no web nests or larders to roll, a crypt has no sunken garden, a magma forge has no library, and the star observatory is likelier in a cavern than a combat pit is. The Fountain Court is rollable again, which it had quietly stopped being.
- **Six new faces are priced and populated like the rest:** pack sizes (menagerie +3, ossuary +2, clockwork +1, vault/observatory/baths -2) and chamber bounties (vault and observatory 1.6x, menagerie/ossuary/clockwork/armoury 1.3x, baths and fountain 0.6x).

### 👹 No chamber is one mob tall
- **Every face now holds a roster of three or four kinds, and so does every dungeon.** A web nest is cave spiders, spiders and silverfish; a vault is vindicators, evokers, ravagers and pillagers; a void hall mixes a blaze with a phantom and an endermite; a tomb mixes strays with polar bears. A pack that mixes a fast body with a ranged one is a room you have to move in rather than a room you stand in.
- **The Monster Cave keeps its diamond-clad patrol as one body in four** and fills the rest of its halls from a roster, so a cave of ten chambers is no longer the same fight ten times. Every kind any roster can produce is now priced on the kill (guardian 400, elder guardian 1,200, phantom 300, vex 325, silverfish/endermite 100, magma cube 275, zombie piglin 300, hoglin 500, polar bear 300, slime 120), so no kill quietly falls through to the default.

### 🖼️ The dressing pass, and a geode that reads as a geode
- **A new last pass dresses every chamber:** sconces between the pilasters with the site's own lamp on each bracket, short chains off the beams with lanterns on the long ones, crates and barrels against the mid-walls, and a medallion inlaid in the floor at each mid-edge. Everything asks `dressable` first and refuses a doorway, so it fills what is left and never paints over a face's own furniture.
