It also priced the refund per *level* while the whole-tree respec priced it per *point invested*. Both paths now free the investment once, at the cost it actually cost, and the per-tree figure the screen shows is cost-weighted to match.
- **The screen is three rows taller, and that is the rework.** Six trees, a point total, a respec and a close button used to share twenty-seven slots and say everything else in a tooltip, because there was nowhere else to put it - a card could not say how far into a level you were *and* what you had to spend, which are the two facts a player opens the screen to read. Six rows buys both, per card: the bar with its percentage, the exact `XP / needed` numbers, unspent points for *that* tree, a ✔ on a finished one, and the whole system's rules (the cap, the capstone, the real cost of the last level, completed trees out of six, total level out of sixty) in the book at the top. The tree page gained a real header - level, XP numbers, points invested, and its own spend - and upgrade rows that print their pips, their cost and exactly how many points short you are.
- Pinned by `skills.the-tree-caps-and-the-capstone-pays` (the curve actually rises; more XP than a tree can hold lands on exactly the cap; a finished tree does not climb further; the capstone pays; the total level cannot exceed its own ceiling; and one finished tree is not a finished player) and `skills.a-refund-returns-each-point-once` (a Cost-3 level reports as three points invested, the refund frees exactly three, the tree is back to what it earned, and it can never spend more than it earned). The build audit fails if the level stops being bounded, if a finished tree banks XP again, if the refund stops freeing the investment, or if the buying rules stop being reachable without a player to pin them.

## 1.8

### 💪 Every boss hits a quarter harder, wears its own aura, and has one move nobody had
- **One layer for fifteen fights, instead of fourteen hand-tunings.** This mod has fifteen bodies that raise a boss bar - the eight original raids, the six scripted ones with their own fight maps, and the reworked Wither and Ender Dragon - each in its own file with its own damage constants. "The bosses are too easy" is now answered in one place, `BossEmpowerment`, so there is no way for the fight next door to be balanced differently for no reason. It is deliberately small numbers: **the bosses deal 25% more damage**, read in the damage pipeline rather than per attack, so a melee swing, a skull, a breath lance and a scripted slam all get it without a single call site to remember - and cannot be doubled, because there is exactly one place it is multiplied in.
- **And every boss now reads as a boss from across the room.** A themed aura follows each one - a rising helix in its own colour, plus the particle family its body already suggests (soul fire for the Wither Skeleton, sculk for the Warden, crimson for the phantom-built Scarlet Devil, void for the dragon) - so no fight is a mob with a health bar any more.
- **Plus one move they all share, because of the one thing every player does.** Standing next to a boss and swinging is the answer to nearly every fight here, and nothing punished it. Below half health, a boss now **winds up in the open for a full 1.5 seconds** - with its own sound, a flash and an action-bar warning - and then everything within four blocks is hit and thrown off its feet. It fires at most once every twenty seconds, never while the boss is invulnerable (arriving, rising, mid-ceremony or dying) and never with nobody within twenty blocks, so a cutscene cannot be interrupted by it and an empty room cannot waste it.
- **Pinned twice.** `bosses.one-multiplier-for-every-fight` requires the multiplier to be a buff and a modest one, the wind-up to be long enough to answer, the cooldown and radii to be sane, and a player *not* to count as an empowered boss. The static audit pins the shape of it: the multiplier may be named in exactly three files - declared in `BossEmpowerment`, applied once in `CombatBalanceMixin`, read by the self-test - and the mixin may read it exactly once, because a second application is a silent doubling that nobody would look for in a fight-balance change.

### 📋 The job board refills as you clear it, and pays for the player you have become
- **Claiming a job rolls a fresh one into the slot it leaves.** The board is still an hourly board - an unfinished job is still replaced when the hour turns - but a player who cleared all five in ten minutes used to spend the next fifty looking at five struck-through cards, which reads as a board that has stopped working rather than one that resets on the hour. The replacement is drawn from the pool that slot has always drawn from, so the board stays five *kinds* of work instead of slowly turning into five mining jobs.
- **And the same five errands are no longer the same fifty dollars for everyone.** Both the goal and the pay scale with the sum of the player's six skill levels (the number their own skill board already shows), and **the pay scales twice as fast as the goal**: a veteran is asked for half again as much work and paid twice as much, where before they were asked for exactly what a first-hour player was asked for, for exactly the same money. A job cannot be levelled into worthlessness that way.
- Pinned by `jobs.board-refills-the-slot-you-claim` (an unfinished job pays nothing *and* is not replaced, a finished one pays exactly its reward, the replacement arrives un-claimed and at zero progress, and no slot pays twice) and `jobs.the-board-scales-with-the-player`, which hands the **same seeded die** to a fresh player and a maxed one - so both draw the same job out of the same pool and the only difference left is the player - and requires the maxed player's version of that job to ask for more and pay more.

### 🌪 A wither that outlives its summoners becomes somebody's job
- **The dynamic board answers what is happening on the server - and one of the biggest things that can happen was invisible to it.** The reactive jobs keyed off the fights `BossManager` can name: a boss that killed a player, a kill spree, a player carrying the Bad Omen. A **player-summoned vanilla wither** is none of those, so the one fight a group can start and then all die to produced no job at all and just kept killing whoever wandered past.
- **It does now.** The party standing there when a wither tears out of the ground is recorded, and the moment every one of them is down or logged out, the board posts the fight - its own job, `§8Urgent: Slay the Wither`, with the largest bounty the board can carry. It also goes up on the first player the wither kills, the same way a boss's does, and completing it pays three gems. A wither summoned alone in a corner nobody saw gets **no** job: an empty party is not "everyone fell", it is a fight no player was ever part of.
- Pinned by `contracts.a-party-that-fell-is-a-job`, which tests the rule as a function - a full party, a party with one member left standing, an entirely fallen party, and an empty one - so the decision cannot drift into "any death posts a bounty".

### 📊 Four more lines, including one you write yourself
- **The board used to be six readouts of things the server knows about you** - the address, your tag and guild, gems, money, how many players are online - and none of them were things you had *done*. It now also carries **your skill levels**, **your playtime**, **the job you are closest to finishing**, and **a line that is entirely your own** (a motto, a goal, a hello).
- **All four behave exactly like the six that were already there**: each is a switch, each is named for a human, each can be moved up and down the board, and each is in the same order list - so `on` / `off` / `toggle` / `move` and the edit screen all understand them without being told. The one new command is `/ff scoreboard custom <text>` (colour codes welcome, blank allowed - a blank line simply draws nothing, which is the one thing the board's other two texts may not be, since they are its identity rather than decoration), and the screen gained a third tile and a matching rename prompt behind it.
- **A board that grows no longer throws away the order you arranged.** A stored line order is repaired by adding what is missing and dropping what no longer exists instead of falling back to the default, because an order that predates these lines is still the right answer for the lines it names. Pinned by `stats.scoreboard-carries-the-player-too`, which walks every line, requires each switch to move only its own line, requires an old six-line order to survive with the new lines landing under it, and requires a blanked own line to leave the board one line shorter.

### 🏦 The vault keeps a statement, and says what it will be worth
- **The bank moves money while nobody is watching, which is exactly what a statement is for.** "It said interest was paid, where did it go" is a question about a number the player never saw change, so every deposit, withdrawal, daily interest payout and upgrade is now recorded - newest first, with how long ago it happened - on a **Statement** tile in the bank window. The log is bounded and lives beside the accounts rather than inside them, so no deposit path can drop a player's own history on the floor.
- **And the tile answers the other half of the question.** "If you leave the vault alone" shows the **7-day and 30-day** projection, **compound and capped exactly as the daily tick is**: each payout is added to the cash, so tomorrow's interest is charged on today's interest - up to the level's own daily cap and the vault's own ceiling, both applied in the projection. A projection that ignored either would promise a number the bank will never pay.
- Pinned by `bank.the-vault-keeps-a-statement`, which fills a throwaway account past the limit and requires the statement to stay bounded with the newest movement first, requires a full level-1 vault to project exactly one capped payout a day over thirty days, requires a small vault to project *more* than simple interest, and requires an empty vault to project nothing at all.

### 📊 The scoreboard is a screen, and it is yours alone
- **`/ff scoreboard` now opens the board rather than announcing it.** The board is per player and every part of it is a choice, so the command opens the screen that shows a player what they have and lets them change it, and the screen turns the board on as it opens. `/stats scoreboard` still reaches the same screen and bare `/stats` still just shows the board in chat. It is also a button on the **menu hub** ("/ff" was the only way in, and a hub that omits the one thing every player has is a hub with a hole in it).
- **A chest cannot draw a sidebar, so the screen writes the board down.** The tiles are the board top to bottom in the order it draws them, each one carrying its live text, and the preview sheet is the board as it reads that second - the banner, then every line that is switched on, drawn from the same lines the client is being sent. The invisible per-line marker that keeps two identical lines apart on a scoreboard is stripped for the GUI, where it would otherwise render as a stray letter.
- **And the two written texts are typed here now.** The title and address tiles open an **anvil prompt** - vanilla's own text box, the same one this mod already asks for token names and shop prices with - so the server address or the banner can be changed without knowing a command. Colour codes work, the result previews the text as it will read, over-long text is cut rather than refused, and the commands stay for anyone who would rather type it. Empty is refused in the prompt: wiping the banner should be a deliberate act, not a slip.
- **Every tile says who it is for.** The board is one objective and one display packet **per player** - that is what makes a personal sidebar possible at all, since vanilla's display slots are global - so the screen repeats it: *only you ever see this board*. Pinned by `commands.scoreboard-is-not-op-only` from the command side, and by three audit rules that keep the screen's two text prompts, the board preview and the hub button from being deleted quietly.

### 📋 Dynamic jobs are drawn, named and priced - not four canned errands
- **The catalogue is seven shapes, not four, and three of them name a thing.** "Dynamic jobs" were four templates with one title and one fixed reward each, rolled from a hat every half hour - so an evening showed a player the whole catalogue and then showed it again. There are now **dig orders** (mine N of *this block*, drawn from 27 ore and stone targets), **bounties** (slay N of *this mob*, from 22 of them) and **standing orders** (sell N of *this good*, from 13), alongside the original miner's rush, hunter's bounty, profiteer and arena star.
- **The goals and the payouts follow from the target instead of sitting next to it.** A goal is drawn from its target's own range - four to twelve diamonds, two to six ancient debris, a hundred and twenty-eight to two hundred and fifty-six cobblestone - raised a little for every extra player online, because the same errand is not the same work for one player and for a lobby. The reward is a fee plus a wage per unit, where the wage is the higher of the shape's floor and the target's own market worth out of `BlockValues` at a premium: a job that asks for diamonds pays like a job that asks for diamonds, and moves with the market instead of with a comment.
- **And the shapes come out of a bag, so no shape repeats before every shape has been used.** A roll per job meant four independent draws could ask for ores three times running, which a player reads as "there are three jobs" rather than as luck.
- **The named jobs are finishable by doing exactly what they say.** A dig order counts a broken block by its registry id *and* counts the deepslate form of an ore, because "mine 8x Diamond Ore" that ignored deepslate diamonds would be a job a player could not finish by obeying it. Bounties and standing orders needed hooks that knew *what* died or was sold: the kill hook now takes the body and the sell hook takes the stack. Pinned by `contracts.dynamic-jobs-are-drawn-not-canned`, which rolls sixty jobs and requires the shape bag to hold, every named target to resolve in the registry, every reward to sit inside the board's floor and cap, and then posts a real dig order and a real bounty and *breaks the block and kills the mob* to watch the progress move.

### ☄️ The meteor shower is one weather among twelve, and a crater is worth a walk
- **It was the likeliest event in the catalogue.** The shower held **two of the thirteen slots** in the automatic rotation while every other event held one - and the rotation could also roll the same event twice, which for a ten minute event is twenty. A meteor is now one slot in a twelve-event rotation, and no event can follow itself: the repeat is not re-rolled (which would only make a repeat unlikely), the roll steps to a different slot, so the rule is exact.
- **And a crater was the best renewable source of the two rarest materials in the game.** An impact every twelve seconds for ten minutes is fifty craters, and each paid two to four diamonds **on one crater in three** and a whole ancient debris **on one crater in four** - a diamond-and-netherite faucet aimed at whoever happened to be standing nearest. Impacts are now thirty seconds apart (twenty a shower, not fifty) and a crater pays iron, gold, coal and experience with **one diamond in five** and no netherite at all: still worth crossing the map for, no longer where netherite comes from.
- Pinned by `events.no-weather-twice-in-a-row-and-no-meteor-faucet`, which rolls the rotation four thousand times (no event twice running, the shower under 12% of rolls, the rare aurora still rarer than it) and then rolls four thousand craters (no netherite material at all, a diamond at most one crater in three, never more than two gold ingots or fourteen items).

### 📊 `/ff scoreboard` - the sidebar a player owns, under the command they already type
- **The sidebar is now reachable where players actually look.** `/stats scoreboard` was the only way in, and `/stats` is not a command most of a server ever types. `/ff scoreboard` shows the board and leaves it on, `/ff scoreboard disable` takes it away, and `/ff scoreboard edit` opens the screen - the same tree, hung off both roots from one builder, so a verb added to one spelling cannot be missing from the other. **Nothing in it asks for a permission**: a board is a thing you look at, so every switch on it belongs to the player looking, which is exactly why it belongs under `/ff` and not only under a staff-adjacent command.
- **The whole board is now settable by typing, not only by clicking.** The body lines had switches on the edit screen and nowhere else - so `on <line>`, `off <line>` and `toggle <line>` are those same switches in chat, `lines` lists every line in its order with its switch beside it, and they join `title` / `ip` / `banner` / `move`, which were already typing commands. A player changing three lines no longer has to open a window to do it, and a switch flipped in chat is painted immediately rather than left to the once-a-second refresh, because a toggle you cannot watch land is a toggle you click twice. `off money` on a board that already has money off says so rather than pretending to have done something.
- **Pinned in three places, because "it works for me as an op" is the failure this exists to catch.** `commands.scoreboard-is-not-op-only` builds a command source from a real non-op body and requires `/ff scoreboard`, `edit` and `toggle` to accept it - with the admin-gated `/ff give` beside it as the **control**, so a probe that quietly read as staff would fail here instead of turning the whole check into a tautology. It also requires the two roots to offer the *same* verb set, so the spellings can never drift into two different boards. The static audit pins the `/ff` root itself, and the check was proved to bite by gating the tree and watching it fail with `/ff scoreboard is op-only`.

### 🐉 The dragon stays dead, the fight stops restarting itself, and the theme plays once
- **A killed dragon is no longer adopted as a live fight.** A body that has just been killed is still an entity in the world for the whole of its death animation, and the search for "the dragon" was asking only whether a body was *there* - so the corpse was picked up on the very next tick, handed the rematch ceremony, and **revived at half a heart**. Kill the dragon and eight seconds later the island brought it back to full health, over and over: a fight with no ending and a kill that was never a kill. The search now asks whether the body is **alive**, adoption refuses anything dead or gone, and the fight tick only ever drives a body the adoption accepted - so a corpse is left to vanilla's own death animation instead of being fought.
- **The rift no longer re-arms the moment a fight has been won.** The hold that keeps vanilla from spawning a dragon exists for *entering* the End, but it was re-asked every tick, and the room that had just killed the dragon was still standing at the middle of the island where the kill happened - so seconds after the corpse was gone the rift tore open again and a brand new dragon came through it, forever, on the theme's own clock. Once the fight's own death finishes, the hold stands down until the body a **rematch** brings (four End Crystals on the portal) is adopted - and that body gets the island's ceremony rather than a fresh rift.
- **The theme starts once, not twice, per show.** The "any live dragon with no music" door was walked in before the arrival and the rematch ceremony, and both of those start the theme themselves on the last tick of their own clock - so every arrival announced itself on the action bar, started the track, and then announced and restarted it three seconds later when the count-up ended, and every rematch did the same across its whole climb. The rule is now three questions instead of two: the dragon has to be alive, the fight has to be unfinished, and no script may be standing on the body (`themeShouldRun(dragonAlive, fightOver, scriptOwnsTheBody)`).
- Pinned by the self-test: the harness death leg now ticks past the death and requires the machine to still read *no dragon* and no music, and the theme's rule is pinned in all three questions. The search's own one-second throttle made that pin impossible to write before - the harness drives the tick on a clock of its own, so every manual tick was answered out of the throttle and the sweep that matters never ran (see `forgetDragonScanForTest`).

### 💥 Every Enderheart ability pays for the fall - and the enchanter's enchants pay too
- **The fall is the damage, on all three abilities.** Impact's shockwave, Gravity Break's landing and Enderfall's dive now scale with the fall behind them, through **one** arithmetic (`EnderGear.fallCurve` / `fallBonus`) instead of three curves that would drift apart: vanilla's own mace curve - **four damage per block up to three, two up to eight, one after that** - capped at **thirty-two blocks**, because a dive off the build limit should hurt rather than decide the fight on its own. Gravity Break measures the drop **per body**, from the highest point the lift actually carried it, so the body the room lifted furthest is the one that pays the most; Enderfall pays for the height its own throw bought.
- **Density, Breach and Astral all count on those blows.** Density is read off the mace that called the ability (`EnchantmentHelper.modifyFallBasedDamage` - half a damage per block fallen per level, vanilla's own `smash_damage_per_fallen_block`). Breach and Astral need no line of their own, on purpose: every ability blow is a player attack carrying the mace, so the game applies Breach inside `CombatRules.getDamageAfterAbsorb` and this mod's own pipeline applies Astral against mobs - a second copy of either would double it. The one honest difference is now written down in the source: the fall bonus reads the stack captured at cast, while Breach and Astral read the hand that lands the blow.
- **The mace's clock comes down, because the fall is the cost.** Gravity Break now recovers in **12 seconds** (was 18) and Enderfall in **20** (was 30) - and since both abilities ride the same item cooldown, those two numbers are how often the weapon may be used at all. Both already charge for themselves in the setup they force: a climb before the lever, and two and a half seconds of throwing, hanging, aiming and landing before the dive, with the landing worth only the height it bought. The clock was a second price for something the ability already pays. Pinned in `ender-set.numbers` as a *band* rather than as two numbers - a real clock at each end, and the mace's biggest move keeping the longer one - so the pair stays tunable without the weapon quietly becoming spammable or unplayable.
- **The awakened mace gets the half of the tier the other two already had.** Gravity Break and Enderfall now have clocks of their own on the awakened tier - **8s and 14s**, against the base mace's 12 and 20 - so the reforged Heart is *faster* at what the base one does rather than only wider. That was the one place the set disagreed with itself about what awakening means: Voidfang's rift closes sooner and Starfall's mark bursts a shot earlier, while the mace only ever got a bigger ring. The dive's landing is the last number the two tiers still shared, and it is split too now: **28** on the awakened mace against the base tier's 22, captured in the `Dive` at cast the way the lever captures its own, so the mace that threw you is the mace that lands. The item's own lore lines say all of it now, and `ender-set.numbers` requires the direction - awakened strictly faster with a real clock still at each end of it, the dive keeping the longer one, and the awakened landing strictly harder - so the promise cannot be dropped again by a tuning pass that only reads the radius.
- **And none of that can quietly stop paying.** `ender-set.the-enderheart-pays-in-fall` pins the curve's bends exactly (three blocks is twelve damage, eight is twenty-two, and past eight it is one per block), the cap, monotonicity across the whole range, and Density's half-per-block-per-level on a real mace - then drives a **real shockwave at a real mob**, bare, with Astral V, against diamond armour, and against diamond armour with Breach IV, and requires each enchant to move the health bar. Getting there found a harness rule worth keeping: a body added outside the spawn chunks is refused by the entity manager with no error at all, so the measurement happens where the world is loaded *and* ticking.

### 💥 Gravity Break brings its own weight down, and the other two weapons get their teeth
- **The lever no longer lets the room walk out of its own slam.** Gravity Break takes the weight off everything standing in the ring and gives it back when the clock runs out - but the giving back was a query on the ring, so a body that stepped out of it was simply not there to be slammed: the answer to the mace's right-click was to walk, and the body that did it kept the **Levitation** it had been handed and drifted off with the ring's weight in its pocket. The landing now reaches **every body the lift caught**, looked up by uuid, wherever it has wandered since - and pulls the Levitation off it. Only a body that has left the world entirely escapes, which is the one escape the ability allows.
- **And the drop is measured against the ground the body stood on, not against where it is.** The landing read `peak − current y`, which at the end of a rise is the top of the rise - so the room was paid the ability's flat opening (14 damage, 18 awakened) and **no fall at all**, and the whole "the weight coming back is worth the height it took" half of the ability was inert. Each body's lift now records the ground it stood on and the highest it got, and the landing is worth the difference: what the weight is about to bring down is the fall it is about to take. It stays per body, because a body raised off a pillar has less of a drop than one raised off the floor.
- **Pinned by driving it for real.** `ender-set.the-lever-gives-the-weight-back` puts two bodies in the ring, raises one and walks the other out of it, runs the lever's clock out through `EnderGear.tick`, and requires the weight to come back to both: the escapee takes the ring's blow, is brought down, and loses the Levitation; the raised body's landing is worth clearly more than the escapee's flat one. Both halves were proved by reverting them one at a time - the escapee check fails with *"the slam only reaches whoever is still standing in it, so the answer to the mace's right-click is to walk"*, and the measurement check fails with *"the landing was worth its flat opening and no fall"*.
- **Voidfang's rift is worth a mark and its clock is shorter.** Rift Slash now cuts for **9** (12 awakened) instead of 7 and 9, on a **40-tick** cooldown (28 awakened) instead of 50 and 34 - the rift is the blade's one button, and it was paying less than the third mark the same swing sets up. **Void Hunger** detonates for **12** instead of 9, in a **90-tick** dead time (70 awakened) instead of 120 and 90, and a mark now waits **six seconds** for the next hit instead of four and a half: three landed blows in melee range is a rhythm, and a rhythm a target can break by backing off for four seconds is one nobody ever plays.
- **Starfall's rain lands harder, on more of the ground, and sooner.** A star is worth **8** (10 awakened) instead of 7 and 8.5, its impact is **3.2 blocks** wide instead of 2.8 (a volley you can walk between is confetti, not a bombardment), the base shot brings down **14 stars** and the awakened **18** instead of 12 and 16, and the charged shot recovers in **18 seconds** instead of 24. **Astral Mark** bursts for **10** instead of 6 - it was paying less than a single one of its own stars for four arrows' worth of work - and a mark now lives **seven seconds** instead of five.
- Pinned as relationships in `ender-set.numbers`, so no future tuning pass can invert them quietly: three marks must pay more than one swing, a mark must wait long enough for three landed hits, the dead time must be shorter on the awakened tier, an Astral Mark must burst for more than one star, and the awakened rift and stars must be the deeper and harder of the two. The awakened Starfall's lore also claimed **"ten stars instead of seven"** - stale since the volley was last retuned - and now says what the bow actually does.

### 🐉 The End rework actually runs every time, and the fight grows a ground guard
- **The dragon now comes through the rift on *every* visit, not just the first.** The hold rode on `EnderDragonFight.findOrCreateDragon`, which is only one of the two ways vanilla makes a dragon: a rematch runs the respawn animation, and that animation's final stage calls `createNewDragon` directly, never touching `findOrCreateDragon` at all. On top of that the hold was written against the fight's *memory* (`hasPreviouslyKilledDragon`) which is permanent after the first win. Together that meant the first fight was held and every one after it dropped out of the sky vanilla-style - the report that "sometimes the rework patch does not apply and it just spawns normally". The hold now asks the level for its own live dragons and is cleared when a dragon is adopted, and a second mixin holds `createNewDragon` itself, so both creation paths go through the same door. When the rift tears it clears the dead body's stale `dragonUUID` too, which is what had let a released rematch sit there with nothing coming through for a full minute. Pinned by new audit rules on both hooks.
- **New move: End Island Protectors.** The island raises its own guard on the ground - up to three **charged creepers**, climbing in around the dragon with soul-fire and void pouring off them. They are the fight's only summon answered by *distance* rather than by a shape on the floor: a charged creeper has to be killed before it reaches you, and killing it where it stands is the whole read. They live under the same one rule as the Lost Dragon Protectors - they only ever hunt players, asked on the way in and re-asked every tick - and they go when the fight does. Rotations are now **14 / 34 / 43**, pinned exactly.
- **The death ceremony lights up the whole map.** The killing blow was already a five-second startup, but the sky barely moved. Bolts now walk the arena in widening rings as the light comes out of the body - visual only, so nothing is set alight and no fall damage lands - each with its own screen flash and a peal of thunder, drawn around the dragon rather than on it so the death reads from every corner of the island.

### ⏱ The Wither does not stop for your stopwatch
- **The Pocket-Watch is refused while the Ascended Wither is live and near, and it is the Wither that says so.** A stopped world is a fair answer to a boss made of scheduled beats, and no answer at all to one whose whole kit is the wither itself: freezing time does not stop the drain. Walking into the fight and clicking the watch now gets **"Your stopwatch withers in your hand - time belongs to the Wither"** in the boss's own voice, a wither effect on the hand that tried it, and no freeze at all. The rule lives in the wither manager so the message and the refusal cannot drift apart.

### 🥋 `/duel bot train` - a dummy you can practise on
- **A real bot difficulty that is not an opponent.** `/duel bot train` (and the **Training Dummy** button on the difficulty screen) drops a stationary, **no-AI, infinite-health** dummy at the centre of the arena. The duel's whole AI stands down for it: it never moves, never swings, never dodges, and its bar cannot be emptied - you can rehearse one combo for an hour without the practice ever ending.
- **You fight with your own kit, and you get it back.** A training duel is the one match that does not hand out gear: your inventory and effects are left exactly as they were, and the snapshot taken on entry is handed back the moment you leave, so nothing you took in is lost and nothing you picked up along the way is kept.
- **A Nether Star in the ninth slot runs the dummy.** Right-click it for a control screen: **heal**, **dress** it in diamond armour to test against a real defence, **strip** that armour back off, and **recentre** it on the arena middle. Same ninth-slot star the rest of the mod already teaches.

## 1.7

### 🏹 Starfall fires, rains, and homes
- **Both Starfall bows now draw.** A bow is the one weapon whose icon is not a single sprite: vanilla swaps the model on `using_item` and then on `use_duration` between `bow` and `bow_pulling_0/1/2`, so the shape changes as you pull. Our custom bows used a plain model node, which is why they stood perfectly still while the player drew them. Both tiers now ship the idle sprite plus **three generated draw frames each** (six new sprites), and the `bow` item definition carries a vanilla-shaped `condition`/`range_dispatch` node with our textures in it - so pulling a Starfall finally looks like pulling a bow. Same generator, same row-checked 16x16 grids, so all eight sprites provably match.
- **The charged shot is a bombardment, not a frame.** It used to be one tick of damage - the stars were on the ground by the time the arrow left - so the set's biggest ability read as a particle budget. Now the sky gathers for about a second, then the stars come down **one every three ticks**, each tracked as its own falling projectile with a trail, so the target area is a place to get out of rather than a place that was already hit. Bigger too: **12 stars base, 16 awakened**, spread over 6.5 blocks instead of 4.
- **The stars softly home.** A falling star drifts toward the nearest player under it, a little per tick, so a volley aimed at a spot still lands on somebody who stayed in it - but not enough to follow a sprint, so stepping out of the circle is still the answer.
- **Starfall no longer uses the right-click cooldown.** It is a bow underneath, and the vanilla item cooldown greys the hotbar and refuses the right-click - so every charged shot took the bow's actual attack away. The ability keeps its own clock and reports it **on the action bar** while you hold the bow, so you can always shoot.

### 💥 Enderheart's Impact finally triggers, and the mace is the Heart
- **The Impact passive was reading a number that no longer existed.** Vanilla's mace consumes and resets the player's fall distance before an aftermath event ever sees it, so `hitter.fallDistance` inside the damage event read zero and the shockwave never fired. Fall distance is now recorded every tick and the blow reads the peak of the last few ticks, so `fall → hit → BOOM` actually happens.
- **Both maces are retextured as the Heart of the End.** The head is now the same two-lobed heart the weapon is forged from, mounted on a real haft with a gold collar between them, with deep ender sockets and the portal still burning inside. Awakened, the lobes gain a gold band, the eyes light up, and the portal fills the whole middle - the item in your hand and the item you crafted it from are now recognisably the same thing.

### 🐉 The dragon fight, tightened
- **The item-drop attack is gone.** The enderbee crystal - the one whose whole trick was emptying a player's hands into the world - is deleted: constant, behaviour, ornament and helper. Nothing in the End takes your weapon away any more.
- **Phase two and three no longer telegraph.** The End Rupture's 50-tick warning is zero, so the cracks and the eruption land together, and the Void Mark's chat countdown is gone - the mark is read off the glowing body, and the answer is the one a fight with no warnings always asks for: keep moving.
- **The End Nova is a place on the floor.** The warning ring and the blast were centred on the dragon's *altitude*, which is a ring drawn in the sky for a room fighting on the ground. The nova is now anchored to the ground under the dragon the moment it is called, and its safe-eye test is a horizontal distance, so stepping out of the circle is something a player can actually do.
- **The dragon's 350 health is now enforced, not merely set.** The attribute was applied when a body was adopted, which covers the paths this class starts but not a dragon whose maximum vanilla wrote before we ever saw it. It is normalised at the top of the fight tick as well, so "the dragon has 350 health" is true for every path rather than for the ones that happen to run.

### ✨ The End's furniture
- **The rematch ceremony is louder.** The island now reels as a ring that walks outward across the whole reel with a heartbeat under it and a flash on every pulse, and the climb adds a vortex sleeve that tightens as the body rises, a column of souls under it and firework sparks on the way up - the body reads as being pulled *through* something rather than as a model sliding upward.
- **The exit portal breathes.** A slow ring of the End's own light turns around the way home whenever the portal is present, on its own clock rather than as part of any fight, because the portal is the one thing in the End that is true before the dragon and after it.

### 🐉 The Dragon's bar is three equal phases, and the rematch is a ceremony
- **The dragon now fights with 350 HP, split into three equal thirds** - one per phase. Phase two starts the moment the first third is gone (two thirds left) and phase three once the second is (one third left), so "a third of the bar" and "one phase" are the same sentence. The old 60/25 ladder made the first phase worth almost three times the last; the phase borders are now `2/3` and `1/3` (`EnderDragonManager.PHASE_TWO_AT` / `PHASE_THREE_AT`), read by the rotation, the last-stand trigger and the action bar from one calculation, and pinned by the self-test and the build audit.
- **The action bar's health readout is persistent, and it now carries the percentage.** The line used to be sent only from the branch that waits between moves - so the moment the dragon threw a lasting attack (a beam, a rift, a gaze) the line on the screen belonged to the attack, faded, and left the bar blank until the next move was chosen. It is now sent on its own clock from the top of the fight tick, so it is on screen between moves, through every lasting move and everywhere in between; and it reads `health/max | NN%` in the phase's own colour, because with equal thirds an actual percentage is what makes "67%" and "33%" legible as the two borders.
- **The dragon is persistent.** It is marked `setPersistenceRequired()` when adopted, so it is part of the world until it dies rather than part of the room - it is never culled when the last player leaves the End, which keeps the fight, its bar and its music alive in a dimension nobody is standing in.

### 👑 The rematch is a proper ceremony
- **The island brings the dragon back instead of vanilla dropping it out of the sky.** When the fight's own death finishes, it records that a rematch is owed; the next body the fight spawns is adopted straight into a ceremony that runs on one clock with three beats - **the island reels** (a low heartbeat and drifting sculk, nothing lit yet), **the crystals reignite one by one** across the reel (a beam and a flash per pillar, one every beat, *not* new End Crystal entities - handing vanilla a ring it did not build is how a rematch ends up with an uncountable, unbreakable crystal), and **the body climbs out of the portal** with a beam of the End under it and the boss bar filling as it rises rather than jumping to full. Only a rematch gets it: a body picked up off a reload or the first arrival keeps its own show. Blows are refused for the duration, so the room cannot write the ceremony's ending.
- Pinned by `dragon.the-rematch-is-a-ceremony-and-not-a-spawn` (the shape: a climb after the reel, a rise, a ring worth relighting) and four audit rules - the ceremony is ticked, it is started on a new body, the rematch is recorded, and the crystals relight individually.

### 🏆 Free the End is fixed - and it was never the code that was broken
- **Killing the dragon now really does hand out the challenge, to everyone in the fight.** Chasing "I killed it and got nothing" to the ground produced the actual bug: `Advancements.award` fetched the player's advancement tracker afresh for the completion check, again for each criterion, and then **returned `true` no matter what the engine answered**. A grant that wrote its criteria to one tracker and read completion back from another reported success while recording nothing - the same nothing, from the player's seat. The tracker is now captured once and the answer is the engine's: a grant that did not actually complete the advancement returns `false` and says so once, loudly.
- **And the branch is now visible.** An advancement whose parent is not complete is *hidden* - the achievements screen omits the whole branch and the challenge shows no toast - so a hand-grant that only wrote the child was, to the player, a grant that did nothing. `grantFreeTheEnd` now completes `minecraft:end/root` first, so a player carried into the dimension by someone else's portal still sees the fight's achievement land.
- **The lesson is in the self-test.** The bug hid for two turns because the check drove a Fabric `FakePlayer`, whose advancement tracker is not the server's - a grant that works on a real server player looks like it does nothing through a fake one. `dragon.free-the-end-is-a-loaded-challenge-and-the-grant-records` now exercises the grant on a real `ServerPlayer` body (a duel bot), requires the advancement to be loaded, non-hidden and a **challenge**, and requires the grant to actually record and to be idempotent.

### ⚒ The Item Forge takes a Heart and Scales at once
- **The two End materials can finally both be placed.** Every material used to be routed to the same slot, so a player who shift-clicked a Heart of the End and then their Dragon Scales watched the second stack refuse to move - exactly "the heart and the scales don't fit in the forge". A material now goes to the first of the forge's two material slots that is empty or already holds the same item, so a Heart in one slot and five Scales in the other seat themselves and both End recipes (the build and the awakening) reach them.

### 🟣 Enderheart is reforged as the Dragon's own skull
- **Both maces are retextured.** The head is now the skull of the boss the weapon is made of: two horns rising off the crown, a pair of glowing ender-teal eyes in deep sockets, a dark brow ridge over a stub snout, and a **gold collar** at the neck instead of a cap. Awakened, the brow gains a gold band and the sockets open straight through on a channel of void. Still deliberately head-heavy - nine rows of skull to five of handle - so it reads as the one enormous moment weapon it is. Same generator, same palette, still an explicit row-checked 16x16 grid.

### 📊 `/stats scoreboard` - a sidebar each player owns and edits
- **Every player can now keep a scoreboard, and it is theirs.** `/stats scoreboard` shows it, `/stats scoreboard disable` takes it away, `/stats scoreboard edit` opens the screen that changes what is on it, and `/stats` on its own is the show command because that is what a player actually wants. The board carries the **server address, your tag, your guild, your gems, your money and the online count**, each of them a line you can switch off independently. The two text lines are editable from the command: `/stats scoreboard title <text>` and `/stats scoreboard ip <text>`, both of which take colour codes like every other string in this mod.
- **It is not the duel sidebar, and that is the whole design.** The duel draws its sidebar the vanilla way - one objective in a display slot - which is *global*: it is fine for a match, which ends, and useless for a personal board, because two players must be able to see different boards at once (one has turned the IP line off, the next has renamed the title). Vanilla's display slots cannot express that, so this one does not use them: each player gets their own objective and the **display packet is sent to that player alone**. The objective names are short generated ids rather than player names, because an objective name goes on the wire and older protocol caps it at sixteen characters.
- **The board is a real object on the world's scoreboard, so it is cleaned up like one.** Removing an objective without resetting its entries leaves ghost lines behind, one per session - and since an entry is keyed by its own text, a player who renames their board twice would leave the old banner routed forever. Both halves are removed together, removed lines have their scores reset when a toggle turns them off, and each line carries an invisible colour-code marker so two lines that happen to read the same can never collapse into one entry.
- **The edit screen is the board, in the order the board draws it.** Seven switches - the master one plus one per line - each with the line's own icon, an ON/OFF in the name *and* in the lore, and the line's live text underneath, so a switch that is off says what it would be showing. Colour alone is never the message: a screen that only distinguishes itself by hue is a screen half the people reading it cannot use. Flipping a switch repaints the board immediately rather than waiting for the refresh timer, because a toggle you cannot watch land is a toggle you click twice.
- **The sidebar is a `ModConfig` feature**, so `/ff config` can switch it off server-wide; the per-player switch lives on top of that gate. Refreshes are once a second - money and gems do not move fast enough to justify churning the world's scoreboard every tick - and a failed refresh is swallowed, because a sidebar is decoration and a sidebar that takes the tick with it is a broken server.
- The build audit pins the manager's four load-bearing pieces (its feature key, the per-player display packet, the take-down path and the line builder), the `/stats` command, the edit screen's vanilla menu type, and the config entry - so none of the four halves of "the board appears" can be deleted quietly. The self-test asserts the shape: off by default, every line on by default, seven switches, and colour codes that colourise.

### ⚔ The End's three weapons can be reforged, and the upgrade is fought for

### ⚔ The End's three weapons can be reforged, and the upgrade is fought for
- **Voidfang, Starfall and Enderheart now have an awakened tier.** A forged End weapon goes back into the Item Forge with **2 Hearts of the End and 10 Dragon Scales** and comes out reforged. That price is the point of the feature: the dragon pays one Heart and five Scales per player per fight, so the tier costs two more dragons rather than a pile of money, and a player who has not fought the fight twice more cannot have it. The upgrade sits above the rune branch for exactly the same reason the assembly does - a Voidfang is a netherite sword, so with the whole set in the slots the rune path would reach it first and refuse the Heart with "weapons and armor take a Rune".
- **The upgrade changes the rhythm, not the numbers.** Every awakened weapon runs the same code as its base form, on shorter clocks: Voidfang's rift closes in 34 ticks instead of 50 and cuts for 9 instead of 7, and the dead time between its detonations shortens from 120 ticks to 90 (still a ceiling, just a shorter one). Starfall's Astral Mark bursts on the **third** arrow instead of the fourth and the charged shot brings down **ten** stars instead of seven, each landing for 8.5 instead of 7. Enderheart's shockwave reaches 6.5 blocks instead of 5 and throws harder, Gravity Break's room is wider and its return hits for 18 instead of 14, and the Enderfall crater is 8.5 instead of 7. None of the three gets faster at being a weapon; they get faster at doing the thing they are for.
- **The tier rides on the weapon, so nothing downstream needs to know about it.** Abilities ask `isAnyVoidfang` / `isAnyStarfall` / `isAnyEnderheart`, so an upgraded weapon keeps every behaviour its base form had - an awakened Voidfang is a Voidfang, not a different weapon with its own rules. Anything that lasts past the moment it was cast captures its own tier: a charged volley in the air was called by the bow that called it, and a body in an Enderfall dive comes down with the crater it went up with. The melee tier is read off the weapon in the hand at the moment of the blow, because a sweep, a crit and a riposte are all one damage pipeline and the hand is the only honest answer.
- **Scrapping an awakened weapon gives everything back.** Recycling one returns **3** Hearts of the End - the one the build cost plus the two the awakening spent - rather than the single Heart the tier-one path returns. Losing fifteen Scales is already the cost of changing your mind; a refund that quietly kept two Hearts would make the upgrade a trap.
- **Three more hand-drawn sprites, in the same generator.** `tools/make_ender_dragon_textures.py` now writes all eight pieces of the set. Each awakened sprite is its base form's silhouette with one unambiguous addition rather than a different weapon: the sword gains a fourth column of blade so the edge and the body separate on every row, with the portal seam running *inside* the steel, a longer guard with a teal gem, and a real three-wide grip; the bow's limbs go two columns thick through the belly and both tips glow ender-teal, with the arrow nocked one row higher so the sprite reads as *drawn*; the mace gains a gold band across its brow and a socket open straight through, so its eye is a column of teal and portal-magenta rather than a dot, plus a second gold band on the haft. Every one is an explicit 16x16 character grid through the set's one palette, row-length-checked on the way in.
- `/ff give` learns `voidfangawakened`, `starfallawakened` and `enderheartawakened`, all three are in the give catalog, and the build audit pins the tier's two prices, its two dispatch points, the `awaken` mapping and every number that makes the upgrade real - because "the reforged weapon does something" has to be a fact the build checks, not a claim in a lore line. The self-test drives all three upgrades end to end and asserts each refused craft as well as each accepted one.

## 1.1.7

### 🗡️ The End's own set: a Heart, five Scales, and three weapons forged from them
- **The dragon now pays everybody, unconditionally.** Every player in the End when it falls is handed **1 Heart of the End and 5 Dragon Scales**, straight into the inventory (or dropped at their feet if it is full). By hand rather than through a loot table, because this fight's killing blow is a scripted `genericKill` with no attacker - `player_killed_entity` can never fire, so a loot table here would pay *nobody*. The whole dimension is named rather than a radius, for the same reason the achievement is: a helper running crystals at the rim fought this fight too. The grant has two callers (the death ceremony and the server's own death event) and is idempotent per player, so both firing on one death is one heart, not two. The ledger empties when a new body is adopted, which is what makes a *rematch* pay again without a late arrival at a corpse being paid twice.
- **The Item Forge assembles the three weapons.** A base **netherite sword**, **bow** or **mace**, plus **1 Heart of the End** and **5 Dragon Scales**, forges **Voidfang**, **Starfall** or **Enderheart**. Five is a real requirement, not one scale standing in for five: the dragon drops five to everybody, so five is what the recipe asks for. The branch sits *above* the rune branch on purpose - a netherite sword counts as "socketable", so with the whole set in the slots the rune path used to reach it first and refuse the Heart with "weapons and armor take a Rune", which is exactly the shape of "I put the heart in and nothing happened". The Forge Bench now hands the forge **both** input slots rather than only the first, because the Heart and the Scales cannot fit in one. `isEnderBaseWeapon` also requires the base to carry no `ff` type at all, so a Distant Memory or a wither blade - which are also netherite swords - can never be eaten by the recipe. Recycling an End weapon gives the Heart back, so a set forged before a player knew which weapon they wanted is not a dead end.
- **⚔ Voidfang - "it doesn't cut flesh, it cuts the space around it."** Right-click **Rift Slash**: a short rift is carved forward through everything in the lane (13 blocks, 2.7 wide) and everything caught in it is cut and marked. The passive, **Void Hunger**, marks every body it touches; the **third** mark detonates for real damage and a shove. The ceiling is what makes it a weapon rather than a hose - marks on one body are dead for six seconds after they go off, so three Voidfangs cannot out-damage the rest of the mod combined by stacking detonations on the same tick.
- **🏹 Starfall - "the stars do not fall. They are pulled."** Arrows that hit apply **Astral Mark**, and the fourth on one body bursts for End energy. The mark is read from the **arrow's own weapon** (`AbstractArrow.getWeaponItem()`), which the arrow carries for its whole flight - so it needs no tag, and it cannot be fooled by the archer swapping weapons while the shot is in the air. Sneak-right-click looses the charged shot: the aim point is found (and dropped to the floor under it), the stars travel for a second and a half, and then seven of them land around it. Precision plus area control rather than "a stronger bow".
- **🟣 Enderheart - "the Dragon's heart still beats."** A mace with a twist: **Impact** keeps vanilla's fall-distance damage and adds an **End shockwave** that throws everything nearby outward, but only on a blow landed out of a real fall. Right-click **Gravity Break** takes the room's weight away (levitation for two and a quarter seconds) and then gives it all back at once - everyone in the area is slammed down and hurt. Sneak-right-click **Enderfall** throws its holder upward, holds them at the top, and brings them down like the dragon: a large area of harm and a hard outward shove where they land. Slow by design - one enormous moment, not a fast weapon.
- **Every one of the three has hand-drawn art, generated and reviewable.** `tools/make_ender_dragon_textures.py` is checked in and writes all five sprites and their models. Every sprite is an explicit 16x16 character grid through one shared palette, with every row length-checked on the way in - the first draft drew them out of `ImageDraw` primitives and produced exactly the thing the ask complained about (a lumpy heart blob, a "bow" that was a vertical scratch, a scale that was two overlapping boxes). Pixel art is decided a pixel at a time.

### 🐉 Seven mechanics the dragon was missing
- **🟣 End Rift - a rift that *stays*.** The fight's rift has always been a particle burst and a hurt call, over in a frame. The new one stands for fifteen seconds: it drags everyone within twelve blocks slightly toward it, leaks an enderman (at most two, ever) and fires void projectiles every second and a half, and then closes on its own. **Hard cap of three standing at once**, because without one a long phase-three fight would eventually cover the island. Two open at once, at the feet of two different players, because the mechanic is about the floor becoming unreliable.
- **👁 Ender Gaze - a beam that follows one player.** The dragon locks on and the beam tracks them for five and a half seconds. The answer is not distance and not cover: it is **movement**. The gaze builds while its target stands still (less than a block and a quarter of ground per tick) and discharges when it has built all the way; running bleeds it back down. Stopping under it is an 18-damage explosion; the room around them takes half.
- **💀 Void Mark - a countdown on a body.** Three players are marked, and the countdown is announced in **chat** rather than the action bar - deliberately, because this is the one warning the fight still gives and the action bar is already carrying the boss line. What lands is a blast that **falls off with distance** rather than a flat hit, which is what makes spreading out the correct answer instead of clustering.
- **🌋 End Rupture - cracks with a bill.** Four jagged lines are read across the floor around the fight, the ground is drawn shifting for two and a half seconds, and then void energy erupts out of every crack. One of exactly two moves in the fight that keeps a telegraph, because here the timer *is* the mechanic: the safe ground is the ground you have reached by the time it goes off.
- **🌀 Temporal Tear - a section of the island stops being there.** A giant circle closes, and then the floor inside it is gone: the region is drawn as air, particles fill it, the void under it bites anyone standing in it, and then it all comes back. The removal is **visual only, and that is the whole safety story** - the server's blocks are never changed, clients are sent a block update, and the real states are written straight back afterwards. The island is the one thing in this fight that must survive it, so this version of the mechanic cannot leave a hole in it even if the fight ends mid-tear (see `clearLastingMoves`).
- **☄ End Nova - the one the dragon gets to spend.** A wind-up collapses a giant circle inward over three and a half seconds, and then the whole arena takes 26 damage and a hard shove - except the eye at the middle, which is safe and says so. **Once per fight**, by request: a rotation that could roll it twice is not a major attack, it is a coin flip that decides the fight. The charge is handed back when a new body is adopted.
- **🪨 The phase-one perch is a fight, not a piñata.** Vanilla's perch is a dragon sitting on a fountain waiting to be hit by a room. In phase one the dragon now fights from it: the **head** snaps at whoever is on the fountain with it, the **tail** sweeps the ring clear, and the **wings** beat a shockwave out along the floor of the whole island - so the openings exist and have to be *taken*, and the window between beats is drawn. After its beats it takes off on its own, which is what keeps the fountain a phase rather than a place to stand. It is noticed from vanilla's own phase machine rather than thrown as a move, because perching is not ours to schedule.
- Rotations are now **14 / 33 / 42**, pinned exactly, with every new move's name checked against a rotation (a move in the enum but in no rotation is a move nobody will ever see). The self-test also drives the forge end to end - the craft that must work *and* the four-scale craft that must refuse - and asserts each number that makes the set what it is.

### 🧾 Also
- `/ff give` learns `heart_of_the_end`, `dragon_scale`, `voidfang`, `starfallbow` and `enderheart`, and all five are in the give catalog. The two materials are declared passive (right-clicking a material that does nothing is a dead click) while the three weapons dispatch through `EnderGear`.
- Build-audit rules pin the seven mechanics (the rift's cap *and* its expiry, the tear's visual-only removal, the nova's single charge, the perch's trigger), the set's numbers, the forge assembly, the three dispatches and all five pieces of art.

## 1.1.6

### 🐛 The Wither's theme actually plays, and Free the End is actually granted
- **The Wither's theme was silent, and the cause was a single line.** The client plays each cut by fading it up from zero - but `SoundEngine.play` **refuses** a sound whose computed volume is zero (it returns `NOT_STARTED`) *unless* its source is `MUSIC` or the instance opts into `canStartSilent()`, and a refused sound is never registered as tickable, so its `tick()` never ran and its volume never left the floor. Every cut was born on `SoundSource.RECORDS` at volume `0.0F`, so every cut was refused: the fight had a soundtrack that could not start, and the client's own warning about `NOT_STARTED` blamed the art pack. The cuts are now born at an inaudible-but-nonzero floor on `SoundSource.MUSIC` (which the engine lets start silently precisely so that a fade-in can happen), so the intro, the loop and the death cut all fade in as designed. The vanilla/Bedrock fallback moved to `MUSIC` with them, so the theme answers to the music slider rather than the jukebox one. Pinned by an audit rule.
- **Free the End is granted off the dragon's *death*, not off one ceremony.** The reworked death spends the killing blow as a scripted `genericKill` with no attacker, so vanilla's `player_killed_entity` trigger can never fire - and the hand-grant lived *inside* that ceremony, after a run of teardown, so any dragon that died another way (a rematch, the rework switched off, `/kill`, a body lost to the void) ended on an achievement nobody received. It is now one method (`EnderDragonManager.grantFreeTheEnd`) with two callers: the top of the death ceremony - **before** any teardown that could throw and swallow it - and the ordinary death event for any Ender Dragon that dies in the End. Idempotent per player, so both firing on one death is a no-op. Pinned by an audit rule.

### 🐉 The dragon aims, the void spares you, and the mace is answered
- **The slam is aimed at a player.** It used to drop straight down onto whatever floor the dragon happened to be over - which punished standing still and rewarded standing *anywhere else*, the same as having no answer at all. The dragon now moves over whoever it picked before it drops, so the ring lands where that player is and the move is about a body rather than a patch of rock.
- **New: Dragon Ram.** The dragon flies *into* the player it picked: the body is moved onto the target, the corridor is drawn from where it left to the body it arrives at, and everything still standing there is thrown backwards off the floor with a big shove and a short slow. Unlike Dragon Dive it is not a landing - it is a hit-and-run through a nominated body, so the pay is knockback rather than a crater and the answer is a sideways step rather than distance. The rotations are now **12 / 28 / 35** and the self-test counts are updated with them.
- **The action bar's health is the phase's own.** The island's kit reads the End's violet, the void reads red, and the corrupted sky reads the deep purple of the border, with a glyph carrying the same three colours - so a glance at the action bar says which phase the fight is in without the fight ever naming one. The last stand keeps its own line, because that is the only number in the whole fight worth shouting.
- **The mace is answered with 30% of itself.** A mace smash is one enormous blow, and one enormous blow against a boss built out of three phases, a last stand and a five-second death ceremony is a smash that skips the entire fight. It is read in the damage pipeline (so it also covers the mace's own smash event, a second damage source with the same attacker), pinned by an audit rule.
- **Falling into the void is a rescue now, not a lost run.** In a fight built out of knockback, pushes, a weightless beat and a body that flies into you, the void was killing players with the dragon's own moves. Below the island the fall is caught: a rift tears where they went through, and they come out on top of the island with **four fifths of their bar** and no fall to pay for it. One fall in ten is gentle and lands with slow falling. The rule is one number (`VOID_RESCUE_HEALTH`) behind one predicate (`isVoidRescue`).
- **Wing Gust throws the room back when it is close to the floor.** At altitude the gust is a shove; down near the ground the wings have nowhere to push but out, so the same move adds a shockwave running along the floor and a much harder backwards launch. That is the specific answer to standing under the dragon and swinging, and it is deliberately absent at altitude where the fight wants you to come closer.

### 🎵 The boss takes the room's music, and the summoning only happens when the dragon is actually coming
- **Re-entering the End replayed the whole summoning, and the cause was one field.** The rift used to be gated on `riteOpened` - this class's memory of having torn a rift - and that memory is a `static` field, so a reload, a restart or (in single player) simply rejoining the world emptied it. Walking back to the middle of an island whose fight was already **won** - or already **running** - then tore the rift again and set the arrival loose on a body that was not supposed to have one. The rift now asks the same question the hold asks (`shouldHoldTheDragon(end)`): vanilla is only *waiting* for a dragon when it has never been beaten and has none out, so a fight that is over is not greeted again, a fight in progress never gets a second summon, and the rematch is still vanilla's. A field can be forgotten; the fight's own state cannot. Pinned by an audit rule.
- **A boss theme now stops every other song.** Both reworked fights have a soundtrack, and both were playing *over* whatever else was running - the End's ambient score, a record in a jukebox, and the mod's Backpack Jukebox, which loops and re-issues itself every tick, so it is the one piece of music that will not stay stopped on its own. A theme now claims the listener for the length of the cut (refreshed every tick of the fight, so a loop for minutes never lapses) and, in that first moment, stops **every** sound on both sources music can live on: `MUSIC` for the game's own score and `RECORDS` for a disc or a boombox. The mod's own jukebox is hushed separately - a new stop-everything action on its payload clears the client's looping copies outright, so nothing heals them back, and the server stops serving that player until the theme lets go. The claim is handed back when the fight ends, when a player leaves the arena, or when a claim simply runs out - which is what makes the Wither's death cut play on to its own end before the boombox is allowed back. The stop is deliberately sent *before* the theme's own first sound, because a stop-all on the source a boss theme sits on would otherwise silence the boss theme itself. Pinned by audit rules for both themes, the two sources, and the tick that hands the music back.

### 🎵 The Ascended Wither gets its own soundtrack, and Free the End becomes a grand achievement
- **The Wither fights to *WITHERED (Jaacken)* now, in three cuts of one track.** The summon plays the opening (`withered_intro`, 0:00-0:21), the fight loops the body of the track (`withered_loop`, 0:21-2:33), and the death ceremony lays the ending over it (`withered_death`, 2:33 on) - played **once**, and only when it actually dies. All three live in the art pack beside the dragon's theme, declared in `sounds.json` and named by `ModSounds`. The server (`economy.WitherMusic`) only ever says *which* cut belongs to the fight right now; the fades are the client's job.
- **Every transition is a crossfade, and the loop is seamless.** A sound packet can neither fade nor loop, so the modded client plays each cut as a listener-glued tickable sound of its own (`client.WitherMusic`) and blends between them: the intro melts into the loop, the loop comes round by handing itself the next copy a fade before this one ends, and a cut to the death theme **overlaps** the music it replaces instead of stopping it dead. A "skip" - the server changing its mind mid-cut - is the same operation, which is why it is not a special case. Volume is what does the work, and it works because a tickable sound's volume is re-read every tick. Clients that cannot take the payload (vanilla, Geyser/Bedrock) are served the same cuts as plain sounds with the loop re-issued a track-length at a time, so nobody is left in silence - they just do not get the fades.
- **Free the End is a grand achievement, and it belongs to the fight.** `minecraft:end/kill_dragon` is overridden (`data/minecraft/advancement/end/kill_dragon.json`) to be a **challenge** rather than a task, and the reworked dragon now grants it - by hand, to **everybody still in the arena** when it falls, 128 blocks around the spot - instead of vanilla's one-advancement-between-the-group for whoever landed the killing blow. Helpers, crystal-shooters and the last hitter all leave with it. Pinned by new build-audit rules for the three cuts, the payload and the grant.

### 🐉 Three hundred health, eleven kinds of crystal, and the transitions the pack is made of
- **The bar is 300 now, and every number that reads it agrees.** `DRAGON_HEALTH` is one constant, and the four things that used to be four numbers are all that one: the attribute, the bar the client draws, the arrival's count-up (it climbs to `getMaxHealth()`, so a dragon that arrives now fills to three hundred rather than two), and the phase arithmetic that hands the fight its rotations. A body **adopted** mid-fight (a restart, a reload) is re-scaled rather than re-health-ed - the *proportion* it was at is kept and re-read against the new maximum - so nothing heals to full and nothing is emptied to replay an arrival. Pinned by `dragon.the-slam-and-the-weightless-beat-land-and-end` and an audit rule.
- **The crystal ring has eleven types, not one.** Borrowed from *Dragonkind Evolved*, where a crystal can be **Caged** (every second blow is the one that lands), a **Forcefield** (projectiles are turned aside, under a magenta bubble), **Fiery** (it burns whatever comes close, orbited by glowing blaze rods), a **Laser** (a charged band at its own height, which a *jump* clears), an **Enderbee** (one random player's hands are emptied; orbited by two bees; only ever one), a **Witch** (a neutral-or-worse effect to somebody who has none; a floating hat), **Anti-Grav** (levitation around it), **Cursed** (no particles at all, and breaking it is a personal matter for whoever is nearest; only ever one), a **Launcher** (an eye over the crystal that fires heat-seeking eyes at anybody flying in its airspace), a **Portal** (up to three players are moved to the tops of the towers; only ever one), and the **10,000** (a golden crown that makes **every other crystal in the ring invulnerable until it breaks**, which turns "shoot the crystals" into a job with an order to it). The pack's difficulty ladder is read as the fight's own: **tier 1 in phase one, 2 in phase two, 3 in phase three, and 4 during the last stand and the finale** - so the ring gets worse as the dragon does, exactly as the pack has it. Every ornament is particles (an iron cage, a bubble, a hat, a crown, a portal standing behind it, a turning eye, two orbiting bees, two blazing rods, and *nothing* for the cursed one, whose read is that it looks like every other crystal until it breaks), and every control effect - levitation, the teleport, the emptied hands - is **refused while an ending is running**, because a finale the room cannot play is a boss that cannot be killed. Pinned by `dragon.the-crystals-roll-a-roster-that-grows-with-the-fight` and `dragon.the-crystal-gates-refuse-and-say-so`.
- **Four more moves, out of the same pack.** **Slam** - the dragon drives its own body into the floor and comes back up; the one move in the kit that is a *body* rather than a shape, and the reason to be somewhere else. **End Rod Spikes** - a field of rods marching out under every player, and under the dragon itself when it is perching, because a dragon on the ground has lost the altitude that normally keeps people away from it. **Shulker Bullets** - a ring of twelve of vanilla's own bullets, or the spikes instead if the air is already full of them: a fallback rather than a second volley, because a wall of homing bullets is not a harder fight, it is a fight with no answer in it. **Zero Gravity** - the room loses its weight and everybody climbs, and then it is all given back at once and the landing is the bill. The last two are *states* rather than blows, ticked like the breath lance is, and both are cleared in one place (`stopLastingMoves`) so a body cannot be left in a dive and a room cannot be left weightless.
- **And three more, from the pack whose transitions the borders are now made of** (*Draconic Ascension*). **Sky Ring**: three bands of sixteen strikes, the outermost fifteen blocks out and each band five blocks closer, so the **middle is the answer and the rim is fatal** - and the bands walking in are the only thing that says so, because nothing in this fight winds up any more. **Ascendant Blink**: eight teleports whose gaps collapse from eleven ticks to four, each arrival landing with its own shockwave, and the last one is not an arrival at all - it is the **slam**, so a chain nobody can hit still ends somewhere the room can name. **Ascension**: the phase borders are no longer instantaneous. For three seconds the dragon climbs out of the room's reach while the island grows eighteen columns of light around it, one more every third tick, and the blows aimed at it arrive at nothing - a **transition** rather than a caption, which is also the thing a title was doing badly. It is on a clock and it only ever starts at a border, so the untouchable window cannot become a fight nobody can finish. Rotations are now **11 / 27 / 34**, pinned exactly, with every move's shape and impact checked by both the self-test and the audit.
- **One bug found while pinning the crown.** The check that proves "a plain crystal is untouchable while the crown stands" was **failing for its own set-up**: the two crystals it places in the End are added outside a server tick, and a level does not hand back a body that was added outside a tick - so the gate was honestly answering "no crown is standing" about a ring it could not see, and the *check* was measuring nothing. The rule itself reads the level (the only source that cannot go **stale**: a crown that has died must stop holding the ring, or the ring can never be opened), and it now reads it through one accessor with one test seam, so the check hands in the ring it means to test - and immediately proves the rule is **not stuck on** by taking the crown away and watching the same blow land. Pinned by two audit rules.

### 🐉 The fight stops captioning itself, the crystals join in, and the music belongs to the fight
- **Every caption is gone, and the fight is louder for it.** The phase titles (`PHASE TWO` / `PHASE THREE`), the line that named each move on the action bar as it landed, the last stand's `ONE HEART` / `IT LANDS`, and the death ceremony's `IT KNOWS` title and `IT IS COMING APART` clock are all removed - what is left on the action bar is the health bar, and during the last stand the one thing worth shouting (`ONE HEART | hp/max - finish it`). The border still flashes, novas and screams; the beats are said in chat, where they are scrollback rather than a caption over the fight. `THE ENDER DRAGON` assembling itself at the arrival stays the fight's only title, because a name is an introduction and a phase number is a scoreboard. The names are **audit rules** now: a restored `chosen.label()` on the action bar, or any of the captions coming back, fails the build.
- **The music belongs to the fight, not to the arrival.** It used to be started by the arrival and by nothing else - so a **player who walked into a fight already in progress** heard the boss in complete silence. The rule is now asked every tick over a live dragon (`themeShouldRun(alive, fightOver)`) plus a second question per player (`shouldServeTheme` - a spectator is never served, nobody is served twice in one loop, and a late arrival gets the same *now playing* toast and the same promise that it is taken back out of their advancement screen a few seconds later). It is deliberately **not** keyed off the rift, because that flag is per-session and false on the first tick after a restart - which would have been the same silence for a save reloaded into a live dragon. Pinned by `dragon.the-theme-follows-the-fight-and-not-the-arrival` and two audit rules.
- **The crystals are guns and a supply line now, not scenery.** Every crystal standing fires a beam of the End's light at whoever is nearest, on a clock **staggered by its own entity id** so ten of them do not fire on one tick, and every crystal standing feeds a trickle of health back into the dragon (0.5 a crystal, every two seconds). So "shoot the crystals" - the job the fight always implied - is finally worth doing in *every* phase rather than the one that pointed at them. The feed is refused wherever a script owns the bar (the finale, the last stand, the death ceremony), because a crystal that healed through an ending is an ending that can be out-sustained; pinned by `dragon.the-crystals-are-guns-and-a-supply-line` plus a call-site audit rule.
- **The body itself is a place.** The passive half of *Aura of the End*: inside six blocks the End comes off the dragon, on the same collar drawn every tick at exactly the radius it hurts - 2 / 3 / 4 damage a second by phase, so melee has a price and the price grows. Nothing else about the fight is always on, which is what makes the space under the dragon a decision rather than an inconvenience. Pinned by `dragon.the-body-itself-is-a-hazard-up-close`.
- **Six new moves, one for each shape the kit was missing.** *Dragon Charge* - vanilla's own ram, given a body and a **corridor**: the dragon leaves, re-enters on the line and everything along it is hit, so the answer is being off the line rather than far from the body. *Fangs of the End* - a **cone** of void teeth out of the mouth, the only attack that punishes standing *in front of* it. *Soul Vortex* - a spin that throws players **around** the body instead of into it, answered by the eye or by leaving. *Void Grasp* - levitation without slow falling, so the fall is the move and where the thread finds you is the question. *Meteor Shower* - impacts scattered across the whole island, so nowhere is simply safe. *Supernova* - the middle survives and the band does not, the one arrangement where the counter to the biggest blow is **closing**, not running. Rotations are now **10 / 22 / 28**, pinned exactly.
- **The VFX were rebalanced against the spam complaint.** The ambient layer was cut by roughly a third (the wing trail, the void veins, the sky leaking), because it was competing with the set pieces rather than framing them - and the budget freed up went into the moments that should be remembered: *Dragon Dive* now lands with a nova, a flash, a shockwave ring and a wall of ash (it was a puff of smoke before), *Wing Gust* throws a pressure ring and a gust front, *Ender Lash* gets a sweep nova, *Tail Sweep* a flash and a rim of end-light, and every new move is built out of the same four-layer kit so adding a move means choosing a colour rather than rebuilding a light show. The particle ceiling (4,200 a tick) still holds, measured across a real finale.

### 🐉 The dragon stops announcing itself: no telegraphs, six new moves, and a budget it cannot exceed
- **Every wind-up is gone.** A move used to be named on the action bar and grow its particles for most of a second before it landed, which made the fight read as a lecture with damage in it - the warning did the work the fight should have done. Now a move lands on the tick it is chosen and the action bar names it *as* it lands: the shape of the move is the read, and the shape was always there (a line, a ring, a radius, a wall). `WIND_UP_TICKS` is a published zero, `shortestTelegraph()` is gone, and both a check (`dragon.every-phase-has-a-rotation-and-it-grows`) and an audit rule fail the build if the arithmetic comes back.
