- **Six more moves, and the ranged half of the kit is real.** *Dragon Fireball* throws **vanilla's own projectile** from the mouth - a body that crosses the arena and leaves vanilla's breath puddle where it lands, the one attack in this fight you can watch coming. *End Shockwave* takes the floor away in three rings walked out of the body. *Lost Protectors* raises up to four **phantoms** above the fight (see below). *Aura of the End* is a place rather than a blow: eight seconds during which the space under the dragon drains whatever stands in it. *Void Dive* leaves, cuts the island on the way down and lands as a shockwave. *Starfall* is a curtain of falling fire, one column per player, which is the answer to a room that has learned to spread out. Phase one is now **8 moves**, phase two **19**, phase three **23**.
- **The Lost Dragon Protectors only ever hunt players.** They are phantoms, because that is what the End already raises and because they come from the one direction a floating island does not cover - and the rule that keeps them from being the fight's bug is enforced twice: they are told whose side they are on when they are raised, and the question is re-asked **every tick**, so anything a protector is targeting that is not a player is dropped on the spot. A summon that can target its own boss is a boss that kills itself, which would read as "the dragon died on its own" rather than as a bug in a summon. They carry an aura of soul-fire, souls and void, and they go when the fight does.
- **The fight now has a particle budget, and it is a real ceiling.** Every particle in the file goes through one spender (`spend`), which clamps each request against `PARTICLE_CEILING` (4,200 a tick) and drops what does not fit. Nothing is refused in a way that breaks a move - damage, state and sounds are untouched - and the absence of any `end.sendParticles(` call in the file is an **audit rule**, so a particle drawn straight from the level fails the build. It is pinned three ways: the ceiling is a number a server can live with, the clamp really bites (a check asks for 100,000 particles and is given exactly the ceiling), and a **real finale** - the densest tick this fight has - stays inside it (`dragon.the-fight-spends-under-its-particle-budget`, asserted inside the harness run).
- **The death stops lying about what is happening.** A dragon pinned at one heart reads as a *phase-three* dragon to any arithmetic that only asks about health, so the fight kept announcing **phase three while the dragon was dying** - the phase is now read from the script whenever the bar is not describing a fight (arrival, last stand, finale, death ceremony), and `onPhaseChange` refuses to fire there even if something asks it to. The departure also loses the custom death roar (vanilla's own death sound is part of the death it is about to have; a second one stacked on it is noise), and gains the **end gateway effect** at the spot the dragon fell: a beam drawn out of the ground, growing, a ring of the End's own light, and then the crack of a new door - `END_GATEWAY_SPAWN`, because the fight ending should sound like a way in rather than an animal dying.
- **The arrival is a ceremony and then its name is written.** No title at the top any more: reaching the middle puts *something is happening at the middle of the island* on the action bar, the rift tears with twice the rings and a column of light standing on it, the dragon comes through with a nova, a pillar and a sleeve of void still holding it - and only once it is fully here does **`THE ENDER DRAGON` assemble itself one character at a time**, with a click of sound per letter, over the subtitle. It is the one title the event spends, and it is spent at the moment the name belongs to.

### 🎵 The theme, and a pocket watch that knows when not to work
- **The End has music now.** *Minecraft - Ender Dragon Theme (J. Rivers)* is converted to OGG, declared in the art pack (`resourcepack/assets/fortuneandfavors/sounds.json`) and played by packet from the moment the arrival finishes until the dragon dies. A three-and-three-quarter-minute track cannot be one packet, so it is a **clock**: everyone in the End is served, the clock runs the track's own length, then everyone is served again - and anybody who arrives mid-fight or reconnects is served as soon as they are in the End, while anybody who leaves has their copy stopped rather than carrying the boss's music into the overworld. The **now-playing toast** is a vanilla advancement: `fortuneandfavors:technical/ender_theme`, hidden, granted with the theme and **revoked three seconds later**, so nothing permanent is ever added to anyone's advancement screen. Pinned by `dragon.the-theme-ships-and-takes-its-toast-back` (the ogg and the `sounds.json` are checked as real shipped resources) and by an audit rule that fails the build if the file, the sound event, or the hidden toast goes missing.
- **The Pocket-Watch refuses to work in the End while the dragon is up.** Ten seconds of stopped clock is a fair answer to a boss made of scheduled beats in a room you can walk out of, and an unfair one to a fight whose whole shape is phases, a last stand and a death ceremony. It refuses *there* rather than being banned from fights - every other boss in the mod is unaffected - and it says so in the watch's own voice. `EnderDragonManager.isFightActive()` is the one question it asks, and an audit rule keeps that call in the file.

### 🏰 A faster fall, and a chest that goes with what is inside it
- **The fade is paced, not fixed.** The deadline is still sixty seconds - that is the contract, and it is pinned - but the wave is now scheduled at **1.45x** the even rate, so the last of the stonework is gone with about a fifth of the window left and what remains of the fall is the part that cannot be walked away from anyway: light out of the seams, the rumbling, the blast. It is one arithmetic (`collapseBudget`) read by the tick and by a check, so "the castle never finishes coming down" and "the castle comes down in ten seconds" are both things a build can fail on.
- **And a chest is taken with its contents.** Taking the block out of the world with the same call the fall uses for stone never dropped the *block* - and that looked like the whole story, which is how a hoard ended up on the ground of a castle whose entire contract is that it gives the field back the way it found it: a container's contents are not the block. The fall now **empties the container the instant before it takes it**, with the same sweep the teardown runs as a backstop, on a clock, while the castle is still coming down. Pinned by `mirage.the-fall-takes-what-is-inside-a-chest` (a real chest, filled, emptied) and two audit rules.

### 🕵️ Vanish: the tells that were left behind
The report was a list, and every item on it was a different mechanism. **Footsteps and sprint dust** were muted by a guard that asked *"invisible, and not invisible by a potion"* - which was a fair description of a vanished body until this mod started handing out short invisibility effects itself (a Puppeteer escape, a scripted flash), at which point a vanished moderator could silently stop being recognised and start leaving footprints again. The question is now asked of one place, `VanishManager.isHiddenBody`: the server's ledger on a server, and on a client a **marker team** (`ff_vanish`, no colour, no name-tag rule, `seeFriendlyInvisibles` off) that vanilla's own scoreboard packets sync - so it is ours and only ours, and a genuine invisibility potion is still ordinary gameplay with its footsteps. **The landing puff** was a packet nobody had touched: `ServerPlayer.checkFallDamage` sends a `BlockParticleOption(ParticleTypes.BLOCK, ...)` to every client in range, so everyone nearby watched a hidden player arrive on the ground. It is muted at vanilla's own gate (`spawnExtraParticlesOnFall`, one line, no fall-damage side effects), which is also why the mixin does **not** cancel `Block.fallOn` - that would have removed fall damage entirely. **The potion cloud** is the last visible thing a body carries: vanilla clears the synced effect-particle list exactly once, when the invisibility flag changes, and any effect added afterwards re-syncs it. Refused at the sync method, and emptied on the vanish clock. **The held item and armour** were already packet-hidden for the hands; the armour is now *swept* every tick rather than stashed once, because it can come back onto the body mid-vanish (a pickup, another system handing out a piece) and worn armour is drawn by a layer that never asks whether the entity is invisible. Pinned by `vanish.a-hidden-body-is-ours-and-not-just-an-invisible-one` and three audit rules, one of which was proven to bite by breaking it.

### 🏰 The collapse, and the King who fights back
- **The fall is worn, not watched.** Everyone inside a collapsing mirage now carries **slowness, hunger and mining fatigue**, re-applied on a clock with a duration that outlaps the gap by a minute - so a milk bucket, a death or a dimension change is not an answer to being buried, and a lagging tick cannot let it flicker off. All three are removed the moment the fall ends, so nobody walks out of a deadline still poisoned by it. Pinned by `mirage.the-collapse-is-worn-by-everybody-inside-it`.
- **And the castle comes apart in an order.** The break used to be a random sample of the plan - the same grey noise everywhere, which is not what a building coming down looks like. It is a **wave** now: a band at a time from the **middle outwards** (the hall the King died in goes first), and within a band **from the top down**, so each floor drops into the one below and the edge stays ragged. On top of that: the sky answers with a flash and a crack above the footprint every few seconds, a heartbeat under the floor, ground haze lying over the whole thing, and - in the last fifteen seconds - **light coming out of the seams** where blocks used to be.
- **The Distant Memory has three new geometries.** *THE CROWNLESS SKY* is a dome that closes onto the hall and drags what it catches inward before it strikes (the answer is distance, the one beat that tells a group to spread out). *HERALDRY OF ASH* is the inverse of his whole kit: an annulus that starts at his feet and widens, so the answer is to be **close** - the move that punishes learning "distance is safe" for three laps. *THE HALL MOURNS* draws three rings on the floor and strikes only where they are drawn, which makes the counter a **place to stand** rather than a direction to run. All three have their own tells drawn from the same progress value the blow is timed on, their own answers on the action bar, their damage in the one table, and entries in the resolved-move list - so `king.the-hall-fights-with-more-than-distance` fails the build if any half of that goes missing.

### 🐲 `/ff dragon`, and the arrival that never started
The arrival show had a bug worth naming, because it was invisible from every direction: the tick asked *"is a count-up in flight?"* **before** anything could ever put one in flight, so the whole three-second bar-filling existed, was pinned by a check, and **never ran**. Writing the `/ff dragon` command is what surfaced it, and the fix is one arithmetic the tick and the check now share - `arrivalDue(awaiting, done, running, health, max)` - which also pins the two failures that are worse than not running: a body adopted at sixty percent (a restart, a reload) no longer gets its bar emptied to replay an arrival, and the show can no longer run twice on the same dragon. The arrival is now explicitly *whose* it is (`awaitingArrival`, set when the rift tears and cleared when it finds its dragon), and `/ff dragon reset` re-arms it, so the one part of this rework that happens once per server life can be watched on demand.

`/ff dragon` (op-only) prints the machine and can push any of its borders: `status` (health, rotation phase *and* the phase the bar reads, current move, beam ticks left, pools standing, arrival ticks, finale and death timers, last stand), `rift` (tears the rift open now), `phase <1-3>` (moves the bar - the only thing a phase is read from), `lance` (fires the breath lance), `finale` (the last stand on the spot) and `reset` (back to the top, arrival and all). Every branch answers either way, so a command that cannot act says why instead of doing nothing.

### 🎆 Twenty-three moves, and a dragon that is never just standing there
- **Five more moves, all told before they are thrown.** *Ender Lash* is the close arc (a full turn of sweeps from 3.5 to 11 blocks, everything caught thrown outward and up). *Void Collapse* pulls the room into the middle and then takes the middle away, so the answer is to be outside the last ring. *Crystal Bloom* raises up to four **real end crystals** around the arena - they fire at whoever is nearest and heal the dragon while they live, and the room is told to shoot them - capped by counting what is already standing, so a long fight cannot bury itself in crystals. *Eclipse of the End* puts darkness and blindness on everyone and then strikes in a **shrinking band of rifts**, which is a move about keeping track of the light. *Abyssal Roar* takes the floor away: everyone in range is thrown **upward** as well as hurt, so the counter is the ring rather than the distance. Phase one is now **7 moves**, phase two **15**, phase three **17** - pinned exactly, plus per-phase name uniqueness, by `dragon.every-move-is-told-and-the-rotations-only-grow`.
- **A VFX kit, so "more particles" is one call site instead of eleven copies that drift apart.** `nova` is the four layers every big blow is made of - a burst core, a shell walking out of it, colour sparks, and wind that reads as a shockwave rather than as smoke; `flash` is vanilla's own lightning **flash particle given a colour**, which lights the whole screen instead of adding a shape to it; `pillar` is the vertical one, used where something arrives. Every impact in the kit now ends in a nova: the shriek, the breath nova, the roar, the storm, both dives, the eclipse, the roar of the End, the void collapse - and the **finale's rings, the landing, the phase borders and the death** get flashes and novas on top of what they had.
- **And the dragon has an ambient layer, ticked every tick of every fight.** Its wings trail end-light; **phase two opens veins of the void** along the body; **phase three leaks sky** - ash falling off it, cracks of end-light, and an occasional bolt flash over the island; at one heart it **bleeds light**, so the last stand reads even from behind a pillar, and it growls on a clock. `aura` is called from `fightTick` under the beam, the moves and the finale alike, so the aura cannot be gated off by any other state.
- Both halves are enforced: the audit fails the build if `nova`, `flash`, `aura` or the aura's own call site goes missing, or if any of the five new moves loses its tell or its impact - and that rule was proven to bite ("nothing calls the aura - it exists and never runs").

### 🐉 The lance, the arrival, and a dragon the harness actually drives
- **The breath lance** is the dragon's first move that *lasts*: eighty ticks of a beam out of its **mouth** (not its middle), sweeping 34 degrees off the line it was aimed on, so hiding behind one block is not an answer. Damage is per tick and small, it sets you alight, and where it touches ground it leaves **vanilla's own dragon-breath cloud** - the pool a dragon fireball makes, same radius, same growth, same instant-damage effect, because players already know how that behaves. Capped at ten and swept on the fight's end, so a long beam cannot carpet a chunk. Pinned by `dragon.the-breath-lance-lasts-and-its-pools-are-vanilla-and-capped` and two audit rules.
- **The arrival now counts the bar up from nothing.** Three seconds of health rising from a sliver (zero is a corpse, not an empty bar) to full, with **one** title - `THE ENDER DRAGON` - and nothing may touch the body while it runs, because a bar being written by a show and lowered by a sword at the same time reads as a bug. It only ever runs for a dragon at full health: a body found at sixty percent after a restart is a fight in progress. Pinned as one arithmetic (`riseHealth`) that both the show and the check read.
- **And the whole thing is driven on a real dragon.** `dragon.the-arrival-finale-landing-and-death-run-on-a-real-dragon` spawns a genuine `EnderDragon` in the harness End - with no dragon fight attached, so the vanilla fight's portal, egg and bookkeeping are never touched - and drives it through phase three, the enormous blow that must **not** land, the finale (asserting the one-heart floor holds through every beat), the one-shot at one heart, the death ceremony and vanilla's own death. It asserts the pools go with it, and it releases the harness in a `finally`, because a test that leaves a last stand behind would follow the next real dragon into its fight. It passed on the first run: 200 HP → finale → *"the last stand is over - one heart, killable, phase one rotation"* → *"the dragon died for real - vanilla death and loot follow"*.
- **Five more moves**, all told before they are thrown: *Wing Wall* (two walls of wind thrown sideways, answered by standing on the axis), *Void Chains* (a small pull, weakness and slowness - a player already moving keeps moving), *Breath Lance*, *Dragon Dive* (the body actually moves: a corridor of ruin, everything standing in it struck, and then the dragon is there) and *Storm of the End* (eight rifts, bolts and lightning at once). Phase one now has six moves, phase two nine, phase three fourteen.
- The harness has its own switch, `harnessMode`, which suppresses **damage to bystanders only** - every state change, every pool, every VFX and the dragon's own death happen exactly as in a fight.

### 🐉 The Ender Dragon: the spawn is a rite, and the fight has three phases and a last stand
- **Walking into the End is not asking for a fight, and vanilla answers with a dragon anyway.** The dragon is **held** until somebody actually reaches the middle of the island - the one hook is `EnderDragonFight.findOrCreateDragon`, which is the fight saying *"I want a dragon"*, so refusing there is precisely "not yet" and every other thing the fight does keeps running around it. Then a **rift tears open at 0, 64, 0** for five seconds - a slash of void that grows, shockwave rings walking out of it, visual-only lightning in the sky above, the growl underneath - and only when it tears does the hold come off and the dragon come through, with an arrival show and a title. The hold is **narrow on purpose**: a fight that has already been won is vanilla's to restart, a fight that already has a dragon is never touched, and the one thing it can never do is delete a dragon. Pinned by `dragon.the-spawn-is-held-until-somebody-walks-to-the-middle`, which asks the real End level and the real fight.
- **Three phases, and each is a rotation rather than a stat boost.** Phase one is the island's kit (wing gust, ender barrage, crystal resonance, tail sweep, island shriek); phase two adds the void (void rift, swarm of the End, breath nova, blink); phase three adds the sky (rift storm, ender rain, withering gaze, roar of the End). Every move is **told** before it is thrown - its name on the action bar, its own call sound, and the particles that show where it will land - it is thrown once, and the rotation moves on without repeating itself. Crystals still fire, still heal, and still matter; the phase changes are announced with their own titles. Pinned by `dragon.every-phase-has-a-rotation-and-it-grows` (each phase has more moves than the last, no two share a name, and nothing is thrown with less than a second of warning).
- **The last stand, at one heart, exactly once.** A blow that would take a phase-three dragon to its floor does not land: the health stops at one heart, the dragon becomes untouchable, and it spends eleven seconds doing the worst thing it knows how to do - rings of fire walking out across the island with real damage in them, volleys of breath, everything dragged to the middle, a gaze that drinks from everybody at once, and then the sky. When that is over it **lands** (vanilla's own `LANDING` phase, so it is the same perch the vanilla fight uses), resumes its **phase-one rotation at one heart**, and is killable with the next hit. Pinned three ways: `dragon.the-last-stand-stops-at-one-heart-once-and-is-then-killable`, `dragon.three-phases-are-one-calculation` (one arithmetic, monotone in health), and an audit rule for the one-heart clamp - because a crystal that heals the dragon here would undo the entire point.
- **And the death is a ceremony.** The killing blow is swallowed and the dragon is pinned for five seconds of light coming out of a body that has already lost: beams cracking out of it in every direction, the island coming apart around it, a countdown on the action bar. Then the **real blow lands** - `genericKill`, an ordinary death - so the vanilla death animation, the experience, the portal, the egg and the fight's own bookkeeping all still run, with the extra VFX playing around the animation rather than instead of it.

### 👻 Vanish, again: the two things that were still showing, and a line that names the third
- **The locator bar was one mechanism and the glow was another.** Vanilla tracks every living thing whose `WAYPOINT_TRANSMIT_RANGE` is positive and nothing else, so invisibility has never hidden anybody from it; the body is stood down declaratively and its existing connections dropped. Glowing was being removed as an *effect* only - the entity's own glowing **flag** is the other half, it is what `/glowing` and a threat system set, and the outline is drawn either way.
- **The held items now have their own clock: every tick.** Armour and held items render by their own layers and neither asks whether the entity is invisible - and vanilla re-sends a full equipment packet the *instant* a viewer starts tracking a body, which is how a player walking up to a vanished moderator saw a floating sword for a fifth of a second. The packets that say "these slots are empty" are now re-sent every tick; the tab list and the locator bar keep their slower clock.
- **And the next report will name the cause.** If anything clears the invisibility flag, the glowing flag or the fire overlay after the vanish set it, the vanished player gets one private line saying which - because "vanish does not work" has cost three rounds of guessing now, and a report should come with a name in it.
- The self-test asks the question a client asks, on a real body: `vanish.the-body-is-invisible-to-another-player` vanishes a player and asserts `isInvisible()`, `isInvisibleTo(viewer)`, no locator-bar waypoint, no tab-list entry - and that unvanish gives all four back.

### 🏰 The fall: it was not killing anybody, and it did not look like anything
- **The blow at the end was real and enormous, so a player who survived it was never hit.** One huge magic blow lands on everybody still inside - and players were walking out of it, which can only mean the blow did *nothing*: something cancelled it and said nothing. **The outcome is verified now** rather than assumed. A blow that left the body exactly as it found it is followed by the deadline itself - and only that, because every save in this mod *changes* the body (a Totem of Undying and a Curse of Undying both put it on one heart, the castle's own rescue moves it to another dimension, and the duel gate and the prison both answer before the damage is applied), so a save is never overruled while a swallowed blow always is. The enforcement is an **ordinary damage type** (`genericKill`, the one `/kill` uses), so keep-inventory, graves, the wormhole and the death message all treat it exactly like any other death. It also **says so in the server log** - which player, where, what health the blow found - so the next report of this is diagnosable instead of a mystery. Pinned by `mirage.the-fall-enforces-its-deadline` (the swallowed-vs-saved rule, both ways, totems included) and the audit fails the build if the fall stops verifying its own kill.
- **And it now looks like a castle coming down.** The dust is each block's **own** dust (the break and crumble particles carry the state that was there a line earlier), the fall has **three intensities** instead of one - a wall giving way here and there, then the walls coming down in earnest at forty seconds, then everything at once with light pouring out of the seams at fifteen - with **shockwave rings walking out of the footprint**, a light pillar standing over the whole castle so it reads from the next biome, the mirage's veil (portal *and* the same light drawn back in through reverse portal), souls and fireflies and end rods rising out of it, and a **layered ending**: thirty-two explosion emitters, three shockwaves, a wind blast, and the blast, the death, the crack, the sky and the light going out - in that order, on purpose. No block damage and no entity damage anywhere in it: the clock is the threat, not the firework.

### 🗡️ Last Remembrance does something now
- **It was a stat line.** The one thing in the castle that was ever really there came off the monarch's body with Sharpness V, Fire Aspect II and lore, and its right-click did nothing a plain netherite sword does not. It has two abilities now, and both are things the castle itself did. **Let go** (right-click) is the mirage's own rescue - the promise that a fatal blow inside it puts you back at your spawn - performed by the blade that was standing in the hall, and it goes through the *same call* the fatal-blow rule uses, so the relic and the rescue cannot drift. Five-minute clock, six hearts on arrival, the castle's light left standing where you were. **The Court Remembers** (sneak-right-click) is one arc of remembered swordwork: everything in a 60-degree wedge in front, a real player-attack damage source, allies skipped the way the sword itself skips them, and the shove applied after the damage rather than as a second hit.
- **It refuses where leaving is somebody else's decision**: the duel arena, an expedition, the prison - and a **standing or falling Mirage Castle**, because the sixty-second escape window is the event's climax and the blade the boss handed you inside it cannot be the thing that skips it. Both abilities share **one clock**, armed with the *later* of the two times, so a twenty-second arc can never shorten a five-minute rescue. Pinned by `relic.last-remembrance-does-something` and the audit (one rescue implementation, the sneak branch, the shared clock).

### 👻 Vanish: off the locator bar, and nothing showing
- **The locator bar is a second tab list, and invisibility is not part of it.** Vanilla tracks every living thing whose `WAYPOINT_TRANSMIT_RANGE` is positive - that attribute, and nothing else - so a vanished moderator was a moving dot on everybody's screen while being invisible in every other sense. The body is stood down from the locator bar declaratively (an injection on `isTransmittingWaypoint`, which vanilla asks on every new tracking pair, so somebody who walks into range a minute later gets nothing) and existing connections are dropped now and re-dropped on a clock.
- **"It shows your items" had two causes and only one of them was handled.** Armour and held items render by their own layers and neither asks whether the entity is invisible, which is why an invisibility potion famously leaves a suit of armour walking around on its own. The armour was stashed for real; the *hands* were stashed too, until the moderation kit started being handed to the staff member straight into slot 0 a line after the vanish, undoing it. Hands are now emptied **in the packets** - the server tells every other client those slots are empty - so the kit stays right-clickable while being invisible. Also **glowing is stripped** (a separate render pass that survives invisibility - a glowing invisible player is a silhouette) and **scoreboard teams are parked**, because `seeFriendlyInvisibles` is literally "render this player to me even when invisible". All of it is re-asserted on a clock, and re-asserted *before* the tick that could show it. Pinned by `vanish.hides-everything-that-renders` and three audit rules, one of which I proved bites.

### 🥾 The kick: it was a disconnect the player could undo by reconnecting
- **Two different things were spelled "kick".** The automatic ladder's first rung is a *warning*: it removes the player and must never be listed as a ban. A moderator's kick is a real action with a person's name on it - and it had no re-entry window at all, so the kicked player rejoined the same second and the moderation action was invisible. That is what "the kick does not work" describes.
- **They are separate types now, and each does its job.** The ladder files `warn-kick` (never a standing punishment, so warnings no longer show up in the banned list as though they were bans); a moderator's kick files `kick` with **a one-minute re-entry window** that the **join path enforces**, so it is felt rather than undone. Pinned by `anticheat.a-kick-is-a-punishment-and-a-warning-is-not`, and the audit fails the build if the join path stops enforcing it or the ladder goes back to filing an ordinary kick.
- **And there is a command for it: `/ff anticheat kick <player> [reason]`**, from the console or from staff. Kicks happen at the moment somebody wants a cheater gone, which is usually not the moment they happen to be watching them through the spectate kit - and the kit's button now goes through the same one path, so the two behave identically.

### 🕶️ A blanket exemption could turn the whole fight off
- **`/ff anticheat exempt <player> *` covered every check in the file.** So an exemption granted for a movement reason - the "antifall" case - also silenced the kill aura, aim, reach, hitbox and criticals checks for that player: a cheater holding a flight exemption could not be caught hitting through walls. An exemption exists to stop *one measurement* being taken, and that was never one measurement.
- **A `*` exemption now covers mechanics and only mechanics.** Anything that judges a fight has to be named to be stood down, which makes the consequence of the switch something the person granting it can see. Pinned by `anticheat.a-blanket-exemption-cannot-blind-a-fight`, which checks the rule both ways - a blanket still covers speed, and a named ``killaura`` exemption still works.
- The "exempt now" readout asks `hasAnyExemption` rather than whether a `*` covers the literal check `"*"`, which with the new rule would always have answered no.

### 🪜 Fast climb has a model of its own now
- **Vanilla's ladder is a constant.** A body on a ladder, vine or scaffolding rises 0.2 blocks a tick whatever its movement attribute says, which is why fast-climb is not a branch of the speed check: the speed model cannot see it at all. `fast-climb` reads the body's own reported rise while it is actually standing on a climbable, needs **four ticks in a row** to be a finding, and breaks its measurement rather than pausing it for anything that can serve a body upward - a shove, a vehicle, water, levitation, elytra, slow falling. Advisory: recorded and alerted, never a correction, because the one thing that legitimately raises a body up a ladder faster than vanilla is a mod this server does not know about. Pinned by `anticheat.fast-climb-has-a-model`.
- **No-slow already existed** (`no-slow`, reading the used item's own declared price) and is unchanged; the gap was the climb.

### 📚 The mirage castle has a library that is a library
- **Every room got the same three things** - a chest, a piece of furniture, a centrepiece - which is a room, not a room of books. The library is the one whose whole identity is what is on its shelves.
- **So it gets the shelf wall:** two courses of shelving around the whole ring of the room, chiselled books above the plain ones, lantern bays left where a passage could be, reading desks with candles, and **two archive cases whose loot is a tier above the room's own** - both of them new cells, because a second spot on the room's own chest would stock the same chest twice.
- **Two cells of the ring are left to the room's design** (the corner it puts furniture in and the cell it is arranged around), and the floor is left exactly as the plan records it. The room-integrity check caught both of those as I built it - first a redrawn floor, then a shelf over the lectern - which is the check doing precisely its job. Pinned by `mirage.the-library-is-a-library`, including the arithmetic: every library raised has to carry the whole shelf wall.

### 🎯 "Invalid move" was refusing a direction because of how the number was written
- **The log says it was never about movement.** Every finding in the report that was kicking a player reads `facing 2127.65` - an angle, not a position, and one that is completely legal: vanilla **wraps yaw into [-180, 180) and clamps pitch when it applies a rotation**, precisely because the number on the wire is not bounded. A client that has been turning left all fight sends 2127.65 and means -32.35. Third-party client mods and camera work send the unwrapped form constantly.
- **So the check now does what the game does.** The angle is wrapped (and pitch clamped) before it is used or judged, every downstream reader - the turn-delta, the spin/aura measure, the stored last yaw - sees the wrapped value, and only a magnitude past `MAX_RAW_ANGLE` (a hundred times anything accumulated turning reaches) is refused. Vanilla tolerates unwrapped angles; we were refusing every packet, twice a second, with a set-back. Pinned both ways by `anticheat.an-unwrapped-yaw-is-a-direction-not-a-violation`, and `anticheat.packet-cheats` now asserts the opposite of what it used to (an unwrapped yaw is *accepted*, 900,000 degrees is not).

### ⚖️ A connection is only cut after repeated flagging
- **One reading, however high the bar, is still one reading.** The enforcement level is a high bar for a single measurement, and a server that acts on the first one acts on whatever its own arithmetic got wrong that tick. There are now **three strikes inside ten minutes** before anything is disconnected, and the two in between are *said to the player* - which finding is stacking, how many of three, and that nothing has been done yet - so "I do not know what I am being kicked for" has an answer before it is a kick. Pinned by `anticheat.one-reading-does-not-cut-a-connection`.

### 🧰 The gem shop: the membrane was never the membrane, and the rules are now enforced
- **"Repairing Membrane" sold a plain phantom membrane.** The shelf was stocked with the vanilla item the mod's membrane is built on, so the name and the price were right and the item was one the forge does not recognise. It stocks `ModItems.repairMembrane()` now, and the purchase hands the same thing over.
- **All four Mystery Keys, not just the common one.** There are four tiers of key and the shop shelved one, so the ladder they exist to be climbed was stocked one rung at a time. Kill one each at 5 / 12 / 25 / 50 gems, with the tier carried from the button to the reward by the shelf label itself.
- **"Never op" is written down once and asked in three places** - the shelf, the lore and the purchase, the last of which also refunds the gems if a reward ever fails the rule. Elytra, Heavy Core, Mace and Dragon Egg are on `NEVER_STOCKED`; the shelf draws a refusal instead of the item, the purchase refuses to hand it over, and the audit fails the build if the list, the predicate or the test seam goes away. Pinned by `shop.clerk-never-sells-an-op-item`.

### 🎒 Shift-clicking into a backpack did nothing, in both directions
- **The guard read "any index at or past the storage slots is a button"** - and every one of the player's own inventory slots is past them, so shift-clicking an item *into* the pack returned empty every time. That is the report. The direction that did work went through vanilla's own `ChestMenu`, which moves things into the container's whole slot range: the toolbar row included, so a shift-clicked stack could land on top of the Workbench button and be replaced when the toolbar was drawn. Now the toolbar is refused in both directions and items from your inventory go into storage and nowhere else.

### ⛏️ The prison arena was dirt over the void
- **One layer of blocks, floating.** Inside the rim a mottle of sand, gravel and coarse dirt - the "random dirt" - and one step past the outer wall *nothing*, which is the "then the void": a knockback over the wall, a walk through a gate, or a patch of floor broken by anything at all was a fall with no bottom.
- **The pit is the top of a solid mass now**, three deep, with an apron of real stone five blocks wide outside the outer wall, kerbed at its edge and railed with a gap at each gate, so the walkway around the pit is somewhere a prisoner can be knocked back onto rather than somewhere they get knocked off. The coarse dirt is gone: the pit floor is sand with grain and gravel trodden into it and the odd cracked slab showing through.

### 📺 The Bedrock question: every screen this mod opens is one Geyser can translate
- Loot boxes open a plain vanilla chest grid, and so does every other menu in the mod - checked, not assumed: a new audit rule reads all 78 menu sources and fails the build if any of them opens a screen type Geyser does not translate, or registers a menu type of its own. That is the one way a Java mod can produce "a Bedrock client cannot open ANY gui": a player the server believes is inside a menu they never received cannot open the next one either.

### 🔥 "Fire spread" was half a rule: it was only asked at the flame, never at the wood
- **The switch was read at the source and nowhere else.** A flame sitting inside a claim that had it switched off was held still - and the flame the neighbour's lava lit one block *outside* the claim was allowed to do anything, because its own position was on open ground. So the block it reached into was inside the claim, where nothing was asking a question. In game that read as "my fire is frozen and their fire walks in", which is the opposite of what the switch says.
- **The destination decides now, exactly as it does for water.** `ClaimManager.fireMayIgniteAt` is the fluid rule's twin, and it is asked every time fire is about to appear anywhere: `FireBlockMixin` hooks `getStateWithAge` (where vanilla builds the flame a spread would place) and refuses with the state that is *already at the position* - a no-op `setBlock` - rather than air, because vanilla replaces a flammable block with its flame and air would delete the plank instead of saving it.
- **Existing fire is untouched.** The hook only speaks when the state being built is fire and the block there is not already fire, so a flame inside a claim still ages, still lights the room and still burns out; it just cannot cross the border or be joined from outside. The old source-side cancel is kept.
- **Pinned three ways.** `claims.fire-cannot-ignite-a-protected-claim` walks all five cases of the rule without a claim in the world to light on fire, and the source audit fails the build if the mixin is deleted, dropped from `fortuneandfavors.mixins.json`, stops asking `ClaimManager.fireMayIgniteAt`, or loses the `getStateWithAge` hook - four ways the half-fix could come back with everything compiling perfectly.

### 🎒 The boombox now plays for people who are not on our client
- **Every note went over the mod's own channel.** The song was sent as a custom payload, its return value was thrown away, and a client that could not decode it got nothing: a Bedrock client behind Geyser, a vanilla client, and a modded client whose channel never registered all heard silence. "It does not work with Geyser players and does not work in general" is one bug with three faces.
- **A send that did not land now answers with a real sound.** `ClientboundSoundPacket` needs nothing from the client but the vanilla sound registry, so it is heard by everybody - including a Bedrock client translated by Geyser. The mod's own sound instance is still used when the channel *is* there, because only it loops seamlessly; the fallback is re-issued a song-length at a time from the disc's own `lengthInTicks()` (so a datapack disc of any duration repeats at its seam), re-anchored to the owner on every repeat, and silenced with `ClientboundStopSoundPacket` when the listener walks out of earshot or the disc is ejected.
- **`BackpackJukebox.shouldServeNative` is the single decision behind it**, read by both the owner's start and the nearby broadcast, so there is no path that plays the song twice or not at all. Pinned by `backpack.music-falls-back-to-a-real-sound`.

### 📚 The library: the custom enchantments had exactly one faucet, and it was a boss corpse
- **Every one of the mod's 29 custom enchantments is now shelved in the mirage's library**, as a real tome - levels I to III, the bosses keeping the top of the scale. The castle's library rooms were stocked with paper and bookshelves and nothing to read; the room whose whole identity is *knowledge* paid in stationery.
- **The shelf covers the entire list, and the check proves it.** `mirage.library-shelves-every-custom-enchantment` walks the shelf against `CustomEnchantments.ALL_KEYS` and then opens four hundred library chests, asserting every tome it finds carries a key that is actually on the shelf. A library that quietly shelved a subset would be the same bug one key at a time.
- **The library's tomes say where they came from.** They used to insist a raid boss dropped them while sitting in a bookcase, so `CustomEnchantments.tome(key, level, source)` puts the truth in the first lore line. Same item, same forge, same right-click behaviour.

### 🧿 The illusions stopped floating up
- **The chase aimed at the eyeline and pushed the whole vector.** Copies were sent toward `target.getY() + 1.0` - one block *above* the target's feet - with the y component included, so every copy was permanently asked to stand a block higher than whatever it was chasing. A body that can never quite arrive climbs for as long as it can see the mark, which is the report of illusions drifting into the sky.
- **They walk now.** The mark is the target's feet, the height difference is a one-block dead zone, and the vertical push only exists when the mark is a real step away - a target on a ledge, or below them. Everything else is the ground plane.

### ❄️ The snowstorm is heavier where the world is already freezing
- **A subzero area has no grace timer.** Standing in the open in a biome the game already snows in - snowy plains, cold ocean, ice spikes, frozen peaks, and any datapack biome cold enough - freezes you on the first tick of exposure instead of after twelve seconds, and the action bar says `SUBZERO AREA` so the rule is something you can play around rather than something you discover.
- **The warmth shrinks with it.** Six blocks of "stand near the fire" becomes two and a half, which is the difference between standing next to the warmth and standing *in* it. The mobs the storm freezes read the same radius, so the rule is one rule.
- **And you can see which storm you are in.** A subzero area gets a second, denser blanket of flakes over the ordinary one on the same packet, plus a grit layer, so the place where the cold is instantly lethal is also the place that looks like it.

The rule is the biome's own base temperature, so a datapack biome the game already snows in is included by construction. Pinned by `snowstorm.cold-biomes-are-subzero`, both sides of the boundary. The line that still told players to "get near a torch" after torches stopped being heat is gone with it.

### 👑 The King's title was announced to the middle of the map
- **`distance` is measured from the command source, and the source stood at 0, 0, 0.** `server.createCommandSourceStack()` is positioned at the overworld origin, so `title @a[distance=..32]` meant "within thirty-two blocks of the origin" - the hall's big on-screen title and sub-title, the announcement written for the room, were sent to whoever happened to be standing near world spawn and to nobody in the throne room.
- **The source is the King himself now** - his own level and his own position - which is the only place a hall-wide announcement can honestly be measured from. Every beat of the rise (the whisper, the bar, his name, the sword) goes through the same helper, so all four are fixed at once.

### 🏰 The mirage comes down when the monarch does
- **Kill the King and you have sixty seconds to be outside the walls.** The castle is a mirage - nothing was ever really there - so the moment the thing it was holding up is gone, it stops being held up. The countdown is on every action bar from the first tick (`THE MIRAGE IS FALLING - 43s - get outside the walls`), with chat marks at thirty, ten, five, three, two and one.
- **And it falls the whole time, visibly.** Ten of its own blocks stop existing per tick, taken with no drops and no neighbour cascade - this is a mirage being withdrawn, not a building being demolished, and the field it leaves behind has to be the field that was there before it rose. A blast walks the halls every second and a half (a real explosion *look* and a real explosion *sound*, with no block damage and no entity damage, because the clock is the threat rather than the fireball), and the four seconds before the end are one long light pouring out of the whole footprint.
- **Then a blast you can see from the next biome, and the field.** Sixteen explosion emitters across the castle, a portal plume over the footprint, and the ordinary teardown underneath it: every block back to what it was, dropped chest loot swept, guards and the court gone with it.
- **What is still inside goes with it.** The death is an ordinary one - a real damage source and a real health pool - so keep-inventory, graves, totems and every other death rule in the mod treat it exactly as they treat any other death. The **one** thing stood aside is the castle's own rescue, for exactly those bodies on exactly that tick: "the mirage lets you go" is the promise for a fight you could not win, and staying in a hall that has stopped existing is the other promise. Creative and spectator are untouched.
- **Pinned:** `mirage.the-fall-is-a-deadline` holds the window at sixty seconds and the countdown arithmetic inside it, and `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the deadline, the trigger, the final blast or the rescue exception go away. My own first draft of that check was wrong in a useful way - it asserted "no collapse is running", which the harness legitimately violates because earlier checks kill monarchs - and it caught a real bug on the way: the escape clock read the tick counter without asking whether anything was falling, so it could show a countdown to nobody.

### ❄ Fire warms you, torches do not
- **The storm's heat list was a wall of lamps.** Torches, lanterns, sea lanterns, glowstone, shroomlights, froglights, furnaces and candles all counted as heat, so the one decision the event asks - find real warmth or freeze - was answered by lighting a corridor. Heat is now **fire, soul fire, magma and lava** (the only warmth in the game that never goes out) plus a **lit campfire**, which is the exception that makes the rule worth playing: a campfire is the one warmth you can carry with you, and it **burns down forty-five seconds after the storm finds it** - going out rather than being taken away, so the block stays where you put it and can be relit. The timer starts when the storm sees it, not when it was placed.
- **Pinned:** `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the campfire burn-down goes away, and if a torch becomes heat again.

### 🗂 A false-positive verdict now does something about the check
- **Staff could mark a check wrong and the check kept shouting.** A verdict cleared the counts for one player and sat next to the evidence, and nothing else changed - so a check that was wrong on a quarter of the server got quieter for exactly one person. Every verdict now raises the bar that check has to clear to reach chat, by a third of it at a time and no more than four verdicts' worth. **It raises the alert bar and nothing else**: findings are still recorded at the same level, the ledger and the case file still show everything, and the allowlist that decides who may be removed is untouched - the point is to stop a wrong check talking, not to stop it looking. `/ff anticheat` names every check staff have called wrong and says what that did to it.
- **Pinned:** `anticheat.reviews-quiet-the-check-that-was-wrong` reads the rule as arithmetic over a count (one verdict is a nudge, four is quieter, fifty is no quieter than four, no verdicts is exactly the baseline) and re-asserts that being reviewed never puts a check back in the enforcement allowlist.

### 🚫 The banned screen listed people who were only warned
- **A warning kick is not a ban.** The automatic ladder hands out a 0-duration kick before it hands out anything else, and the banned screen listed every record with a punishment on it - so a player kicked once an hour ago and playing normally now sat next to somebody who was actually timed out, and the one person the admin opened the screen for was somewhere in that list. A record belongs there if it has a **running timeout** or a punishment that is not one of the ladder's kicks; the kicks stay on the record and the case file, where they are evidence rather than a status.
- **`/ff anticheat` opens the records screen from a player.** It used to print a guide: a person typing it is about to do something (look at who was flagged, open a case file, lift a punishment) and a page of text answers none of that, so the account half opens the screen the actions already live on and the status lines still print underneath. A console has no screen and keeps the text, and `/ff anticheat status` is the text-only form for anyone who wants it.
- **Pinned:** `anticheat.a-warning-kick-is-not-a-ban`, as arithmetic over a single punishment's own fields rather than by filing a fake offender into the live store.

### ⏱ The Pocket-Watch stopped doing true damage
- **The bank was paid out as one number, so the game's arithmetic ran once.** The stop swallowed every hit inside it and delivered the total through a single damage call: armor, protection, resistance and absorption each applied once to a sum, so five sword hits that would each have been halved by a netherite chestplate landed as one blow nothing reduced - which is exactly what "it does TRUE damage" describes. The **individual hits are kept** now and handed back one at a time through the source each of them arrived with, inside the same tick, with i-frames cleared only between the banked hits of that one payout. Every point of the reduction is vanilla's again, including how much health the victim has left to lose.

### 🧭 Two more reasons "invalid move" was firing on normal play
- **A body held still by its own trinket is not the server failing to keep it anywhere.** The "the server has been repositioning this body for N ticks" rule counted the position writes a Pocket-Watch or a stopped clock makes every tick, which is how an INVALID_MOVE finding landed on players who were being pinned by their own ability. It now stands down for a reason that *holds* a body in place - a stopped clock, a bed - and deliberately **not** for a teleport or a shove: a body the server keeps teleporting is exactly what a client abusing stand-downs looks like, and the check that a constant correction is itself a finding depends on that still counting.
- **One unexplained eight-block step is worth a fifth of a finding, not a whole one.** A spike, a shove whose packet the module never saw, a dismount, another mod moving the body - every innocent cause produces exactly one, and all of them looked identical to a client doing it on purpose. The first is recorded (ledger, case file, evidence) and cannot reach chat on its own; the second one inside the window carries full weight, and a client that is really teleporting produces it within seconds.
- **Killaura is quieter.** The refusal threshold went from 6 points to 10, a target switch inside 3 ticks no longer counts as an aura signature, and a hitbox miss contributes half a point instead of a whole one - the hitbox finding is already recorded on its own, and letting the same measurement also fill the aura meter is how one stale box became two findings against a player who was aiming correctly.

### 🗡 The Distant Memory does not shove what it hits
- **A blade with no cooldown that also decides where a fight happens was the part nobody asked for.** It carries a negative attack-knockback modifier, which is the game's own knockback term - so the base shove, the sprint bonus and a Knockback enchantment on top all go away together, because all three arrive through that one number. Vanilla clamps a non-positive shove, so targets stand where they were hit rather than being pulled.

### 🧭 The automatic half only acts on arithmetic now
- **"I got kicked as soon as I clicked and moved, and I do not know what for" has a cause and it is not a threshold.** The failsafe could act on *any* check that reached the enforcement level, including the ones whose verdict is a **measurement** - the hitbox ray, "invalid move", the aim window, the click pattern, the knockback shortfall. Every one of those is a statement about a moment, and every one of them can be true of an honest player: a pocket watch stops one body and moves another, a lag spike lands between a break's start and its stop, a proxy batches two packets. The action now reads an allowlist, `AUTO_ENFORCE`, and the whole of it is the checks with a **physical model** behind them - speed, flight, air-walk, ground-spoof, no-fall, scaffold, fast-mine, fast-place, block reach, x-ray. A check added tomorrow is advisory until somebody argues it belongs there, and being wrong about an advisory check costs a line in a log rather than a player's evening.
- **And a kick now says what it was for.** Every removal carries the check and the evidence it measured, on the disconnect screen and in the player's chat first - because a player removed by a threshold they cannot see has no way to tell a false positive from a real one, and neither does the admin they complain to.
- **Bedrock players through Geyser are not measured on Java geometry.** Geyser translates Bedrock's hitboxes into the server's and batches its packets, so the hitbox ray, "invalid move", reach, the aim windows and the packet-timing checks are statements about the translation layer rather than about the player. They are asked of Geyser's own API, not of a permission, and **nothing else is relaxed** - x-ray, scaffold, fast-mine and the physical movement model judge a Bedrock client exactly as they judge anyone else. The API lookup and the answer are both cached, so a busy server does not pay for it per finding.
- **The hitbox check needs a repeat, and a stopped clock stands it down.** It fired on a single miss, which is what a stale box looks like: the client aims at the box as of the packet it last received. Two misses in a row is still nothing an honest client produces, and a client aiming at a grown box misses every time - and a hit landed inside a Pocket-Watch or the Time Lord's stop is no longer read as a target with a bigger box, for the same reason it was already not a knockback hack.
- **Pinned:** `anticheat.only-a-model-check-can-remove-a-player`, `anticheat.a-bedrock-client-is-not-judged-on-java-geometry` and `anticheat.a-kick-says-which-check-fired`, all as predicates over names so they hold with no proxy and no client. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the fake-disconnect line, the "a moderator is now watching you" line or a moderator in spectator mode ever come back - including inside a comment, because comments are stripped before the match.

### 👁 Watching somebody is now a kit, and it is silent
- **`/ff anticheat spectate` said it out loud, which defeated the point.** A player told they are being watched does not behave like a player, so the announcement is gone: nothing is sent to the target at all. The moderator is now **vanished and in creative** rather than in spectator mode - a spectator cannot open a chest, break a block or hold anything, and every one of those is a thing a moderator needs while deciding.
- **Their inventory is replaced by a nine-slot kit, and put on disk first.** *Confirm hacking* files the verdict and opens the punishment screen, *False positive* deletes every count and records the verdict, *Kick*, *Timeout 5m*, *Lift the punishment*, *The record* (opens the same screen in its list mode), *Go to them* (one attributed position write, so a moderator moving is not read as a moderator teleporting), *Watch closely*, and the ninth slot **stops spectating** and puts everything back. Every item carries the watched player's uuid, so a restart cannot unpair the kit from the player.
- **It survives a logout and a restart, because the gear is a file rather than a variable.** The thirty-six slots are written through the item codec under the staff member's uuid before a single one is cleared, and the way back is one call: kit out, wardrobe back, file restored over the top, place and mode from the anticheat store. A moderator who crashes, logs out or is there when the server stops gets their items and their position back on next join - with a line saying so, because silently replacing nine slots would be worse than the bug it fixes.
- **`/ff vanish` was not vanishing anyone.** Every part of vanish is a flag another system can clear - invisibility is entity data, the tab list is re-sent by scoreboards, guilds and joins alike - so setting it once lasted until the next writer. It is re-applied once a second now, including the tab-list removal, which is the only way to be sure a player who joined a minute ago cannot see a name everybody else cannot. The name above the head goes too, and the fake "left the game" and "joined the game" lines are gone: an observer sees nothing, which is the entire point.
- **Pinned:** the kit's stash write, its ninth slot and the join-time recovery are all audit rules now, so the batch cannot come undone by a rename.

### 👑 The Emerald Sovereign stops vanishing without a payout
- **His fight ended the moment his body could not be looked up.** An entity in a chunk nobody is standing in cannot be found by uuid, and the tick read that as "he is gone" - tore the fight down, boss bar and all, and dropped nothing. There is a **ten-second grace window** now: a body that is merely out of sight comes back the moment somebody walks near it, and a body that is really gone is a fight that is really over, which pays its loot **at the place he was last standing** instead of losing it with him. Whatever route takes him out - a command, a plugin, the void, a crash - the fight he was in pays what it promised.
- **Pinned:** `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the grace window is removed.

### 🗡 The Distant Memory looks like a sword
- **It is a plain sword texture now and holds like a sword.** The art is the supplied blade - teal edge, dark outline, wrapped handle - written at 16x16, and the model's parent moved from `item/generated` to `item/handheld` so it sits in the hand and in the hotbar the way every other blade does. Nothing about the item changed: it is still a diamond sword with its own damage and attack speed, so every sword enchantment still applies, Knockback included.

### 🐷 The Z pit is a fist fight now, and the harbour is a place
- **The arena was a car park with a pond in it.** A flat stone square, a cobblestone-wall ring and a 9x9 pool: functional, and the kind of thing that reads as a test level. It is a gladiator pit now - sand and gravel over a chiselled rim, a two-high curb open at four barred gates, a five-block wall with an arched window in each face, banners on the inside, a fence railing on top, lamps on the aisle corners and a lamp over every gate. The gates are one list (`ARENA_GATES`) that the frames, the lamps and the spawn points all read, so a fifth gate cannot exist in one of them and be missing from another.
- **And the horde is zombies, and only zombies.** Spiders, pillagers and witches are gone: the pit is where you find out whether you can fight a crowd, not whether you can answer a crossbow across open ground. Every one of them comes out of a gate instead of materialising in the sand behind you, wears a **leather helmet**, and carries **nothing at all**. Two lines do the real work. `setCanPickUpLoot(false)`, because a vanilla zombie picks dropped gear up - which is how "no weapons" quietly becomes a zombie swinging the axe a player died next to. And the drop chances are zeroed, because an arena that rains leather is an arena with a farm in it. Difficulty is carried by how many arrive and how much they can take: health scales with the wave, boots appear at wave 4, and one zombie in four from wave 6 is a full-leather veteran with 60% more health.
- **The fishing spot became a harbour.** The pool was 9x9 with three decorations; it is a stone bench now with a sand-and-gravel beach, a two-deep sand-bedded pool with a stone curb, a plank pier on log pilings with two lamps at the end, a bait shack with a crafting table, a barrel and a lamp, reeds where the bank meets the water, lily pads on it, crates along the walk and lamp posts around the bench. **A prisoner used to be teleported into the middle of the water** to fish; the drop is the pier now, dry and facing the pool.
- **The mine floors are dressed.** Timber frames on the nine-paces, a plank cornice under the ceiling (skipped where the wall already carries an ore stud, because the studs are what tell a prisoner which floor they are on), rubble in the rock, a lamp bracket on each face and a chiselled base under the spawner. Every block of it is deliberately **not** in the ore slab: the floor is income and none of this is allowed to touch what it pays.
- **Pinned:** `prison.the-horde-is-unarmed` walks four waves and reads each body's hands, helmet, pickup flag and drop chances; `prison.the-arena-and-the-harbour-meet` asserts the harbour bench ends exactly one block west of the arena's wall - the prison world is a void world with no layers, so a block of gap between them is a fall out of the world, and the rework could have left one. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the pickup flag, the empty hand, the helmet, the gates or the shared gate list goes away, and if a sword, a crossbow, an iron chestplate or a diamond helmet ever appears in the method that builds the horde.

### 🗿 A body can outlive its fight, which is the same bug from the other side
- **The reconciliation from last release only ever ran one way.** A fight whose body is gone is put down through the one teardown; a body whose *fight* is gone had nothing looking for it. The state that drives a boss lives in memory and dies with the process, so a crash or a hard restart between a teardown and a save leaves a King, a Slime King, a Stone Golem, an Elder Warden, a Mindbinder or a Snow Queen standing in the world with no entry in the fight map - no AI, no ticking, no loot, and nothing that will ever claim it. Seven scripted managers have swept for exactly this since the orphaned-statue bug; `BossManager`, the one with six bosses and the busiest spawn paths in the mod, did not.
- **It could not, and that was the real finding: these bodies carried no mark.** A sweep keyed on `ff_boss` would have reaped a live Clockwork King or Puppeteer, since the scripted bosses wear that tag too, and a bare type check would have reaped an honest wither skeleton or an iron golem a player built. They carry a marker of their own now (`ff_rigged_boss`), and it is applied by **the loop that drives the fights** rather than at each of the seven spawn sites - a list of spawn paths kept by hand is the drift this whole audit is about, and a boss added tomorrow is marked by construction. `sweepOrphanBosses` then asks the only question that separates a leftover from a bystander: is this a body a fight in this file owns, and is that fight gone?
- **Pinned:** `bosses.a-leftover-body-is-swept-and-a-bystander-is-not` plants a marked wither skeleton and an unmarked one beside it in the real world and asserts the first is gone and the second is still alive - the discriminator, both directions. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the sweep, the marker, the mark in the fight loop or the call site disappears.

### ⛏ Fast-mine was measuring the wire, not the player
- **A lag spike between the two packets shortened the measurement.** The elapsed time is between a break's start packet and its stop packet, which travel the same wire, so a steady connection cancels out on both ends and the estimate is right. A spike *between* them does not cancel: the stop gets out while the start is still queued, the break looks shorter than it was, and the client that suffers it is an honest player on a bad connection - which is who this check was refusing blocks to. The player's own measured round trip is borrowed back now, the same correction the reach, placement and ore checks have made through `latencyTicks` for a while, and the bar itself is untouched: half the block's real break time is still the line, so a client genuinely breaking at twice the speed lands on the wrong side of it whatever its ping.
- **And the map that check reads had an escape hatch.** Opening a break on sixty-four positions and never finishing them used to *clear* the record, so a client could wipe the start of the one block it meant to break - and a break with no recorded start has no elapsed time and is never compared against the estimate at all. That is a fast-mine check switched off by the party it is aimed at, reachable by writing sixty-five packets. An overflow is evidence now (a `packet-flood` finding, weighted low and corroborated like everything else) rather than a silent reset.
- **Pinned:** `anticheat.break-estimate` asserts a 40-tick break measured in 18 ticks **passes** for a player with a six-tick round trip, still fails with no lag at all, and still fails at 2 ticks measured with the same tolerance - so the correction cannot become cover. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the latency argument, the call site or the overflow report goes away.

### 🧱 A source file nobody wrote could fail the whole build
- **`class MiningZoneManager is public, should be declared in a file named MiningZoneManager.java`** - pointing at `MiningZoneManager 2.java`: a file nobody had written and nobody had edited, because iCloud had left a stale copy of it beside the real one. Gradle hands every `.java` in the source set to javac, so a conflict copy of a source file is not a stray file the game ignores - it is a *duplicate public class*, and it is a hard failure of `compileJava`. The resource roots had already learned this lesson (every copy task excludes conflict copies and `scrubICloudConflicts` deletes them); the **Java** source sets never did, and the scrub was wired only to the tasks that build a game - `jar`, `runClient`, `runServer` - never to `compileJava`, the one build that reads the sources and nothing else.
- **Two guards, because the deletion half is the half that failed here.** The Java source sets now refuse conflict copies outright, so a stale copy can never be compiled whether or not it has been reached yet, and every `JavaCompile` task asks for the scrub first. Deleting is still the hygiene half, and it still runs: the scrub took **834** conflict copies out of the tree when it was first pointed at this, this one included - it had survived every previous build because no previous build had asked for the scrub before the compiler.
- **Pinned:** `ffAuditSources` asks the **real source sets** what they would hand javac, rather than pattern-matching a file, and fails if any conflict copy is in there - *"main source set compiles 1 iCloud conflict copy/copies (MiningZoneManager 2.java) - one duplicate public class in there fails the whole build"*. Proven both ways: with both guards off and a copy planted, `compileJava` fails with exactly the message above; with only the source-set exclusion on and the same copy still on disk, it compiles green.

### ⚰️ Every way a boss can leave the world without dying, and the one door all of them use
- **A body can leave the world without dying, and the five ways are not all ours.** An operator's `/kill`, a plugin, the void, another event's teardown taking the body with it. Nothing in this mod calls a boss's killed-handler for a body that is not there, so the loop that drives the six bosses in `BossManager` - the King Wither Skeleton, the Slime King, the Stone Golem, the Elder Warden, the Mindbinder, the Snow Queen - answered a missing body by doing *nothing*: the fight stayed registered, its health bar stayed on every screen it had been sent to, and every stick of its rigging (the King's rods and soul fires, the Slime's goo and scorpion cages, the golem's boulder, the Warden's tangles and homing charges, the Queen's mists) stayed in the world for the rest of the session. The loop reconciles now: it asks whether the ground the body was last seen on is loaded, because a loaded patch with no body on it is proof of removal and an unloaded one is only distance, and a proved-removed fight is put down through the one teardown at once.
- **And a boss who is merely away loses his bar, not his fight.** The same lookup answers null for a body in a chunk the server is not ticking, so treating that as an exit would have ended a fight for walking away. A fight whose body cannot be found and has never been seen keeps its state; only the bar comes down, because a health bar over ground the boss is not standing on is a lie on the screen, and it goes back up when the body does.
- **The server stop that never existed.** The mod registers a teardown for seven scripted managers and registered none for the six bosses that live in `BossManager`, so a stopped server saved their bodies and all of their rigging while the state that drove them vanished with the process - the orphaned-statue bug, exactly. They leave by their own doors now, because those doors know things a bare removal does not: the Snow Queen hands her realm's players back before she goes, the Elder Warden returns what he stole, the Mindbinder takes the corruption off the people he had it on.
- **One teardown, and the drift it ends.** Each boss's own despawn used to hand-list which rigging applied to it, and the lists had drifted - the Slime King's forgot his soul fires and the King's rods, the Elder Warden's forgot the shared ones, and `/ff boss clear` (which still keeps its promise and leaves the bodies standing) never knew about the golem's boulder *entity*, the King's poison zones or the Warden's tangles and homing charges. Every exit now goes through `dismissBossCore`, and a leftover map added tomorrow is cleared by construction rather than by memory.
- **The reworked wither's bar was the same bug.** Its health bar is the wither's own vanilla `ServerBossEvent`, taken off screens only by the death path - so a wither removed by a command, a plugin or a world event left its bar up over an empty patch for the session. The body-gone branch hides it, and `/ff boss wipe` now discards the bodies and the entourage instead of only forgetting them, which is what made "the wipe does not work" look true.
- **Pinned per path:** `bosses.a-body-taken-out-of-the-world-takes-its-fight-with-it` (a live Elder Warden, a real tick, then the body simply stops existing), `bosses.a-boss-who-is-only-away-loses-his-bar-and-keeps-his-fight` (the discriminator, both directions), `bosses.every-despawn-door-takes-the-whole-fight-with-it` (every kind of rigging planted, including kinds the boss has no business having), `bosses.the-operator-clear-takes-the-rigging-with-it`, `bosses.a-server-stop-leaves-no-boss-and-no-rigging-behind` and `bosses.a-scripted-fight-drops-its-bar-when-its-body-is-taken` (the Clockwork King, for the pattern the scripted managers share). `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the reconciliation, the discriminator or the per-boss dispatch disappears, and if any map the leftovers probe asks about stops being cleared by the one teardown.

### 🖼️ The item art is a pack now, and it cannot be switched off
- **"The textures unload when there is too much loaded."** The art has always shipped inside the mod, but *loose* - which means it rides on Fabric's shared **mod resources** entry in the resource-pack screen. Turn that entry off (a mis-click, a pack shuffle, another launcher rewriting `options.txt`) and every custom item in the mod renders as the plain vanilla thing it is built on: the art is still on disk, so nothing looks broken, it just stops. That is what "the textures unloaded" is, and no amount of art fixes it.
- **The pack is registered as the mod's own, `ALWAYS_ENABLED`.** The same art is staged a second time inside the jar as a pack root of its own (`resourcepacks/art`) and registered as a built-in pack that is always on, so it goes into the stack unconditionally - nothing to install, nothing to keep in step, no zip that can arrive stale or disabled. It never throws: if the registration is ever refused, the loose copy is still there and the art still loads, and the log says so.
- **Pinned:** `resources.the-item-art-is-a-pack-that-cannot-be-switched-off` asserts the staged root exists in the jar with its `pack.mcmeta`, an item definition and real art in it, that the registration and the staged root agree on **Fabric's own convention** (`resourcepacks/<pack path>`, the one wire a Fabric update can move without a compile error), and that the pack was actually accepted at startup. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the registration, the activation type or the staged root goes away - or if a call site goes back on the bare gate below.

### ⏱️ The once-a-minute work was stacked on one tick
- **The same bug as the five-minute sweeps, one period down.** Eight world sweeps were all spelled `tick % 6000 == 0` and were given their own slots; three unrelated tasks were still spelled **`tick % 1200 == 0`** - the mining zones' containment sweep (which walks every zone on the server and the hostiles inside it), the expedition cooldown prune and the guild mailbox cleanup - so once a minute a single tick paid for all three, forever. They all still run every minute; each owns its own slot in the cycle now (offsets **100 / 500 / 1100**), chosen to clear both neighbours as well: none is a multiple of 200, where the mining zones' own hostile spawn and the mindbinder's expiry prune sit, and none lands on a five-minute sweep's slot either.
- **Pinned:** `perf.once-a-minute-work-does-not-stack` walks the whole cycle and asserts no tick owes more than one of them, that none collides with a five-minute sweep, and that none sits on the 200-tick gate; `ffAuditSources` fails the build if any of the three goes back to `% 1200 == 0`.

### 🧪 The speed check was measuring the machine, not the module
- **`anticheat.sustained-speed-keeps-being-detected` passed or failed on how busy the computer was.** A finding's confidence is discounted by the server's *measured* tick health - deliberately, because that is what keeps an honest player on a struggling server out of a correction - and the self-test is itself a struggling server: every check in the harness is a burst of work inside a single tick, so the tick-time buffer reads eleven to fifteen ticks a second for as long as the suite runs, and whether the once-a-second sample happened to land on a heavy moment decided whether the escalation to a real setback was reachable. Unpinned, the check could not tell "the module stopped escalating" from "the machine was busy", and it failed two runs in five while nothing about the module had changed.
- **The tick rate is now pinned for the length of that check**, so what it tests is the escalation it is named for. The discount it was accidentally measuring is pinned instead where it can be read without a server: **`anticheat.confidence-falls-with-the-server`** asserts a real finding clears the bar on a healthy server, is worth strictly less on one missing a third of its ticks, is below the bar there (an honest player on a lagging server is not moved), and is still recorded rather than discarded at three ticks a second.

### 📖 The monarch keeps two things back, and one of them is above the table
- **An Efficiency VII book and an Astral tome, rarely, off his body.** The hall pays its treasure every time he falls; these are the reason to come back. Two **independent** rolls - **12%** for the book and **8%** for the tome - so a run that finds one is not promised the other, and 80% of kills leave neither. They are marked as his the moment they are minted, which is what spares them in the castle's teardown sweep: they came off the body that was really there, exactly like the blade, so a player who kills him and then lets the event end does not find a rare drop deleted off the floor it fell on.
- **And the book does what it says.** This is the half that would have shipped broken: **Efficiency VII** is above the enchantment's own maximum, and vanilla's anvil *clamps* a merge to that maximum - so a book reading VII would have gone onto a pickaxe and become Efficiency V with nothing to say for itself. The anvil now recognises a book that is above its own maximum and hands it to the mod's own merge, which writes the level it was handed. Everything else about the anvil is unchanged: a book is still not a licence to enchant an anvil, and ordinary books still go through vanilla.
- **Pinned:** `mirage.the-monarch-keeps-two-things-back` walks the whole band - all 100 tickets, most paying nothing, each keeping still rare - then reads the book for a real stored **Efficiency 7** and asserts it is genuinely past the maximum (so the check cannot pass on a book the anvil would clamp), confirms the second keeping *is* the Astral tome, rolls every tier against every kind of room to prove neither is ever a furnishing, and then plants both on a real throne hall's floor and gives the ground back underneath them to prove the sweep spares them while it still takes a diamond. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the over-max recognition, the teardown's exception or the marking disappears, or if the book is minted anywhere other than the one roll.

### 🔨 The monarch's fall pays a blade, and his bar leaves with the castle
- **The one thing in the castle that was never a mirage, off the body that was swinging it.** The hall, the guard, the treasure and the throne are all somebody else's room - held in the world for the length of the event and handed back block for block when it ends, which is why the loot leaves with the walls. His greatsword is the exception the whole fiction rests on, so **Last Remembrance** drops from him: netherite, **Sharpness V · Fire Aspect II · Unbreaking III**, once, and **not in a single chest in the structure**. It is minted by the same call that arms him at the fourth beat of his announcement, so the blade he swings and the blade he drops cannot drift into two different items - and the teardown's sweep of dropped loot now excepts it, because killing the boss and then letting the event end must not delete the unique drop off the floor.
- **The health bar goes with the castle now.** A bar is a `ServerBossEvent` handed to players, and the only thing that ever took one off a screen was the killed-handler - which walks bodies still in the world.
