The castle takes its King out by *discarding* him, and a discarded body is a body that handler never sees: a castle that ended mid-fight left its monarch's bar up over an empty field for the rest of the session, with nothing left alive to take it down. There is now a way to put a fight down without a kill - bar and leftovers, no payout - and both the castle's teardown and a hall whose body has already gone use it.
- **`mirage.the-kings-bar-goes-with-the-castle`** drives the real hall: a real King, woken the way a player wakes him, then the castle taken down underneath him - and asserts the bar that was on is off and the fight is gone. **`mirage.the-monarchs-blade-is-his-and-it-stays`** walks the whole life of the drop: it is not in the castle's loot, the sword in his hands *is* it at Sharpness V, felling him puts one on the floor, and when the ground is given back the blade is still there while a diamond planted beside it is not. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the dismissal, the drop, the arming call or the sweep's exception disappears, or if the blade is ever minted more than once in a file.

### 🏰 The tribute is material, and it leaves when the castle does
- **A beacon and a finished netherite chestplate are out of the throne's chests.** The castle's best tier is *material* now - scrap, ancient debris, raw treasure - because the best chest in the structure is where you should get what a court would have kept before it was worth anything: the stuff you go and *make* the trophy out of. Diamond blocks, an enchanted golden apple, a totem, XP and the shulker box stay, and **the netherite scrap and ancient debris stay** - the two that made "a chest paid me a finished build" are the ones gone.
- **The loot goes with the castle, and so does anything else on the floor.** Giving the ground back puts every chest back the way it was found, which vanilla answers by dropping the contents where the chest stood - so a castle ending on a full vault sprinkled its loot across the grass through walls that were no longer there. The King's payout lands the same way. Every dropped item standing inside the footprint now leaves with the walls, with a puff of smoke, while anything outside the footprint is untouched. Both halves are pinned: the loot table is rolled 200 times for the banned items, and a real dropped stack inside a real castle is asserted gone after the teardown (and one outside it asserted still there).

### 🔓 The break-out ends at a locked gate now
- **The run had no door in it.** A loose brick opened a corridor, guards flooded in behind you and a glowstone pad 26 blocks away paid 1,200 - less than two guard bounties, for the only run in the block that can get you killed. It is a set piece now: the tunnel ends in a **barred gate, three by three**, with a lantern over it, and the gate opens for **the Warden's key** - and nothing else.
- **The Warden runs the tunnel.** A break-out now sends him down it as well as the patrol, because the key is his: put him down and he hands it over (`KEY TAKEN`), and the bars come out when you walk up to them. Stand at the gate without it and the action bar says so. The clock went 45s → **90s** to cover the fight, and the jackpot went 1,200 → **3,000 + 700 a rank**, which at the top of the ladder is a whole rank-up out of one tunnel.
- **And the gate cannot be walked around.** A prisoner is issued an iron pickaxe and the tunnel is carved through a mine slab, so a wall beside a locked gate was a detour measured in seconds. The last stretch's own rock - two blocks before the gate, past the exit, three to each side, floor to above head - is held for the duration, through the same funnel that refuses a standing Mirage Castle's walls, and given back exactly as it was found when the run is sealed.

### 🪶 The Gladiator's wings are gone - the map is the opponent
- **An elytra was never really an item in this mode, it was a rewrite of it.** Walking the map *is* the fight: with wings, the fighter losing any exchange simply leaves it, reaches the far caches first and crosses the arena in a straight line while everyone else goes around a hill. Even the one-durability version was the item that answers every question the map asks, so what the far rim pays in that slot now is **three ender pearls** - a burst of distance you have to aim and a fall you have to have thought about - with a **slow-falling splash** in the same cache, which is the half of an escape that makes the fall a decision instead of a death. Both cost you the ground you were standing on, not the ground between you and them. `duel.gladiator-gearing-truce` reads the real pool of every tier and refuses a cache with wings in it.
- **The map grew with it: 216x208 → 240x232** (with the plot stride and the cache spread pushed out to match - 52 field caches and 12 underground vaults, so the density is what it was and the walk between them is not). Landmarks in a 240-block arena have to be *found*, which is the point of taking the sky away: the far rim is a decision now, not a trip.

### ⛏ The prison ladder pays, and full Heat sends the Warden
- **A rank used to buy exactly one thing**: which ore was in the rock under your feet - over a flat price table, so rank F's diamond paid what rank A's did and climbing was the same work with a bigger number on it. Prisoners now earn a **trustee rate: +2% per rank, up to +50% at Z**, on everything they sell. It is applied in the one place that pays a sale (so it can never be applied twice), shown on the Prison Menu's rank line, and announced when you earn it. Deliberately modest: prison cash converts to main cash 2:1, so this is the one number in there that reaches the rest of the economy.
- **Full Heat had no climax.** The manhunt escalated by arithmetic nobody can feel - four guards instead of two, arriving a little sooner, which is the same fight held longer. At Heat **75** the block now sends **The Warden**: netherite, 140 HP and 9 damage at rank A (scaling with rank), one at a time, and putting him down pays 2,500+ and **wipes your record to 0** - the only clean record that isn't bought by selling. Run from him and the block waits 90 seconds before sending him again rather than letting you farm the same fight.
- **The way out of prison is remembered across a restart.** The spot a prisoner was booked in from lived in memory only, so a restart released them at the world spawn - a place they had never stood. It is written to `prison.json` with the stash now, and `prison.the-way-out-survives-a-restart` drives the real save and the real load.

### 🪂 The elytra is out of the castle - including the one already in a chest
- **"Not in the loot table" was a comment, and a comment cannot empty a chest.** The castle's loot has not rolled an elytra by design for a while, and the reason is sound: this is the one structure in the mod that cannot be broken out of and cannot be escaped by dying, so the item that skips both is the last thing it should hand you. What the comment could not do is reach the chest that already holds one - a chest is part of the save, so a stack that got in under an older rule, or that a player put there, stays there for the life of that world no matter how the loot tables read. That is exactly what "the elytra is still in the throne room" is.
- **The rule is enforced twice now, and the second half is the one that matters.** One list says what the castle withholds; the single call that builds its loot refuses those stacks, so no room, tier or tribute chest can put one back; and while the castle stands its own chests are swept every five seconds, so an elytra that is already in one comes back out (with a quiet line to everyone nearby - it is not dropped on the floor, it was never the castle's to give). `mirage.the-castle-pays-no-elytra` rolls every tier against every kind of room - the same call the stocking path makes - and then plants an elytra in a real chest of a real castle and asserts the sweep takes it out; `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the withheld list, the filter or the sweep disappears.

### 🗺️ `/raidboss` drew buttons that answered nothing
- **The overview was two hand-kept lists, and they had drifted.** Every boss cell drew its token from one list and answered clicks from another, and only the first eight cells had a click route - so the Clockwork King, the Starbound Magister, the Void Shaper, the Emerald Sovereign and the Puppeteer showed a button that did nothing, and the Puppeteer's own cell was then painted over by the padding loop, so even his token was invisible. From in game that reads as "the new recipes are not in /raidboss", which is exactly how it was reported.
- **A button is one row now**: the cell, the page it opens and what it says, in a single table that both halves of the window read. The padding asks that table before it paints, and a page with no cell, or a cell that opens nothing, is no longer something this window can express.
- **The terminal list was missing two bosses.** `/raidboss help` printed twelve rows and the Elder Warden and the Puppeteer were not among them, so a console user could read every summon recipe but theirs. That list is one row per boss as well, and it covers all thirteen. `commands.raidboss.every-button-opens-the-recipe-it-draws` insists every page has a cell, every cell opens its own page, nothing is drawn over, and every page is named in the terminal list; `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the drawing and the clicking become two lists again.

### 🏰 The castle's ground floor was the courtyard
- **The courtyard is paved last, because it is the ground *between* the buildings - and it was paved across the whole footprint.** A moment after the keep and both wings had laid their own floors, the paving went straight over them: every room of the bottom storey was the courtyard's stone with a lantern and a chest standing on it, and nothing could tell, because the room's floor had been planned and laid. The courtyard and the garden now walk around the buildings, which is what they always meant to do.
- **Thirty-five identical boxes are a castle now.** Every room was a five-by-five patch of one of six palettes with a lantern, a chest and a single piece of furniture chosen by `(i + j) % 5`, so the armoury and the chapel were the same room with a different barrel in it. A room is a plan: an **armoury** that pays in iron, a **library** that pays in paper, a **barracks** with a bed in it (a bed, so the castle is somewhere a body can sleep), a **banquet hall**, a **chapel** with candles, a **garden**, a **workshop** and a **treasury** - and the middle cell of every storey is still the **vault**, worth three tiers. A rug now runs through every doorway, so the court has a shape you can see from inside it.
- **Loot follows the room.** The tier still says how good a chest is; the room now says what it is good at, so an armoury chest pays in armour before it pays in tier. `mirage.every-room-is-the-room-it-was-designed-to-be` walks the settled castle against the design it wrote down - every room's floor, its own light, its furniture, what it is arranged around, its chest (stocked, not empty) and both halves of every bed - and insists a full castle contains every kind of room in the table. That walk is also what caught the courtyard paving over the ground floor, a lantern the wing's own staircase was climbing through, and a chest the throne hall's runner had been laid across.

### 🪑 The King was standing inside his own throne
- **He is a 2.4-block wither skeleton and the bay over his chair offered two blocks of air**, so he stood in the throne's own gold canopy: invulnerable and inert, he never suffocated, never moved and never complained about standing inside a block. He is placed by a search now, against his own body and the hall's real collision shapes - the chair first, then the dais in front of it, at every level that has something to stand on - so a taller body, a lower canopy or a re-furnished hall cannot put him inside anything in silence. `mirage.the-king-waits-in-the-castle` now asserts it on the real court in the real hall.

### 👑 The Distant Memory's regalia, his bite, and three more moves
- **Full netherite at Protection III under a golden crown**, and the crown is enchanted like the rest of it - a golden helmet on a wither skeleton is the silhouette of a court nobody is left to serve, and it is not a hole in his armour. He swings for **30** with the greatsword he stands up with: **Sharpness V, Fire Aspect II**, and nothing he wears drops, because a castle pays out of its own ledger.
- **Every blow in his kit now does a whole health bar before armour is counted**: the arc, the mark, the dust, the crown, each answer of the ultimate, and the three new moves. Nothing else in the mod swings this hard, and that is the point - he is the boss a castle is sealed around.
- **Three new moves, in two rotations.** **THE GOLDEN VERDICT** burns a lance down the hall along whatever he is facing: twenty-two blocks, three wide, so the answer is to be off the line. **A THOUSAND YEARS** opens the sky over the hall and lands a falling star on the ground each fighter was standing on - twice - so the counter is movement rather than a place to stand. **THE CROWN'S JUDGEMENT** marks one fighter when it is announced and falls on them *and everyone still beside them*, which makes the counter everybody else getting clear of the one the crown picked. Each one has a telegraph that is drawn where it lands and a named answer on the action bar, and `bosses.the-forgotten-monarch-is-armoured-and-hits-like-a-war` reads the body and the kit off the real fight: the regalia, the Protection on every piece, the Sharpness on the blade, the melee damage, and that his cheapest blow is already a full bar.

### 🪽 The vault does not hand out an elytra
- The castle's deepest loot is netherite, a totem and an enchantment worth the walk in - **deliberately not the mod's best movement item**. A castle that cannot be broken into and cannot be escaped by dying should not also be the free source of the one thing that skips both, so the elytra stays out of it and the vault keeps the rest.

### 🩸 The Gladiator's totem gate never asked whether the blow would kill
- **It asked one question: is there a totem in their hands?** Not whether the blow was fatal - so *every* punch in a match spent one, and because the totem ritual drops the body to one heart, a fighter at full health who took two damage lost the item that was supposed to save their life and was left one hit from death with nothing left. That is exactly "getting hit at max hp, if I have a totem I instantly die and pop totem": two bugs wearing one coat. The bot path had always asked the right question, so this only ever happened to players.
- **The decision is one function now, and its order is the fix.** *Would this blow kill them* first - through `LethalBlows`, the same arithmetic the ordered walk uses, after armor and with absorption counted - and *do they have a totem* only then. A survivable hit is let through untouched, which is what vanilla does and what a duel with a totem in the map's own loot is supposed to feel like.
- **`duel.a-totem-is-only-spent-on-a-blow-that-would-kill`** drives the four cases that matter at a body: a scratch at full health (totem kept), a survivable hit at half health (kept), a lethal hit (spent), and a lethal hit with no totem (nothing claims it). `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the gate stops asking whether the blow would kill.

### ☄️ Meteors fall from higher, and slower
- **A pair of numbers, not one.** They used to come from seventy-four blocks over three seconds; they now start **a hundred and forty blocks up and fall for seven**, which is a whole block a tick against the old one-and-a-quarter - slower, and from nearly twice the height. Dropping them from higher while keeping the old fall time would have made them arrive *faster*, which is the opposite of a warning you can watch.
- `events.a-meteor-falls-from-higher-and-falls-slower` asserts the **descent speed** rather than either number, so a later "make it more dramatic" cannot change one and quietly speed the streak up - which is precisely what the first attempt at this change did, and the check caught it before it shipped.

### 🩸 A higher-tier Blood Prism bites harder *and* faster
- **The Prism is a forge legendary, and its tier did nothing.** A Tier III drain behaved exactly like a Tier I one, which made the whole upgrade cosmetic. Each tier past the first now takes half again as much blood per pulse and arrives three ticks sooner: **5% of the target's health every 10 ticks at Tier I, 7.5% every 7 at Tier II, 10% every 4 at Tier III**, against a fifteen-second channel in every hand. The action bar names the tier when the ritual opens, and there is a floor on the pulse so a drain never becomes an instant effect.
- `scarlet.a-higher-tier-prism-bites-harder-and-faster` asserts both halves in order (a bigger bite with the same rhythm is a bigger number; a faster rhythm with the same bite is a faster nothing).

### 🎍 Sugar cane is worth farming, and existing worlds get the new price
- **One was the price of a bamboo stalk**, and a sugar-cane field is the most productive crop in the game: growable on sand beside water and harvestable every few minutes at full height, for nothing. It is **25** now - below an iron ingot (40) and above cooked beef (14), so it pays for a farm without replacing mining.
- **The default alone would have reached nobody.** `block_values.json` is written on a world's *first* boot and the loader only fills in keys that are missing, so every server older than this change would have gone on paying one forever while the changelog said otherwise. There is a one-time, **recorded** value migration; recorded, so a server owner who deliberately sets it back to one keeps their decision. `economy.sugar-cane-is-worth-farming` asserts the price on the harness world, which was born before the change - so the check is of the migration, not of the constant.

### 🎻 The Distant Memory Of A Forgotten Monarch, fighting his own fight
- **He was a summoned King with a different name.** The seated monarch shared the summoner's code path, so the whole kit came with him: the fireball charge, the blood ritual, the devouring hold, the skeleton horde, the soul tether - a warlord's clocks, ticking over a hall that had supposedly been sealed for centuries. Nothing about the one boss in the mod you have to *find* was about the place he was found in.
- **Nine moves of his own, in three laps, one at a time.** **REMEMBERED EDGE** sweeps a real cone, so the answer is to be behind him; **WHERE YOU STOOD** marks the ground you were standing on when it was announced and burns it; **THE HALL REMEMBERS** raises two of his own guard; **SOVEREIGN GRIP** drags the room onto his blade; **THE LONG REIGN** sends wither skulls down three lanes; **DUST OF AGES** withers everyone inside his reach, so distance is the answer; **THE HALL REMEMBERS YOU** re-marks every fighter and answers them three times over - the ground hits wherever you were, so the counter is movement, three times; **CROWN BREAKS** rings the floor and leaves him on one knee for forty ticks, which is the reward for having left the ring; **LAST REMEMBRANCE** fills his veins from whoever is still standing on the dais. Phase two adds the moves that shove the room around, phase three leads with the ultimate and ends on the remembrance, and each border is announced with its own title, its own bar and its own rotation - announce, land, breathe, next.
- **Every tell is the real footprint** - the cone, the ring, the lanes, the reach, and a small circle where each fighter is standing - and every move names its answer on the action bar while the countdown runs. `bosses.the-forgotten-monarch-fights-his-own-fight` drives the whole thing: three rotations, no repeated move in a lap, a longer lap and a shorter gap in the last phase, a tell and a named counter and a *resolving* branch for all nine, and a woken King whose first beat is a move of his own rotation rather than a leftover clock. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if the seated King stops being distinguished from a summoned one, or if the list of moves that actually land goes missing.

### ⛏️ The excavator dupe was a clock, not a gap
- **The rule that stops a placed block from paying mining bonuses lived in memory with a thirty-minute expiry.** So the exploit was to wait: mine a natural ore, place it back, come back later - and the perk paid as if the world had grown it. The same map was emptied outright when the server stopped, which turns "place a pile of blocks, restart, mine them all" into free drops, and `MiningPity` and the mining zones each kept their own copy of the same broken ledger.
- **There is one placement ledger now, and it is on disk.** A record is checked against the block actually being broken, retired by the break that removes it, and written out and read back across restarts - so a relog cannot launder a build into world generation and neither can an afternoon. `mining.a-placed-block-is-never-natural-in-memory-or-on-disk` drives all four answers (a placed block, a stale record for a different block, the retiring break, and the save/load round trip), and `ffAuditSources` fails the build if any of the three perks stops asking who placed the block, or if the ledger stops being written, loaded or saved.

### ❄️ "The snow does not disappear after the storm" was one deleted file
- **The storm restored the chunks that happened to be loaded, then threw the whole record away and deleted its file.** Everything it could not reach in that pass - snow in a chunk nobody had open when the event ended, which is most of a storm that lays cover around players and then stops while the server is busy - was lost along with the only note that it had ever been the storm's. That snow stood in the world permanently, which is the one thing an event is not allowed to do to a world.
- **The cover record is dimensional and durable.** A position is not an address without its level, so the record carries the dimension and restores into the level it was placed in rather than whichever level a loop reached first; the clear retires only what it actually took back, keeps what it could not reach, writes it to disk and retries it every five seconds - and the on-disk copy (now five fields, with the old four-field form still readable) means a restart finishes the job too. `events.the-storm-takes-its-snow-back-even-out-of-sight` drives one position the clear can reach and one it cannot, and insists the unreachable one is both kept and on disk; the audit fails the build on a blanket `STORM_SNOW.clear()`.

### ⚖️ One decision for "this blow kills you", in one published order
- **The same two lines of arithmetic were written in five places.** *Would this kill* was `health + absorption - amount` in the expedition, the Mirage Castle and the prison, a better copy (armor-aware) in the duelling gate, and a hand-rolled copy of vanilla's own totem ritual in a fourth file - and nothing anywhere said who was allowed to take a blow the player's totem would have taken. That is the whole of "totems do nothing in the Gladiator": the duel's own gate absorbs a blow by cancelling it, and cancelling it is what stops vanilla ever reaching its own totem check.
- **So the arithmetic, the totem and the order live in one file now.** A fatal player blow is read once, after armor and toughness (the figure vanilla's own save-or-die check uses - and armor was the third reason this used to fire on survivable hits and throw players out of fights they were winning). The player's own totem is a **rule in the walk**, at a published precedence, ahead of every territorial rule - so a totem wins by construction rather than by three files each remembering to check a hand - and a rule that wants to take a blow a totem would have saved has to declare that out loud. The expedition, the mirage and the prison are rules in that list; the ritual that spends a totem is performed in exactly one place, by the duel gate and by anything added later.
- **Pinned from both sides.** `lethal-blows.one-ordered-decision` asserts the walk's order, that no rule sits above the totem without declaring that it may beat one, that a five-damage blow at four health is fatal bare and a scratch in twenty armor, that a totem's blow is handed back rather than taken, and that a fatal blow nobody has a rule for is still a death. `ffAuditSources` fails the build if any of the three territorial files starts doing the sum itself again, or if the damage layer stops asking the walk.

### 🧱 The castle's staircases were the one part of it nobody could walk
- **Four separate things were wrong with them, and all four are geometry.** The run carried one tread too few, so the last thing a player did on the way up every storey was jump; the "second lane" beside the run was open air, which is a ledge that needs a jump per step rather than a lane; the opening that lets a flight through the floor above was punched by hand four blocks wide, three deep and *beside* a run one block wide, so two of those columns were over no stair at all - a hole in the floor of every storey, big enough to fall through; and the keep had a battlement crown with no roof deck under it, so the top flight arrived at a hole in the sky.
- **A flight is one thing now, in one place.** Two lanes, both stepped, solid underneath and clear above, a rail that follows the flight on the open side, an opening through the slab exactly over the two treads whose heads are inside it and no wider, and a top tread level with the floor it arrives on - the reason the run is `steps` treads and not `steps - 1`. The keep has three flights (ground, first, and the roof), each wing has two, and the roof deck exists.
- **`mirage.the-stairs-are-walkable` walks the world after the castle settles**: every tread the design laid out is a stair with something under it and clear air above it, the count is exact (seven flights, ninety treads, twenty-eight columns opened), every column opened through a floor is a tread's own headroom, and the top of every flight steps onto floor rather than into a jump. The roof deck was found by that check, not by reading the code.

### 👑 The King has one address now, and it is the castle
- **His hall used to be generated by an expedition** - one chunk in fifty, six chunks deep into a maze, in a place nobody had walked to yet. So the one boss in the mod who has to be *found* by speaking to him was sitting in the one place nobody could find him, and the hall went with the run when it ended. It lives in the Mirage Castle now: the keep's ground-floor centre cell, raised into a court with a dais behind a gold lip, a carpet runner from the door, braziers, banners, two tribute chests on the step and four Royal Guards standing at attention around it.
- **He is seated when the castle settles**, inert and invulnerable, with five lines of dialogue for whoever walks in; a right-click is the audience that starts the fight, and the announcement still arrives one beat at a time (whisper, bar, `The Distant Memory Of A Forgotten Monarch` read out through the game's own title command, then the greatsword). His fall pays a royal drop, his guard stands down with him, and the whole court is retired with the castle - a King left sitting in an empty field is the leftover this design exists to avoid.
- **Pinned twice.** `mirage.the-king-waits-in-the-castle` raises a real castle, asserts the throne is in the keep's centre cell, that he is seated there with four guards and cannot move or be damaged, and that taking the castle down takes the court with it; `ffAuditSources` pins the hall's wiring to the castle and fails the build if `ExpeditionManager` mentions a throne again.

### 💀 The harness dies and comes back now, which is where the last bug was hiding
- **Every death check asserted the state after the death and never after the respawn** - and the respawn is the step that loses the pack, because vanilla builds a *new* player and carries an inventory across only for keep-inventory or a spectator death. That is exactly how "the corruption 3 death still takes your items" shipped green. `deaths.the-pack-survives-the-new-body-and-a-restart` now drives the pack through both halves: the handover to a fresh body of the same UUID (vanilla's own `respawn` when this harness can run it, and otherwise the body it would have built, handed over through the same call the event makes - which `ffAuditSources` pins), and the on-disk copy with the memory thrown away in between, which is the restart the hold in memory never survived.


### 🎭 Guild score, and why it had stopped moving
- **Both scores were paid to an attacker a blow named, and neither fight has one any more.** Every raid boss ends its own fight: the mod catches the killing blow, plays the death out and then removes the body **with its own damage**, so the death arrives with no attacker at all - the bosses die, their loot drops, and the score asks "who killed this", gets nothing, and moves on. The killer is read off the body's own memory now (the last player who hit it), which is the same trick the Time Lord's finale already used. **PvP had the sibling hole**: a duel refuses the blow that would kill the loser, so a duel kill never reached a death event at all - duels credit their own winner now, at the one place a duel is decided, and a death inside a live duel is left to the duel so nothing pays twice. `/guild` may still show the leaders you remember; from here they move again.
- **And the score has its own listener.** Wrapping the two grants in `Safe.run` never protected them, because a wrapper only protects its own action: both sat inside the giant entity-death handler, where an exception in any boss, revenant or raid system above them skipped the rest of the handler - the exact "the score won't go up" bug the comment on that code claimed to have fixed. Guild score is its own death listener now, registered ahead of the big one, and `ffAuditSources` fails the build if anything puts it back inside.
- **A boss pays once, whoever reports it.** A ceremony boss is reported twice (by its own ending and by the death that follows), so the kill goes through a ledger keyed by the boss's UUID - and a kill nobody could be paid for is not spent on, so a boss whose first report comes from a guildless passer-by still pays the guild that actually killed it. `guild.a-boss-kill-is-paid-once-and-only-to-a-guild` pins it.
- **The actual reason nothing moved was worse than the score.** Moving the score out of the giant entity-death handler took the handler's own registration with it, and nothing complains about a listener nobody subscribed: `onDeath` - every boss's loot ledger, the Mindbinder's pack restore, player feats, duel endings, streak credit, prison kills and expedition credit - compiled, shipped and was never called. Every one of those features was silent at once, which is what a guild score that will not move looks like from the outside. It was two red checks in the harness (`deaths.no-item-loss`, `deaths.corruption-kill-keeps-the-pack`, both of which drive a real death) that found it, and `ffAuditSources` now fails the build if `AFTER_DEATH.register(...onDeath...)` ever goes missing again - a rule with teeth, because the failure it guards is invisible from inside the game.

### 👑 The throne hall, and the King who was already sitting in it
- **Rare, deep, and built as a hall rather than a room.** Six chunks or more into the maze, once per run, at a rate of about one chunk in fifty: the vault is raised three blocks, the end of it is a two-step gold-trimmed dais behind a sealed doorway, and the King sits at the top of it on a throne - carpet runner up the middle, two banners over his shoulders, braziers, lanterns, cobwebs, four **Royal Guards** standing at attention, and two rich chests at the foot of the dais.
- **He does not get up, and he cannot be made to.** His body is invulnerable and still, so no stray arrow and no swinging player starts the fight from behind a doorway: the only thing in the world he answers to is a **right-click**, which is the audience that begins it. Until then he talks - five lines, one every few seconds, aimed at whoever walks in.
- **The announcement arrives one beat at a time**, because a boss whose name arrives all at once is a health bar, and this one is supposed to arrive as a memory: a whisper ("SOMETHING REMEMBERS"), then the boss bar with only part of his name, then **The Distant Memory Of A Forgotten Monarch** read out by the game's own `title` command, then the sword - and only then is he on his feet, with `1000` hearts of King and the four guards waking with him. He is untouchable through all four beats. His fall pays the expedition a large bonus on top of his own loot and turns the hall quiet again; when a run ends, the seated King and his guards are retired with it, so a maze cannot slowly fill up with abandoned Kings. `bosses.a-throne-king-arrives-one-beat-at-a-time` pins the tag, the single waking, the order of the beats and that the last one is the one that puts him on his feet.

### 🧠 The Mindbinder is a fight now
- **Everything he does comes out of one rotation, one move at a time.** The old fight was a flat coin toss every few seconds with the mind grip and the shared-mind beam firing on their own clocks, which is why nothing could be learned from it: moves arrived in no order and up to three overlapped. Each of the three phases now runs its own **ordered** list - the first four spells teach what a tell looks like, the second adds the arena (a ring to leave, a wave to leave, a body to find), and the last is led by the move that unmakes the room - and the scheduler refuses to start anything while a move still owns its slot, clearing every field a move holds when that slot ends.
- **Every move telegraphs, and names its own counter.** A tell is drawn on the ground as a ring that closes, named on every screen in range ("UNMADE - run, the whole room is the blast", "TREMOR - get off the ground, jump the ring", "MIND GRIP - get ready to SNEAK"), and held for a phase-appropriate window that is always at least twelve ticks. `mindbinder.every-move-tells-before-it-lands` fails the build if a rotation ever schedules an id with no tell, if a name or a counter is blank, or if a move's slot is shorter than its own telegraph.
- **Being worn is a minigame with two verbs, not a waiting room.** A fully controlled player was a spectator behind their own eyes: no bar, nothing to press, no way back except somebody else ending the fight for them - "braindead". The hold now runs one shared pulse for both kinds of possession (the Mindbinder's and the staff's), with **JUMP to struggle** and **SNEAK to SABOTAGE**: sabotage staggers the Mindbinder's own rotation, tears real health off the body wearing your face, slows it and lights it up for whoever can still help. Both verbs are visible in the bar, which now carries the meter, the cooldown and the fact that nobody is left to fight for you.
- **And it cannot outlive its own fight.** Dying while worn ends the takeover before the pack is copied: the totem the Mindbinder tore out goes back in first, and the game mode goes back to survival, so a victim never respawns a spectator with somebody else's camera. Nobody left within sixty-four blocks who could end the fight lets the hold go, because a possessed player cannot leave and cannot win alone.

### 💀 A held death keeps the pack across the respawn, not just across the tick
- **A respawn is not a teleport.** Vanilla builds a *new* player for it and only carries the inventory across when the keep-inventory rule says to or when the body that died was a spectator - and a held corruption death owned neither, so the copy written back into the dying body stayed in the corpse and the player came back with nothing. That is the whole of "the corruption 3 death still takes your items", surviving every check in the harness because the harness never respawns anybody. The pack is now parked for the body that follows and spent once, into empty slots only.
- **And the last-known-inventory fallback is the hold's alone.** `LastInventoryHolder` has a snapshot of everybody, taken at every death, so reaching for it on *any* death was free keep-inventory for the whole server - which the harness caught the moment a control check was added for it. It is now asked only of a death the hold really caused.

### 🎟️ Totems work in the Gladiator
- **The save is made in the duel's own lethal gate.** Every elimination branch absorbs the blow itself, and absorbing the blow *is* cancelling the damage - and this gate runs in front of vanilla's own totem check, so handing the blow back popped nothing. The totem is spent by hand instead, exactly as vanilla spends it (health to one, regeneration, absorption, fire resistance, the burst, two seconds of grace, the title), and the blow is refused. **Excalibur** still cuts through one, and a bot's totem is still popped by the bot code so nobody spends two.

### 🏰 The Mirage Castle is a castle now
- **Thirteen blocks across was a shed.** It is **101×101** - a curtain wall with four thirty-block towers and a battlemented gatehouse, a three-storey keep of **twenty-seven rooms**, two wings of eight more, a courtyard with four fountains, lantern-lit roads and fourteen trees. Around **fifty thousand blocks**, and the whole thing was thirteen by thirteen with four corner posts. Loot is by room now rather than by crater: an ordinary hall holds iron and gold, the wings hold diamonds and emeralds, and the keep's upper rooms hold netherite scrap, an enchanted golden apple and a totem.
- **It rises instead of appearing.** Fifty thousand `setBlock` calls in one tick is a stall, not a structure, so the plan is computed in full, written to disk, and then placed at the figure the rebuild queue already proved safe - reading as the thing growing out of the air, with portal particles climbing its own walls as it goes. The plan goes down **before the first block**, which is what makes an operator's `/ff event stop`, a server kill and a single-player relog all the same operation.
- **Nobody can break it, and it includes explosions.** The walls were already protected; blasts were not, so one creeper in the courtyard opened a hole in a structure whose whole promise is that it cannot be touched - and the undo could never have put it back exactly. Explosions now refuse Mirage Castle positions the same way they refuse claims.
- **The Royal Guard is mirage too.** Twelve vindicators, six pillagers and a ravager, all tagged, all persistent, and every one of them **turns to dust** when it dies: no loot table, no equipment, no experience, a puff of ash where it stood. `dropAllDeathLoot` is the one call vanilla makes for everything a death leaves behind, so that is where the rule lives.
- **And a fatal blow inside it takes you home instead of killing you.** An unbreakable box full of armed guards with no way out but dying is a trap, not an event: a genuinely fatal blow inside the castle is refused, and the player arrives at their own spawn point - their bed, or the world spawn - at six hearts, with the line that says what happened. Absorption hearts and a Totem of Undying still turn a lethal hit into a survivable one, exactly as the expedition's own rule has it.

### ☄️ The meteor is a body that falls, and its crater heals
- **You can see it coming.** The old streak was forty frames of flame particles drawn instantly down a line. It is now a **block display** two and a quarter blocks across, on fire, falling for three seconds with a tail, a shockwave on impact and a column of smoke after it - plus a warning in chat, a horn in the sky, and an approach sound, because the move is only fair if the thing landing on you is legible.
- **And the crater is a real explosion.** The old code carved the hole by hand *specifically* to avoid the rebuild machinery, which left permanent craters wherever the weather chose to land. It goes through `explode` now, at radius six, which means the game's own knockback, damage and shape - and the blast is captured by the rebuild queue, **always**, whatever the global Explosion Rebuild setting says: a crater left by an event nobody aimed is not a choice anybody made, which is the same rule the wither's wreckage already follows. Pinned by `events.a-meteor-lands-and-its-crater-heals`, which drives the real sixty-tick fall and then the real rebuild tick with the setting *off*.

### 🔁 Every refusal tells the client, not just the refused break
- **The ghost block was one instance of a general shape.** A client predicts more than a break: it predicts the block a placement puts down, the stack an item use spends, and - for an attack - it runs `player.attack(target)` on *its own hand* before the server has agreed to anything. So the four refusal doors in the event layer (use-block, use-item, attack, entity interaction) and the second break layer (`Level.destroyBlock`, which owns the mirage's walls) each answer with the truth now: the predicted placement position and the block under the cursor, the full inventory state, and the attacker's own hand.
- **And it is pinned twice.** `ffAuditSources` fails the build if any of the four refusal layers stops mentioning the resync, and the mirage check refuses a real `destroyBlock` on a real castle wall and asserts the wall is still there - a break layer that cancels without resyncing is a wall the player watches vanish.

### ⏳ The Time Lord's last stand was a coin flip
- **The aging beam existed and almost nobody saw it.** The final judgment waited for **one percent** of his health - six hearts of six hundred - and a raid that landed one big hit passed straight through that window inside a single critical. It starts at **8%** now, in phase three, which makes the beams a beat of the fight rather than a rumour. His hand also went from 12 to 16 damage and his aging ray from 4/6 to 6/8, and the ray draws a bright second thread all the way along itself plus a bloom on the body it lands on.
- **And he has a ring.** "The hour that never was": three seconds of a clock face closing around him, drawn on the floor he is standing on with a sweeping hand above his head, and then everything still inside sixteen blocks is aged twice over, slowed, weakened and hurled out of the middle. It is his only move answered by a *direction* rather than a block, and the telegraph is the move.

### 🎻 The strings are visible now, and he talks less
- **Puppets and worn players both wear their strings.** Four strands off each body - shoulders and legs - running up and out with sparks down them, a crown of string over the head, a soul wisp on the torso, and both of his hands drawn from himself to whatever they are holding. The Final Knot draws two strands rather than one, so being walked reads as being walked.
- **The dialogue was five readouts at once.** Identical-line deduping already existed; what was missing was a limit on *talking*. He is now held to one line of taunt every six seconds, while bars, phase banners and mechanic explanations are exempt - those are the lines the room has to read. The line he says when he takes a body names the player it is wearing.

### ❄️ The Snowstorm was raining, and a roof was a complete answer to it
- **The rain had one specific cause, and it was ours.** A level's rain level is not a private render value: `ServerLevel.advanceWeatherCycle` reads it every weather tick and sends it to every client as a `RAIN_LEVEL_CHANGE`, so the storm pinning that figure at 1.0 every ten seconds for ten minutes *was* rain - the event that is supposed to be the one snowy thing in the game spent its whole duration looking like a wet afternoon, and no amount of snow particles on top of it changed that. The storm no longer touches a sky at all: it draws its own snow, which is the only way to get flakes over a warm biome anyway, because vanilla will not snow there. `ffAuditSources` now fails the build if this file ever sets a rain level above zero again.
- **A roof is not shelter.** Three things made a body immune, and the first was the whole event: being unable to see the sky - a doorway, a tree, a one-block hole, a house - stood the clock down completely, so the answer to "it snows everywhere" was to stand inside and the heat the event is built around never had to be found. What saves a body now is heat and nothing else, and the storm stops reaching at **12 blocks under the surface** - past a two-storey house, short of a mine shaft - so a body in a building is in the blizzard and a body underground is not punished for mining.
- **The lag was a thousand block reads per body per tick.** The heat search is a six-block disc six blocks tall, and it ran for every player on every tick. It is cached for half a second now, which is far longer than a torch takes to appear.
- **The snow is batched.** One send carrying a count is one packet however large the count is: the storm was spending fourteen packets a cycle on fourteen single flakes, and it now draws twenty flakes in a single send every four ticks - a thicker storm for a third of the bandwidth.
- **And the frost does not outlive it.** A body the storm was holding at the freeze figure had its frozen ticks abandoned with the event, and frozen ticks decay at one a second - so an event that ended left everybody it had frozen shivering and crawling for seven more seconds. The bodies this file froze are remembered and thawed when the storm ends, and nobody else's frost is touched.

### 🧱 A refused break put the block back, and a ghost block is what happened when it did not
- **The client predicts the break; vanilla is what corrects it.** A client removes a block the moment it swings, and the only thing that tells it otherwise is `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock` - which sends the block back for both of the refusals it owns. This mod refuses breaks *in front of* that, from two independent layers (the fail-closed claim backstop in the game-mode mixin, and the Fabric block-break event that wraps the same call), and neither said a word to the client. The miner watched the block disappear, the server still had it, and it came back on a relog: the classic ghost block, and the third variant of the same idea as the elytra - a refusal that vanilla would have explained, made in a place vanilla cannot explain it from. Both layers now resync the block they refused, and `ffAuditSources` fails the build if either stops doing it.
- **The same shape, one file over.** An event that sets a level's rain level has to take it back, and "the event ended" has three ways of happening while only two of them run any ending code - a server that stops mid-event comes back with the event already cleared and its sky still applied. The sky is now swept on load and on every ending path, for every event, because a stuck sky is not a weather bug, it is a missing teardown.

### 🎭 The Wooden Marionette could not be crafted, and its help page drew the grid anyway
- **A boss summon nobody could make.** `/raidboss help puppeteer` has always drawn a crafting grid - eight string around a carved pumpkin - and no recipe for the Wooden Marionette shipped at all, so the page described a craft that did nothing and the Puppeteer could not be summoned by anybody who was not handed the item. The recipe and its recipe-book unlock ship now, and the audit that reads model ids out of recipes was widened to the same authority the art check uses: the marionette's model id is handed out through a named constant, so the narrow `cmd(...)` pattern could not see it and a recipe minting it would have been reported as rendering as a plain armor stand.
- **The Scarlet Devil's page was drawing the wrong recipe.** Eight redstone blocks around a ghast tear, where the craft that ships (and that the console prints) is four ghast tears at the corners, four redstone blocks at the edges and the bottle in the middle.
- **So the pages are audited against the recipes now.** `recipes.every-boss-page-draws-a-recipe-that-ships` walks every page of the help window, requires its result to be one of the thirteen boss summons, requires a recipe to ship for it with the right `custom_data.ff`, and compares the grid ingredient for ingredient - counts, not just names - against the one the page draws. It is the first check that asks "does this boss actually work" end to end, which is the question nobody owned: the page is in a menu, the item in `ModItems` and the recipe in the datapack.

### 💎 A streak freeze costs gems, not tokens
- The window has said "5 gems" since the day it was written and the code charged five favor tokens, which is the currency of a completely different shop. It takes gems now - the same balance the gem shop and boss loot pay into - and the purchase is checked against the balance at the moment it is taken rather than the moment it was read, so two windows cannot both spend the same five.

### 🦀 The Crab Claw's reach is the equipment's, not the attribute's
- **The claw is written onto the player by a tick hook, and a swing arrives before that tick has run.** The packet that says "I hit this" is handled on the connection's thread, *before* the player's own tick has re-applied the mirror - so for a moment the client was reaching with a weapon the server could see while the attribute still said vanilla's three blocks, and the one swing that came of it read as a reach hack. How far a body may reach is now asked of the **equipment** - the six worn slots, which the server can see without trusting a word from the client - and the body's own range is corrected first, so the number is real rather than merely excused. Taking the claw off leaves nothing behind, because the entitlement is read from what is worn and nothing else. Applies to both halves of the grant: entity reach and block reach, since one item extends both and the clients that place and swing with it in the same tick. Pinned by `anticheat.crab-claw-range-is-not-a-reach-hack`, which measures a real body at 4.2 blocks - out of the bare arm, inside the claw's - with and without the weapon on.

### 🪂 An elytra glide the server refused for good
- **Vanilla's answer to `START_FALL_FLYING` is final, and the client never asks twice.** If the chest slot the server is holding has not caught up with the wings the player can see, vanilla answers the request by *stopping* the glide - and the client, which is already gliding, asks again never. From that tick on the player flies for as long as they like while the server believes they never asked: not one bad tick, but every tick of the whole flight, reported as flight and speed. The request is now **kept rather than decided**, and vanilla's own question - is there something on this body that can glide? - is asked again for half a second until the slot can answer it. Nothing is granted to a body without wings, and a claim that never becomes honourable expires and grants nothing. This is a repair rather than a check, so it runs whether or not the checks are switched on.
- **The boundary ticks either side are named, not judged.** A glide begins and ends on packets that land a tick or two either side of the movement they belong to - the leap off a ledge, the crash into a wall, the rocket fired on the tick the wings open. Eight ticks in either direction are now read as the wings' work and said so in the readout ("elytra (just out of a glide)", "elytra (glide begun late, chest slot behind)") instead of being measured as a body that is impossibly fast and impossibly high. Pinned by `anticheat.an-elytra-is-not-flight-or-speed`, which refuses a glide to a body with nothing that can glide, grants it the tick the wings arrive, and then checks the tail is still in force once the flag is gone.

### 🛡️ A body that is hard to shove is not a body that hid the shove
- **Knockback resistance has its own regime, and the floor stops meaning much in it.** Vanilla scales the shove it writes by the victim's resistance, so the figure the check measures is already the reduced one - but at half resistance and above, what is left to travel is a fraction of a block, which is where the model stops separating "kept the shove" from "never moved". Half or more is left unmeasured now. One worn item (the Colossus Plate) sits exactly on that line.

### ⚖️ "It keeps saying invalid move", and the four checks that were reading silence as cheating
- **The invalid move was the improbable being treated as the impossible.** A per-packet jump allowance is a statement about one packet; when a client sends three over it inside a ten-second window the code *refused* them - and a refusal is answered with a set-back, so a stuttering connection was moved backwards, repeatedly, by name. The class's own rule, written at the top of `PacketEngine`, is that only the impossible is refused outright, and a twelve-block packet is not impossible: it is a lag spike, and the speed check already has a way to answer it that cannot move anybody. The repetition is still **reported** - once, at the moment one packet becomes a pattern - so the ledger and the staff alert see exactly what they saw before, and the body is never put back for it. Only a jump past 32 blocks or a coordinate a client cannot produce is refused now. Pinned by `anticheat.late-packets-are-not-impossible-moves`.
- **Knockback wanted half of a modelled shove.** The travel the check compares against is a model, and a body sprinting, fighting uphill, standing on a slab edge or holding W into a hit keeps far less than half of it without touching anything. The floor is a **quarter** of the model now, and the shove has to be worth **1.5 blocks of modelled travel** before the check reads anything at all - which means a bare fist (0.87) and every "low knockback" hit in the game are no longer measured. Pinned by `anticheat.low-knockback-is-not-measured`.
- **Aim wanted twenty hits and three degrees.** Twenty consecutive hits is a duel, not a sample, and three degrees of average error is a good player with a mouse, not a machine. The window is **thirty hits**, the average has to be inside **1.6 degrees**, the spread inside 0.8, and the samples that count are only the ones landed on a body that was actually moving. Hitbox expansion gets a wider tolerance in the same spirit: both aim rays are inflated by a third of a block on top of a tick of the target's own movement, the latency-compensated box by two reach margins rather than one, and a finding is worth half the aura score it used to be. Reach allows a full block of lag rather than three quarters. Pinned by `anticheat.pattern-checks-keep-their-tolerances`.

### 🚪 `/ff anticheat op on` means "check me", not "kick me"
- **Two consents, two switches.** `/ff anticheat op on` makes operators *visible* to the checks - an owner needs to watch a finding land on their own screen to know the detector is alive - and it did not say anything about the automatic half, which read them, reported on them and then kicked them off the server mid-build. An operator is now **never** kicked or timed out, whatever the checks are allowed to look at, until `/ff anticheat op kick on` is run - which is the one command that says it out loud, and the one that makes the failsafe testable at all, because the tester's own presence used to be what stood it down. The status line prints both switches. Pinned by `anticheat.op-kick-is-a-second-consent`, which asserts the rule without a server: a staff rank is unpunishable, and checking them does not imply punishing them.

### 🎻 The Final Knot is five seconds
- **Fifteen seconds was a second fight.** With the drag walking the victim toward his hands the whole way, the outcome was decided in the first two seconds and the other thirteen were the room watching it happen. It is five now, the contraction per body taken still applies (5s / 4.5s / 4s), and the escape is twenty blocks - inside what a sprint can honestly cover against the drag in five seconds, which the self-test now measures rather than assumes.

### 🟩 The Slime King gained three moves, each with a job the old seven did not do
- **bounce** is the positioning punish: the floor he is standing on becomes a trampoline, everyone on the ring is thrown into the air, and the ground they left turns to goo - so the launch is not the escape it looks like. It is the one move in his kit that punishes standing in the *tightest* clump rather than the furthest body, and it is in the opening rotation because a group learning him needs it early.
- **absorb** is the add tax, paid either way. Every spawn of his own alive inside his reach is swallowed for a share of his maximum health, with the strings drawn so the health can be seen travelling. The answer is the one the tinies always had - clear them - except it is now worth doing *before* the beat lands. Kill them all and the beat resolves into two more instead, so ignoring the adds is not a way to starve the move.
- **volley** is the second half's answer to the tactic the first half teaches: the spit marks the ground under one body, and this marks it under every body at once, so spreading out to make one mark irrelevant stops working. All three are pinned by `bosses.the-slime-king-fights-in-an-order`, which now knows the new names and insists each is wired into the rotation rather than merely listed in it.

### 🪨 The Stone Golem learned to bring the fight to you
- **Every one of his old moves was area denial at range**, which makes the winning line "be far away and patient" and makes the arena's own terrain the answer to nothing. **magnet** plants his feet and drags every body he can *see* - so a stone wall is counterplay for the first time in this fight - and the pull ends where it was always going, under his hands, with a slam behind it. It is weaker than a body's own walking on purpose: it wins by being free while you are also being hit, so the group that keeps its feet and its back to a wall barely moves.
- **cage** raises a ring of stone around the floor one body is standing on, two blocks high - the number that decides whether a box is a boundary, since a player clears one block and not two. The body inside has to break out or fight him from where it stands, and he cannot be kited by the rest of the group while they deal with it. The tell marks the exact spot, so stepping off is the clean answer, the walls are ordinary breakable stone, and they come down on their own after eight seconds - there is no state in it that can hold anybody. Pinned by `bosses.the-stone-golem-has-moves-that-close-the-range`.

### ❄️ New event: the Snowstorm
- **It snows everywhere, and the cold is a clock rather than a colour.** Vanilla weather cannot snow in a warm biome, so the event draws its own snow over every biome and drives the sky to a full rain level for the ambience. Standing outside with no heat within six blocks chills a body for twelve seconds - long enough to place a torch, short enough that ignoring it costs you inside one fight - and then it freezes: the body is held at vanilla's own full-freeze figure of 140, so the overlay, the shiver, the reduced movement and the freeze damage are the game's own powder-snow behaviour rather than a house effect. Step near a torch, a campfire, a fire, a lantern, lava, a magma block or a lit furnace and the chill is thrown away; walk into a house and the frost goes with it. The chill cannot outlive the storm: the event ending clears both. Pinned by `events.the-snowstorm-is-a-clock-not-a-colour`, which is a check about playability - a grace period a torch can be placed in, a reach that makes a campfire shelter, and a freeze figure that actually reaches the damage.

### 🪵 Gladiator: a bigger, deeper world, and the icon of what it actually hands you
- **The map was capped by a number written in five places.** A plot is a stride away from its neighbour, and the stride was a bare `200` at every site that computed one - which is why the map could never exceed 184 wide. It is a named constant now, the stride is 232, and the Gladiator world is **216x208** (about 60% more ground than before) with the whole plot sweep, the startup leftover sweep and the entity discard grown with it. Every other arena is a fraction of a plot and is untouched except by the spacing.
- **It is deeper too.** The rock bottom moved from y=70 to y=52, so the column under the grass is 48 blocks instead of 30 and the deepslate line moved down with it, and the gearing window grew to three minutes so the deepest ore in the map is still reachable inside it. The ore table, the depth curve and the layer the deepslate starts at all follow the same constants, so depth still pays.
- **The icon is a stick.** It was an iron pickaxe - the icon of the *reward*, the tool you go and earn - which read as "this mode is about mining", and it collided with the mining skill, the prison mine and the shop's own pickaxe rows. A stick is what the mode actually hands you: nothing at all, in a world of stone, until you pick one up. It lives in one accessor now instead of three menus, and the stats screen no longer throws on a Gladiator, Sumo or Archery record - three modes whose icons were missing from a switch whose default was an exception.

### 🕯️ The Wither keeps its own altitude
- **Vanilla's drift climbs and the rework's follow only ever pulled it sideways**, so nothing ever brought the boss back *down*: a long fight, or a group fighting from under a roof, ended with it in the sky, out of reach of anything but a bow and no longer a boss of the arena it was summoned in. "It keeps going so high" is what that looks like from underneath. It now answers for its own height every tick, capped at fourteen blocks above whoever it is fighting - above its own hover and above a slam's launch, so no scripted move is affected - and it comes down visibly rather than blinking there. Pinned by `bosses.the-wither-stays-in-the-fight`.

### 🟩 The Slime King fights in an order now, and every move warns
- **He never lacked moves - he lacked a sequence.** Seven abilities ran on seven independent clocks, so a fight could open with a stomp, a goop wave, a sticky floor, a tremor, a toxic spit and a volley *on the same tick*, and not one of them announced itself. From inside the arena that does not read as a moveset, it reads as weather. One move is in flight at a time now, in a rotation where each has a job: **stomp** denies an area, **pogo** closes the gap and punishes standing still at range, **spit** puts a pool under whoever is running, **goop** holds the floor, and the second half adds **sticky**, **tremor** (anti-kite) and **tiny** (bodies you cannot ignore).
- **Every beat is announced, and the announcement names the answer.** The stomp's ring is drawn on the floor for the whole tell, the pogo marks the ground it will land on, the spit marks the floor under whoever he is aimed at, and the action bar carries the move's name and its counter rather than only its name - a telegraph nobody can name is a cutscene. The first move a group ever sees gets the longest tell of the fight; the second half is both tighter and faster. Pinned by `bosses.the-slime-king-fights-in-an-order`.
- **The arrival is an arrival.** He is fused out of seven bodies and then rises growing, and now the settle announces itself with the bar naming what he is doing; his first beat is scheduled off the *end* of the arrival rather than the summon, so a fight opens by settling instead of with a move already in the air. And the split - his phase two - renames the bar and tells the room that he has three more moves now, because a rotation nobody was told about is just a harder pile.

### ❄️ The Snow Queen has three rotations instead of one flat list
- **The flatness was arithmetic.** Every ability clock started at zero, and `now` is a game time in the millions - so on the first tick of the fight, and again on the first tick of every phase, *every clock she owns was already due*. Nine moves on one tick. That is the whole of "a flat list of abilities rather than a rotation", and it is fixed where it happens: the cadence is re-armed at spawn and at each phase change, so no clock is due and the first beat is scheduled rather than pending. Pinned by `bosses.the-snow-queen-rotates-by-phase`, which arms a throwaway cadence and asserts every clock is in the future.
- **Each phase has its own order.** Phase one teaches with **ring** (step out of the circle) and **hail** (watch your head); phase two adds the tracked **laser** (move, a shield will not save you) and the **blizzard** (move, or raise a shield - the one move a shield genuinely answers); phase three opens with the **nova** (distance) and stays tighter and faster than the two before it. The light pressure - mist, spikes, orbs - and her servants and mirages stay on their own staggered clocks, because holding those behind a telegraph would make her slow rather than fair.
- **The telegraphs are drawn where the move lands.** The ring is drawn at the wider end of the radius she actually lays, the hail is drawn in the air above every head it will fall on, the beam is drawn along her own line of sight, and the nova is drawn at the distance the nova is survivable from. Each phase change names what she has gained.

### 🎻 The Final Knot: the move that takes the player, not the arms
- **Phase three, and it needs strings on you first.** The one thing he does that uses the *body* rather than an arm. He walks the victim at his own hands while they fight their own legs, and it is a **contest**: three hands, and every blow landed on him by anyone pulls one back out - the victim's own, a teammate's, or a friend's blow that lands on the victim instead. Get 26 blocks clear and the strings are not that long. Ten percent of his maximum health is what the contest is worth to him, so it is the group's fight being spent, not just one player's.
- **Losing it costs the player, entirely.** He takes the body: it cannot move, cannot swing, cannot mine, cannot eat, drink, throw or place, and it is turned on the people it was standing next to, with their own gear, at their own pace, until **he is dead**. The readout on the action bar never changes, because the way out never changes. Every one of those doors is gated where the player's hands actually go through it (the attack hook, the use-item hook, the use-block hook, the block-break hook), the same shape the Time Lord's stop already uses.
- **It cannot soft-lock, and the reason is structural rather than remembered.** Every end of the fight goes through one release path, so his death, an operator's `/kill`, the arena emptying, a stray he left behind being swept and a server stopping all free the body by the same line. The one end that is not about his health is handled where it belongs: if the only people left are the ones he is already wearing, he leaves, and his leaving is what frees them. A disconnect is not a release - the mark is keyed by UUID and waits for the body to come back - and a restart takes the fight with it, so the player who rejoins is free, which is the only outcome that cannot trap anybody. A forty-five minute valve exists for the end nobody has thought of yet.
- **His bar tracks it, and the self-test pins the shape.** Pinned by `puppeteer.the-final-knot-is-a-contest-that-costs-the-fight`: at least two hands, a tell that contracts but never below a reaction, an escape inside the string's own reach, a price between zero and a reset, a valve longer than any fight, and a cooldown longer than the contest it follows.

### 🖼️ The item art that was wrong was wrong in the *generator*, and it was hiding behind its own output
- **Fifteen vanilla items were quietly downgraded by our own overrides.** Every custom item in this mod is a vanilla item wearing a `custom_model_data` dispatch, and the vanilla definition is embedded as the dispatch's `fallback` so every ordinary stack still looks vanilla. It *was* embedded - once, the first time a file was written. `tools/make_item_definitions.py` re-read the fallback out of the existing file on every later run, so whatever an older generator had written was frozen forever. That older one flattened the vanilla node into a single model, which cost: **every bow lost its draw animation**, **every trimmed piece of armour lost its trim** (iron, golden, netherite, leather), the **chest lost its christmas texture**, the **clock lost its seasonal dial**, the **goat horn lost its toot**, and the **diamond spear lost its in-hand model in the GUI**. Fifteen files, none of which could ever repair themselves because the file existing was the condition for not repairing them.
- **The fallback is re-read from the client jar on every run now**, so it is vanilla's own current node rather than whatever is in the file being rewritten, and the top-level keys alongside it (the spear's `swap_animation_scale`) are carried over every time too. The tree is now asserted: all 55 overrides embed their vanilla fallback verbatim, byte for byte, and the standalone pack zip is regenerated from the same tree.
- **Also fixed while in there:** the *Slime Boots* entry existed on both `leather_boots` and `iron_boots` - they are minted on iron, so the leather copy was dead weight that looked like art - and the standalone zip is rebuilt from the corrected tree in the same pass rather than being left a release behind it.

### ⚖️ "It fires on hits that deal no knockback" and "it says invalid move while I am just playing"
- **The knockback check was measuring the player's own feet.** The impulse it recorded at the moment of a hit was the victim's *velocity* along the line away from the attacker, which is mostly the player's own movement - so a player sprinting away from an attacker was credited with a shove the server never applied, and the instant they stopped moving (released the keys, stood still to drop a stack, went still in a time stop) the shortfall read as having eaten the knockback. It now measures the **change** the hit made to that velocity, sampled against the previous tick, so a hit that applies nothing measures nothing while a knockback-delay hack is still measured against exactly the number the server just wrote - a number no client can influence. Pinned by `anticheat.hits-with-no-knockback-are-not-measured`.
- **The per-packet jump allowance ignored the gap between packets.** Eight blocks is an allowance for a packet *every tick*; a client whose connection stalled for three ticks and then said where the player actually was arrived twelve blocks from the last accepted one - over the per-packet figure, then over it again, and three of those in a window is a refusal and a set-back on somebody playing perfectly normally. The allowance now grows with the time since the client's last packet at **terminal velocity** (3.9 blocks a tick, so an honest gap can never cross it) capped at a second, and a jump the packet was entitled to no longer counts against the next one. A client talking to us the whole time still gets refused for twelve blocks a packet, and a thirty-two block jump still refuses on its own. Pinned by `anticheat.late-packets-are-not-impossible-moves`.

### 🧭 A model code only has to be unique *per base item*, and that is how a compass lost its art
- **The Death Compass was a plain vanilla compass.** It handed out `custom_model_data` **4550040** on a *compass*, and that number already belonged to the **Sculk Medallion** on an *echo shard*. A code only has to be unique per base item, so nothing objected - and because one code can only ever have one item's art drawn under it, the item that lost was the one nobody had drawn yet. It owns **4550042** now, with its own texture and model (slate case, soul-fire needle, the red pivot at the last breath) drawn by `tools/make_missing_item_textures.py` and its own entry in `assets/minecraft/items/compass.json`. Exactly one item in the mod was in this state; the check below is why it will not happen again.
- **The audit that exists to catch this was reading the wrong thing.** It pooled every code in `ModItems.java` and every definition in the pack into two global sets, so a code that belongs to one item and is handed out by another counted as covered. It now pairs each code with **the base item the method that hands it out actually mints** - `new ItemStack(Items.X)` in the same method - and requires that item's own definition file to carry it. Run against the tree before this release, it fails with the Death Compass on one line, which is the whole point of it. Comments are stripped before any of it is read: a comment naming an old code (which is how this one is documented now) would otherwise count as the item *having* it.

### 🖼️ The pack is on both sides, and the shipped pack is asserted rather than assumed
- **The art was mirrored into the client source set only.** The client jar *is* a resource pack and always carried the art, but a dev-only run - which is where the self-test boots, and what "the textures are broken on the server side" looks like from a build - loaded a mod with no art in it at all. `resourcepack/assets` now feeds `processResources` directly as a second resource root, so one copy lands in the main output that every side loads, and the copy task that used to write into another task's output directory is gone.
- **`items.art-ships-and-resolves` reads the art the way the game will.** For every catalog item it takes the stack's model code, the *base item it is really on*, and then resolves the definition, the model the entry names and the texture that model draws - all out of the mod's own resources, through the classpath, not off the `resourcepack/` tree the generators write to. A hole anywhere in that chain is a failed assertion now: the `resourcepack/` tree can be perfect while the thing that ships is not, and that gap is exactly what a player sees as "some of the stuff is still broken".
- **The Astral Mantle itself resolves.** Code, definition on `netherite_chestplate`, model and texture are all in the shipped jar, and this check proves it on every run. So a client still showing a plain netherite chestplate is not a data fault on this side: it is a client that never received the pack - a vanilla client needs the server to send it (`resource-pack=` in `server.properties`), and an *older copy of the standalone zip* installed in `resourcepacks/` sits **above** the mod's built-in art, so it shadows new items with old art. Delete the stray pack and the mantle is the mantle.

### ⚖️ "The anticheat says invalid move", and "knockback is way too sensitive"
- **The invalid move was the server's own write.** The packet layer measured a position packet against the last packet it had *accepted* - so when something moved a body without leaving a position in flight (a rewind, a scripted shove, an ability that put somebody down and never told the module), the client answering with the place it had just been put read as a jump with nothing to explain it: refused, and set back, mid-ability. The check now also asks where the **server** has the body, and a packet that agrees with that is not a forgery whatever the last packet said - while a cheater cannot use it, because a cheater is not where the server thinks they are.
- **A stopped clock is not a knockback hack.** Time is not running for a pinned body at all, so the shove has nowhere to land and the shortfall was the ability rather than the player; the knockback check stands down for it by name (a time stop, not "movement we decided not to judge", so a Slow Falling cloak cannot buy the same exemption). It also stands down for a body the server teleported inside the measurement, and refuses to start measuring at all on a body the server is driving.
- **"Low knockback stuff" is not a measurement.** The travel the check compares against is what is left after the body's own friction, the surface under it and a tick of lag have had their turn, so a shove worth a fraction of a block is inside the noise - a step, a slab and a berry bush all move a body further than it. Below `KNOCKBACK_MEASURABLE` (0.75 blocks) there is nothing to judge, and the floor for what counts as the knockback landing is **half** the expected travel rather than a third, so half a shove going missing is within the model instead of a finding. Pinned by `anticheat.low-knockback-is-not-measured`.

### ⏳ The Time Lord keeps his blow - and leads with the beam
- **A body at zero health is removed by the server on its own next tick.** His death is scripted: the killing blow is absorbed, seventy ticks of a rift that will not hold play out, and then he comes apart and pays. But his health stayed at zero for the whole ceremony, so a fall, a stray arrow or the shulker of damage a passing player lands would have the server remove him mid-line - which reads in game as *he just dies*, with nothing said and nothing dropped. His health is floored for the length of it, exactly as the Puppeteer's is.
- **And the fight pays even if the body is gone.** Every other exit now goes through one once-only ledger: the tick that finds him missing announces him and pays the half that does not need a body - the loot boxes straight into the inventories of everyone who fought, and the kill - before releasing the fight. Paying nothing was the one outcome indistinguishable from a broken boss.
- **A tagged Time Lord with no fight behind him was immortal.** The lethal hook cancelled any killing blow even when there was no script to play the ceremony for, which is the opposite of the bug it was written for. With no fight, vanilla decides. Pinned by `bosses.the-time-lord-keeps-his-blow`.
- **The summon is never a fight built around nobody.** `addFreshEntity` answers with a boolean and the answer was being dropped: a refused body (an arena chunk that is not ticking yet, which the server does with no error at all) left a bar, a script and nobody in it - *he never attacked and then he was gone*. The chunk is brought in and the add is tried once more, and a second refusal is reported instead of a fight being built around it.
- **His headline move cannot be the one move you never see.** The first arena beam was due 220 ticks after he rose, and the 1% finale outranks the arena ladder - so an endgame party that takes him from full to the finale inside eleven seconds never saw the aging beam at all. The ray is now his **opening** (80 ticks after the rift), and the finale waits for it: he has to have beamed once before he starts judging.

### ☄️ Four new world events, and the reason events felt impossible to spawn
- **The spawn bug was arithmetic across restarts.** Every event timer is saved as a *tick count*, and a tick count starts again at zero on every boot. A server that had been up for six hours therefore saved a `next_roll` six hours into the future **of the next session** - so after a restart nothing could roll until the server had been up as long as the previous run had lasted, and the longer the server ran before a restart, the longer the silence afterwards. Stale timers are now clamped on load, an event that was "still running" when the last session ended is closed out rather than stuck on forever, and every new event's own cleanup runs at boot too.
- **☄️ Meteor Shower.** Meteors come down near a random player with a burning streak, a crater and loot in the hole - ingots, experience, and occasionally diamond or ancient debris. Aimed at people rather than at the map on purpose, because the event is a race: a meteor four hundred blocks from anybody is scenery.
