Refused on claimed ground and inside a duel realm, and the crater is carved by hand rather than with an explosion, so the explosion-rebuild machinery does not quietly refill the hole.
- **🌑 Solar Eclipse.** The sun goes dark and the monsters stop waiting for night. The darkness is the game's own: a thunderstorm with the rain switched off, which is the only daylight darkness Minecraft actually has - so no rain, no lightning, no wildfire event, just a dimmed sun and a light level that drops. The mechanical half is a hook on the rule the game itself applies (`Monster.isDarkEnoughToSpawn`), relaxed while the eclipse holds, so caves, biome rules, mob caps and the claim check all still apply.
- **🌌 Aurora.** A rare peaceful one (4% instead of the usual spread): green and violet curtains above every player, and **nothing hostile spawns at all** while it lasts. The refusal is in the normal spawn path rather than a parallel one, so switching the event off restores vanilla on the next attempt with nothing to unwind.
- **🏰 Mirage Castle.** A castle rises near a random player. It is **unbreakable** while it stands - a castle anybody can mine is a free quarry instead of an event - and it takes itself away afterwards, putting back exactly what was there before. The undo list is written to disk *before* the first block is placed and cleared after the last one comes back, which is what makes four different endings the same operation: the event ending, an operator calling it off or starting another event on top of it, a server killed mid-event (the next boot finishes the job), and a single-player world closed and reopened. Bedrock and barriers are never overwritten and never claimed, so the undo only ever names blocks it may put back.

### 🛡️ A claim now covers dispensers and mobs too
- **A dispenser is not a player, and neither is an enderman.** Everything else was already refused inside somebody else's land - breaking, placing, using, opening, dropping, pistons, fire, hoppers, explosions, fluid - but those two were the holes. A wall of dispensers one block outside a border could put fire on a roof, a water source in a basement or bone meal through a farm; an enderman carries the wall away block by block and a ravager flattens the crops. `Level.destroyBlock` is the single funnel every mob-breaking path goes through and the only one handed the entity that caused it, so the rule lives there: a non-player entity may not destroy a block inside a claim. A dispenser may act on the block it faces only when that block is in a claim the dispenser is also inside, so an owner's own farms keep working - which is the line that keeps a fix like this from being reverted.

### 🏛️ Gladiator: the prep window is two and a half minutes and it says so
- **"Prep time is 0" was the window being set lazily.** The deadline was written on the first tick of the mode's script, so any path that never reached that line - a branch that did not match, a fight whose realm was gone for a tick, a reading taken from a different level's clock - left the truce already over. It is anchored at the moment the fight actually starts now, which is the one moment guaranteed to happen, and the script only reads it. The window also has a countdown on screen once a second, with announcements at 60, 30 and 10 seconds, because "how long have I got" should be a number rather than a feeling.
- **The cache elytra has one flight in it.** A fresh elytra was the strongest item in the mode and not because of anything it killed with: a hundred and eight seconds of glide removes the map, lets a losing fighter leave, and reaches every cache first. One point of durability keeps the diving, the rocket chase and the one escape, and turns it into the decision it should be.

### 🤝 Kind Sir, and the anticheat's ladder in three kicks
- **Say gg in the five seconds after a duel and keep the moment.** The rematch screen is the one moment manners are worth something - the person who just lost has five seconds to say it - so a `gg` (generously read: "GG", "gg!", "gg that was close", but not "lol no gg") inside that window grants the **Kind Sir** advancement. A mark on a profile, not a currency, because anything farmable would just be typed after every match.
- **The gg rule was never attached to anything.** The window was pinned by a self-test, the achievement was defined, the grant was written - and nothing in the mod read a chat message, so the gesture could not be made at all. A rule nobody can satisfy reads exactly like a rule that does not work. `ServerMessageEvents.CHAT_MESSAGE` now hands every player message to it, so `gg` in the five seconds after a result is finally read. That last link is the one piece with no self-test behind it, because reaching it means a real client typing.
- **Three kicks, then a five-minute timeout.** The automatic half - which only ever acts at high confidence, only when the failsafe is on, and only with no moderator online - now gives the benefit of the doubt three times. Stages 1, 2 and 3 are kicks with their own escalating wording; the fourth trip and every one after it costs five minutes. The whole ladder still resets at **noon New York** (by zone id, not offset, because Eastern observes daylight saving), and nothing in it ever bans.
- **The Puppeteer is held on his feet for the whole ceremony.** His death is scripted: the killing blow is absorbed and four and a half seconds of string-cutting plays out. What could still end it early was everything else - a second blow, a fall, the void, an operator's cleanup - any of which takes a body at zero health and lets the server kill it, which reads in game as "he just dies" with no strings cut and nothing said. His health is floored for the length of the ceremony, so the ceremony is the only way out.

### 🏆 Ten new feats, and the screen that has to show them
- **That Was Close** (survive on half a heart), **Wrong Place** (an event starts while you are below sea level with no sky over you), **Absolutely Not** (get 96 blocks clear of a rare event, or leave the dimension, while it still runs), **How Did You Even Do That?** (the sixty-second six-beat chain: a blaze sets you alight, a player snipes you from fifty blocks while you burn, you hit a zombie on fire, a creeper detonates on you, a shulker strikes you below half health while you are chained, and the fall finishes it), **Free Real Estate** (twenty distinct beds), **Local Idiot** (five deaths in one chunk, per dimension), **First Blood**, **Comeback** (win after touching one heart), **Untouchable** (win without taking a hit) and **Clutch** (win on two hearts or less). Beds and chunk deaths are the only persisted state; the chain is a real ordered state machine rather than a checklist, so a beat that is out of order does not count.
- **And they are in the jar.** The ten definitions had been written but the built artifact was older than they were, so the mod being played had none of them - which is the other half of "they are not in the UI" and is invisible from a dev run, where the resources on disk are read directly. The jar is rebuilt and checked for all ten by name, and the standalone pack zip regenerated, so what is shipped and what is tested are the same thing.
- **Loadable and *visible* are different questions, and only the first was being asked.** An advancement can load perfectly and still never appear in the achievements screen - no display means there is nothing to draw, a hidden one means the screen omits it, and a parent that failed to load drops the whole branch - which is exactly the report "the new achievements are not in the UI". `advancement.the-feats-are-in-the-tree` now requires each of the ten to be loaded, visible, non-hidden and hanging off a parent that also loaded, so that report is a build failure instead of a play session.

### 🎭 The Puppeteer's kit had model ids that belonged to somebody else
- **Five items carried art ids that were already taken.** A `custom_model_data` id only has to be unique *per base item*, so nothing ever complained when the Wooden Marionette, the Puppeteer Loot Box, the Puppeteer's Mask, the Marionette Strings and the Empty Mask were minted against **4550280-4550284** - the numbers Excalibur, the Mystery Box and the four Mystery Key tiers already own. The five were therefore rendering as the plain vanilla armor stand, chest, carved pumpkin, string and skeleton skull they are built on, and the proof is that no texture had ever been drawn for those numbers: nothing had been commissioned under them. They now own **4550290-4550294**.
- **The art exists, and both sides ship it.** `tools/make_puppeteer_textures.py` draws the five textures and their models (porcelain over wood with the smile painted on, the marionette with its four strings leaving the frame, the blank mask with violet slits and no mouth, the control bar and its bundle of string, and his box with a mask for a latch), the pack gains item definitions for `armor_stand`, `carved_pumpkin`, `string` and `skeleton_skull` plus his own box on `chest`, and the standalone zip is regenerated so the server has something to send. The **audit that was supposed to catch this was reading the wrong thing**: it collected `cmd(N)` literals only, so an id handed out through a named constant was invisible to it - which is how five items shipped pointing at somebody else's art. It now checks every model id the file names, and reports **104 custom model codes, all backed by pack art in 107 models**.
- **The Wooden Marionette is not placeable.** It is built on an armor stand, which is not a block item, so the fail-closed rule never saw it and right-clicking the ground spawned an ordinary prop while eating the stack - the summon, gone, and a vanilla armor stand where the boss should have been. Placement is refused, and the block click is handled as what it is meant to be: the summon, so pointing it at the ground works.
- **He manipulates you better.** His hand now drives the body it is on: the forced swing's cadence is the **held weapon's own attack cooldown** (clamped to 14-44 ticks, so no item turns him into a shredder and a deliberately slow one still reads slow) instead of a flat 22 whatever you carry, and the legs step at **your** movement-speed attribute - a Speed potion is walked by him faster, because it is your body. He also names whose head he has turned you toward when the hold takes, which is the part of being used that a player needs in time to shout about it.

### 🧵 A summon that places nothing no longer leaves a bar over an empty arena
- **`addFreshEntity` answers with a boolean, and the answer was being dropped.** When the arena chunk is not ticking yet the server refuses the body with no error at all - the chunk still reads as loaded from the outside - so the summon built a fight and a health bar around a boss that was never in the world. From inside the arena that is a boss who never swings, because there is nothing there to swing with, and a fight that ends by paying out nothing. The body is now looked for, the chunk is brought in and the add is tried once more, and a second refusal is reported instead of a fight being built around it. Pinned by `bosses.a-puppeteer-who-dies-off-script-still-pays`.
- **A tagged Puppeteer with no fight is a leftover, not a boss.** A fight that was released without a death - everybody walked away, an operator cleaned up, a reload dropped the state - left an immovable, AI-less Evoker standing in the arena forever: nothing could command him, he answered nothing, and the next summon in that arena found a Puppeteer that was not his. Strays are swept on a slow clock now, and the test hook sweeps them immediately.

### ☠️ A boss that dies outside its ceremony still gets paid
- **The Puppeteer could still be killed by everything that is not a player.** His death is scripted - the blow is absorbed, the strings are cut one at a time - but any *other* route (an operator's cleanup, a command, the void, a plugin, a body that reached zero health where the damage hook never saw it) is removed by the server without ceremony, and the fight tick read the missing boss as "the fight is over": it tore the fight down and dropped nothing. Which is precisely *he just dies, without attacking or dropping anything*. There is a second way out now, on the same once-only ledger the four newest raid bosses pay through: the ordinary entity-death hook and the tick that finds the body already gone both call it, `paid` makes the second caller a no-op, and a fight that never ran its ceremony still says the two lines that matter and still drops his loot.
- **He is invulnerable while he is being lowered in.** Fifty ticks of a boss who cannot move, cannot answer a swing and has not spoken yet - every other hand-driven boss in the mod is untouchable for exactly that window and he was the one that was not. The same one-line-a-tick guard the Elder Warden's arrival uses clears it, so a rise that was cut short cannot leave an untouchable boss standing in the arena.
- **A tagged Puppeteer with no fight behind him is no longer immortal.** The lethal-blow hook cancelled the blow even when there was no fight to play the ceremony for, which is the opposite of the bug it was written for: a boss nothing can kill is not a protected boss, it is a stuck one.

### 🗿 The Stone Golem is cracked open, plate by plate
- **The armored half of the fight was one bit of state with one answer.** He turned every player blow into a single point of damage and the only way through was to bait his own boulder back, so the fight was a puzzle you could solve once and then repeated - and every hit in between went visibly nowhere. He now wears **three plates**: each converted blow **chips** the plate it lands on (twelve chip it off, with the count on the action bar so the progress is readable), and a plate coming off is itself a damage window because he is off balance for 90 ticks. His own mistakes - the returned boulder, a charge into a wall, his fissure - take a plate off in one, so both routes are real and neither is the "correct" one. The bar carries the count, because that is the one thing a bar cannot show.
- **The last plate is the endgame, not a reset.** All three gone means **CRACKED OPEN**: the conversion stops for good, the plates do not come back, the bar turns purple, and he gains the one move he was holding in reserve - a two-second tell and then the arena floor gives way, with three shock rings and a real hit for anyone who stayed on it, followed by a 60-tick stagger of his own. It reuses the slam's windup and impact rather than adding a second system, and the telegraph is deliberately twice an ordinary slam's, so a party that was not watching him can still get off the floor. Pinned by `bosses.the-golem-has-something-to-break` - plate count, chips per plate, the window, the tell and the gap between finales.

### 🔎 "The anticheat does not detect speed" is usually a sentence, not a bug
- **Two exemptions, and only one of them was on the screen.** The module ships **off** until an admin turns it on, and it skips operators *and* economy staff. The status readout said "Operators: exempt"; an economy admin was skipped by the same code path and mentioned nowhere, so staff who had granted themselves economy permissions read "Operators: checked", moved with a speed client and were correctly flagged by nothing - which is indistinguishable from a detector that does not work. `/ff anticheat` and the test-mode heartbeat now name **which** skip is in force on the body asking, `/ff anticheat on` warns the operator who ran it that they are still skipped, and `anticheat.a-skipped-body-says-why` pins that the named reason and the actual gate agree. Both live speed checks - `anticheat.live-speed-detection` and `anticheat.sustained-speed-keeps-being-detected` - drive a real player at three times the walking ceiling and continue to pass.

### 🎭 Kind Sir works at the moment it means
- **The window opened three seconds late, behind a screen you cannot type in.** "Say gg in the five seconds after a duel" was measured from the *re-offer* of the rematch screen, which happens sixty ticks after the result - so the instant manners are worth something was three seconds of nothing, and a player who said it immediately was outside a window that had not opened yet. The screen's own wording made it worse: the rematch screen is a chest menu, so it holds the keyboard, and the honest sequence is press Escape *then* type. The window now opens with the result line itself, on the tick the duel ends, and is no longer gated on the screen being up.

## 1.1.5

### 🎨 The item art that was never drawn - and the check that makes it part of the build
- **Six custom items had a model code and no art.** They have carried a `custom_model_data` number for a while and rendered as the plain vanilla item they are built on, which is why they read as "broken textures" rather than as missing items: a **Distant Memory Sword** was a normal diamond sword, a **Bounty Compass** a normal compass, a **Distant Memory Shard** a plain echo shard, the **Mindbinder Shroud** a netherite chestplate, the **Raid Banner** a plain banner - and the **Clockwork Trophy**, whose art existed on disk, was simply missing its entry in `assets/minecraft/items/gold_block.json`. The five missing textures and models are drawn by `tools/make_missing_item_textures.py` (checked in, so the art is reproducible rather than a one-off binary) and the pack regenerates with the six entries. This is the part that was invisible from the server: the art only ever existed in the standalone pack, and the client jar - which *is* a resource pack - was shipping none of it.
- **The build now refuses to ship an item without art.** `ffAuditSources` cross-checks every `cmd(...)` model code in `ModItems.java` against the pack's `assets/minecraft/items/*.json`, resolves every model each definition points at, and verifies every texture those models draw is on disk - plus every other model in the pack, so a typo in a block or entity texture path is caught before it becomes a checkerboard in game. It reports what it proved: **97 custom model codes, all backed by pack art in 102 models; 102 pack models resolve their parents and textures**. Verified against the shipped jar: the pack's assets and the jar's are now 1:1.

### 🛡️ Claims: fluid no longer flows in, and the fix cannot quietly disappear
- **A bucket was all the griefing a claim needed.** Every player path asked the claim question - breaking, placing, using, opening, dropping, pistons, fire, hoppers, explosions, even other people's withers - but a fluid is not a player, so water or lava poured one block outside a border flowed straight in and washed away crops, torches, redstone and the floor of a build. `FlowingFluidMixin` now drops the spread entries that would cross into another owner's land, which stops both the flow and the flowing block, for water and lava alike, however the fluid got there.
- **The rule is only about crossing.** Fluid inside a claim still flows, and fluid moving between two positions the *same* owner claims is that owner's own water, because a rule that refused every crossing would freeze every farm in the world and read in game as "water is broken". Pinned by `claims.fluid-cannot-cross-a-border`, which checks all four cases plus the world-aware form on open ground. And because a claim rule nothing calls is not a claim rule, the audit now fails the build if the mixin is deleted, dropped from `fortuneandfavors.mixins.json`, or stops asking `ClaimManager.fluidMaySpreadInto` - three ways it could silently reopen while compiling perfectly.

### ⏳ The Time Lord's sprint now stops, including when he dies
- **The restoration is an invariant, not a branch.** A sprint was ended by whichever code path happened to reach the end of it, so a rate written by a fight object that no longer existed - replaced by a fresh summon, saved mid-sprint and loaded back, or a boss removed by an operator - was left accelerating with nothing alive that would ever put it back. The rate is now *owned*: `forceClockIdle` runs on every tick the module is alive and puts the ordinary rate back whenever no live sprint still claims it, and every ending (the sprint finishing on its own, a release, a despawn, a death, a server stop) goes through the same release.
- **"He is dying" ends it there and then.** A death ceremony is several seconds long, which is several seconds of a world still running at five hundred times its own rate if the only thing that would have stopped it is a branch the ceremony never reaches - so a dying fight can no longer keep the sky sprinting at all (`clockSprintStillRuns`). The calm between sprints is also twice the sprint now (two minutes of ordinary sky instead of forty-five seconds), which is what "it does not stop" actually looked like from inside the arena: a sky that was accelerated more often than not. Pinned by `timelord.the-sprint-dies-with-him`.

### 💀 The corruption death is now proved end to end, by a test that fails without the fix
- A check that asserted the hold *exists* would pass with the death hook deleted, because cancelling a drop and writing the items nowhere is the exact bug this feature exists to prevent. `deaths.corruption-kill-keeps-the-pack` stages a real full control, kills through the **drain's own lethal step** (the same code a live fight kills through, now factored out as `drainCorruptionStep` so nothing can drift), and checks three separate things: the pack is intact with nothing duplicated on the ground, the hold was spent by the death it was taken for, and the grip releases afterwards rather than forgiving every later death.
- **And then it does it with the pack already gone.** In the harness the death never empties the slots - the drop is cancelled first - so "the pack is still there" is true even with the restore removed. The same check therefore stages the state every real victim was in: the hold, the emptying vanilla performs, and the death. Mutation-tested: deleting the restore now fails the check with *"a corruption death with the pack already emptied handed back 0 of 7 diamonds"*, which is the sentence the old check could not say.

## 1.1.4

### 🛏️ Bed Wars: an actual map, an actual chest, and a bridge egg that lays a bridge
- **The map has lanes instead of voids.** It was five islands and a lot of sky: every objective was reached by walking off your spawn island and bridging, so the opening of every match was identical, and the two side islands were decoration nobody visited on purpose. Each spawn island now has a 3-wide lane to the middle, the middle is bridged to both side islands, four pillars stand on the middle island as a landmark you can navigate by from spawn (and the only cover that is not a block you placed), and each bed sits behind a low wool wall so a bed in the open is no longer a formality.
- **The team ender chest is a normal chest.** You open it and see your team's resources, and the left-click quick-store still banks Iron/Gold/Diamond/Emerald - but the totals it reports now come from the chest itself rather than the old invisible list, which would have told everyone their deposit went into an empty chest.
- **The bridge egg works, and this was a real bug.** The tag that marks a bridge egg lives on the *item*; the block-laying mixin read the component map of the thrown egg *entity*, which is empty, so a bridge egg was an egg. On top of that the bridge was pinned to y=100 and laid only where that spot was already air - which is the deck the spawn islands stand on - so throwing it on your own island laid nothing at all, and over the void it laid a staircase one block below the egg. The bridge is laid flat at the level the egg left the hand, and `duel.bridge-egg-is-recognised-from-its-stack` pins both halves: a real bridge egg must be recognised and a plain egg must not, or every egg anyone throws would lay wool.

### 🕳️ Dying to the Mindbinder costs the walk back and nothing else
- A full hold ends with the Mindbinder draining your last two hearts *itself* - no boss has to land a blow - and the victim then dropped their entire pack on the spot, which made being taken the most expensive death in the mod and the easiest one for it to inflict. The inventory is copied one tick before that death and written straight back once it has landed, so the echo still rises and the Mindbinder still laughs and nothing is lost. Registered in `DeathDropGuard` as a re-granting hold, which is what stops it becoming free keep-inventory: the drop hook only holds for a player who is actually under the hold.
- **The controlled body has real health now.** It used to be *set* to the victim's health on every drain tick, which quietly undid every hit that landed on it within a second - so the copy you are meant to be able to beat off the person inside it had, in effect, infinite health, and the only thing that ever killed it was the drain script deciding to. The mirror only ever takes health away, the body can be killed, and when it dies the hold breaks and the player gets their body back instead of spectating until the timer runs out.

### ⏳ The Time Lord's new phase-three signature: "the same second, again"
- **He does not attack you with it.** From the last phase onwards he stops trading blows for two seconds and winds the last eight seconds of the fight back over everybody's head: every participant is put back exactly where they stood eight seconds ago, and he takes his own injuries from that moment back with him, capped at a third of his health so a rewind is a setback rather than a reset. It is the only move in the fight that costs you progress instead of hearts.
- **And it is the only move whose answer is to stand still.** A body that spent the tell planted on the spot has nowhere to be sent back to, so nothing happens to it - he has spent the whole fight punishing players for holding ground, and the way to keep your ground here is to hold it. Pinned at the boundary by `timelord.the-rewind-only-reaches-the-moved`. It is wired into the phase-three rotation two ways: gated from the last phase onward on its own 45-second cooldown, and its cooldown is cleared when the judgment begins, so the endgame opens with it rather than whenever the timer happened to land.

## 1.1.3

### ⭐ The Starbound Magister's sixth charge is a beam, not a nap
- **Six of six is now judgement rather than a self-stun.** She used to spend the whole overcharge on a blast and then stand defenceless for five seconds, which turned her escalation into a reward for the player: the best line was to let her fill up and then hit a boss that could not answer. Judgement inverts it. She stops, draws a line of light across the ground for two seconds, and then cuts along it - the ray sweeps ~110 degrees over another two seconds, stops at the first solid block, and hurts in narrow bands with a ten-tick per-body cooldown, so cover and footwork both work and standing in the open does not. The cost is the same six charges and a doubled recovery interval afterwards, so there is *no* free-hit window any more; the punish is the two seconds she spends standing still to aim. Pinned by the tell, not by the damage: `judgementTellTicks() >= 30` and a nonzero reach, because a beam with a tell shorter than a reaction is an ambush.
- **Astral Dash is gone.** She no longer closes or flees by dashing fourteen blocks in fourteen ticks; the ray is her reach now, which is what a caster should have instead of a lunge. Her blink stays - it is her answer to being cornered - but it is on a 220-tick cooldown rather than 90 and it puts her back *inside* her own casting band (11-14 blocks) instead of 17-21, so a player who chases gets back on top of her in under two seconds. If the dash is what you missed, say so and I will bring it back as a short sidestep.

### 👑 The Emerald Sovereign's court grew three new ceremonies
- **Crownfall.** He throws his own crown off his head and it comes down in seven pieces - six thrown around whoever is nearest and one on himself - each a growing ring with a fuse under it. Available from phase one, so the first half of the fight is no longer bell, volley, decree on repeat.
- **Sovereign's Wheel.** Three rays turning a full circle around him in about fifty ticks, with a gap between each arm, and every ray stopped by the first solid block it meets. The counterplay is the shape: stand in the gap, or break an arm with a pillar. It is the only move in the fight that makes the arena's *furniture* matter.
- **Emerald Coffin.** A green circle appears on the ground for 1.3 seconds, then four walls of emerald block come up around whoever is still standing on it and hold them with the court for two and a half seconds. The tell does not follow anybody, so moving is a complete answer; the walls are the mechanic and the damage is a landing hit, not a grind. The walls are taken down by the move, by his death, and by an abandoned fight, and only blocks this move placed while they are still emerald are removed - anything a fighter built in their place survives.

### 🗡️ Two more fights that needed something unexpected
- **The Scarlet Devil rolls a tide.** A ring of her own blood runs outward across the floor three times in sequence, and the band that hurts is narrow and grounded - so the answer is to **jump** it, not to out-run it. It is the one thing in her fight that punishes a player who has spent the whole encounter strafing, and the third wave is faster than the first two for anyone relying on muscle memory. Phase two, on a long cooldown.
- **The Void Shaper turns gravity over.** Levitation for two and a half seconds across the whole arena, taken back by the move itself rather than left to expire, and then a hard drop with a flat landing hit that does not compound with fall damage - so the answer is a water bucket, a slow-falling potion, or eating the landing. Everything that falls while it holds falls *upward*, because being lifted without understanding why is a bug rather than a mechanic, and the anticheat already knows about levitation, so nobody gets flagged for flying.

### 🕸️ Two things that outstayed their welcome
- **The Puppeteer's strings let go sooner.** The top string held for thirty seconds, which is long enough that a rope stops reading as an effect with a timer and starts reading as a state the player is stuck in. It is twenty seconds now (240/320/400 by level, so the escalation survives), and the item's tie is ten seconds instead of thirty. Both still announce themselves when they go slack, and the ceiling is pinned by `puppeteer.strings-expire`.
- **The chair recipe is gone.** The recipe and its recipe-unlock advancement are deleted; the chair block itself is untouched.

### 🌍 Gladiator: a bigger, deeper world, and a truce to dig it in
- **The map is 184x172 and the rock under it goes 30 blocks down.** It used to be a four-block shell of stone lying on its own bedrock: every ore in the world was visible from the grass and a pickaxe was a formality. The floor is at y=70 now, deepslate below y=88, and the ore table changes with depth - coal and copper under the surface, iron and gold through the middle, and redstone, lapis, diamond and emerald only in the bottom half. The shallow seams still poke through the grass so a fighter with no pickaxe can see where the map is rich. Pinned by sampling the table itself: `duel.gladiator-gearing-truce` requires the deep sample to be able to produce diamond and emerald, the shallow sample not to, and the deep rock to be deepslate.
- **Caches: 6 in the middle, 44 across the field, and 10 sealed vaults underground** - carved 3x3 pockets rather than chests buried in rock, so a shaft that breaks into one drops you into it. A fourth tier exists at the far rim (full diamond, and one of a mace, an elytra or a notch apple) and every tier draws three to six extras from a much longer pool - TNT and flint, cobwebs, ladders, scaffolding, honey, hay, bucket tricks, splash potions, an enchanting table at the rim.
- **Nobody can hit anybody during the gearing window, and the window is two and a half minutes.** A blow landed by a player on a player simply does not happen until the hunt opens (the match says so once, and the attacker is told why); falling down your own shaft, your own lava, and the void are all still yours. Verified as a pure function - `gladiatorTruce(huntOpensAt, now)` - because a rule that can only be checked by staging a live match is a rule that gets checked by playing one.
- **The Hunter's Compass works from the first second.** It used to be gated behind the end of the gearing window, so for the ninety seconds where a direction is most useful the fighters had no tracker at all - "the compass does not work" is what that looks like. It is handed out and re-aimed from the first tick now, and it tells you it is there.
- **Gladiator out-of-bounds is an elimination, not a fatal blow.** Leaving the map used to deal `Float.MAX_VALUE` out-of-world damage and rely on the lethal-blow hook to absorb it, which meant the rule that keeps fighters alive in the gladiator world was, at its most dangerous moment, a lethal blow held back by another rule. The fatal blow is simply never dealt now: a fighter who leaves the map is eliminated with the same ceremony as any other. And as a backstop, the match tick turns any gladiator body that died through some other path into the elimination it should have been, so nothing can leave a corpse in a world whose whole rule is that a mistake costs you the match rather than your life.

## 1.1.2

### ⌚ A Pocket-Watch stop no longer decides PvP on its own
- **Damage from a player to a player inside a stopped world lands at 60%.** The stop exists to delay damage, not to hand whoever is holding the watch a free kill: a fight that resolved inside the bubble used to be delivered as one full-strength burst to a defender who could not answer, and the bank is what made that possible. Mobs, the environment and the boss are untouched, self-damage is untouched, and the rule is a function of three booleans (`pocketStopDamageFactor`) rather than a condition buried in the damage path - pinned by `timelord.a-pocket-watch-softens-pvp-only`.

### ⏩ The Time Lord sprints the clock
- **New ability: five days in one minute, and nothing but the sky runs faster.** Every tick of the sprint asks the world clock for a ramped multiple of its own rate, so a minute of standing still covers five complete day-and-night cycles, eased in and out over three seconds at either end. It moves *time*, not ticks: mobs, cooldowns, damage and the fight all run at their ordinary rate, which is why it can happen in a live fight at all. The clock is saved data, so the rate is put back when the sprint ends, when the fight is released **and** on server stop - a sprint interrupted by a shutdown would otherwise leave the world permanently accelerated. The curve is pinned by its integral rather than by its constants: `timelord.the-clock-sprint-is-five-days` sums the same function the live tick uses and requires it to be worth five cycles.

### ⚙️ Overclock did nothing at all (and other boss enchants, checked one by one)
- **The Clockwork King's proc excluded the one body it exists for.** It queried the entities around the target with `getEntities(instance, ...)` - the API's "everything except this entity" overload - so the target was left out of its own cleave and, with no crowd around it, the enchant produced no damage, no effect and no tell. Only a neighbour standing within five blocks, and only on a 60% roll, ever showed anything. The query now excludes the *attacker*, the target always overloads, and the neighbours stay a roll. Reported as "some of the boss custom enchants don't work"; this is what that was.
- **Every boss-dropped enchant is now measured, not assumed.** A new suite hits a real mob with a real enchanted weapon through the live damage pipeline and compares against a control, so an enchant that quietly does nothing is named instead of guessed at: Overclock, Starfall, Voidrend, Sovereign's Levy, Aging and Astral each have a check. The one failure it produced on its first run was the bug above.

### ✨ Astral (rare): more damage to mobs, forever nothing to players
- **A new rare enchant, +7% damage per level to mobs up to III.** Its damage lives inside the mob-only branch of the damage pipeline rather than behind a condition, so "never players" is structural: there is no line of code a player victim can reach. Pinned both ways - `bosses.enchant.astral-never-touches-players` requires strictly more damage against a mob *and* damage within a thousandth of an unenchanted sword against a player - and it drops from the Starbound Magister's loot box at a rare rate, with a `/ff tome astral` alias for operators.

### 🪨 Two fights that would not hold still
- **The Void Shaper's blocks shove roughly half as far.** The kinds keep their order (logs still shove hardest, deepslate still hits heaviest) and the vertical is scaled with the horizontal so the arc matches the distance; a log to the chest used to cross most of an arena.
- **The Starbound Magister stops running.** Inside her preferred band she used to strafe sideways every single tick, which made fighting her a footrace with no moment to swing. She now holds her ground and casts, backs off if you close and steps in if you run, at about half her old step - she is a caster, and a caster with her range has nothing left to do but cast.

## 1.1.1

### 💀 Bosses that died off camera
- **The Elder Warden never had a lethal-blow hook, so his death was only handled after the fact.** Every other boss in the mod refuses the killing blow and plays the sequence on a living body; he took the hit, fell over, and the animation ran over a corpse - which from the arena is a boss with no death animation and no i-frames. His blow is caught now (`BossManager.onWardenLethalDamage`), he is held at one point of health with his AI off and his invulnerability on, and the hold ends by sending the death for real, so every drop, loot box, advancement and experience grant on the ordinary death path still runs exactly once. His hold is three seconds, the shortest ceremony in the mod, and it is pinned end to end: `bosses.a-held-death-ends-with-a-body-that-can-die` summons a real Elder Warden, refuses a real killing blow, and then requires the body to be dead by the end of the hold - a hold that cannot end is an immortal boss.
- **An awakened Elder Warden could also stay invulnerable for good.** His invulnerability is turned off on exactly one tick - the tick his rise ends - so anything that skips or restores that moment (a reloaded fight, a state written before the field existed) left a boss standing there that swallowed every left-click, which reads as "hitting the Warden does not work". The tick loop now enforces the invariant instead: an Elder Warden past his rise and not holding his death is never invulnerable.

### 🧵 The Puppeteer's health bar did not work
- **Nothing was writing it.** He carries an ordinary evoker's entity, and vanilla only writes bar progress for its own boss mobs, so the bar sat full from the summon to the string-cutting while the health behind it drained - the same bug the Clockwork King's comment records being fixed for him. It is driven by hand every tick now, and it carries the two things a bar cannot: his phase, and how many hands are still holding strings. Pinned by `bosses.the-puppeteers-bar-tracks-his-health`, which summons him, halves his health, ticks the fight and requires the bar to read half - while asserting the name is written at all, because a bar that only empties tells a group nothing about the room.

### ⚙️ The Clockwork King survived his own funeral
- **His shutdown ceremony cancelled every killing blow, including the one it sends itself.** Seventy ticks of teardown ended by asking the level to kill a body whose own hook refuses to let anybody kill it, so the King never died, the fight was never released, and the plating stayed because the fight still owned it - which is exactly "the block displays do not disappear after death". The ceremony now declares its final blow, and the hook lets that one through. A King with no fight at all is also no longer damage-immune: cancelling the damage of an untracked one made an orphan immortal instead of merely unlooted.
- **The plating sweep only looked near players.** It swept a 96-block box around each online player, which is precisely the plating nobody had to look at - a suit left standing in an arena the group walked away from stayed there until somebody came back. It now sweeps every loaded entity in every loaded level, and the fight's own teardown discards the plating and the arsenal together. Pinned by `bosses.a-finished-ceremony-can-still-kill-its-boss`, which runs a real ceremony to its end and requires the body to be gone.

### 🧑‍🌾 The anticheat and players who are not hacking
- **A long clean record now raises the bar for moving somebody.** Ten minutes of judged movement with nothing found (12,000 ticks, counted rather than read off the world clock, so a relog or a slow server cannot manufacture one) and a real setback needs twelve findings on one check instead of eight. It is deliberately not immunity: a module still reaches the higher bar within a second or two of cheating, every finding is still recorded and announced at the ordinary bar, and the clamp - the correction that stops a body gaining without moving it anywhere - is untouched, so a clean player's body never gets anything the model does not allow. The rule is `confirmedFindingsFor(cleanTicks)` and is pinned without a server.
- **A correction now says which check moved you.** "You were moved back" told a ping-ponged player nothing, which is the same blind spot that made the earlier teleport bug take a jar-decompilation to diagnose. The message names the check.
- **The case file says whether the module is still firing on a player.** `/ff anticheat info`, `diagnostics` and the staff menu all gained a `clean record` / `detection now` line, because a busy past and a silent present are different answers to a moderator's next question and "last seen" cannot tell them apart.

## 1.1.0

### ⚔️ Sword blocking with the Distant Memory was flagged as a no-slow module
- **The check was reading the pose instead of the price.** Vanilla prices item use in the item's own data - a `use_effects` component whose `speed_multiplier` the client multiplies its movement by, 0.2 for an item that declares nothing - and this mod deliberately gives the Distant Memory sword `use_effects` at **full** speed, so blocking with it while running is design rather than a hack. The check inferred the penalty from the use *animation* instead, assumed four fifths for anything but `NONE`, and therefore reported a legitimate full-speed block for as long as the sword was raised. It now reads the item's own number, stands down for an item that has no penalty left to remove, and moves its threshold with the item: a bow is judged at 0.48 of full speed, an item that prices itself at 0.6 is judged at 0.87. Pinned both ways, because a check that simply stopped flagging would pass the first half and give every no-slow module a free pass: `anticheat.an-item-that-grants-full-speed-is-not-no-slow` drives a real block with the mod's own `blocks_attacks` + `use_effects(1.0)` data and requires nothing, then the same movement with the same block at 0.2 and requires a finding.

### 🍗 Anti-hunger needed the one thing the module hides
- **The check waited for a sprint flag that a no-hunger client never sets.** Vanilla charges hunger from the *server's* sprint state, so the module it is aimed at works by running at sprint speed without ever telling the server it is sprinting - and the food arithmetic could then never fire, because the flag it insisted on was the thing being hidden. The pace is now read from the body: a grounded step above the walking ceiling and no faster than the sprint ceiling, on a surface that does not carry momentum, in no vehicle, not climbing and not flying. Surfaces, vehicles and airborne ticks are all excluded by name, and the control is an honest sprinter with the food actually moving - the check needs both halves.

### 🪂 No-fall: the claim that could hide behind a fight
- **A client that reports itself standing on the way down deletes the fall from the server's bookkeeping, and being shot at made that invisible.** The landing test needed no hurt marker at all, so any damage at all - the commonest state in PvP - excused a fall nobody was ever charged for. Each tick of a descent now records whether the client claimed the ground while the nearest solid thing was two or more blocks below it, and a fall of seven blocks or more that lands with such a claim is reported with the height it was made at, whether or not anything hurt the player. The control is the same thirteen-block fall with the same hurt marker and no claim: nothing may be reported, and nothing is.

### 🏹 Arrow dodge: what a defender knew, now visible
- **A dodge module does not move faster and does not fly - it decides earlier, and that was the one thing nothing read.** The new `arrow-dodge` check asks two questions each tick: is anything about to pass through this body (the closest approach of any projectile within four blocks is inside a tick or two), and did the body turn sharply just now? A right-angle turn taken on the tick a projectile became unavoidable, three times inside twenty seconds, is a client that saw the arrow before the server did - fifty milliseconds against the two hundred a hand needs. It is in `NEVER_TELEPORTS` because one coincidence is a lucky strafe, and the self-test pins both halves: six right-angle turns with nothing in the air must be silent, three with an arrow two and a half ticks out must not.

### 🔨 Corrections that actually move somebody
- **A pattern check can now earn a real setback, which is what "the correction does not work" was about.** A clamp costs a module nothing - its next packet can carry the speed it wanted - so a body that keeps producing findings after three clamped corrections was never being corrected at all. Eight findings, three unheeded clamps, both policy bars (level *and* confidence, so an honest player on a bad connection cannot reach it by construction) and a finding inside the last two seconds now earn one setback. The evidence is spent exactly as it is for the checks that have always teleported, so a player still hacking re-earns it in a second and a player who has stopped cannot earn it at all - which is what keeps the earlier report, "it kept putting me back after I stopped", from coming back. The rule is five numbers and is pinned without a server.
- **A correction no longer deletes the evidence that justified it.** The speed window was thrown away on every correction and on both sides of the discontinuity it opened, so the more thoroughly somebody was corrected, the less of the case against them survived. The write path re-anchors the window on the place the body was moved to (the distances between samples still describe the player's own path), the window survives the module's own arrival window, and a client that answers a correction by reporting itself back where it was hacking from no longer buys itself a fresh second either.

### ✅ Pinned
- **104 checks, 0 failures**, on a real `ServerPlayer` in a real world - including five new ones: the full-speed item and its control, the hidden sprint and its control, the mid-fall ground claim and its control, the arrow dodged on the last tick and its control, and the five-number setback rule.

## 1.0.99

### 🧱 The write path is now enforced by the build, not by discipline
- **The body is moved with arguments, so the mutating calls live in one method.** `writeBody` used to take a callback, which meant each correction's actual `setDeltaMovement`/`hurtMarked`/`teleportTo` lived in its own synthetic lambda - three writes in three different methods, each of which had to remember to declare itself separately. It now takes the destination and the velocity as values, so every operation that moves a player's body is named in one method and nowhere else. The module's own moderation moves (spectating a player, and returning a moderator to where they stood) go through it too, which also fixes their attribution and stops their arrival window being counted against the staff member as "the server keeps repositioning this body".
- **`anticheat.only-one-method-writes-a-body` reads the compiled package, instruction by instruction.** It walks the bytecode of every class in `com.fortuneandfavors.anticheat`, resolves every method and field reference, and fails if any method but `writeBody` names `setDeltaMovement`, `setPos`, `setPosRaw`, `teleportTo`, `teleport`, `hurtMarked` or `knockback`. A comment promising to use the write path is worth nothing to it. Two controls keep it honest: the write path itself has to be found, and it has to still be the thing that does the moving - so a scan that reads nothing, or reads nonsense, fails rather than passes.
- **The walk refuses to lose its place.** A bytecode walk that slips by one byte reads operands as opcodes, which can hide a mutation as easily as invent one; the guard asserts the walk consumed each method's code array exactly and reports the method it could not follow. The failure was verified by planting a stray `setDeltaMovement` in the package and watching the check name it: `AntiCheat.plantedStrayWrite -> [setDeltaMovement]`.

### 📣 A lagging server can no longer silence a working check
- **Found the other half of "it detects nothing": the confidence discount was hiding detection.** Confidence is multiplied by how well the server is keeping time and how far behind the player's connection is, which is the right instinct - a measurement taken at thirteen ticks a second is worth less than one taken at twenty. But it is applied to the *announcement* bar, so on a busy server every check's confidence fell under 55% and the alerts stopped, while the ledger went on filling up: the checks were working, the server was busy, and nobody was told anything. A finding that has repeated six times at full evidence weight now reaches staff regardless of the discount, because a lagging server cannot manufacture a body that trips the same pattern six times - the discount is a property of the *server* and the repetition is a property of the *player*. The rule is four numbers and is pinned without a server.
- **Announcements are counted separately from findings.** `diagnostics` reports `alerts sent this session: N of M findings`, so a check that is firing without reaching anyone cannot look like a check that is broken.

### 💥 A crash the new test found: a backup thread writing the profiler mid-tick
- **`PerfMonitor.endTick` could throw a `ConcurrentModificationException` in the middle of the server tick, and that is a server crash.** The scheduled data backup runs on a thread of its own and reaches the profiler through `Safe.run`, so a backup that began while the tick thread was flushing its timings put an entry into a `HashMap` mid-iteration. It is reachable exactly when a tick is slow - which is when a backup is most likely to start, and which is why a self-test that deliberately makes the server run two seconds behind found it. The accumulator is concurrent and the flush iterates a snapshot; timings that arrive from another thread are counted on the next tick, which is the right answer for a timer and cannot abort the tick it interrupted.

### ✅ Pinned
- **99 checks, 0 failures**, on a real `ServerPlayer` in a real world, twice in a row - including the new write-path guard, the corroboration rule, and the bytecode walk over every class in the anticheat package.

## 1.0.98

### 🔒 One write path, so the engine cannot punish itself
- **Audited every place the anticheat reads state it also writes, and every writer now declares itself in one place.** A correction has a shadow: a velocity write sets `hurtMarked`, vanilla's entity tracker answers that by sending the player a `ClientboundSetEntityMotionPacket` *themselves*, and a position write sends a position packet. Both arrive at the outbound hooks looking exactly like somebody else moving the player, and a check that believes them stands down for two seconds - so the module's own correction becomes its own blind spot, at the exact moment it has the most reason to be watching. Three instances had been found by hand in one week; there were three writers (the setback, the speed clamp, and the refunded knockback) and they each had to remember to mark themselves separately. `AntiCheat.writeBody` is now the only way to move a body, it declares the write on the way in, and `bodyWrites`/`lastOwnWrite` report what it did, so the audit is a readout instead of a code review.
- **The correction that was never declared: refunding a cancelled knockback.** When the knockback check fires, the module puts the knockback back by writing the body's velocity - which the tracker then echoed straight back as a shove, standing the movement checks down for two seconds immediately after the module had caught somebody. It is declared now, like the clamp and the setback.
- **The correction is credited to the anticheat, not to the API it went through.** A position write lands in the server's own teleport API, whose hook cannot know who called it, so a moderator reading `/ff anticheat why` was told the server had teleported the player when it was the module putting them back. `writeBody` leaves its name where that hook can read it, and a teleport the server really performed is still credited to the server - in the same tick, "who moved them" and "what do I do about it" are opposite questions.
- **An answer to an old teleport could cut a new shove's window down to nothing.** The client's acknowledgement collapsed the stand-down, correctly, but it collapsed *whichever* window was open - so a body the server had just shoved (a knockback, an arrow, an explosion) more than half a second after an answered teleport was left with almost no stand-down at all. The answer now speaks only for the window it answered - compared against the end of the window the teleport itself opened, rather than against a constant, so it cannot quietly stop working if the two grace lengths are ever tuned apart - and `serverMotionGrace` reads the same function the tick loop decides with, so the readout and the decision can no longer disagree about how long the stand-down lasts.
- **Three copies of "everything a discontinuity invalidates" are now one.** The teleport reset, the unexplained-step reset and the server-write reset had drifted into three near-identical lists; `resetForTeleport` now calls the shared one, because a window that survives a teleport is a window measuring movement across two places.

### 🥊 A knockback measurement that could never run, now can
- **The check had a source of evidence that was arithmetically unreachable.** Measuring the knockback from the victim's own velocity only works when they were not already moving toward their attacker - the knockback is added *after* the damage lands - and the case it could not measure was the case it quietly gave up on: the pending measurement was dropped, which meant the motion packet the server sends next (the one that carries exactly the figure it could not read) could never fill it in. `if (!knockPending || knockExpected > 0)` was therefore never true on that path, so the "second, independent source of evidence" the design promised did not exist. Being hit while running in is the commonest case in PvP, and it was never judged at all.
- **The measurement is deferred instead of abandoned, and only the hit's own packet may fill it in.** The expectation is left at zero and armed; the first motion packet after the damage supplies it, with one entity tick's worth of slack, because a knockback sets the mark exactly once. A packet that arrives later (an arrow, an explosion) is refused, since an expectation modelled from a shove pointing somewhere else is an expectation the player would fail. A hit whose motion never arrives is still judged as nothing at all.

### 📣 A check that is still firing keeps saying so
- **The alert cooldown could hide a module that was working.** "It was detected once and then went quiet" is what a cooldown looks like from the outside, and staff cannot tell it from a check that stopped detecting. A finding whose count has *doubled* since the last announcement now cuts through the cooldown, because that is a second round of evidence rather than the same evidence again; the spacing still grows with each doubling, so it cannot become a spam channel. The rule is four numbers and is pinned without a server: `mayAlert(4, 2, 0, 400)` passes a closed cooldown and `mayAlert(3, 2, 0, 400)` does not.
- **The ledger now separates findings from announcements.** `diagnostics` reports `alerts sent this session: N of M findings`, so a check that fires without telling anybody can no longer look like a check that is broken - and the same line reports this module's own writes to the body, with the last one named, which is the audit a moderator cannot do from the outside.

### ✅ Pinned
- **New check `anticheat.a-correction-names-itself`** drives a position correction through the real packet layer and requires all four things the write path has to get right: the write is counted as the module's own, the arrival window it is owed is granted and named as the module's, the arrival window collapses when the client answers (and does *not* collapse when nothing is pending), a later shove still gets the full window despite that answer, and a teleport the server really performed is still credited to the server.
- **`anticheat.sustained-speed-keeps-being-detected` now asserts the invariant per correction rather than once at the end** - immediately after each echo of the module's own clamp arrives, the body must be inside no stand-down at all. It also distinguishes an echo the module produced from a shove vanilla produced (damage sets `hurtMarked` through `LivingEntity.hurtServer`, which is exactly why the count of those is printed on failure), and requires the correction to arrive through the one write path.
- **98 checks, 0 failures**, on a real `ServerPlayer` in a real world.

## 1.0.97

### 🧭 The quiet teleports are recognised by name, and that was two bugs in one
- **`/tp`, ender pearls, respawns and the wormhole potion no longer read as forged positions.** The module knew about a teleport only when the server *sent a packet* about it, and the most common way a player is moved on any server sends nothing: `TeleportCommand` reaches `Entity.teleportTo`, whose `ServerPlayer` override repositions the body with no `ClientboundPlayerPositionPacket` in sight, and a same-dimension `ServerPlayer.teleport` does the same. That left exactly two readings for a `/tp` - the body is somewhere it did not walk, and no packet was watched going out - which is the definition of a forgery, so the honest player was filed as `invalid-move` and answered with a setback. Three injections on `ServerPlayer` (the `TeleportTransition` entry point plus both `teleportTo` overloads) now tell the anticheat, by name, before anything moves. A teleport is correlated with its cause instead of guessed from its silhouette.
- **A respawn is a new body, and the record of the old one is gone with it.** Dying can put a player hundreds of blocks away, in another dimension, or in mid-air, and nothing about where the last body stood is still true - so every respawn arrived as an unexplained step *and* a fall that had never started, which is two findings produced by dying. `AntiCheat.onRespawn` anchors the new body, resets the windows, and keeps the history of what the player has been *failing*, because dying is a reason to forget where they stood and not a reason to forget what they did.

### 🔁 The corrections stop when the forgeries stop
- **Found the loop behind "they stopped and it kept putting them back".** A refused position packet was still committed as the packet layer's baseline, so the position the server had just corrected the player *away from* was the position every honest packet that followed was measured against - and each of those honest packets was therefore the next forgery, one setback at a time, forever. A refused packet is no longer believed, and a correction re-anchors the layer on the place it moved the body to.
- **A correction now needs evidence it has not already acted on.** The level a pattern check accumulates decays at two hundredths a tick, which is a hundred seconds from the ceiling, so a level that had been earned once sat above the correction bar for a minute and a half and moved the same body over and over on the same five seconds of behaviour. A correction is spent against the findings it acted on and clears the level it used; a player who is still hacking re-earns it in two findings, and a player who has stopped cannot earn it at all. `anticheat.corrections-stop-when-the-forging-stops` drives six forged positions and then forty honest ones, and requires the second half to produce no correction whatsoever.

### 🎯 Three ways this was being walked around
- **Chaining teleports bought unjudged movement.** Every server-driven reposition stood the movement checks down for two seconds, which meant a player could pearl, warp and `/tp` their way into staying permanently unjudged. The grace is now a grace for *arriving*: it is at its full length until the client answers the server's position with a packet of its own, and half a second after that, because from that moment the body is where the server put it and the rest of the window is not a grace - it is a hole.
- **A step nobody could explain handed out the same stand-down as a teleport.** A module crossing eight blocks a tick bought itself a second of unjudged movement with every step, and the position it invented became the place the module was willing to put the player back to. An unexplained step now breaks the windows it should break and gets nothing else: no stand-down, and no safe spot that was written by the client.
- **A repeated unexplained step is worth more than the first one.** One body somewhere nobody sent it is a lag spike; a body that keeps doing it is a teleport module, and both used to cost the same, so the tenth attempt cost what the first one did. Repeats inside a ten-second window escalate the finding to double weight and say so in the evidence - and the finding weight is now bounded at two rather than clamped to one, with the certainty ceiling left at one, because *how much* and *how sure* are different questions and only one of them should have been able to reach two.

### 🔍 A speed hack was silencing the check that caught it
- **Found why speed is spotted once and then never again, and it was the module's own doing.** A speed finding clamps the body's velocity and sets `hurtMarked` - which is how the server asks vanilla to tell the client about it - and vanilla's entity tracker answers that by sending a `ClientboundSetEntityMotionPacket` straight back to the player. The outbound hook read every one of those as the server having *shoved* them, so each correction stood the entire movement suite down for two seconds, and the stand-down throws away the speed window. A body held at 13 blocks a second for twenty seconds produced **zero** findings, with the window stuck at 11 of its 20 samples because it was being restarted every two seconds. The faster it detected, the blinder it got.
- **A correction is not a shove.** A velocity this module wrote is now marked as its own for the two ticks it takes the tracker to send it, and the outbound hook ignores it: no stand-down, and no knockback bookkeeping for a packet that was not a knockback. This affects every check that corrects anything - speed, simulation, flight, air-walk, motion - because they all correct through the same clamp. It also matters far more often than a *hack*: a low-confidence finding clamps too, so an ordinary laggy player was buying the module two seconds of blindness every time they wobbled.

### ✅ Pinned, both ways, and the numbers are printed on failure
- **Six new checks.** `anticheat.sustained-speed-keeps-being-detected` drives a body at three times the walking ceiling for twenty seconds and requires at least eight findings, with the entity tracker's own two lines - consume `hurtMarked`, send the motion packet - standing in for the tracker a fake player does not have, because that is the production path rather than an approximation of one. It requires zero `invalid-move` violations in the same run: the module's corrections may never be filed as the server repositioning a body. `anticheat.server-teleports-are-correlated` counts the handlers that reached `ServerPlayer` and requires an arrival through the server's own API to be both stood down and *named*; `anticheat.respawn-anchors-the-new-body` requires the body that walks away from its own death not to be filed as a forger; `anticheat.a-refused-position-is-not-believed` requires the first honest packet after a refused forgery to be accepted, which is the exact loop above; and `anticheat.repeated-unexplained-steps-are-worth-more` requires the repetition to cost strictly more.
- **The failure messages carry the engine's own readout.** `diagnostics` now reports the last server write (and whether the client answered it), the corrections this session, and the reported unexplained steps against the ones seen - because the first version of that last check failed for a reason its own message could not describe, which is a test worth fixing rather than a test worth deleting.
- **96 checks, 0 failures**, on a real `ServerPlayer` in a real world.

## 1.0.96

### 🔬 The anticheat is now driven by its own tests, and that found the bug
- **The engine had never once been run against a real player by a test.** Every existing check asserted a number, a bound or a wiring detail, so "the anticheat detects nothing" and "the anticheat looks at nothing" produced identical evidence - an empty ledger - and the whole module could stand down in play with ninety green checks behind it. Two new checks now drive a real `ServerPlayer` through the real per-player tick, in a real world: one walks a body at vanilla's ceiling and then at three times it, one walks a body across a water surface, and both assert what the detectors actually produce.
- **The first thing they caught was the two-clock bug.** The tick loop measured the movement stand-down against `MinecraftServer.getTickCount()` while everything reached from the packet side - a teleport the server sent, a velocity it pushed, the damage hook - stamped itself with `ServerLevel.getGameTime()`. Two counters that are *almost always* the same are the worst kind: a one-tick difference makes a grace window self-refreshing, and a grace window that never expires is an engine that never judges anybody. There is now one detection clock, set at the tick boundary and read by everything.
- **The synthetic trace separates the detector from the client.** Every live test of a speed check needs a hacked client, so "the detector is broken" and "the client is broken" used to look the same. `/ff anticheat probe` drives the *production* window and the *production* model with a synthetic trace, so the honest ceilings (walk, sprint, sprint-jump) must come back clean and the hacked ones must come back flagged - and if that pair ever stops holding, the build goes red.
- **Fixed a fairness bug the trace found immediately.** The sprint-jump ceiling was being modelled as a body sliding at jump speed *along the floor*, which is impossible and which the model was right to flag. The honest ceilings are three, not two, and the third one is only honest in the air.

### 🩹 A step larger than eight blocks is evidence, not an exemption
- **The biggest movement hacks used to be the invisible ones.** Any single tick that crossed eight blocks was classified as a teleport and stood every movement check down, which meant a module moving more than eight blocks a tick made *every one of its ticks* look like a teleport. The check now asks the question the old code answered with distance: did the **server** write this body here? A position packet this mod watched go out, a velocity it pushed, or a dimension the body just arrived in are all recognised by name; a step with none of those behind it is the strongest thing a client can say, and is recorded.

### 🔁 Being corrected every tick is itself the finding
- **Closed the loop that made the movement checks blind to what they exist to catch.** A correction is a teleport, a teleport stands the checks down for two seconds, and the speed window is cleared with it - so against a client being corrected every tick, the engine spent its whole session stood down. In effect the hack kept the anticheat blind *by being detected*. Twelve unbroken seconds of server-driven movement is now a finding of its own; a single occurrence is only recorded, well below the staff alert bar, and physics exemptions (boats, water, ladders, levitation, elytra) are not counted at all.

### 🩺 `/ff anticheat why` and `/ff anticheat probe`
- **`/ff anticheat why [player]`** prints every gate in the order it is applied - global switch, operator exemption, the *named* stand-down (`elytra`, `creative mode`, `time is stopped`, `server moved them`...), the speed window's fill, its last full reading against the model, the violation levels and confidence, and the alert bar they must clear. A silent engine can now explain itself instead of being indistinguishable from a clean player.
- **`/ff anticheat probe`** prints the detector's own arithmetic with no client involved.

## 1.0.95

### 🧪 Test mode no longer exempts the thing being tested
- **Fixed a second false-negative in live testing:** creative, flying and spectator state was still classified as scripted movement even after `/ff anticheat testmode` enabled operator checks. An OP testing speed or flight could therefore move impossibly and receive no movement evidence at all.
- **Test mode now overrides only administrative exemptions.** Boats, water, lava, climbing, elytra, levitation, slow falling, and server-driven time-stop movement remain exempt because they are real physics. Creative/flying/spectator movement is deliberately testable while test mode is on.
- **Fixed Jesus on shallow water.** The old water check first asked whether any collision existed within 1.6 blocks and then evaluated water. A solid bottom under shallow water made the player appear grounded, so the Jesus branch never ran. It now checks the immediate fluid surface directly; swimming, water vehicles, and lily pads remain excluded.
- **`/ff anticheat testmode` remains the diagnostic path.** Its heartbeat and direct finding messages should now expose speed and Jesus findings even when the tester is an operator.

## 1.0.94

### 🧪 `/ff anticheat testmode` proves whether live hooks are running
- **Added `/ff anticheat testmode`.** It enables the anticheat, includes operators, prints a heartbeat every second, and sends the tester an immediate message for every detector finding. Turning test mode off only removes the extra telemetry; it does not silently change the server's normal anticheat setting.
- **The heartbeat reports the actual production path:** anticheat enabled state, operator-check state, whether the player is active, server tick calls, movement packets observed, attack packets observed, gameplay attack events, findings, movement samples, and packet-engine state.
- **This separates a detector failure from a wiring failure.** If `ticks` increases but `moves` stays at zero, the movement packet mixin is not reaching the server-thread handler. If moves increase but `findings` stays at zero while a controlled test is running, the detector threshold/state model is the problem. If findings increase but no normal staff alert appears, the remaining issue is only notification permissions or cooldown policy.
- **Test-mode messages do not alter violation levels, confidence, enforcement, or movement correction.** They are an observation channel only, so the diagnostic cannot itself make the anticheat more aggressive.

## 1.0.93

### 🧪 The documented test switch now actually enables testing
- **Found the primary production-path failure:** `/ff anticheat op on` only removed the operator exemption; it did not enable the global anticheat. Because the system defaults off, an admin could follow the documented test instructions, move with a hack, and correctly receive nothing because no checks were running.
- **`/ff anticheat op on` now enables the anticheat and checks operators immediately.** `/ff anticheat op off` restores operator exemption without changing the server's global anticheat setting. `/ff anticheat status` reports both states.
- **The no-rubber-band policy remains intact.** This fixes the enablement gate rather than reintroducing position corrections: heuristic findings are recorded and staff-notified, while impossible packets remain the only default hard correction.

## 1.0.92

### 🔔 Sustained speed hacks now notify immediately
- **A real speed window was being recorded without producing a notification.** The prediction check waited for a full window plus twelve consecutive over-model samples, but then used the small distance margin as its confidence score. A modest sustained speed hack could therefore be genuine evidence and still sit below the alert gate indefinitely.
- **The alert decision now recognizes that window as corroboration.** Once the full prediction window and twelve consecutive over-model ticks have passed, the original `speed` check can notify staff immediately when server health and latency confidence are acceptable. One noisy tick, a single packet, a combat event, and every other heuristic still use the normal alert threshold.
- **No automatic punishment was added.** The notification path is staff-only; failsafe enforcement still uses its separate violation-level and moderator-presence rules.

## 1.0.91

### 🧭 Speed detection restored and the lag-compensation foundation is real
- **The windowed detector keeps reporting under the original `speed` check name again.** The new prediction wording remains in the evidence, but existing staff ledgers and tests no longer lose speed findings simply because the implementation gained a separate prediction label. The per-tick simulation remains independent, so a sustained speed module can be caught by either its acceleration or its travelled-distance pattern.
- **Added an actual bounded per-player history layer.** `LagCompensatedHistory` keeps up to eight authoritative position samples and uses the attacker's measured latency, capped at six ticks, to evaluate delayed reach and target-box checks. A hit is only excused if a real historical server position was in range; latency cannot grant extra reach.
- **Added local per-player world snapshots.** `WorldSnapshotHistory` captures a small authoritative 3×5×3 collision/visibility window around each player for the last eight ticks. Delayed scaffold placements can be checked against where the player really was when the packet could have been sent, and an ore face that was open in a recent snapshot is not misclassified as X-ray merely because the player moved before the break arrived.
- **The snapshot system is bounded and server-thread-owned.** It does not read Minecraft world state from Netty threads, does not trust client-declared blocks, and does not grow without limit. `/ff anticheat simulate` now reports position-history and local-snapshot counts. Server logout, periodic cleanup, disable, and shutdown all clear the corresponding history.
- **This is the safe first stage of Grim-style world replication, not an overclaim.** It provides authoritative local history for reach, scaffold, and ore visibility; a complete chunk-palette replica for every client version would be a larger subsystem and is not silently being described as finished.

## 1.0.90

### 🧭 Server-side movement simulation and no more heuristic rubber-bands
- **The speed check now has two independent layers.** The windowed `speed-prediction` check measures the distance a player actually travelled against the surface, movement attribute, sprint state and airborne samples in that window. The new `simulation` check measures the *change* in horizontal motion per tick: a legal sprint-jump is not judged by the ground model, while eight ticks of gaining more momentum than the player's own input can add are evidence of a speed module. This is the important distinction: a hack can stay under a speed threshold, but it cannot gain velocity from nowhere forever.
- **Fixed the speed-window latch that made the check inconsistent.** The old window remembered one airborne tick and then judged every later window using the jump ceiling; it also kept the worst slipperiness forever. Samples now carry their own grounded state and surface and age out of the ring with the position they belong to. Ice and a sprint jump are still given the generous side of the model, but a single jump no longer becomes a session-long speed exemption.
- **Heuristic movement checks no longer teleport players by default.** Speed, simulation, flight, flight prediction, air-walk, long-jump, bunny-hop, Jesus, no-slow and timer findings clamp horizontal velocity only after high-confidence evidence, and never write the player's position. The only default position correction is for an impossible packet; the `anticheat_hard_setback` config key is an explicit opt-in for servers that accept the old tradeoff. This is the direct fix for normal sprint/strafing rubber-bands.
- **Flight prediction now starts from the observed vertical velocity.** The previous fall prediction implicitly started every arc from rest, which made a normal jump's initial positive step look like flight. The predictor now integrates from the first real airborne step under vanilla gravity and drag, so jumps and ledge falls follow their own path while a hovering/flying body accumulates drift.

### 🛡️ Combat and packet evidence is split by cause
- **Reach now checks the server's actual entity interaction box**, not a centre-distance approximation, with the attacker's own interaction range and the measured surface distance in the evidence. The new `hitbox` check asks whether the attacker's ray can meet the server-side target box, inflated only by one tick of the target's movement; a target that was treated as larger is investigated separately from an attacker whose arm reached too far.
- **Killaura is no longer one bucket.** `killaura-6h` identifies several target swings inside one tick, `fast-swing` identifies repeated attacks against one target that ignore the weapon cooldown, and `critical-ground` correlates an airborne/falling claim with direct collision support beneath the attacker. Aim patterns now include constant-rate, linear/eased and module-360 streams, and only sample while there was a recent attack - rotating at a menu or looking around is never aim evidence.
- **The packet layer now measures timer movement independently of tick-end packets.** A client that suppresses tick-end packets but sends movement at a faster clock is still visible; the movement stream is measured over five seconds with a catch-up minimum and the normal per-category token bucket still protects laggy players.
