- **No-slow, fast-use and anti-hunger are server-state checks.** Item-use slowdown is compared against the held item's actual vanilla animation, consumables are timed against their own use duration and only count when the stack was consumed, and sprinting is measured against the server's own food plus saturation totals over a long distance. Food/drinks no longer accidentally receive a free no-slow exemption.

### 📚 Grim-inspired, not Grim-copied
- The architecture follows the useful public ideas from GrimAC: predict from the player's real state, account for collision/surface/vehicle context, keep evidence separate from enforcement, and prefer lag compensation over a magic threshold. No Grim source was copied, no client-mod name is a ban signal, and no asynchronous Netty access was introduced into this Fabric server code without a thread-safe world replica; Minecraft world reads remain on the server thread rather than creating a new race condition.
- The checks remain conservative by design: impossible packet values are rejected, heuristic patterns are recorded and correlated, and the correction policy is deliberately safer than the old rubber-band path. `/ff anticheat simulate` and the live check ledger are the place to inspect why a test session did or did not accumulate evidence.

## 1.0.89

### 🎭 The strings on the wildlife were never decoration
- **Phase two now ends with a throw.** He was already dragging the local mobs to him on strings - that was the phase's whole visual - but the bodies themselves were still just a moving hazard. Now he **takes the string back, spins the body up in the open, and hurls it at somebody**: a zombie is not a projectile until somebody decides it is.
- **The windup is a real tell and the throw is a real commitment.** Thirty ticks of the body spinning in front of him, announced with a tripwire attach and a rising pitch as it gathers speed, and then the throw is **aimed once, at the moment the windup ends — it is not steered.** Walk out of the line during the spin and it lands on the floor. That is the whole counterplay, and it is the same counterplay as everything else he does: read the tell, move.
- **It is a punctuation mark, not a machine gun.** The body has to land, the string takes it back, and it walks in again before he can throw it a second time - an eight-second cooldown against a fifty-tick throw. And a thrown body is worth **less than his own swing**, deliberately: the strings must never be the deadliest thing on the field, because then the correct play becomes standing in them.
- **It reads as a system, so the player can learn it.** The body stays visibly strung on the way in and on the way back, spins in place while it is wound, flies in a straight line, and is released back to its own AI the moment the throw is over - it is the *same* mob the whole time, not a fresh one spawned for the hit. `puppeteer.a-stringed-mob-becomes-a-thrown-body` pins the shape rather than the numbers: the spin has to be long enough to read, the flight short enough that it cannot be dodged mid-air (or it would be a homing missile), the throw lighter than the full-control tell it shares a phase with, the cooldown more than double windup-plus-flight, and the damage below his own phase-two swing.

### 🧾 The client's own words, admitted as context and nothing else
- **Section 22 is implemented as a structural claim rather than a promise.** Clients that have this mod now send a small self-report - protocol version and mod ids - over its own payload on join. It exists so a moderator reading a finding can be told **which allowances are already in force** and whether the client is one of the ones that names itself after a cheat.
- **The guarantee is enforced by the test, not by a comment.** `anticheat.a-mod-name-is-never-evidence` walks `ClientContext`'s public API by reflection and fails the build if any method takes or returns an `AntiCheat`, a `Track` or a `Violation` - so a future edit literally cannot add a route from "the client said `wurst`" to a finding without turning that assertion red. The note is appended to a violation that was raised **on its own evidence**, after the level and confidence have already been computed, and cannot add to either.
- **"Said nothing" is a state, not a smell.** The readout prints `unknown` and says in the same line that it is not a signal either way, because the alternative - treating silence as suspicion - is what corrupts an anticheat into flagging vanilla players. A client can rename itself in one line of code, which is the honest reason a name decides nothing; the test pins that an absent report produces an empty note and an empty mod list rather than gap-filling with `none`.
- **It is bounded because it arrives from an unverified client.** Garbage, wrong types, a null and 2000 fake mods are all pinned: parse is total, blank ids are dropped, ids are normalised and trimmed, and the list is capped at 128. A hostile client must not be able to make the server throw or grow a map with one packet. Reports are dropped on logout and on server stop, and a report older than five minutes is reported as stale.

### 🧰 The movement allowances, and the false positive one of them fixed
- **The compatibility list now covers the movement checks, and two of the entries relax nothing on purpose.** Auto-jump and step-assist are real behaviours that a movement check could plausibly misread - so they are documented rather than silently tolerated, and `/ff anticheat compat` prints each one's "relaxes" and "keeps" halves side by side.
- **Auto-jump needed no allowance at all, and the entry says why.** It produces regularly spaced jumps, and the bunny-hop check does not look at jump spacing - it looks for a body that **descends and then rises without touching anything**, which auto-jump cannot produce because it can only jump from the ground like everyone else. Writing "trust auto-jump" would have been a hole with no reason behind it.
- **Step-assist exposed a real false positive and the fix is a world query, not an exemption.** Falling down a stairwell is descend-descend-rise, which is exactly the shape a mid-air re-jump has, produced entirely by the terrain - and a raised step height does the same thing. The check no longer assumes vanilla's `0.6`: it asks the world whether anything beside or under the player's feet is within **their own `maxUpStep`**, so a modded step height raises its own tolerance without anybody declaring anything. The toggle then means something concrete - off returns it to `0.6` and nothing else - which is why the assertion compares the number in both states. Fails open on any error, like every geometry read in the package.
- **`anticheat.qol-compat` was rewritten to name the modes it requires instead of counting them.** It used to assert `keys().size() == 3`, which cannot tell "a new mode shipped" from "an old mode was deleted" - and it failed exactly that way the moment two modes were added. It now requires the three placement/inventory modes **by key**, so growth is silent and a regression is not.

## 1.0.88

### 🌬️ The four movement checks that were missing
- **AirWalk, LongJump, BunnyHop and Jesus are in, and each needs a pattern rather than a tick.** These are the four the module had no coverage for, and every one of them is a false-positive minefield: honest players ice-skate, bunny-hop, get knocked into the air and stand in shallow water constantly. So none of them can fire on a single strange tick.
- **AirWalk is a split, not an addition, and that is the point.** The old flight check owned both "hovering" and "reporting ground that isn't there" — two *opposite* hacks with opposite signatures, filed under one name. That is why an admin reading the ledger could not tell which one was firing. Hovering stays `flight`; claiming a floor is now `air-walk`, with the horizontal step added as evidence, because standing on nothing only matters if it is being used to walk.
- **LongJump measures one whole airborne arc, judged once, when it ends.** That is what makes it the *length* of a jump rather than the speed of one, which is the thing a long-jump hack changes and a per-tick speed check cannot see. The allowance is **the model or the step the body actually left the ground with, whichever is larger** — one line that keeps an ice take-off, a Speed potion, a sprint off a ledge and a knockback they walked out of on the legitimate side, because each of them raises its own ceiling instead of tripping it. Arcs shorter than ten ticks are not judged at all.
- **BunnyHop is the one signal vanilla makes impossible, tempered twice.** A jump is applied only from the ground, so a body that *descends and then rises* without touching anything has been jumped again by something. One of those is also what a server shove and a modded launch look like, so the check needs **two inside three seconds**, and at least three ticks of descent first — which clears the single honest way to gain height mid-air (clipping a block and being stepped onto it, on the boundary tick).
- **Jesus requires movement, because the name is about movement.** A body held up by a liquid surface and going nowhere is a float, and the flight check already owns hovering. Height has to hold still *and* the body has to be travelling sideways for twelve ticks. `isInWater` being part of the shared exemption list is what covers swimming and shallow water, reading the **fluid state** rather than a block list covers flowing, modded and waterlogged water for free, and vanilla's own lily pad — the one block a player can appear to stand on while the server counts them in the water — is excused by name.
- **All four break rather than pause on a served tick.** A teleport, a knockback, a firework dive or a shove out of a boss's hands arrives as `scripted`, and the answer is to *reset* the measurements, not skip one. A knockback mid-jump that merely paused a tally would be counted as part of the jump that carried it, which is how a movement check ends up flagging a player for being hit.
- **`MovementPhysics.airborneAllowance` is the same curve as the ground one.** A jump is a window like any other, and giving the airborne case a private tolerance is how two checks drift apart and start disagreeing about what a fast player looks like. The test pins the sprint-jump case at **above 4.3 blocks and below 8.6 for a 12-tick arc** — it has to clear an honest sprint-jump and not fit a long-jump hack.

### 🎭 Your puppet keeps one thing of yours
- **A reborn puppet now inherits exactly one trick from the player it was copied from** — a bow, a shield or a totem — read off what they were actually carrying. That is the difference between "a mob wearing your name" and *your friend, fighting you again*.
- **Two of the three need almost no code, and that is deliberate.** The shield blocks and the totem revives **because a shield and a totem do**: the copy is handed the item and the game's own pipeline takes it from there. A puppet blocks with the same rules a player blocks with — facing cone, bypass tag, enchantment-driven reduction — instead of a damage number invented in a file. The totem hook only *narrates* the revive, so a puppet's second life looks exactly like a player's.
- **The inherited bow fires the copy's own bow, at its own numbers.** The arrow is built with the player's actual item as its weapon, which is what gives a Punch II bow its knockback and a Flame bow its fire — vanilla reads those off the weapon, so they arrive for free and cannot disagree with what the bow really does. Only base damage is stated by hand (Power at the same rate vanilla applies it), and a crossbow shoots the flatter, harder bolt it is supposed to: **stricter, not looser**, because neither is an attack that the item could not make.
- **The priority is total and fixed — bow, then shield, then totem — so a player carrying all three still gets one answer**, and the test pins that adding a totem to a bow kit does not change what its puppet keeps. It also pins that the inherited shot can never out-damage the Puppeteer's own hand: a puppet must not become the bigger threat.
- **The inheritance is announced in chat when the puppet stands up**, and named in the boss help menu, so nobody has to guess what it kept or why it suddenly got back up.

## 1.0.87

### 🤝 The tell belongs to the group, and it gets shorter
- **A teammate's blow on the Puppeteer now breaks your windup too.** The answer to the tell was always "hit him", but that read as advice to the person being strung - and that person is often the one who cannot turn around, because his hands are the problem. Now *anyone* who lands a hit on him breaks the windup of the **nearest other player who is winding up**, within 24 blocks. Both halves get the message, because a teammate who saved somebody needs to see that it worked.
- **And hitting the person being strung breaks it.** Same shape as the Mindbinder's own "a friend shakes you free", in the same hook: the blow still lands normally, and breaking the hold is just what it *also* does. Only the windup breaks this way — once the three strings have landed, the answer is still the strings.
- **The tell contracts every time it lands on you.** Without that, "run or hit him" is a lesson a player only has to learn once: the first windup teaches the answer and every one after it is sixty free ticks of knowing exactly what to do. It now shortens 60 → 45 → 30 ticks, floored at 30, counted **when the hold lands** (not when a windup is broken) so it measures how many times it has happened to you. It is per fight and per player — a fresh fight hands the full three seconds back, so this escalates *inside* an engagement rather than compounding into an ambush eventually.
- **`/ff test puppet 1-3`** spawns him if he is not up, drops him in rather than playing the fifty-tick descent, and puts that many strings on you. One and two land immediately because they are just pulls; **three runs the real windup**, so one command reaches the tell *and* the hold instead of bypassing the tell to reach the hold.

### 🔧 The anticheat: policy is tunable, physics is not
- **The gap the last audit admitted is closed — with a line drawn through it rather than around it.** `/ff anticheat tune` lists and sets eight enforcement dials: alert confidence, alert and enforce levels, the violation-level ceiling and decay, and the alert / enforce / setback cooldowns. Every one is clamped to a safe range, persisted to its own `anticheat_policy.json`, and live immediately. **Retrofitted from `private static final` constants to live reads — eight values at six call sites**, which is why this was contained enough to do safely.
- **What is deliberately *not* tunable is the point of the split.** Reach margins, the friction-aware speed model, scaffold geometry, the killaura angle — these decide whether an event is **possible**, and the specification ranks falsely punishing a legitimate player as the worst thing this module can produce. They stay compiled in and test-pinned. If a reach margin were settable from a command, the fastest route to a false ban would be a typo in that command.
- **The floors are the guarantee.** `enforce-vl` bottoms out at 14, which is more than a single ambiguous event can produce, so no setting of any dial can turn one wobbly second into a kick on its own. The decay can be slowed but never stopped. A `NaN` falls back to the tested default rather than poisoning the arithmetic, and a clamped `set` reports **the value it stored**, never the one that was asked for — a caller reporting the request would lie to an admin mid-incident.
- **`/ff anticheat status` now says whether the policy is custom.** A tuning file someone edited eight months ago and forgot is exactly the kind of thing that makes an anticheat look broken; now the state screen names it.
- **Pinned both ways.** `anticheat.policy-is-tunable-physics-is-not` requires every listed key to have a dial, every dial to have a real range with a default inside it, the enforcement floor to stay above one ambiguous event, clamping to hold in *both* directions and against `NaN`, and — the assertion that matters most — that none of `reach`, `block-reach`, `speed`, `scaffold`, `aura` or `knockback` is reachable from the tunable surface at all. `puppeteer.a-teammate-can-break-the-tell` pins the contraction as a *sequence*: the first tell is full length, every landing shortens it, it stops at a floor that is still reactable, and help reaches further than the victim can run.

## 1.0.86

### ⏳ The third string announces itself
- **Full control now has a tell, and it lasts three seconds.** The string that takes the player's arm is no longer tied on the tick he reaches for it: he **winds it**, visibly. The target gets a purple boss bar counting down ("§5STRINGS CLOSING §7- §frun, or hit him"), strings gathering on them in a ring that **closes as the timer runs out** so the distance they see them start from *is* the countdown, a rising tripwire click as it tightens, an opening line from him, and a system message that names both ways out. Anyone standing nearby sees the ring too, which is what makes it a call for help rather than a private warning.
- **Two ways out, both of them answers the fight already teaches.** Getting **18 blocks** from his hands breaks it, and so does **landing a blow** - which takes a string off, and therefore leaves him with no third string to land. The blow is honoured on the same tick it lands rather than the next one, because a player who reacts in time must not be owned by a string that arrived a tick after they stopped it.
- **The cancellation checks run before the completion check, deliberately.** A player who breaks the windup on the exact tick it would have landed gets the escape, not the hold: reactions that arrive on the last frame should count.
- **The invariant that matters is pinned, and it is an ordering rather than a number.** `puppeteer.the-third-string-is-announced` requires the windup to land in a 40-120 tick band, to be breakable by a distance that is **strictly less** than the distance that snaps a whole string (`18 < 34`), and not by a single step - because if that ever inverts, the only answer to a windup takes longer than the windup does. It also pins that a phase which cannot reach full control cannot start a windup at all, and that a windup only ever precedes the full hold.
- **Every way out of the hold clears the bar.** Expired, snapped, cut, killed, fight released, fight abandoned, server stopping - a windup bar left behind would sit on a corpse's screen for as long as the fight lasted, so the teardown is checked on all of them and centralised in one place.

## 1.0.85

### 🧵 Too many strings, and they stop being yours
- **The Puppeteer does not settle for one string any more, and the count is the mechanic.** His strings now **stack on the same player** instead of going one-per-person. Each one pulls harder *and* comes round faster, so the number on the action bar is the whole state of the hold rather than a label. Phase one will only ever tie one to you, phase two two, phase three three - so widening the cast is still what the early fight is about, and tightening is what the end of it is about.
- **At three strings his hand arrives: he swings your arm for you, at the person next to you.** That is the part that mirrors the Mindbinder. You keep every input you had - this is an extra hand laid over yours, not a takeover of the body - but he walks your legs into range and then swings your own weapon, at your own damage, into the nearest eligible player. The forced swing is broadcast as a swing animation, because the client never sent one and a friend taking an unseen hit reads as a bug rather than as a boss.
- **The line between this and the Mindbinder is deliberate, and it is the counterplay.** The Mindbinder takes the keyboard away and gives you a struggle meter. This does not take the keyboard: the player is still the one at the controls, still walking, still swinging, and an extra hand is pushing theirs. It ends the way a string always ended, just three times over - **one string comes off per blow that lands on him**, so the third hit gives you your hands back and the action bar says so every time. Getting 34 blocks away still drops all three at once, and the **Empty Mask's own escape is honoured**, because a decoy is exactly the right thing for a string to close on.
- **Who he aims you at is gated, not just chosen.** Another player first, because a friend is the only thing worth making somebody hit - and only where `ClaimManager.pvpAllowed` says those two can fight, so a hold can never be used to launder a kill through somebody who never consented to one. With nobody eligible it falls back to an ordinary mob, and never to a marked boss or another Puppeteer.
- **He no longer idles once the cast is as wide as the phase allows.** The thread ability used to stand down when he was holding his maximum number of people; now it spends that window tying the next string onto whoever has the *fewest*, nearest him breaking ties, so nobody gets rushed past the others into full control. The prompt naming that is fixed rather than silent, which is what makes the escalation readable instead of sudden.
- **Pinned by test.** `puppeteer.strings-stack-and-then-own-your-hands` requires the per-phase ceilings to climb, full control to be reachable only at the last phase and only *at* the threshold, one string to behave exactly like the unstacked pull it replaced, each further string to add force and shorten the interval, the interval never to collapse to every tick, and the forced arm to stay in the 16-60 tick band - a rhythm a player can read and react to rather than a shredder.

## 1.0.84

### 🧩 Client-mod compatibility, without switching anything off
- **Three modes, and every one of them names what it *cannot* relax.** `Accurate Block Placement Reborn`, `Inventory Walk` and `Mouse Tweaks / inventory sorting` are supported by name now, and `/ff anticheat compat` prints each one with both halves: the specific signal it removes from a check, and the checks it is explicitly not allowed to move. "We support ABP" is the sentence that turns into an argument six months later about why reach hacks are not being caught; what it actually changes, stated next to the switch, is the answer.
- **There is still no mod fingerprinting anywhere, deliberately.** Nothing in this module asks a client what it is running. Section 22's point is that a mod name proves nothing, so these are compatibility modes for *behaviours* real mods produce - and each one ships **on**, because the server is one where players have mods and a check that requires a vanilla client is a check that flags everyone.
- **The scaffold check was wrong, and ABP is what exposed it.** "A block under you, while airborne, without looking down" is not a description of a cheat - it is a description of crouch-bridging, ninja-bridging and jump-towering, all of which are ordinary competitive play. It now removes every case it can name an honest reason for before counting anything: the placement has to be under the player's **own** column (0.8 blocks, down from 1.5 - a bridge step is one to two blocks *ahead* and fails immediately), the player cannot be standing on anything, cannot be **rising** (that is jumping, and jump-placing to climb is a skill players practise - the flight check still owns rises that are not the player's own doing), and, when the crosshair is meaningful, cannot be looking at the face they are placing against. What is left is a block landing under an airborne player who is neither rising nor looking at it - and the ones while **descending faster than any jump can descend** are the ones nothing legitimate produces more than once. A single one is a block clutch. Three inside a second is a floor being rebuilt.
- **Accurate Block Placement's allowance is the crosshair, and only the crosshair.** Precise-placement mods raycast the block face themselves, so where a player is looking no longer has to match where a block legitimately lands - which was the only thing pitch was ever standing in for. With the mode on, that test is dropped rather than kept, and the check leans on the physical pattern instead, which is a stricter question rather than a looser one. What is *not* relaxed, at all, under any mode: **reach**, the **one-block-per-click** limit, the packet budget, and every combat check. ABP plus an impossible reach is still an investigation, which is exactly the shape section 16 asks for.
- **Inventory Walk's correct support is "nothing", and it says so.** No check in this module reads whether a container is open, so moving with the inventory up is judged against the same physics as any other movement. The entry exists so the next person to add a check knows not to make an open container into evidence - and a test pins that the only place a container menu is consulted at all is validating which slot a click names.
- **A real bug this uncovered: the inventory packet bucket was dead code.** `PacketEngine.window(...)` was driven by the self-test and by nothing else - no live container click ever reached it, so a shift-drag or a sort was outside the packet budget entirely. It is charged now, at the one door container clicks come through, with an **80-packet allowance** on top of the standard bank while Mouse Tweaks compatibility is on: a 54-slot chest drag or a double sort is one gesture to the player and dozens of clicks to the server, and the difference between a burst and a flood is that a burst ends. The flood limit itself is untouched.
- **The mods that need no entry are named too.** Performance, HUD, cosmetic and animation mods get no compatibility mode, because nothing in this package reads a frame rate, a HUD, a skin or an animation - a client running all four sends exactly the traffic an unmodded one does. `anticheat compat` says so out loud, because "not handled" and "nothing to handle" look identical from a command's output and only one of them is fine.
- **Pinned, both ways, with the modes on.** `anticheat.qol-compat` requires all three toggles to exist, ship on, and say what they refuse to relax - and requires the reach allowance and the placement limit to be *identical* with every mode on and every mode off. `anticheat.qol-placement` drives real placements through the packet hook: three under a level airborne player are accepted with ABP on and refused with it off, while a placement twelve blocks away is refused either way and files a `block-reach` violation, and a whole row inside one tick is refused by the **sequence limit specifically** rather than by reach catching the far end. `anticheat.qol-inventory` requires 72 honest sort clicks to be accepted and the allowance to move the budget by exactly its own size.

## 1.0.83

### 🖱️ The anticheat reads patterns, not just events
- **Two new checks, and they are the first that judge a stream rather than a thing.** Everything the module had until now can point at a single event: a distance, a stopwatch, a block. The hacks that are hardest to see are the ones that never do anything impossible - an autoclicker at a rate a hand can reach, an aimbot that corrects no more than a good player would - and both are invisible to any threshold on one number and perfectly visible in a distribution. `autoclicker` and `aim` are that distribution.
- **Click timing is measured against your weapon's own cadence, and that is the whole design.** In modern combat a player who simply holds attack produces a *perfectly regular* interval stream - the most machine-like pattern in the game, made without touching a macro - so any interval at or above the weapon's own interval is discarded, unjudged. What is left is taps that arrived faster than the game could use them, which is the only place an autoclicker exists at all. With a genuinely instant-swing weapon (this mod's Legacy 1.8 enchantment is +1024 attack speed) holding and macroing are the same thing and the check stands down completely rather than flag every player who holds attack - a missed cheater over a false one, by name.
- **The tell is a band, not a threshold.** Twenty-four consecutive taps whose every gap is within eight milliseconds of every other gap is not a fast player, it is a program - and it stays true whether the program clicks at a constant rate or adds jitter to hide it, because the jitter is smaller than the spread it would need to be. A rate past what a hand holds, or a window almost entirely made of repeated values, is required alongside it: two independent signals or nothing is said.
- **Aim is judged by how well the hits that landed were aimed, not by where the crosshair went.** Hits that connect with a target's *centre* to within three degrees, twenty times running, against something that was strafing - that is a crosshair being placed rather than pointed. A hit on a body is honestly several degrees off centre, so a player aiming at the body never trips it; hits on a target that is standing still are dropped rather than averaged in, because anyone can hold a mouse still; and a hit the wall check can already explain belongs to `killaura` and is not counted twice.
- **One finding per window, not one per click.** A saturated rolling window would otherwise report on every single tap and make a violation level a packet counter, which section 26 rules out by name. Ten seconds between findings mean a level of 12 is twelve distinct pieces of evidence - a player who has been at it for minutes.
- **Both have a switch of their own.** `/ff feature anticheat_aim` and `/ff feature anticheat_autoclicker` silence either one without giving up reach, speed or the packet layer, because the two pattern checks are the ones most likely to annoy a server that does not want them.

### 📤 The anticheat reads the packets the server sends too
- **Section 3, from the side that knows.** Everything inbound has to *infer* what the server told the client, and that inference is exactly where the false positives live: the server teleports a player, the next position packet arrives a hundred blocks away, and from the inbound side alone that is indistinguishable from a forged position. `ServerOutboundMixin` now watches `send` and records the two things that change what a client's next packet is allowed to look like.
- **Teleports stand the movement checks down from the moment the packet leaves**, not from the moment a jump is observed - so the grace is open before the client could possibly have answered, and lasts long enough to cover its latency. It is also tested: `anticheat.outbound-correlation` sends a teleport and a shove through the same door the real packets use and requires the stand-down both times, because "why is nothing being flagged" has this as an answer.
- **A shove the client was told about is a second, independent figure for the knockback check.** The impulse is normally measured from the player's own velocity at the moment of the hit, which is authoritative for a hit and useless for a body that was already moving toward its attacker. When that measurement comes back unusable, the figure the client was *sent* fills the expected travel in instead.
- **Verified where it can be.** `anticheat.click-timing` hands the check a hand, a fast human, a held attack key, an instant-swing weapon and a humanized macro, and requires the first four silent and the last caught. `anticheat.aim-statistics` does the same in both directions - a body-aiming player, a still target, a wall hit and a centred stream. Every assertion runs both ways, because a detector that fires on everything passes every one-sided test.

## 1.0.82

### 🔌 The anticheat reads the packets now
- **Four new checks, at the one layer the others cannot see.** Everything else in the anticheat reads a *state* the server already accepted: a position it applied, a break it timed, a hit it landed. By then the packet that carried it is gone, and with it the proof that the packet could not have come from a client. `packet-flood`, `invalid-move`, `packet-order` and `client-tick` read the inbound packets themselves, before vanilla has made sense of them.
- **A packet budget per client, per kind of packet.** Every inbound movement, action, inventory and mod packet is charged against a bucket that refills off the wall clock - not the tick loop, so a struggling server cannot tighten it - and banks enough for a client to spend its maximum ten catch-up ticks in one burst. That last part is the whole point: a player who freezes for two seconds and comes back with sixty packets in one tick is the most common shape of honest traffic there is. Crossing the bank drops the packet; crossing it two hundred times inside ten seconds is a flood, and vanilla only writes a debug line about those.
- **Positions and rotations no client produces.** NaN and infinite coordinates (recorded, then left to vanilla's own disconnect), a single packet that moves a body further than the game can move it, an accumulating yaw past a thousand degrees, a pitch past vertical - and a hotbar selection for a slot that does not exist, which vanilla ignores silently. A packet the *server* asked for is never judged: while a teleport is in flight, everything the client sends is the answer to a question we asked.
- **Timer, from the one packet that can show it.** The client sends exactly one tick-end packet per tick of its own, which makes it the only place the server is ever told how fast the client thinks time is passing. Measured over ten seconds, so the burst from a catch-up averages out against the seconds it lost.
- **Two tiers, because a single strange packet is also what an unaudited mod produces.** Anything beyond a chunk inside one packet is refused outright. The merely improbable - further than an elytra dive, less than a chunk - has to happen three times inside ten seconds before it is more than a counter.
- **Spinning counts as combat evidence, not as an offence.** A client snapping most of the way round on packet after packet while swinging adds to the killaura score instead of filing its own violation, because a shaky player in a fight genuinely can turn a few thousand degrees a second.
- **Pinned.** `anticheat.packet-legit` drives two minutes of ordinary play, a lag-out and its catch-up burst, a client on a third of a second of latency, a firework dive, a realm hop, a hard turn mid-fight and a stale container click through the engine and requires *nothing* back from any of them. `anticheat.packet-cheats` requires each forged value to come back named. `anticheat.packet-wiring` loads the listener, which is what proves every injection applied.

## 1.0.81

### 🎭 New raid boss: The Puppeteer
- **Summon him with the Wooden Marionette** (`/ff give marionette`, or 4 String + a Carved Pumpkin). He is lowered into the arena hanging from his own strings, and he fights on wires: a `setNoAi` puppet whose every move is scripted, like the Time Lord.
- **Threaded.** He ties strings to players. A string is visible, it pulls, and it snaps. The rule the whole mechanic is built on is that it never takes control away - at every tier a pull is an *impulse* added on top of whatever you are already doing, so the answer is always to keep walking and keep swinging. **I** pulls occasionally, **II** pulls twice as often and harder, **III** adds a brief forced step (his hand moves your legs once, then you have your body back). A string also snaps on its own if you get 34 blocks away, and **landing a blow on him cuts yours immediately** - that is the counterplay, stated on the action bar when it lands on you.
- **Puppet Rebirth.** A threaded player who dies while he lives does not just drop. Their puppet stands up where they fell wearing their face (the profile is announced, which is what makes the skin render at all), a copy of their gear, a copy of their weapon, 40 health and one idea. It fights the people still standing: rushes if they were a melee, keeps its distance and shoots if they carried a bow, guards him if they were armoured.
- **Three phases.** 100% strings and a lash; 60% multiple strings at once and a puppet summons; 30% every puppet the fight has created comes back at once, plus **Puppet Swap** - he trades places with one of them, so the body you were hitting is not always the one holding the wires.
- **It does not vanish when it dies.** The killing blow is absorbed, his strings are cut one at a time (each snap audible, with a counter on your action bar), every puppet stops where it is standing, and only then does **THE LAST STRING HAS BEEN CUT.** land and the loot fall.
- **His three legendaries.** *Puppeteer's Mask* (worn - a kill has a 25% chance to raise a 30-second puppet of the dead that fights for you). *Marionette Strings* (right-click an entity to tie a string to it, right-click again to yank it toward you; a 30-tick cooldown, and it snaps past 24 blocks). *The Empty Mask* (worn - your display name is blanked, hostile mobs shrug you off, and a lethal blow is spent on a decoy puppet that takes your death while you get five seconds invisible and quick; three-minute cooldown).
- **Loot box family 13**, with its own pity counter, previews, three tier cards and prize table.
- **Pinned.** `puppeteer.threads-pull-but-never-hold`, `puppeteer.phases-and-puppets` and `puppeteer.gear-rules` - the tiers have to escalate, a pull can never be a launch, a string has to be able to snap, the puppet is reduced, the mask cannot publish your name, and the family predicate has to be exactly his three legendaries.

### ⏳ The aging beam takes five years
- A blast of his beam now costs a **full five years**, not two: one beam leaves you on your last year, the overlay counts it down in years and says how many are still yours, and *anything* that ages you after that finishes it. It is deliberately not a one-shot - the beam is a hitscan with no dodge, and a hitscan that kills outright is the unfair thing, not the dangerous one.
- The finale beams use the same number, and the judgment's own end still reduces anyone who never landed a hit to bone.

### 🟯 The Shaping Sigil aims
- **It leads its target.** Throwing "wherever you are looking" is why the Sigil read as a building tool with a combat animation: against anything that moves, a block thrown dead along the crosshair arrives behind the target. It now looks for a body inside a cone in front of the throw, leads it by its own speed, and says so when it does.
- **Gripping has a cooldown and a message.** Grabbing and letting go could previously be thrashed every tick; taking hold is now a deliberate action with a short recovery, and a refused one says why.

### 📸 The old bosses arrive and die on camera
- **An arrival ceremony, once, for every boss of the old kind.** Each one used to rise, assemble or unfreeze with its own particles and then the fight simply started - nothing said "it is here now". They all reach the same instant (the tick the boss stops being invulnerable), so the ceremony is deferred to it: a crack running through the ground, a column of light, a shockwave that shoves anyone standing too close off their feet, and one line naming what just showed up. The rise animations are untouched, and now they land on something.
- **A death worth watching.** The old `deathVfx` was four particle bursts in a single tick, and the entity was already gone - a raid boss died exactly like a cow. The light now goes out first, the ground answers with the same rings the arrival had, the debris falls and the shock throws whatever is standing in the crater, before the body is removed.

## 1.0.80

### 🔧 The Item Forge finds the tome wherever it is
- **ENCHANT only ever looked in two fixed slots.** A tome dragged into the item or material slot, or an item dropped into the tome slot, read as "no tome" or "no item", the click did nothing at all, and the book stayed exactly where it was put - which from the outside is "the forge gave me my book back and never enchanted the item". It now resolves the pair across all three input slots, so whatever you drop where, it fuses; the material slot got the same treatment for FORGE. On success the item lands in the first slot and the consumed tome's slot is explicitly cleared and re-synced, so the client can never be left drawing a book the server has already eaten.
- **Pinned.** `forge.tome-consumes-and-enchants` drives the same entry point the button does - a vanilla sword and one of the mod's own legendaries - and asserts the enchant lands, the item keeps its identity (it never becomes an Item Forge item), and the tome is gone.

### 🐛 The Elder Warden copies your gear - it takes nothing
- **The theft is gone.** It used to take each stack as it dropped, grow stronger from them, and hand them back later through a Sculk Orb. It now grows from a *count* of what you were carrying and touches none of it: your drops fall where they fell (or are kept by Nice Keep Inventory), and the Sculk Orb return is gone with it because there is nothing left to return.
- **Same rule as the mind-controlled copy.** Both bosses now wear copies and take nothing.

## 1.0.79

### 💥 Explosion rebuild no longer gives up on big blasts
- **A huge blast stopped queueing part-way through.** The rebuild queue had a single 100k cap and `capture` silently `break`ed the moment it was full - no log, no message, just a crater that never healed. The cap is now split: bare blocks are cheap and the queue holds far more of them, while the entries that pin block-entity NBT (chests and their contents) have their own ceiling. Past that ceiling a block still rebuilds - it just comes back empty instead of not coming back - and a refused capture now warns once instead of vanishing.
- **Blocks in an unloaded chunk were dropped forever.** The drain removed an entry and released its column *before* restoring it, and a restore into an unloaded chunk fails - so a blast whose chunks the server had since unloaded left those holes permanently. Such a block now stays queued and retries for ten seconds, and only then is given up on.
- **The queue is a `LinkedList` now.** The drain removes with an iterator, and on an `ArrayList` every one of those is an O(n) shift: at 512 rebuilds a tick, a large queue meant tens of millions of element moves per tick - a stalled server, which also looked like the rebuild stopping.

### 🗣️ Boss dialogue has a volume limit, not just a duplicate limit
- **Identical-line dedupe never fires on the lines that spam.** A taunt that names a player or counts a number is different every single time, so a boss on a timer could broadcast one per second and every one counted as new. `BossChat` now also enforces a per-speaker minimum gap, and the six raid bosses each got their own channel so one going quiet does not gag another. The Mindbinder's every line now goes through the same gate.

### 🎭 Puppets have bodies - the Illusioner's copies actually fight
- **`PossessedPlayer.tick()` was an empty method.** Not lightweight - empty. Every puppet in the mod stood exactly where it was spawned and ignored gravity, knockback and its own movement, which is most of why the Illusioner's Spellbook felt broken: the copies were meant to fight in the caster's place and they could not walk. They now run the same real player-body tick the duel's own bot uses (the body half, with the client half discarded).
- **The copies deal damage.** They mimed the swing and dealt literally nothing, which made the spellbook a light show - a copy that cannot hurt you is decoration, not a decoy. Half the caster's weight, a little more with the cloak on.

### 🧠 Dying to the Mindbinder leaves a mind-controlled copy, not a weak echo
- **What rises is you.** A player who dies while the Mindbinder holds them used to spawn a zombie labelled "Echo of ...". It is now a full mind-controlled copy: their skin (announced to clients), their name, their gear, and a real player body that walks at the nearest player and swings the weapon it copied.
- **The items are copied, never taken.** The puppet is dressed from a duplicate of the victim's equipment and the victim's own slots are untouched.

### ⏳ Aging is visible while it counts
- **Four silent hits looked like four hits of nothing.** The enchant only *did* anything at the fifth stack, so there was no way to see the clock running. Every stack now pops its number (and how many are left) with a sculk-soul puff, so a player can see it coming and back off.

## 1.0.78

### ⚔️ Every weapon's real damage reaches a bot, not just Sharpness
- **The duel was keeping its own copy of what each weapon is worth, and it only remembered Sharpness.** `weaponDamageFor` read `Enchantments.SHARPNESS` and nothing else, so the Smite swords the mod's own kits hand out did nothing at all against a bot, and neither did Bane of Arthropods, Impaling, or the mace's Density outside the smash path. Every enchantment, every new weapon and every future attribute the game adds had to be hand-copied into that table or it silently did nothing.
- **The swing is now the game's own arithmetic.** `vanillaMeleeDamage` assembles the blow the way `Player.attack` does - the `ATTACK_DAMAGE` attribute (which already folds in the weapon, Strength and Weakness), the attack charge as `baseDamageScaleFactor` computes it, the enchanted damage through `EnchantmentHelper.modifyDamage` against the victim, the item's own `getAttackDamageBonus` (the mace's fall bonus and Density), and the crit on vanilla's own conditions - in vanilla's own order. A diamond sword hits for what the game says a diamond sword hits for, a Smite blade hits what it says, and nothing here has to be updated when a weapon is added.
- **Both directions.** A player's blow on a bot and a bot's blow on you now come off the same pipeline; only the difficulty still scales a bot's side.
- One honest note: an uncharged swing is worth its charge, exactly as it is for a player - the blow is no longer full value however fast it is clicked.

## 1.0.77

### 🔨 A mace smash lands on a bot now - the slam, and the damage
- **The damage was being applied and the game was being told the swing missed.** A blow on a bot is applied by the duel rather than by vanilla, and the one `boolean` that came back answered two different questions at once: "vanilla, don't apply this again" *and* "the swing landed". That second answer is the one `Player.attack` reads, and it decides whether the attacker's own post-hit work runs at all - which for a mace is the ground slam, the slam knockback that throws everyone nearby, the critical-hit particles and the sound. Every blow on a bot reported "not taken", so the smash never happened: the damage landed, silently, and the slam did not.
- **The pacing window was eating the one heavy hit of the fight.** Even once the smash was handed back to vanilla it arrived through the bot's damage gate like any other blow, and a player lining a smash up has usually just swung - so the four-tick anti-spam window swallowed it. Projectiles were already exempt because that is how a bow was made to work; a smash is exempt now too, and the question "is this a smash" is asked of vanilla (`MaceItem.canSmashAttack`) rather than a second copy of the fall threshold written out here.
- **`BotBlow` says which.** `taken` is "the duel owns this damage", `landed` is "it connected", and the mixin now reports the second to `hurtOrSimulate`. The fall bonus, the slam, the sound and the shockwave are all vanilla's again.

### 💪 Strength and weakness reach a bot's damage, both ways
- **A duel blow never reads the attribute Strength modifies.** Strength and Weakness are attribute modifiers on `ATTACK_DAMAGE`, and a bot's damage is rolled off the duel's own weapon table (a player's blow on a bot) or its weapon table scaled by difficulty (a bot's blow on you), so the attribute never came into either - a Strength potion did literally nothing in a duel.
- **The numbers are asked for, not written down.** `MobEffect.createModifiers` is what vanilla itself uses to turn an effect into attribute modifiers, so the bonus comes from the game and follows any future effect that touches `ATTACK_DAMAGE` instead of being a second copy of "+3 a level" that can drift. `duel.strength-reaches-a-bot` pins nothing-without-an-effect, a bonus with Strength, more at level II, and a penalty with Weakness.

### 🦶 TNT Run: the bot stops walking on air
- **It was never flying - it was walking where the floor had gone.** `botGroundY` answers "nowhere under you" with the height the bot is already at, and the walk called it every tick, so a bot over a hole simply kept going at its own altitude. On a map whose entire mechanic is that the floor leaves, that reads as flight.
- **A bot may only step where there is ground - the next step, and no further.** The old test included the block *two* steps out, so a bot treated its own solid tile as a gap whenever there was a hole anywhere within two blocks; and the bridging branch that was supposed to handle that is guarded on having wool, so a bot with none fell straight through to the ordinary walk and stepped out over the hole anyway. Standing on nothing now stands the AI down entirely and hands the fall to physics, which is what the `falling` flag was always for.
- **A bridge is a block, then a step - in that order.** The out-step used to be taken whenever the placement timer was two ticks from firing, so the bot walked out over the gap *before* anything was laid in it. It is now gated on the block being there.
- **And the TNT Run bot hops tile to tile.** It used to pick a destination seven blocks away and then discover its own holes on the way there; it now looks at what is next to it on crumbling floor, which is what a player does.

### ⏳ The Time Lord stops burying himself, and stops running
- **Two of his moves teleport him by hand with no collision check at all.** His time-stop strike landed him at `target.getY()`, which is inside the hillside the moment the arena is not flat, and his judgment step-out searched for a floor "near where I already am" - which answers with the block his own feet are level with when the destination is uphill from him. Both are now searched from the top of the column, and both are refused outright when there is nowhere to stand, rather than taken anyway.
- **He cannot walk through the world either.** His closing move writes his position directly, so a hill between him and the player was something he walked through; the step is now taken only where the ground under it is real, one axis at a time, the way a walking mob resolves a wall.
- **And if anything has already put him inside the terrain, he comes back up.** `BossGrounding.resurface` lifts a boss out of the column it is buried in - which the old downward clamp could never see, since the floor above his head is not below him - and runs every tick of the fight and throughout the judgment.
- **He no longer backs away from you.** His judgment used to retreat a little every tick once you were inside 26 blocks, with no block check on the step: walking up to him pushed him through the terrain behind him and left him drifting away over the far side of a hill. He holds his ground now, glowing and on the surface, which is what "hit him once to live" needs.

### 🏆 Two new achievements
- **Your Own Worst Enemy** - kill a Blood Revenant that rose wearing your own face. The copy is compared to its victim by the uuid on the corpse rather than by the victim being online, because the revenant outlives the player it was made from.
- **Dust to Dust** - die to the Time Lord's aging beam. Both halves are caught: a beam that kills outright, and being aged to the fifth stack (or judged out at the end of the finale) and reduced to bone.

### 🧹 The duel exit path, audited for a uuid it was assuming
- **One rule, in one place.** Everything the exit path stores - the restore queue, the result ledger, the last-duel memory, the wager book, the sword-block flags, the duel lookup map - is keyed by a player's uuid, and a bot has none, deliberately. `Participant.hasPlayer()` (backed by `DuelManager.ownsState`) is now the single answer to "does this fighter own uuid-keyed state", and every teardown site asks it instead of assuming. The one that mattered: a `Participant` shaped like a player but without a uuid would have filed its exit state under a null key, where nothing can ever find it and nothing ever clears it.
- **The spectator bet menu is players-only too.** It offers a bet on either side *by uuid*, so a bot duel simply does not open it rather than filing a bet against nobody.
- **`duel.exit-state-needs-a-player` pins the rule** - a bot is not a player even if something hands it a uuid, and a fighter with no uuid owns nothing, in both directions.

## 1.0.76

### 💥 Finishing a bot duel no longer voids the match
- **The crash was in the wager bookkeeping, and it was every single time.** A bot `Participant` is created without a uuid - deliberately, since it is not a player - and the teardown path filed a match's wagers under a key built by comparing the two fighters' uuids. `UUID.compareTo` is an *instance* method, so a bot's absent uuid threw a `NullPointerException` - and because that runs from the duel teardown, the throw took the whole tick with it and voided the match. Every bot duel that ended went out that way, which is why the log reads `duel tick error - voiding the match`.
- **A fight against a bot now takes no wagers, and says so.** There is no player on the other side to bet on, so the wager key is null rather than a comparison of two nulls, and the three places that look bets up - locking them at the start, settling them at the end, and counting them for the sidebar - all treat "no key" as "nothing to do".
- **The ante cannot pay a fighter who does not exist.** An ante is only ever put up by two players, so it is now gated on the winner's uuid being real as well as the ante being non-zero - otherwise a bot duel that somehow acquired one would have credited a balance to a null account.
- **`duel.wager-key-null-safe` pins it.** Both null cases, both argument orders, and that two real players still land on the same key - because if that key were order-dependent, a bettor and a target would file their wagers separately and neither could ever be settled.

## 1.0.75

### 🔍 `/duel diag` now dumps the floor, block by block
- **The arena is photographed the moment it is built.** Every arena records its own floor - the block identifier at every single cell - as the builder finishes with it, and writes a report to `run/fortuneandfavors/duel-diag-<mode>-plot<n>.txt`: a legend naming every block present, then the grid itself, one character per block. It is taken at build time on purpose, because the useful question later is what the floor *was*; by the time anyone runs a diagnostic it may have been mined, blown up or walked on.
- **The layer is found, not assumed.** "One above the void line" is right for a flat arena and wrong for a map with terrain in it - a gladiator world puts its bedrock at y=98 while its void line is 96, so the floor count was reporting a fully built world as an empty floor. The layer is now the one in the run above the void that holds the most blocks.
- **The floor is drawn in chat.** `/duel diag` prints the digest - layer, size, solid count, a hash - plus how many blocks have changed since the build, the report path, and a map of the floor sampled until it fits, with a legend. Whether there is anything there at all is usually the whole answer to "the arena does not render", and that should not need a second command.
- **Drift is called out.** "Since built: 4,012 solid, nothing touched" versus "340 block(s) changed" is the difference between an arena that is as it was built and one that has been rewritten since.

### 📡 `/duel diag probe` - what the client actually received
- **A question the server cannot answer for itself.** "The arena was never built" and "the arena was built and this client never received it" look identical from the server's side: both are simply a client with nothing to draw. So the server sends its build-time floor to your client, the client reads the same layer out of its own loaded world, and answers with its own copy - block for block, `-1` for any cell inside a chunk it does not have.
- **The three grids line up.** The report gains a *client* panel and a *diff* panel beside *built* and *now*, so a cell that is solid in `built` and `?` in `client` is a chunk that never arrived, and one that is `!` is a block that arrived wrong. The chat says which of the two this is, in words: none of the plot, some of it, or every block arrived and some of them are wrong - which means something is rewriting them.
- **The comparison is verified, not assumed.** Block identifiers go over the wire varint-packed and offset by one. The offset is load-bearing: air is block state id **zero** and is also the most common cell on any floor, so a raw stream has no spare value left to mean "no chunk here". `duel.arena-floor-probe` pins the round trip - including that air does not come back as the no-chunk marker - plus the floor search, the digest, and the row-major cell maths both machines have to agree on.
- **Nothing new is trusted.** A client that has not installed the mod still cannot receive the dump and is told so instead of being sent an undecodable packet; an oversized footprint is refused rather than walked; the client caps a probe at the same 40,000 blocks; and a probe that fails can never refuse a match, because the digest is captured inside its own guard.

## 1.0.74

### 👻 The Scarlet Devil, the Time Lord and the other four can be seen again
- **The six newer raid bosses were invisible to the rest of the mod.** There are two kinds of boss here: the eight originals, which register through `BossManager` and live in its `bosses` map, and the six that came later - the Time Lord, the Scarlet Devil, the Clockwork King, the Starbound Magister, the Void Shaper and the Emerald Sovereign - each of which keeps its own fight map and was **never registered with `BossManager` at all**. So `isBoss` answered *no* for every one of them, and any system that means to keep its hands off a boss treated them as ordinary mobs.
- **The rare-mob variant system is what put them under.** A variant below Mythic is hidden on purpose - an ambusher that reveals itself the moment it notices a player. A raid boss is a `setNoAi(true)` puppet the server moves by hand, so it never sets a target and never *notices* anyone: it acquired the invisibility and kept it. The visible body was gone; the name tag and the boss bar stayed, which is exactly what "stuck in invis" looks like.
- **A shared marker, and one summon-time guarantee.** Every fight now marks its boss with `ff_boss` through `BossManager.markBoss`, which also makes it persistent and **clears any invisibility it arrived with**. That tag is saved with the entity, so it survives a restart rather than being rebuilt from scratch. Bosses are excluded from the variant roll, from `applyTier`, and from the tracker's upkeep - and one already inside the tracker is dropped and **revealed** rather than hidden.
- **Nothing is switched off to achieve it.** The variant system still works exactly as before for ordinary mobs - the ambushers are still invisible, still roll all five tiers - and `bosses.never-hidden` pins both halves: a marked boss cannot be hidden or given a tier, and an ordinary mob still can.

### 💾 A bot you build is a bot you keep
- **Custom profiles are saved.** They live in the duel state file beside the pending restores and are written by the same two-minute autosave, so a custom bot survives a restart instead of quietly reverting to Normal. Every dial is re-clamped when it is read back, so a hand-edited file still cannot produce a bot past what a player can do.

### 🩺 `/duel diag`
- **The live state of the duel system, in an admin's hands.** Plots claimed out of 64 and which ones - a leaked plot is what "the arena is broken" usually is, because the next match claims the plot next door. The startup sweep's progress, because a sweep that never finished is a realm with rubble in it. Per fight: mode, phase, finalisation, and the arena's own floor counted block by block at floor level, reported as solid against air rather than assumed. Per fighter: online, eliminated, whether a live saved state exists, how many items the durable snapshot holds, and - for someone who is offline - whether their exit state is queued or **missing**. Plus any spectators and any arena teardowns still waiting on the tick loop.

## 1.0.73

### 🧱 Bridging stops looking like a scaffold hack
- **The crouch is held, not flicked.** The sneak key was cleared at the top of the footwork and set again inside the bridge branch, so a bot laying a bridge pressed and released sneak twenty times a second. That twitch is half of what "they still have scaffold" meant. The crouch is now held across a whole bridge and released when it stops.
- **The feet move at a sneak's pace.** The out-step was 0.22 blocks a tick - a walk - while the block placements were slowed down, so the bot laid one block every five ticks and still crossed an island at four and a half blocks a second. Stepping out onto a new block is now capped at a sneaking player's ~1.3 blocks a second, which is the other half.

### 🎒 You keep what you brought
- **A duel that ends while you are logging out no longer eats your inventory.** The exit state was only filed for a rejoin when the match was still *running*; logging out in the end-of-match window filed nothing, so coming back put you in the arena as a spectator holding the duel kit and nothing else. The state is filed whenever the match has not been finalised yet.
- **Coming back is a full rescue.** Being pulled out of the arena is only half of it - the fight leaves you in spectator, and a spectator teleported to spawn has no way to act. The mode goes back to a playable one in the same breath as the move.
- **The exit cannot strand you.** Teleporting a client into a chunk the server has not loaded is what parks it on "Loading terrain" forever, so the destination chunk is loaded before the move, a missing home dimension falls back to the overworld, an unbuildable teleport falls back to spawn, and a failed one is retried rather than left in the arena.
- **A broken map refuses the match.** If the arena build throws halfway you get a floor with a hole in it and a wall that stops before the corner. Nothing has been taken from you at that point, so the duel is declined instead of played on the wreckage.

### 🛠 `/ff restore` remembers more, and overwrites when you ask
- **Snapshots taken before a duel.** The snapshot store deliberately refused to run inside a duel, which was right about the kit a duel hands out and wrong about the inventory you walked in with. That state is filed explicitly now, before a single item is swapped.
- **Snapshots taken before a dimension change.** Walking into another dimension is the other moment a mod can quietly take an inventory with it. The pre-travel state is saved the instant the dimension changes.
- **`/ff restore <player> confirm` now replaces.** Merging left a half-broken inventory sitting underneath the restored one, which is the worst of both. It clears the slots and writes the snapshot back slot for slot; `/ff restore <player> merge` is the old additive behaviour, kept for when a grave holds something the snapshot does not.

### 🎛 Build your own bot
- **A Custom difficulty, with four dials.** Speed, strafing, clicks per second and smartness - left-click a dial to turn it up, right-click to turn it down - plus a randomiser and a reset to Normal. `Custom Bot` sits beside the presets in the difficulty menu, and `/duel bot custom` opens it too.
- **Smartness is one quality, in four places.** The dial moves reaction time, how wide the aim wanders, how much lead it puts on an arrow, how fast it turns its head, and how often it reaches for the clever answer together - because a "slow but clever" bot built from separate sliders is a bot that quietly becomes an aimbot.
- **The numbers cannot leave the room a person lives in.** The ceiling on speed is a sprint, the floor on clicks is a real click, and every value is clamped by the profile itself rather than by the menu, so the builder cannot produce a bot past what a player can do even if a step is added carelessly later.

### ⛏ Mining, smelting and crafting all cost time
- **Ore comes out of the ground raw.** A gladiator vein used to hand the bot an ingot with a note explaining it had no furnace - and the note was the bug. Raw iron and raw gold now come out raw.
- **It sets up a camp.** A furnace and a crafting table are placed as real blocks two steps from the bot, visible on the map and breakable by anyone who finds them, and the bot stands still at them while it works - which is the whole survival cost of gearing up.
- **A furnace's own burn, and fuel to spend.** Smelting takes vanilla's 200-tick burn and burns coal the bot dug on the way, or planks, or logs. A bot with ore and no fuel has rock in its bag, as it should.
- **A blade takes a beat at the table.** Crafting spends ingots with the table in front of it, so "mine, smelt, craft" is three things that happen in three places rather than one instant swap.

### 🥊 Boxing, gone
- The mode is deleted outright - enum entry, kit case, menu handling and its self-test assertion - rather than parked under Legacy 1.8.

### ✅ Checks
- The self-test pins the new custom-bot rules directly: the clamping floors and ceilings, every preset being expressible as a profile, and more clicks a second meaning a shorter gap between swings (60 checks).

## 1.0.72

### 🥾 A bot can only move the way a person moves
- **No more backwards sprinting.** Every step the footwork chose was normalised to a flat 0.32 blocks a tick the moment the bot moved at all, so a bot backing out of a trade - or out of a crystal, or away to eat - withdrew at 6.4 blocks a second, faster than anyone can run forwards. Travel is now the speed the difficulty allows and no more (`botMaxStep`, 0.18 to 0.28, against a sprinting player's 0.28), and a step away from the player is scaled down again to a walk.
- **It climbs what a player climbs.** The collision-aware step-up lifted the bot a whole block whenever the space above an obstacle was clear - and the arena's ring wall is exactly that shape, so a push against a wall ended with the bot standing on top of it, outside its own arena. The ceiling is a player's own 0.6 step height now, a taller rise is cleared with a real jump, and a barrier blocks by name as well as by shape.
- **It stands on the floor.** The height written back each tick came from "the block one below `floor(y)`", which is only the right block when the feet sit on a whole number. On a slab, a stair, a path or the bridge it had just laid, the bot named a block one too low and wrote its feet half a block high - the floating bot. `botGroundY` looks down from the feet instead.
- **Hunger is spent.** Attacking, being hit, sprinting and jumping all cost a player food; the bot does none of those through vanilla's own paths, so nothing it ever did made it hungry. It now pays exhaustion for all four - which is what "it has infinite food" was.

### ⚔ What the weapon in its hand is worth
- **Damage follows the weapon.** The table's fallback was five - a stone sword's damage - for anything it did not recognise, so a pickaxe, a shovel, a block or an empty hand all hit exactly as hard as a stone sword. Every entry is now the real item's attack damage and the fallback is a bare fist.
- **The best weapon it owns goes in front.** The bag was only tidied when slot 0 already held a sword, so a bot that looted a diamond sword into slot 9 spent the match swinging whatever the kit left in front - which is why its damage looked identical whatever it was carrying. Kit items that live in slot 0 on purpose (the boost feather, a bow, the Kits star) are never displaced.
- **The axe it does not have.** The inventory is forty-one slots wide and the armour and offhand are two of them, so "does the bot have an axe" was answered by a looted axe parked in its helmet slot. Searching, and looting, now stop at the thirty-six backpack slots.
- **1.9 combat has a cooldown.** A modern bot swung on the difficulty's rhythm and nothing else, because its blows are applied by hand and never spent the attack cooldown. The swing is now read off the bot's own attack-strength ticker, over vanilla's own charge curve: a legacy bot - whose attack speed the mod raises to nothing - swings freely, and a modern one swings when its sword has come back up.

### 🧪 Potions it is right to drink
- **Only the correct potion.** Any drinkable potion used to count as a last-resort heal, so a Potion of Weakness, a Potion of Poison or a plain water bottle went down its throat at low health and healed nothing. A potion is now only used when its own effects are a heal or a buff.
- **Splashes are thrown, at the right person.** A harmful splash - harming, poison, wither, weakness, slowness, mining fatigue, blindness, nausea, hunger, levitation, darkness - leaves the hand at the opponent from the distance a potion can cross; a helpful one lands at the bot's own feet. It used to drink them.
- **A meal is a real bite.** The food, potion and gapple the bot eats are spent by vanilla's own use-item path, and the meal is interruptible mid-swing.

### 🪓 Mace, crystals and the bot's own answers
- **A real mace smash now triggers on the bot.** The swing was intercepted by hand before vanilla's attack could run, so `MaceItem`'s own smash - the fall bonus, the shockwave, the knockback, the blast on everything around the impact - never fired; the hand-rolled path could only imitate it. A genuine smash is handed back to `Player.attack`, which lands on the bot through `hurtServer`, so the bot's shield and armour still decide what it costs.
- **Mace PvP is fought, not scripted.** The bot teleported along a hand-built sine arc, straight through anything in the way, and then applied a fixed number as the smash. It finds the mace wherever it is (the routine hard-selected slot 1), jumps with real forward momentum, and lands the smash on the way down with the fall it earned. Wind charges get thrown from the awkward range in between.
- **Crystal PvP is not only a mode.** Obsidian and an End Crystal in the bag are a trap wherever they were looted from, so a bot that finds both in a Gladiator cache or a Skywars chest plays the crystal game too.

### 🏰 Bedwars and the modes around it
- **Bedwars defends, then pushes.** The bot walks its own forge, buys its upgrades and only crosses the map once it is holding an iron sword and blocks - and it walls its own bed in with them, one real block in one real place. It fights anyone who walks into its base, and it fights anyone standing in its path on the way to the enemy bed instead of marching past them. Its purchases go to its own team now: the buy loop used to write them onto "whatever part is at index 1".
- **Skywars and Gladiator turn around.** Both used to beeline caches and ignore the fight entirely. Anyone who closes inside seven blocks is answered now, whatever is left to loot.

### 🏔 Gladiator is a world, and the bot works it
- **Twice the map.** 168 x 156 instead of 96 x 74 - ground, rock, trees, a cache in the middle and twenty-four scattered caches whose tier scales with the walk, all inside the plot the realm actually owns. The clear and the chunk load follow the map's size, and the startup sweep covers it.
- **Nobody spawns in the ground.** The spawn pad wrote its own level and everything below it, but the terrain pass had already put grass up to three blocks higher - and the loop never took the hill down, so the middle of the map was solid and the spawn was inside it. The pad clears the rise first now, and a free-for-all's ring of spawn points is placed on the surface that is really there.
- **The caches are worth crossing for.** Every tier now draws its contents and a handful of extras from its own pool: armour for every slot, axes and swords, a shield, a bow and arrows, a crossbow, potions built the way the kits build theirs, pearls, a wind charge, a mace, cobblestone and planks for the bridge home.
- **The bot digs.** A gladiator bot used to walk from cache to cache for the whole match, because a chest within reach always beat turning around. It answers a player in its face, loots what is left, and then works the ore in the ground the mode exists for - and a bot with metal and no blade forges one, which is the other half of the same promise.

### 🧹 Menus
- **One door into the 1.8 games.** "Legacy 1.8 extras" was a second icon opening exactly the submenu the Legacy icon beside it opens, so it is gone from both the duel and the free-for-all pickers. Boxing - which sat in the main duel list while belonging to the 1.8 games - now appears only under Legacy 1.8, with its own icon rather than a blank page.

## 1.0.71

### 🎒 The bot brings a kit bag, not just a sword
- **Totems go in the free hand.** A totem anywhere in the bag was only a number the damage gate could read; the one that actually saves you is the one in the hand you are not swinging with. The bot now stages a totem in its off hand the way a player does, and stages the next one after the gate pops it. `botStageTotem` moves the stack, it does not conjure one.
- **The bag gets tidied, and it can fill up.** Every couple of seconds the bot keeps its best sword in the slot the AI actually swings from - a diamond sword looted into slot 14 used to be a diamond sword it never swung - and, when the bag has four slots or less left, it drops four mob-drop items (`botJunk`) to make room. A full inventory is an inventory that cannot pick up the gapple that would have won the fight.
- **Potions are spent, not carried.** The bot reads `POTION_CONTENTS` and either drinks the potion (a real use-item action, interruptible mid-sip like any other) or *throws* it - a real `ThrownSplashPotion`, at the target or at its own feet. A splash potion used to apply instantly the way a drink does, which is not a thing that exists.
- **It picks its own kit.** Bots no longer take a random class: `botPickFunKit` and `botPickSkywarsKit` narrow the pool as the difficulty climbs, so a hard bot does not turn up to a Skywars island in Mage robes. The choice is recorded in `kitChosen`, so it survives the same path a player's `/duel kit` does.

### ⚔ The rest of a duelist's vocabulary
- **Crits are earned, and they are rare.** Vanilla only calls a hit critical when the attacker is falling, so the bot has to jump first and connect on the way down. A bot that crits every swing is not a better player, it is a damage multiplier, so the interval is 6.5 seconds on Normal and 1.3 on Hacker, and Easy never does it at all.
- **The mace is a mace everywhere, not just in its own mode.** It used to be a heavy iron sword in every other mode. `botMaceStrike` jumps, lands the smash on the way down, and pays the fall bonus - up to +7 damage, scaled by how far it actually fell.
- **Wind charges get thrown.** The same `WindCharge` entity the item throws, with the item's knockback, burst and self-boost, at the awkward middle distance where neither sword reaches. A Mace PvP bot used to carry 128 of them and never throw one.
- **1.8 sword blocking.** A legacy duel is fought with the sword *up* between swings. The block is the mod's own `blocks_attacks` stance, so it is a real use-item action: while it is raised the bot walks and strafes but does not swing. The 50% reduction the `LegacyBlockMixin` gives players hangs off `actuallyHurt`, which a bot's damage never reaches - its blows are applied by the duel's gate - so the same 50% is applied there, with the same front-facing cone.

### 🧱 Bridging looks like bridging
- **Crouch, place, walk.** The bot used to place a block and step onto it every single tick without slowing down, which is precisely the motion a scaffold hack produces and no player can make. It now sneaks at the edge, lays one block every five ticks, walks onto that one, and sneaks again. It holds position on the last two ticks of each placement rather than drifting onto nothing.

### 🏝 Skywars and Bedwars play like their modes
- **Skywars loots before it fights.** The bot keeps working the map while there is a cache worth walking to and the opponent is still more than nine blocks away, and only turns to fight when the gap closes or the nearby caches are gone. It used to beeline the first chest and then walk straight into the fight - "rush" was the right word for it.
- **Bedwars holds its base.** The bot's whole plan used to be "run at the enemy bed": it left its own bed open, never stood on its own generator long enough to bank anything, and pushed the moment the fight started. It now works a role - gather, defend, push - defends immediately when the enemy is on its bed, sits on its own forge until it has an iron sword and blocks, and only then crosses the map. It also fights anyone who walks into its base instead of ignoring them.

### 🦶 TNT Run stops being a coin flip
- **The next tile is chosen, not rolled.** Candidates are scored on being close enough to reach, kept at fighting distance from the opponent, off the tile they just left, and - the part that decides the mode - on a tile that still has neighbours, because an island is a tile about to fall. A bot standing on already-crumbling floor always moves.

### 🧭 Gladiator gets a direction
- **A Hunter's Compass, and it points.** When the quiet window ends, every fighter is handed a compass whose needle is aimed at their nearest opponent, re-aimed every second, and the hunt is announced. It is a real vanilla compass: `LODESTONE_TRACKER` is the component the client's own needle reads, so this needs no lodestone and no custom renderer. Nobody's inventory is overwritten to make room - if the bag is full, the compass is simply not handed out.

### 🛡 Anticheat: three new checks and a sharper X-ray tell
- **`no-fall`.** A no-fall hack never changes the descent, only whether the server hears about the landing, so the descent is measured from where the drop began and the cost is read off the landing tick. It stands down for every honest soft landing: hay, slime, powder snow, cobwebs, scaffolding, water, vehicles, leviathans, elytra Safe Landing, Slime Boots, and the mod's own chrono fall guard.
- **`block-reach`.** The server is told where a placement landed, so a placement cannot reach past the arm that made it. Vanilla tolerates far more of this than it should, which is how reach hacks on building survive it.
- **`fast-place`.** A person places one block per click and a tick holds one click at most; a fill hack drops a whole column inside one tick.
- **X-ray, judged over a window instead of a session.** The old tell compared valuable ores against everything mined since the session started, which is trivially diluted by mining stone afterwards. The new one is a rolling hundred-block window: an x-rayer can bury an average, but they cannot un-mine the hundred blocks they just walked through. The face test and the bee-line test are both still there.
- **The bot's own survival limits are real again.** Environmental damage - falls, fire, drowning, freezing - no longer runs through the shield's block check or the armour's reduction, so a bot in diamond gear is no longer immune to the map.

## 1.0.70

### 👀 The bot fights what you are doing, not its own script
