- **It reads you.** The bot knew how far away you were and nothing else, so a player standing behind a raised shield, drinking a potion in its face, drawing a bow at ten paces or walking up holding an end crystal got exactly the response a player doing none of those things got. A `BotRead` is now taken off the target every tick - blocking, eating or drinking, drawing a bow, holding an end crystal or respawn anchor, and whether it has an axe of its own - and the fight answers that instead of the difficulty dial.
- **Blocking means the bot stops feeding you free hits.** Swinging into a raised block was a full-cost swing for nothing. It now takes the swing only when it has an axe, which is the whole reason a player carries one into a shield fight, or when you are mid-chew and the swing is free; against a block it has no answer for, it raises its own shield and waits you out.
- **Healing is punished, not ignored.** A player eating is a player who is not swinging, so the bot closes and swings on the fastest rhythm the mode allows - it used to keep its normal cadence and let the gapple land.
- **An arrow in the air is what a shield is for.** A drawn bow (or crossbow) in range makes the bot reach for its shield, which actually stops the arrow. Without a shield it closes and weaves instead, because a bow is at its worst up close and a shot has to lead a moving target.
- **An end crystal is a reason not to walk forward.** Somebody holding a crystal - or a respawn anchor, or the glowstone for one - now gets backed away from instead of charged, for as long as it is still in their hand.

### 🛡 The bot has the rest of what a player has
- **A real shield.** The AI decides to raise it and vanilla does the rest: `isBlocking()` goes live after the shield's own warm-up, and the duel's damage gate runs the blow through `LivingEntity.applyItemBlocking` - the same entry `hurtServer` uses - so the block angle, the item's own reduction, the durability cost and the block sound are the shield's rules rather than a number invented in the duel. The Modern Arena bot got an axe *instead* of a shield, on the reasoning that a shield the AI never raised was just a stat; it now gets both.
- **Real eating, with the item's own duration.** The old bot applied a gapple's effects the instant it touched one and then pretended to chew for 32 ticks by refusing to fight. A meal is now a vanilla use-item action: the stack stays in its hand, `LivingEntity.updateUsingItem` counts the ticks down, and `ItemStack.finishUsingItem` fires when the bite lands. Interrupt it and the meal is lost, exactly as it is for you. Axe-duel bots also carry a *drinkable* healing potion now - their kit only had splashes, which are thrown, never drunk, so they had nothing to drink at all.
- **Sprint-jumps.** Sprint into the gap and jump, with vanilla's own +0.2 forward boost, on the space-closing band. This was impossible before for a specific reason: the AI set the bot's altitude to the floor every tick from `groundYAt`, so the arc was erased before it began.
- **Riptide.** The bot spins a trident for the ten ticks vanilla asks for and then lets go, and `TridentItem.releaseUsing` does the launch - `Player.push` plus `startAutoSpinAttack`, in water or rain only. No velocity is written anywhere. A bot with a trident in the roulette and lucky kits carries riptide so it has one to spend.
- **`duel.bot-blocks-with-shield`, `duel.bot-eats-with-duration`, `duel.bot-sprint-jump` and `duel.bot-riptide`** pin each of them: a raised shield blocks and costs durability, a meal pays out only after its duration and only if it finishes, a sprint-jump carries the boost where a standing jump does not, and releasing a spun trident sets vanilla's own spin-attack flag.

### 🧱 The bot stops running its own gravity
- **The hand-rolled fall integration is gone.** 1.0.69 gave the bot the real player tick, which means `LivingEntity.travel` was already doing its gravity, drag, collision and landing - and the duel's own `fallSpeed` loop was still doing them a second time underneath. That double gravity is why the bot's drops came in faster than a player's. `DuelBot.steer` now lets physics own the altitude the moment the bot is airborne and the AI own it only while the bot is standing on something, which is also what makes the jump and the riptide visible. Falling is now a question - "has this bot left the bridge" - and the one answer physics cannot give, the arena's floor of no return, is still checked.

### ⚔ A third asymmetry, and this one was the bot's
- **The bot's own Knockback enchantment did nothing.** The player's Knockback cut the bot, because the bot's incoming path reads it; the bot's did not, because the bot does not go through `Player.attack` and nobody had added the term by hand. A bot holding a Knockback stick shoved exactly as hard as bare hands, and a non-sprint hit with a Knockback weapon shoved not at all. Both directions now use the same half-a-block-a-level the player side always did. `duel.bot-shove-honours-knockback` pins it.

## 1.0.69

### 🧍 The duel bot was only half a player
- **It ran no player simulation at all.** The bot's tick counted down two timers by hand and did nothing else - `ServerPlayer.doTick()`, the body half of a player and the half a real connection drives, never ran. The consequences were everywhere: a potion effect never expired, so one hit's Slowness stayed on a bot for the rest of the duel; the Regeneration from a golden apple never healed a single heart, because nothing was ticking effects at all; fire never burned it; air never ran out; and nothing integrated the velocity anything shoved it with. That last one is the reason it could not be knocked back in the first place.
- **It now runs the real player tick.** Fire burns and is put out, effects run down and heal or poison, air runs out, food is spent, and `LivingEntity.travel` carries the bot's velocity with real gravity, friction, collision and landing - the same physics the player across the arena is running. `duel.bot-is-simulated` puts a 20-tick Slowness on a bot, ticks it 40 times and requires the effect to be gone, which is exactly what used to be impossible.
- **The hand-rolled knockback integration from 1.0.68 is gone**, replaced by that physics. The bot still receives every shove in the game through the same delta movement - sprint hits, Knockback, mace smashes, explosions, fishing rods, the mod's own gear - it is simply vanilla that carries it now, so the arc, the drag and the landing are a player's.
- **Vanilla death is suppressed on the bot.** The duel owns death - last-stand lives, Totems of Undying, Bedwars beds and Skywars' void all decide what a killing blow means, and the duel tick acts on that decision. Vanilla player death would instead have scattered the bot's kit across the arena floor and broadcast a death message for a name that was never on the server.
- **Landing cancels banked fall velocity.** With real gravity running, a bot standing on the arena floor would otherwise accumulate a downward velocity every tick that the next knockback had to spend itself cancelling.

### ⚔ The last changelog was wrong about Sharpness
- **Sharpness was never missing from duel damage.** `weaponDamageFor` already applies vanilla's own linear bonus - 1.0 at level 1, then +0.5 a level, so 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 2.5 / 3.0 - which is exactly what this mod's own shipped `sharpness.json` defines. The 1.0.68 note that said enchantment damage did not reach duelists was simply mistaken, and this is the correction.
- **The asymmetry was the other way round.** The *bot's* Sharpness was ignored: Bedwars hands a bot the same sharpness upgrade it hands a player, and the bot-to-player damage table had no enchantment term at all, so an upgraded bot was hitting for bare weapon damage while the player's Sharpness cut straight through it. The bot's own Sharpness now adds the same bonus.
- **The bot's Sharpness now counts, and here is what that costs you.** Bot-to-player damage, before and after, and the hits needed to take 20 HP off a player in iron armour:

  | bot's sword | Sharpness | before | after | | armour on the player | hits before | hits after |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | iron | 1 | 6.0 | 7.0 (+17%) | | none | 4 | 3 |
  | iron | 3 | 6.0 | 8.0 (+33%) | | iron | 7 | 6 |
  | diamond | 1 | 7.0 | 8.0 (+14%) | | diamond | 13 | 11 |
  | diamond | 3 | 7.0 | 9.0 (+29%) | | diamond + Prot IV x4 | 36 | 30 |
  | netherite | 3 | 8.0 | 10.0 (+25%) | | | |

  So an upgraded bot now ends a fight in roughly a sixth fewer landed hits, and it lands them on the rhythm it already kept. A Bedwars bot only ever buys Sharpness I, so the common case is the +17% row; the higher rows are roulette and draft duels.
- **Crits are earned now, not rolled.** Both directions used a flat dice roll for bonus damage (9% for the player, a 12% x1.15 for the bot), so a duelist who jumped off a ledge onto a swing got nothing for it while someone standing flat-footed got a free bonus a tenth of the time. Both now use vanilla's real critical conditions - a fall, off the ground, not climbing, swimming, riding or sprinting - for a genuine x1.5. On flat ground that is slightly *less* average damage than the roll was; off a ledge it is worth jumping for.

### 🤖 The bot fights like a person instead of a turret
- **It retreats to eat.** Chewing already stopped it swinging, but it only backed off if you were inside three blocks and otherwise stood still and ate in your face. It now sprints away to about six blocks - out of sword reach by the time the bite lands - and only then comes back.
- **It remembers where it was hurt.** Nothing the bot learned about a fight used to outlive the tick it learned it in, so it walked straight back onto the crystal that had just taken half its health, and back into the corner that lost it the last trade. Wherever it takes a hit is now remembered for six seconds and sidestepped.
- **Two fights are no longer the same fight.** The strafe phase ran on a fixed clock, so the tenth duel against a given difficulty traced exactly the same footwork as the first. Each duel seeds its own offset, so the pattern, the sway and the dodge timing differ between fights.

## 1.0.68

### ⚔ The duel bot could not be knocked back
- **Its shove was a private impulse, not real knockback.** The bot carried three hand-rolled fields (`ffKnockX/Y/Z`), applied by teleporting it a little each tick, and the only thing in the mod that ever wrote to them was one melee path. Nothing else in the game could move the bot, because nothing else knows those fields exist: not an explosion, not a fishing rod, not a mace smash, not the mod's own boss gear, not a riptide - all of which shove a player through the ordinary delta movement that a real client integrates. The bot's knockback is now exactly that channel, and the duel tick is the client that consumes it. Damping reproduces the old impulse decay (a lift shrinks by 0.08 a tick, everything else by a quarter) and the ground and fall logic below still owns the descent, so ring-outs and launches behave as they did. `duel.bot-knockback` pins the contract against a real `DuelBot`.
- **Knockback now scales the way knockback scales.** A sprinting hit shoves harder (+0.3) and every level of the **Knockback** enchantment adds half a block, so a Knockback Stick finally shoves harder than a plain stick - previously the flat number was the same whatever the weapon said.
- **A Fire Aspect blade sets the bot alight**, four seconds per level. A duel kit ships a Fire Aspect 255 sword that did exactly nothing before.
- **Still hand-tuned, deliberately:** the two weapon-damage tables in `DuelManager` remain tables. They are the duel's balance, not a defect, so Enchantment damage (Sharpness and friends) is still not applied between duelists - the one place a duel is knowingly not like the overworld.

### 💀 The traitor stood on their teammates' bodies and got nothing
- **`otherParticipantsDeadOrGone` never asked whether the player it found was still alive.** A dead player's body sits exactly where they fell, which is almost always inside the 128-block bubble around the raid centre, so "every defender is dead" was the one case that could *never* satisfy the test: the betrayer would be the last one standing, the check would keep finding the corpses, and the villain victory - the hoard, the cash, the gems, the "Villain Of the Village" title - never settled. Corpses are no longer counted as defenders.

### 🏆 Eight achievements that fired into the void
- **The four newest raid bosses granted summon and kill advancements that were never written.** `summon_clockwork`, `kill_clockwork`, `summon_magister`, `kill_magister`, `summon_voidshaper`, `kill_voidshaper`, `summon_sovereign` and `kill_sovereign` were all passed to `Advancements.grant` from code, and no definition file ever existed - and `grant` looks the id up and returns *silently* when it does not resolve, so there was no toast, no title, no fanfare and no record. Every older boss has a working summon/kill pair; these eight now do too, chained summon into kill with icons, colours and descriptions in the mod's voice.
- **`advancement.grants-resolve`** walks every id the mod hands to `Advancements.grant` and requires each one to have actually loaded, so a reward that resolves to nothing fails the build instead of quietly doing nothing.

## 1.0.67

### 🏷 The four weapon tags now have names
- **The mod was shipping four untranslated item tags**, which is a warning every world load prints (`Untranslated Item Tags detected. Please translate your item tags so other mods such as recipe viewers can properly display your tag's name.`) and shows up as a raw key in any recipe-viewer tooltip. `ff_damage_weapons`, `ff_impaling_weapons`, `ff_infinity_weapons` and `ff_breach_weapons` define which items each enchantment applies to - they are what make Sharpness, Smite, Bane of Arthropods, Impaling, Infinity and Breach work on the mod's own weapons - and all four now carry display names. Nothing about enchantment behaviour changed; the tags were always correct, they simply had no name.

## 1.0.66

### ❄ The ice staff froze the wrong water
- **The spray is a cone along your aim; the water-freeze was an axis-aligned box.** `freezeWaterInCone` built its scan from raw world coordinates - `x` from `origin.x - radius` to `origin.x + reach`, `z` the same way, `y` across a six-block slab at eye height - so it never looked at the aim direction at all. Ice appeared a fixed distance to the **world-east and world-south** of the caster whatever they were facing: aim north and the beam froze the water beside you while the water you were actually spraying stayed water. Aim straight down into a lake and it froze **nothing**, because the water was below the eye-height slab. The volume is now the beam's real cone: march the aim ray, and a block freezes when it is ahead of the caster, inside the staff's range, and inside the beam's radius *at that distance along the beam* (measured perpendicular to the beam axis, not to the world axes). The cone opens from a narrow tip at the staff to the full radius at maximum range, which matches the particle stream the same code draws. The frozen block budget is unchanged, water is checked before the cone maths (the box is mostly air), and the beam no longer freezes the block at the caster's own feet - it used to be possible to seal yourself into the ice you were standing in. `items.ice-spray-cone` points the beam along four axes and requires it to cover the block eight along its own axis, refuse a block seven to the side at that distance (the old box accepted it), refuse everything behind the caster, and refuse anything past its range - the straight-down and off-axis cases are both failures of the old shape, so the check is not vacuous.

## 1.0.65

### ⏱ The five-minute hitch
- **Eight world sweeps all ran on the same tick.** `machine stale sweep`, `spawner stale sweep`, two display sweeps and four stray-boss sweeps were every one of them gated on `tick % 6000 == 0`, so once every five minutes a *single* tick paid for all eight on a fixed, predictable schedule - the kind of lag that reads as "the server freezes for a moment every few minutes". They all still run every five minutes; each now owns its own slot in the cycle, so the work lands in eight quiet ticks instead of one. `SWEEP_OFFSETS` is the schedule, and the new `perf.sweep-stagger` check walks a whole 6000-tick cycle and fails if any tick ever owes more than one sweep.
- **Four of those sweeps charged a 257-block entity query to every player.** The Warlord's, the Magister's, the Void Shaper's and the Sovereign's crash-recovery sweeps each looped over *every* online player and asked the level for every mob inside `player.getBoundingBox().inflate(128.0)` - a box 257 blocks on a side. On a server with twenty players standing together that was eighty full entity queries in one tick, all answering the same question. The radius is now 96, which is deliberately just wider than the widest arena in the mod (90) so a stray body is still found from anywhere inside its arena, and a player already inside a box swept this pass is skipped rather than queried again. `perf.stray-sweep-radius` pins both ends.

### 🗂 The data folder had grown to 3447 files holding 40 documents
- **Nothing ever removed iCloud's conflict copies.** This project lives in iCloud Drive, which mints a copy of a file every time it sees it change twice ("`contracts.json 2`", "`challenges.json.bak.5 39`"), and the mod rewrites and rotates every one of its data files on a **two-minute timer** - so it minted them continuously, and not one was ever cleaned up. The folder held **1577 conflict copies**, one of them a 39th-generation copy. Every data backup walked all of them, every one the backup carried made it bigger, and on a synced volume merely reading one can stall the calling thread on a download.
- **Two fixes.** The mod now skips a save whose bytes are identical to the last one it wrote for that file, so the two-minute save no longer rewrites and rotates forty files that did not change - which is most of what fed the churn. And at boot it sweeps conflict copies out of the data folder (`icloud conflict sweep`): in this checkout that removed **3156 of 3448 entries**, leaving the forty documents and their `.bak` generations untouched. The rule only ever deletes a copy whose original is sitting right next to it, and traces a *chain* of copies back through `...bak.5 39.bak` to the document it came from - there were 1207 of those that no simpler rule could match. `backup.conflict-scrub` runs it against a real directory, and `backup.icloud-conflicts` pins the name rule including that a real `.bak` generation is never mistaken for junk.

### 🔊 A failing tick that said nothing
- **`RaidGearManager.tick` wrapped its whole driver in `catch (Exception ignored)`.** That is not the same thing as defensive: a throw anywhere in it silently skipped every horn raider, vex and illusion copy after the throw point, for that tick *and every tick after*, and wrote nothing anywhere - `Safe.run` would have logged it, but this catch consumed the error first, so the summons simply stalled forever with their state maps still populated. It logs now, throttled to once a minute. The vex-targeting block that appeared twice in a row (the first copy fully subsumed by the second, costing an extra `getLastHurtMob()` per vex per tick) is gone.

## 1.0.64

### 🛡 An anticheat, and it is off
- **`/ff anticheat on|off` - default OFF, always.** A mod that ships detection nobody asked for is a mod that starts flagging players on a server whose owner has never heard of the feature. It also ships **exempting operators**, because the people who own and test the server cannot build, fly or teleport around with a movement check shoving them back - `/ff anticheat op on|off` is the switch that includes them, and it exists precisely so staff can turn it on and try to trip it themselves.
- **`/ff anticheat`** reports the state (it never flips it, and that is now covered by the bare-toggle self-test), the eight checks, and what happens when one fires. `/ff anticheat list` shows everyone flagged since the restart, worst first, with the name of whoever is still online. Both the anticheat and the operator switch are also buttons on the `/ff config` screen, saved to `config.json` like every other server setting.

### The eight checks
- **Speed** - more than 0.55 blocks of horizontal movement in a tick for twelve ticks running. The allowance is not a guess: a sprint-jump is about 0.36, and the mod's own fast movement is added to it explicitly (Speed potions, elytra, riptide, boats, water, ladders), because a check that trips on a legitimately fast player is a check that gets switched off.
- **Flight** - forty ticks airborne without ever losing height, or twelve ticks of reporting `onGround` with nothing underneath. Both stand down while the player is flying, spinning, riding, swimming, climbing, sleeping, in creative, or has slow falling or levitation.
- **Reach** - **the one thing vanilla does not do.** Vanilla's own server accepts an interaction at `range + 3.0`, which is six blocks on survival and is exactly why a reach hack survives it. This compares against vanilla's own test with a 0.75 block allowance instead, so a hit outside three and three-quarter blocks never lands.
- **Killaura** - a swing 75 degrees or more off the target (120 is worth double) or snapping between targets inside six ticks, with a decaying score, so a runner who turns fast is not flagged and a client that swings at what it never faced is.
- **Scaffold** - three blocks placed directly under a player who is falling, without their pitch ever going near a look down. Aiming at your own feet is something a client can do and a person cannot.
- **Fast-mine** - measured from the packet that started the break, against vanilla's own arithmetic (tool speed, the Efficiency bonus, Haste, and the hundred-tick penalty for the wrong tool). Only blocks whose estimate is six ticks or more are judged at all, so leaves and torches are never in scope, and half the estimate is the line: obsidian in twenty ticks out of a hundred and sixty-seven is refused.
- **Knockback delay** - knockback is what the client does not have to keep, so the impulse is recorded at the moment of the hit and the player's movement is measured over the next eight ticks. A hack that cancels the knockback is not just reported: it is re-applied.
- **Ore vision (X-ray)** - an ore broken with no open face anywhere on it. A miner has to break the covering block first, and that break is what opens the face; a client that is looking through the wall does not need one. This is the only check that cannot refuse anything - a client does not need the server's permission to look at blocks - so it counts and reports instead.

### 🧪 What it does when it fires
- Alerts go to **staff only**, once per check per player per twenty seconds, with the check, the player and the measurement, and every firing is logged to the console as well. Impossible hits, breaks and placements are **refused**; speed, flight and knockback delay are **corrected** (set back to the last position they were standing on, or given the knockback they dropped); ore vision is reported. Nothing is auto-banished, jailed or muted - this reports and refuses, and the judgement stays with a person.

### 🧪 Seven new self-test checks, because none of this can be checked by eye
- **`anticheat.off-by-default`** pins both defaults and both switches, including that a null player answers as unchecked rather than throwing into the server tick.
- **`anticheat.thresholds`** asserts the reach margin is strictly inside vanilla's tolerance and strictly outside a real hit, that every violation buffer survives a laggy tick, and that the check list has not quietly shrunk.
- **`anticheat.break-estimate`** pins the arithmetic the fast-mine check compares against: obsidian is ~167 ticks by a bare netherite pickaxe, ~43 with Efficiency V, less with Haste, more with the wrong tool, one tick when it is an instant break, and unbreakable when it is bedrock. It also pins the decision - half the estimate or less, never for a two-tick block.
- **`anticheat.commands`** runs `/ff anticheat on`, `op on`, `list`, `status`, `off` and `op off` for real and requires the setting to follow each one, because a sub-command that does not parse looks exactly like a feature that does not work. It also asserts an unknown sub-command is refused.
- **`anticheat.attack-probe`** puts a target two blocks away and another ten blocks away and requires the first hit to land and the second to be refused - the reach check has to be loose enough to play with and tight enough to catch the hack.
- **`anticheat.break-probe`** drives a real break packet through `ServerPlayerGameMode`, which is the only way to prove the timing mixin applied at all on a headless boot (touching the class loads it, and a failed injection takes the load with it), and that an aborted break does not leave a start tick behind for the next one to be timed from.
- **`anticheat.ore-vision`** builds a buried diamond ore in the test world and asserts it reads as unseen, then opens the face the way a miner would and asserts it stops being suspicious.

## 1.0.63

### 🌍 A backup prune that never pruned
- **`deleteTree` was guarding against the wrong exception.** Pruning old snapshots walked the backup folder and deleted as it went, inside a `catch (IOException)`. `Files.walk` is a *lazy* stream that reads attributes while it iterates, and when a file the walk has listed is gone by the time it is read, java.nio reports it as an `UncheckedIOException` - a `RuntimeException`, which that catch never saw. The self-test's own server stop hit exactly that on an iCloud conflict copy inside a backup, printed a stack trace, and left the directory where it was: the prune aborted **every time**, so the snapshot folder grew one full copy of the region files per backup forever. The walk now finishes into a list before anything is deleted, and each delete is defended on its own.
- **Backups stop carrying iCloud's conflict copies.** A `"<name> <n>.<ext>"` file or `"<name> <n>"` directory is not part of the world - it is junk that the daemon also deletes underneath a running walk, which is how the prune died in the first place. Both the tree copy and the file copy skip them now, exactly as the `scrubICloudConflicts` build task does for `src/` and `build/`.
- **`backup.icloud-conflicts`** pins the name rule so it cannot drift away from the build's: a duplicated data file, a duplicated directory and a duplicated world file are recognised, and `level.dat`, `playerdata` and a dated backup folder are not.

### 🎁 Nothing that is an item can become a block any more
- **The placeable check is fail-closed.** 1.0.62 listed every loot box in `isNonPlaceable` and stopped the five that had been left out, which fixed the boxes and nothing else: the list was still a list, and an audit of every base item in `ModItems.java` found seven more custom items drawn on real vanilla blocks that were never on it. The **Sovereign's Bell** is a bell, the **Raid Banner** a dyed banner, the **Boulder Baby** chiselled stone bricks, the **Golem Core** stone, the **Sovereign's Trophy** an emerald block and the **Warlord's / Scarlet / Clockwork trophies** plus the **Token Redeemer** gold blocks. Right-clicking a block with any of them placed the block: the legendary or the summoner was destroyed and a plain block stood where it had been.
- **The mod no longer asks "is this item on the list".** It asks *"is this a block item?"*, and a custom item is placeable only if it is deliberately one of nine - the buy and sell signs, the two hoppers, the Spawner Infuser, the Item Forge, the chair, the elevator and the spawner, which is a block on purpose. A future boss item drawn on a chest, a bell or a skull is protected by default instead of by somebody's memory, and the four gold-block trophies can still be *displayed* in item frames, which is what a trophy is for.

### 🧪 `items.placeable-guard`
- The self-test now walks the whole `/ff give` catalog, and for every custom item that is a vanilla block item it requires either a deliberate entry or a refusal, failing the build with the list of leaks otherwise. The set of placeable custom items is then compared against a **second, independent declaration** in the test, so an item cannot become placeable by being added in one place and not the other.

### ⏳ A line that described the fight before the fix
- **The Time Lord's finale message promised he was hanging in the air.** That was true of the version of the fight that put him in the sky, and it is what the last release changed: he steps out of the melee *on the ground*, glowing, and the message now says so - "stands his ground" rather than "hangs in the air", which was both the bug and the instruction not to look for him down there.

## 1.0.62

### Loot, honestly
- **Every raid boss now pays 3 loot boxes to everyone who fought** - the same number the Slime King and the King Wither Skeleton have always paid. The Scarlet Devil, Time Lord, Clockwork King, Starbound Magister, Void Shaper and Emerald Sovereign used to pay one box for a clean kill and two for a phase rush, so the reward depended on how efficiently you killed them. `BossPayout.BOXES_PER_KILL` is now the single source of truth for all of them.
- **A boss drops its forge material, its boxes, and a *chance* at one legendary** - not its whole set. Each of the six new fights used to hand out two or three of its legendaries on every kill, plus a pile of raw iron, redstone, obsidian, deepslate, emeralds and bread to make the pile look bigger. That is a mob's loot table, not a boss's: kill it twice and the second kill is worthless. Bosses now drop trophy + forge material, a 15% enchant tome, and a **single 20% roll** at one of their three legendaries. The set is what the box is for.
- **Loot boxes can no longer be destroyed by a full inventory.** The new bosses handed boxes over with `placeItemBackInInventory`, which drops the overflow on the floor in the middle of the fight. Everything now routes through `BossPayout.giveOrCollect`, so a box that will not fit goes to `/claim loot`. The raid's boxes and the Warlord's kill drop use the same path.
- **Loot boxes are unplaceable, everywhere.** Five of them (Scarlet, Clockwork, Starbound, Void Shaper, Sovereign) were missing from `isNonPlaceable`, so they could be placed as ordinary chests - a ghost block standing in the world that had silently eaten the box. All five are listed now, and `ServerPlayerGameModeMixin` refuses the placement outright as a fail-closed backstop.

### Bosses stay on the ground
- **No boss hovers.** Several fights are `setNoAi(true)` puppets that the server moves by hand, so vanilla never applies gravity to them, and two of them (the Magister, built on an evoker; the Scarlet Devil, on a phantom) were explicitly `setNoGravity(true)`. The Magister was spawned three blocks above the summoner and never came down; the Scarlet Devil's unstick loop only ever pushed her *up*, so a fight that began with her buried in a hillside ended with her permanently above the treeline. A shared `BossGrounding` now owns the question "where is the floor under this boss", and every fight clamps to it - so the Magister's blink lands on the ground, the Void Shaper lands in the crater he just made, and the Scarlet Devil stands on the surface instead of climbing into the sky.

### The Time Lord
- **The aging beam no longer teleports him into the air.** He used to step out of the reach of the judgment and *upward* - `target.getY() + 3` - leaving him hanging in the sky for the whole finale, which made a fight you must reach him to win. He now steps out on the ground.
- **More time to answer it.** It takes five stacks of aging to be reduced to bone, and a beam used to land every second: five seconds to work out the rule and cross the gap. A beam now lands every 1.5 seconds, and the judgment lasts 16 seconds instead of 10.
- **His blinks are shorter.** The far branch reached 18 blocks, which put him outside the arena and out of range of every melee weapon in the mod.

### Dialogue: quieter, and switchable
- **`/ff config` has a Boss dialogue toggle.** It silences every taunt, telegraph and ceremony line from every boss at once, and it is honoured by the Shared `BossChat` gate rather than by each fight separately - so a new boss cannot forget to respect it. Mechanic telegraphs, sounds and boss bars are unaffected: turning dialogue down never hides a wind-up you need to dodge.
- **Repeated lines are dropped.** Several fights re-enter the same code path from more than one place (a phase transition checked both on damage and on tick), and the same line went out two or three times in a row. An identical line inside 2.5 seconds is now suppressed.
- **The Void Shaper has no dialogue at all.** He is a thing that takes the world apart, not a character with opinions about it, and the running commentary competed with the one message the fight actually needs to deliver - which block is in his hands. Every mechanic telegraph is kept.

### `/ff config` reads like a screen
- **Laid out on the grid.** The feature toggles were written straight into slots 0..16, which put half of them on the iron frame and left the rest in an order with nothing to do with the categories. Features now sit on the interior of the top three rows with a header that counts how many are running, the server settings on the row below, and the actions on the bottom row.
- **The reset button actually resets.** It armed its confirm and then cleared the flag on the very next click, so the two-step confirm could never fire.
- **`/ff` opens the config screen directly** instead of dispatching `ff config` and re-checking permissions through the command dispatcher.

### `/ff restore` remembers more than one thing
- **A short snapshot history.** A player who dies twice in quick succession - once to a boss and then again on the way back - used to have the second, poorer state overwrite the first, so `/ff restore` could only ever hand back what they had at the *last* death. The three most recent superseded snapshots are kept per player, on disk, and the restore screen lists them with their age and item count so an admin can pick the good one. The picker is also no longer paging three at a time.

### Time Lord and duel fixes
- **Sumo is a 1.8 mode now.** It was sitting on the top-level duel menu beside Modern and Legacy as though it had its own ruleset; it is a 1.8 game - no cooldown, knockback is the entire weapon - so it lives under Legacy PvP with Axe, Combo and UHC, and the top-level slot now opens the Legacy options.
- **Automaton Armor is a pair of leggings.** Every other boss legendary occupies its own armour slot (the Mantle and Plate are chests, the Warlord's Cloak is a chest), so as a chestplate the Clockwork King's piece could never be worn alongside the set it belongs to.
- **Excalibur is a greatsword.** It was drawn as a slim arming sword - a two-pixel blade on the same diagonal as every vanilla sword - which made the mod's rarest duel drop look like an iron sword with better colours. The art is now a broad two-handed blade with a canvas-wide barred crossguard and a long wrapped grip.

### The loot-box pity is real
- **Soft pity.** Past four boxes the table is drawn twice per open and the first legendary wins; past seven, three times; past nine, four. The old counter was invisible until the very last open, so the "guaranteed legendary at ten" promise only mattered on the tenth box - now the box the bar calls *warm* is genuinely a better box.
- **You can see it.** A ten-cell bar, a description of how warm the box is, and the same text on the action bar after every open.
- **The guaranteed pick shows every option at once.** The raid box has seven legendaries and the picker showed them three at a time behind a Next Page arrow, so most players never saw the item they wanted.

## 1.0.61

### ⏳ Time stops now look like time stopping
- **A shared visual layer for every stop.** The Time Lord's stop and the Pocket-Watch were drawing their own unrelated handfuls of particles, so a strong mechanic read as "my arrows hung in the air". Both now open with a white bloom and three expanding rings, hold with a clock face whose hand sweeps once per stop, and close with the rings falling inward and a shatter chime.
- **Held things are visibly held.** Every projectile, dropped item and mob inside a stop gets a mote of light on it every half second. Before this, a frozen arrow was indistinguishable from an arrow that missed.
- **You can hear the countdown.** A tick every half second, rising in pitch as the stop runs out, and a bell every two seconds.
- **Titles.** "TIME STOP" for the caster and everyone caught in the bubble, "TIME RESUMES" when it lifts - plus an action bar with a ten-cell drain bar on both the Time Lord's stop and your own Pocket-Watch.

### ⚔️ Duels: four new modes, and a match report
- **Sumo** - a bare 20-block platform in the void with no barrier walls at all, because the loss condition *is* being knocked past the rim. Knockback II blade, cloth armour, no healing.
- **Archery Duel** - bows only, and melee is **cancelled outright** rather than merely discouraged: swing at someone and the blow does not land. The bot kites and keeps firing at any range.
- **Gladiator** - no armour, a sharp blade, two gapples, permanent Speed I. Positioning beats gear.
- **Boxing** - fists, no armour, no healing. Fists hurt because of permanent Strength I, not because the fight is allowed to last an hour.
- **MATCH REPORT** after every two-player duel: hits, damage dealt and taken for both sides, the health each finished on, your best combo, the match length, and an S/A/B/C grade. Every number is a real ledger entry booked from landed blows - including hits against bots, which are applied by hand and never passed through vanilla damage at all.

### 🗡️ 1.9 PvP actually plays like 1.9 PvP
- The Modern Arena kit was iron armour, an iron sword and **no offhand at all** - so "Vanilla 1.9+ combat, shield ready" was a promise the kit broke. It now gives diamond armour with Protection II, a Sharpness II sword, a Power II bow, 32 arrows, 8 gapples and a **shield** in the offhand. The bot gets an axe instead of a shield it would never raise.

### 🤖 The bot fights like a player who knows what they're doing
- **W-tapping.** It sprints into a hit and releases the sprint on the swing, so its hits carry real knockback and it can actually start a combo. Before, every bot hit was a soft shove the target simply walked out of.
- **Gapples are real gapples.** It eats at a threshold that scales with difficulty (45% / 55% / 65% / 75%), gets the actual Regeneration II plus Absorption, and pays for it with three seconds of standing still - which is a window you can punish. It used to heal one flat hit point on a timer while still swinging.
- **It strafes in every mode.** Strafe movement was legacy-1.8-only, which meant a hard bot walked in a straight line at you in every modern mode.
- **Its arrows lead you.** Shots are aimed where you will be, scaled by difficulty and distance, so a hard bot reads a strafing opponent instead of decorating the ground behind them.

### 🔍 Two mode-metadata quirks found and left as-is
- The self-test that pins the duel mode registry surfaced two deliberate oddities: each family lists its own base mode ("Classic") inside itself, and UHC is offered under both Modern and Legacy because it takes its combat style from whichever one you opened it from. Both are intended, so the assertion documents them rather than "fixing" them.

## 1.0.60

### ⚙️ The Clockwork King has real mob AI
- **He walks.** He was a `setNoAi(true)` puppet nudged by hand every tick, which is why he hovered after his own Piston Slam and walked straight into walls. He is a normal mob now: vanilla pathfinding sends him round an obstacle instead of through it, and vanilla gravity brings him back down. His scripted attacks layer on top, unchanged.
- **Deliberately no melee goal.** The iron golem's default kit was cleared out first — its village-strolling goals would have walked him out of his own arena, and its 18-damage fist would have sat on top of an arsenal that is supposed to *be* his damage. What is left is what the fight wants: pick a player, walk at them, stop just outside reach.

### 🧹 Seven teardown hooks that nothing ever called
- **A whole class of "haunted arena" bug is fixed at the root.** Every boss and gear system had a `onServerStopping` teardown written for it, and **not one was registered with the server lifecycle**. So every world stop left the live fights' entities behind — bosses, machines, rigging, guards, summoned servants — while the in-memory state that drove them vanished with the process. What came back on the next boot was a body with no fight attached: no AI, no ticking, no loot. All seven are wired now, before the world is saved rather than after.
- The periodic stray sweeps added in 1.0.59 are a backstop for crashes now, not the only line of defence.

### 🩹 Fixes
- **Per-player combat state no longer leaks.** `CombatGear` — the oldest of the gear systems — released nothing when a player logged out, so it kept one entry per player who had ever landed a hit or charged a super jump, forever. It now releases its rows on disconnect and resets entirely on server stop, matching what every newer gear class already did.
- **`/changelog` was eleven releases stale.** It reads a bundled copy of the changelog, and that copy is now generated from `CHANGELOG.md` by the build instead of being maintained by hand.

### 🧪 Three new guards, because dead code compiles
- **`ffAuditSources` fails the build on an unwired shutdown hook.** The bug above was invisible for so long precisely because a teardown method nothing calls is perfectly legal Java. Proven to fail by unwiring one on purpose.
- **`bosses.clockwork-ai`** asserts the King's goal rig: movement present, player targeting present, melee and wandering absent, and that both AI accessor mixins actually apply.
- **`legendary.documented-numbers`** makes nine accessors load-bearing — they existed as "test hooks so the documented numbers cannot drift" and no test had ever called them. It now pins the Gauntlet's wind-up and ram reach, the Codex's spell cycle, Starfall and Void Reaver cadence, the Seal's cost, the Magister's overcharge window, and the Void Shaper's whole block-throwing table (stone, magma, ice, leaves, ore, log).
- **`changelog.current`** fails if the bundled in-game changelog is not the version the mod actually is.

## 1.0.59

### 🧱 The four new raid bosses are actually killable now
- **Their health is cut to endgame parity.** The Clockwork King, Starbound Magister, Void Shaper and Emerald Sovereign shipped at 1200 / 900 / 1400 / 1100 against the Scarlet Devil's 900, which is what "they just have WAY too much health" was. They are now 700 / 620 / 760 / 680.
- **The shields no longer multiply it.** The Clockwork King's machinery was worth up to a **four-tier Resistance** and the Sovereign's court up to **three** - an 80% damage cut, permanently reapplied while anything lived, which turned a 1200 HP boss into more than 6000 effective HP. Both cap at **one** tier now: breaking the arsenal or the court still matters, but the armour is never a wall.
- **The Sovereign heals less.** His Royal Tribute banked 12 HP per player taxed; with a full court in range he could out-heal a group's damage. It banks 5.

### 🎁 They always drop their loot
- **One loot path, paid exactly once.** Loot was granted only from the death ceremony, so any death that found another route - a command, an admin `/kill`, vanilla's own body falling over - dropped nothing at all. All four bosses now pay through a once-only ledger that the entity-death hook also calls: whichever route runs first pays, and the other is a no-op.
- **The ceremony can no longer be interrupted into a loss.** It used to mark the boss invulnerable to protect the sequence, which meant a crash or restart mid-ceremony left him **permanently unkillable** - alive, invulnerable, at 1 HP, with no fight to drive him. It now cancels blows instead, so a boss can never be left immortal.
- **Bodies orphaned by a crash are swept up.** Because a fight lives in memory, a boss saved when the server died comes back as a statue that never moves, never dies and never drops. A periodic sweep clears any boss body with no fight attached.

### ⚙️ The Clockwork King walks now
- **He no longer flies.** He is a no-AI puppet, so vanilla never applied gravity to him and never walked him anywhere: the Piston Slam puts him six blocks in the air and he simply stayed there, which made him a boss you could not reach from the ground. He is driven by hand now - grounded every tick, closing to just inside melee range - and rooted only while a telegraphed move is charging.

### 🧪 Two new self-test assertions
- **`bosses.new-are-killable`** - no new boss may out-tough the Scarlet Devil, and no shield may stack past one tier.
- **`bosses.new-loot-once`** - the loot ledger must survive being called with nothing to pay and nobody to pay it to, because the death hook calls it on every entity death and a throw there would be swallowed and silence the payout.

## 1.0.58

### 🎨 Art the new bosses and their loot were missing
- **Evoker and Illusioner cloaks and spellbooks redrawn.** All four are new 16x16 art, so the Evoker's and Illusioner's sets read as their own gear instead of a recoloured blur.
- **Excalibur drawn.** The duel roll's 1-in-1000 legendary finally has art of its own rather than looking like the netherite sword it is underneath.
- **The Mystery Box and all four Mystery Keys drawn.** The Box rendered as a plain barrel and every key tier rendered as the *same* tripwire hook - a Legendary Key and a Common Key were indistinguishable. Each tier has its own model now.
- **The Clockwork King's trophy and gauntlet redrawn** so the two pieces of his kit are no longer near-identical brass shapes.

### 🔮 The Illusioner's Spellbook does what it says
- **True invisibility.** The caster is hidden with the entity flag rather than the potion effect, so the particles they give off stay visible while the body does not. **Armour is hidden from other players, not removed** - an empty equipment packet is sent, so full netherite still protects exactly as it did. Mobs drop the caster's target and are refused new ones, so armour no longer widens the range you are spotted at.
- **Illusions update.** Every copy re-wears the caster's *current* kit each tick, so swapping a weapon or chestplate mid-window changes every clone with you instead of leaving them standing in the armour you cast in.

### 🧪 Each of the four new bosses has its own enchant
- **Overclock** (Clockwork King) - hits discharge an arc that overloads the target and drags in whatever is packed around it with Weakness and Slowness.
- **Starfall** (Starbound Magister) - the blow lands heavier and a star falls on the victim, cleaving the enemies around them.
- **Voidrend** (Void Shaper) - hits tear a seam through armour and drag the target *in* rather than knocking it away, the opposite of Seismic.
- **Sovereign's Levy** (Emerald Sovereign) - hits mark the target for tribute and the kill shakes emeralds loose.
- Each drops as a Tome from its own boss and fuses in the Item Forge like every other enchant: `/ff give overclocktome`, `starfalltome`, `voidrendtome`, `levytome`.

### 🩹 Fixes
- **The Starbound Magister dropped nothing.** Her lethal-blow hook was returning "allow the killing blow", so she died instantly and the death ceremony - and with it `grantLoot` - never ran. The same bug was in the Clockwork King, Void Shaper and Emerald Sovereign; all four are fixed.
- **The Sovereign's Bell now makes villagers fight.** Vanilla villagers have no attack goal, so buffing them left them standing there being hit. A rung bell turns nearby villagers into Royal Subjects that close on the nearest hostile and land a real pitchfork strike, credited to the villager.
- **The Shaping Sigil no longer throws blocks at you.** Gripping and hurling were the same click, so looking at a wall picked a block up and flung it. Right-click grips, sneak-right-click hurls.
- **The Void Reaver works.** It threw whatever you were looking at, which in a fight is the enemy and never terrain - so its whole gimmick was dead exactly when you were using it. It finds its own ammunition beside the victim now.
- **Excalibur was a dead item.** The self-test's right-click probe caught it with neither a handler nor a passive declaration; its 255 Strength and Sharpness live in a held-item tick, and it is declared passive now.

## 1.0.57

### ⛓ Prison is now The Cell Block, and it has a second axis
- **Heat.** Rich ore does not come for free any more. Every ore you mine adds Heat - nothing for stone, a point or two for coal and iron, five for diamond, ten for ancient debris - and it cools on its own, a point every second and a half. Past **45 Heat** the cell block notices you.
- **Guards.** They spawn in your tunnels and hunt you, with bounty, health and damage scaling off your rank, and they get more numerous and quicker to arrive into full **lockdown** at 75. Put one down for a cash bounty, Heat **-15**, and progress on the Guard contract. Your record going quiet at 20 Heat calls the whole sweep off.
- **You cannot die in here.** A lethal blow is intercepted exactly like an expedition fatal blow: you are dragged to **Solitary**, a real 5x5 boxed cell high above the mine, for 15 seconds of Blindness, Slowness and Mining Fatigue. Your mined haul is confiscated; your **stashed gear is untouched**, so nothing a player owns can be lost. It is a setback, not a wipe.
- **Contracts.** Three jobs sit on the noticeboard in the Prison Menu - mine ore, slay inmates, take down guards, or sell a haul - each paying bonus cash and refreshing when claimed. Reroll the board for cash if you do not like the work.
- **Break-out.** Every now and then a **loose brick** works free of your mine wall. Break it and a 26-block tunnel opens with guards flooding in behind you and a glowstone exit at the far end: reach it inside 45 seconds and you walk free with a jackpot, a Heat wipe and a line in the newspaper. Fail, and the tunnel is sealed back **block for block** and your Heat jumps 25.
- **The Prison Menu is a control board now.** Six rows showing your Heat meter, the guards currently on you, break-out status, all three contracts with progress bars, and the usual sell / rank / upgrade / convert / travel buttons. Selling launders Heat, so the haul you were carrying is also the thing that gets the dogs off you.
- **A fishing pond that paid nothing.** `onFishCatch` existed, was documented as paying $30-$80 a catch, and was **never called from anywhere** - the pond was decorative. It is hooked to the reel-in now, jackpot catches included.
- **An arena exploit that printed money.** The wave ticker ran for every player in the prison regardless of where they were, so starting the arena and walking back to the mine left an empty arena "clearing" wave after wave and paying the full bonus each time, forever. Waves now only advance while you are actually standing in the arena, leaving abandons the run, and no free payout is issued.

### ☄ Expeditions are rolled, not repeated
- **Anomalies.** Every run opens with one, announced on arrival and echoed in the action bar: **Rich Veins** (ore pays double), **Blood Moon** (kills pay double, and there are far more of them), **Gilded Caches** (chests pay double), **Unstable Reality** (collapses 30% sooner, everything pays half again), or plain **Stable Reality**. Two trips into the same site no longer play the same way.
- **Site events** every 30-60 seconds: **ORE RUSH** seeds a real vein in the floor near you, **SUPPLY DROP** lands a rich loot chest, **SWARM** floods the halls, **SENTINEL** sends a mini-boss, and **BLACKOUT** takes your sight away and lets the dark do the rest. Each announces itself in chat and in the action bar.
- **A run summary card.** Mobs slain, ores mined, chests opened, distance explored, minutes survived, your anomaly - and a **C/B/A/S rating** against the site's expected haul, with a real bonus paid on A and S. The card also prints on an empty-handed escape, because knowing the run was worth nothing is still information.
- The action bar now carries anomaly, threat tier, the active event and the escape hint alongside loot and time.

### 🧪 Three new self-test assertions
- No contract can pay for work that was not done, a contract slot that does not exist cannot be claimed, and rerolling is never free.
- A survivable hit inside prison is never intercepted, so a scratch cannot jail you and confiscate your ore.
- Every anomaly has a name, a description, non-zero loot multipliers and a timer multiplier that never extends a run.
- The fishing mixin originally injected a non-returning callback into `retrieve`, which **failed to apply and broke server startup outright**. The self-test caught it before it shipped.

## 1.0.56

### 🛠 Four new raid bosses, with their full item sets
- **⚙ The Clockwork King** - called by a **Clockwork Core**. His armour *is* his machinery: every live turret, blade, piston and drone is worth a tier of Resistance, and tearing one off chips his real health and opens a window. Kill the arsenal or hit a wall. Drops his Loot Box, Trophy, **Mech-Scrap**, the **Clockwork Gauntlet** (every 4th landed hit winds Overdrive and drives a piston ram through whatever is in front), the **Mechanical Heart** (self-repair that only ticks while you are *not* being hit) and **Automaton Armor** (plating plus fireproofing).
- **✨ The Starbound Magister** - called by an **Astral Compass**. A wandering fight with **no arena at all**: she keeps her distance and blinks away when you close. From 60% she banks Astral Energy - each point widens, quickens and hardens her spells - and at six she **overloads**, detonating it all and dropping her guard for five seconds. So the fight rewards interrupting her instead of out-poking her. At 25% she goes to Starfall: marked meteors, a Gravity Well, an Astral Dash, and **Celestial Collapse**, a telegraphed last spell she is spent after. Drops her Loot Box, Trophy, **Magical Essence**, the **Starpiercer**, the **Astral Mantle** and the **Magister's Codex** (three real spells: Star Bolt, Gravity, Meteor).
- **🟯 The Void Shaper** - called by a **Void Anchor**. He does not shoot fireballs: he rips blocks out of the world as floating displays and hurls them, and **the block decides the blow** - stone is heavy with huge knockback, magma burns, ice slows, ore flies fast, logs throw you far, leaves shatter into fragments. Ender Pull, Block Wall, Ender Step and Void Slam round him out, and at 40% he hoists five to eight blocks into orbit and **loses control** of all of them at once. Drops his Loot Box, Trophy, **Voidsteel Scrap**, the **Void Reaver**, the **Colossus Plate** and the **Shaping Sigil**.
- **👑 The Emerald Sovereign** - called by the **Sovereign's Crown**. He fights with subjects: a **Bell Toll** that staggers you and slows you, **Royal Decrees** that summon **Royal Guards**, a **Royal Tribute** that taxes vitality (never items), and at 50% **Sovereign's Judgement**, a beam you have to walk out of. His court is his armour - clear it and he opens up. Drops his Loot Box, Trophy, **Royal Tribute**, the **Royal Contract** (summons a real Royal Guard that fights for you), the **Sovereign's Bell** (staggers nearby hostiles, empowers nearby villagers) and the **Emerald Seal** (cheaper trades, emeralds from hostile kills, and a Royal Tribute buff for five emeralds).
- All four have custom item art, item definitions, model data, crafting recipes and recipe advancements, and appear in **/ff give**, **/raidboss help** and the boss codex names.
- **⚙ The King is built out of blocks.** Twelve **block displays** bolt his armour onto him - an iron visor and a lamp for an eye, an iron chestplate over a glowing core, deepslate shoulder plates on iron brackets, back plating, a hip carriage and iron legs - laid out in *his* frame so the whole suit turns with him. The suit is gated on the same number as his Resistance shield, so **stripping his machines visibly blows his armour off plate by plate**, and when he rebuilds he bolts it back on. His machines get chassis blocks too (dispenser, trapdoor, piston head, observer) riding them, so a turret reads as a turret instead of as a shulker. None of it has a hitbox or deals damage - it is the state of the fight made visible. Plating is tagged, so an orphaned piece left by a crash is swept up within 15 seconds instead of standing over an empty field forever.

### 🩹 Fixes
- **Art for every new item is real.** 28 new textures plus their model JSONs, and all seven loot-box families' dispatch entries are generated, so none of them render as the vanilla item they are built on.
- **A loot box that did nothing on right-click.** The four newest boxes were being swallowed by the boss-gear branch before the loot-box branch could see them, so clicking one returned `PASS` - dead in the hand. The self-test's right-click probe caught it.
- **No-AI bosses now actually move.** A no-AI puppet never runs its own physics step, so driving one with `setDeltaMovement` leaves it hovering in place; the Magister and the Void Shaper are moved by position each tick, exactly like the Clockwork machines.
- **Leaves can be thrown.** The void-shaping eligibility test used `isSolidRender`, which is false for leaves - so the documented "leaves shatter into fragments" attack was unreachable. It now tests the *collision* shape, which is what "a full block" actually means.

## 1.0.55

### ⏳ The Pocket-Watch cannot stop a Time Lord
- **He refuses it, and then proves the point.** Against him the watch no longer freezes the world: the tick freeze is dropped, no player is pinned, and the bubble only stills the **mobs** around you. He walks straight through it, laughs - *"You stopped time with a trinket?"* - and a beat later answers with *"Let me show you how it is done"*, stopping time himself so you can watch a real stop from the wrong end of it.
- Everyone else in the bubble is left alone too, so nothing about the rest of the fight pretends to be paused.
- **A refused watch is a quiet one.** Three things followed from the same mistake - that a bubble he walked through was still a stopped moment. His blows were being *banked* and handed back as a single combined hit (the exact burst the bank exists to prevent), arrows that never stopped moving were getting the i-frame bypass, and the stop's "time resumes" stinger fired while his own stop was still running. None of it happens now: only a stop that actually holds the world banks damage, only a stop that is really holding projectiles tags them, and a refused watch ends silently. The bubble also outlasts his retort, so its cleanup can no longer wake the mobs while he is still holding them.

### 🎁 Loot boxes pay the people who earned them
- **The Scarlet Devil and Time Lord boxes go straight into the inventory of every player who fought**, instead of a single box on the floor for whoever happened to be standing nearest when the body dropped. Each participant gets a delivery line telling them how many arrived.

### 🩸 A Blood Revenant takes nothing from you
- **It wears copies. You keep your gear.** The servant used to confiscate your whole inventory so `/claim loot` could hand it back, and that contract kept breaking - it fought Nice Keep Inventory, split stacks with the grave system, and made you kill your own corpse to get your tools back. Now it loots *nothing*: it wears a look-alike of your armour and weapon, **drops nothing**, and whatever you were carrying stays with you. It works with Nice Keep Inventory on or off.
- **A claimed death no longer swallows anything - this one was losing inventories.** The claim cancelled *every* drop so the servant could carry the real stacks. Once the servant stopped carrying them, that cancellation had nothing left to give them back, so a player killed in the King's blood phase or by the Scarlet Devil silently lost their **entire** inventory. The hold is gone: a claimed death is now an ordinary death, so Nice Keep Inventory keeps what it keeps and the rest falls where it fell.
- **The servant still looks like the person it was raised from.** Vanilla clears each slot the instant it has dropped it, so the kit is now captured at the very first drop of the death - the last moment the whole inventory exists - and the servant dresses from that. The capture is cosmetic and never writes back, so it cannot duplicate or consume a real item.

### ☠️ The Wither, harder and cleaner
- **The nuke is a real storm: ~40 skulls per player**, plus a carpet around the boss so the blast zone is not a safe spot. Supercharged and King variants scale it higher again.
- **Wither wreckage always rebuilds.** Every block it carves now enters the shared Explosion Rebuild queue **unconditionally** - not gated behind the `Explosion Rebuild` toggle, which previously meant an admin turning player explosions off also condemned every arena the Wither had visited to permanent craters.

### ⚔️ Raid art remade, player raids readable
- **Every raid texture is redrawn** - the loot box, the raiders' upgrader, the Warlord's Cloak and Trophy - while the **Warlord's Axe** and the **Captain's Horn** are left exactly as they were.
- **A live raid readout in the action bar**, once a second: which wave you are on out of how many, how many raiders are still standing, then how many bosses remain and how long the fight has run. The *"no defenders"* countdown rides the same line, so the one piece of raid state nobody can afford to miss is impossible to miss.
- **Broadcasts are scoped to the raid's own dimension.** The abandonment warning used to be server-wide, telling a player mining two dimensions away about a raid they had never seen.

## 1.0.54

### 🩸 Her three legendaries actually work
- **The Grimoire speaks in the action bar.** Cast, cooldown, spell-switch and "nothing in reach" all report to the overlay bar, so cycling spells mid-fight no longer floods chat.
- **Its spells land.** Blood Spears resolved its target through the *player* list only, so aiming at a mob cancelled the entire volley on the next tick - it now finds anything living by id. Blood Rain only looked for `Monster`s, so over a field of ordinary mobs it drained nothing; it now drinks from every living thing in range that is not yours. Bloodsuck falls back to the nearest target when the crosshair is a degree off.
- **The bats are a swarm.** Night Swarm's bats were ordinary bats with no owner and no target, so they flapped off into the night. Each one now hunts the caster's nearest enemy, bites it, and drifts back to the caster when the field is clear.
- **The Blood Prism is a ritual duel.** Channelling now roots you where you stand: move or take a single hit and the channel breaks. The drain pulses the target's health away, and if their health reaches zero they die there and then.
- **A player killed by the ritual owes nothing.** Their inventory is copied before the kill and written straight back - the death is real, the walk back is real, and no item is lost. The servant that rises wears only a *copy* of their armour, holds no gear, and drops nothing.
- **A Revenant can never bite its owner.** Its vanilla goals could set the wrong target between our ticks; a damage gate now refuses any hit from a servant or bat aimed at its own master, and the tick loop clears such targets outright.
- **Servants are named for who they were.** "Blood Revenant of <victim>", mobs included, so a raised cow is unmistakable.

### ⏳ A frozen volley lands as a volley
- **Pocket-Watch projectiles ignore invulnerability frames.** Everything hanging in the bubble is tagged while time is held, and those arrows punch through the 10-tick i-frame window as they land - stop time, empty a quiver, and every arrow counts.

## 1.0.53

### 🩸 The Scarlet Devil, finished
- **Three legendary weapons, not one.** Her loot box now rolls exactly three legendaries - the **Scarlet Fang** (a real diamond spear: it thrusts, and every hit drinks a share of the damage), the **Scarlet Grimoire** (casts her own move set; sneak to cycle spells) and the **Blood Prism** (bleeds a mob *or a player* into a blood servant of yours). Her Trophy and the Bloodsoaked Core stay out of the legendary pool, because a trophy and a forge material are not weapons.
- **One forge material instead of two.** The Crimson Essence did the same job as the Bloodsoaked Core and only made the drop table confusing; she drops Cores only now.
- **Her health bar actually moves.** It was created at summon time and never once told what her health was, so it sat full no matter how much she bled or drank. It is driven every tick now.
- **She no longer heals too much.** Her bite drinks a small capped share of the bite, the blood rain drinks a capped share of what it drained, and neither can hand her a whole health segment.
- **She no longer hides inside blocks.** The unstick ran only in the baseline-movement branch, so the swoop - the one move that drives her through terrain - could park her inside a wall until the fight happened to reach that branch. She is unstuck every tick and mid-swoop.
- **Blood Revenants give your gear back.** A servant captured its victim's gear from the *live* inventory, but by the time a death is reported vanilla has already emptied every slot - the only surviving copy is the keep-inventory snapshot, which the death handler deliberately leaves for the hand-off. Servants now take their gear (armour, held items and all) out of that snapshot, so `/claim loot` is never empty, and a servant left behind by a despawned boss still hands everything back.
- **The "she can't be summoned again" state is gone.** A fight whose phantom had already vanished is swept on the next summon instead of blocking the summoner forever.
- **Her textures and models render.** The item definitions that make a chest / glass bottle / diamond spear draw *her* art had been deleted from the pack; they are regenerated, and the pack ships them.

### ⏳ The Time Lord's finale, action bar and free launches
- **Stop, in the action bar.** Everyone caught in a time stop now sees a live countdown in their action bar for its whole duration.
- **His 1% finale.** At the last percent he stops trading blows, floats clear and **glows** (so you can find him through terrain), then walks a thick aging beam across every player in turn. Fail to land one clean hit on him in time and the years catch up all at once: you fall where you stand and a skeleton wearing your name takes your place. Land a hit and you are released instantly. Either way the fight then resolves - he is spent, and his death ceremony (with its loot) plays out.
- **Being launched no longer costs you the fall.** Every move of his that throws a player now marks them, and their fall damage is zeroed until they land.
- **The Pocket-Watch is a real stop now.** It uses the server tick freeze, so arrows and thrown items **hang in mid-air** and resume when time does, while the wielder walks through the frozen moment. Everyone else in the bubble is pinned. And **every point of damage dealt inside it is banked**, then delivered in one blow when the stop lifts - you can no longer be bursted down during your own watch.

### ☠️ The Wither, reworked properly
- **Skull Rain works.** Its meteors were cast at the world-surface heightmap, so in any arena that was not under open sky they spawned inside the ceiling and died there. They now spawn in the lowest *open* column above their target and arrive as a real barrage.
- **The Wither Nuke is a skull storm.** Instead of a single blast at its own body, the sky opens and skulls rain across the whole arena - around each player and around the boss - so standing still is the only way to be hit by all of them. The explosion itself is smaller now, because the storm is the damage.
- **The skull shield no longer breaks the fight.** It used to park the boss in place for ten seconds and left the health bar frozen (vanilla only writes bar progress from its own AI step). It is five seconds, it moves normally through it, and the bar is refreshed by hand.
- **Its loot box is guaranteed.** Whatever path ends the death animation, the rewards are now granted - a death captured by `/kill` or an interrupted sequence used to vanish with no loot at all.
- **Two new moves:** **Grave Burst** (a forward cone of skulls) and **Wither Wail** (a rooted, withering scream with darkness on top), and the nuke is no longer phase-2-only, so a normal fight can actually see it.

### 📦 Item art
- **Every custom item definition is back in the pack.** The generated `assets/minecraft/items/*.json` overrides had been removed from the working tree, which is why the Time Lord's summon item, legendaries and loot box, and the Scarlet Devil's whole set, kept rendering as the vanilla items they are built on. Regenerated, included in the mod jar, and the resource-pack zip rebuilt with the current metadata.

## 1.0.52

### ⏳ The Time Lord's time stop is a real stop now
- **The world freezes, and you do too.** The stop already froze the server tick rate, which is what hangs mobs and projectiles mid-air - but players are client-driven, so they kept walking and swinging. Every participant is now **pinned in place** for the length of the stop, mid-air players included, and cannot move, attack, use an item, open a block or mine while frozen.
- **He is not frozen with it.** The world stops, so his own AI would have too - a boss that stands still is a boss that does nothing. While time is stopped he now crosses the gap and strikes directly, so the stop is something he does *to* you rather than a pause he sits through.
- **The knockback lands when time resumes.** The damage you take inside a stop is banked, and the moment it releases you are shoved away from him, scaled by how much you were hit and hard-capped so it can never turn into a launch.
- **No more launching.** The old stop applied **Jump Boost 128**, which is not a "no jumping" flag on this version - it is a rocket. That is what threw players above the clouds mid-stop, and why the boss then despawned: they had left the arena. Player pinning replaces it, so a mid-air stop now holds you exactly where you were.
- **The arrows stopped one-shotting.** Volley damage was dialled back and the crit flag removed (arrow damage also scales with arrow speed, so the base is capped), and the phase-two ring now fires sixteen lighter arrows instead of twenty-four heavy ones.
- **More time to spend.** Three new moves join the rotation: **Chrono Bombs** (countdowns underfoot that go off together), an **Aging Ray** that hitscan-ages its target, and **Rewind** - below half health he once per fight steps back through his own timeline to heal and blink away.
- **He says each line once.** Identical lines inside a short window are dropped, and the death ceremony's two speeches announce exactly once each instead of repeating every ten ticks.

### 🎁 Loot boxes: the new boxes actually open
- **The Time Lord and Scarlet Devil Loot Boxes were missing from the menu's box-name and box-consume tables**, so opening one claimed *"You don't have a King Wither Skeleton Loot Box anymore"* - a box nobody had. Both families are recognised now, and the Scarlet box spins its own table (Scarlet Fang, Trophy and Bloodsoaked Core as the legendaries) instead of falling through to the Wither pool.

### 🗂️ The resource pack was invalid, so no custom item looked custom
- **The companion pack shipped without a `pack.mcmeta`.** The client rejects a pack with no metadata, which is why every custom model - the bosses' items and the Time Lord Loot Box included - kept rendering as the vanilla chest / bottle / crystal it is built on. Since 1.21.9 the format is declared as a `min_format` / `max_format` range rather than a single `pack_format`; the pack now carries the correct 26.2 metadata (format 88), and `tools/build_resourcepack.py` writes it and rebuilds the zip so it can never be lost again.

### 🪂 Sonic Boom no longer cancels your elytra
- **The launch marked the glider as standing on the ground**, and a fall-flying player who is on the ground has their flight cancelled - so the move that was supposed to be a burst of speed folded your wings instead. It now only sets the velocity.

### ⌚ Pocket-Watch cooldowns
- **60 seconds for the 5-second stop, 80 seconds for the 10-second one** - replacing the old 3- and 5-minute waits - and the item lore reads the real numbers.

## 1.0.51

### 🩸 The Scarlet Devil - a new endgame raid boss, summoned by the **Scarlet-Blood**
- **Craft the Scarlet-Blood** from 4 Ghast Tears + 4 Redstone Blocks ringing a Glass Bottle, then right-click it: the scarlet mist rolls in, the sky reddens, and she rises out of a pool of her own blood.
- **Bloodsuck.** Her diving bite is heavy and heals her for a share of the damage it lands - a blocked or whiffed bite drinks nothing, and the heal pops over her head so the lifesteal is visible.
- **Dying inside her arena does not scatter your gear.** Her blood claims the body: a bone servant rises wearing the victim's armour and carrying their weapon, hunting everyone still in the arena, and destroying it returns everything through `/claim loot`. Nice Keep Inventory stands down for exactly that fight, so the grave system and the servant can never both own the same inventory.
- **She laughs when she kills you** - "AHAHAHAHAHA! Say hello to your new body." - heals herself, and raises the servant in the same beat.
- **Phase two at half health: blood rain.** The sky bleeds for ten seconds at a time; every exposed player in the arena is drained and withered, and she drinks a share of what the rain takes. The rest of the move set is a full rotation - the swoop, thrown blood spears that leave her essence behind, a blinding Night Swarm of bats, and taunts.
- **A proper arrival and a proper death.** Her spawn ceremony (mist, reddened sky, her first line) and her death ceremony (a last uncontrolled rain, one final speech, a crimson implosion) are both played out - and she refuses to simply fall: the killing blow is cancelled and the ceremony runs instead, with loot granted once at the end. Drops are her Loot Box (a second one for a full phase-two clear), the Scarlet Devil's Trophy, Bloodsoaked Cores, Crimson Essence, and the **Scarlet Fang**, whose on-hit lifesteal is the same bloodsuck she uses.
- **Everything ships with it**: textures, models, item definitions, the crafting recipe and its recipe-book unlock, `summon_scarlet` / `kill_scarlet` advancements, her own Loot Box family in the loot-box GUI, `/raidboss help scarlet`, `/ff give` aliases, and the regenerated companion resource-pack zip. `items.use.invoke` proves the vial summons a real fight and that the self-test tears it down again; `commands.raidboss.pages` covers her aliases.

### 🛒 Chest shops: a sales ledger, and a warning before you sell out
- **Every trade is recorded.** Each shop keeps its last 60 trades - what, how many, for how much, to whom and when - from both directions: a customer buying out of a buy shop, or selling into a sell shop.
- **A Sales Ledger screen** opens from the shop settings. **Recent Sales** lists them newest-first with buyer, amount and "4m ago"; **Top Sellers** aggregates the whole ledger per item so you can see what actually moves. Clearing the history is two clicks, and it never touches your prices, stock or mode.
- **Out-of-stock alerts.** When a buy shop's line sells its last unit, the owner gets a chat warning - silenced for five minutes per item so one hot line cannot spam you, and skipped entirely while the owner is offline.
- **Turn it on and off** with the RESTOCK ALERTS button in the settings GUI or `/chestshop alerts`, and `/chestshop ledger [n]` prints the history in chat for owners who would rather read it there.
- **The preference and the history both persist** in `chestshops.json`, and `shop.lifecycle` now proves it headlessly: it records both directions, checks the totals and the best seller, round-trips a ledger entry through its own JSON, and asserts the alerts toggle survives a save/load.

### 🤝 Guilds: leaving asks first, and five skills instead of none
- **`/guild leave` no longer drops you out of a guild on a single typo.** It states what leaving costs and waits: `/guild leave confirm` goes through, `/guild leave cancel` backs out without doing anything, and the guild menu's Leave button follows the same path.
- **Five guild skills**, one rank per guild level, each shown in the skills menu with its exact effect: **Wisdom** (+5% skill XP per rank), **Might** (+4% damage to mobs), **Rally** (+5% damage to raid bosses), **Ward** (-4% damage taken from mobs) and **Fortune** (+5% earned cash). A rank can be refunded for a fee, which returns the point to the pool.

### ⚡ `/ff event` actually happens now
- **The old odds were one event per ~10 hours of play** - a roll every 30 minutes at 5% - which is why a whole evening could go by with none. It now rolls every 5 minutes at 35%, with a 10-minute cooldown after each event: an average gap of about twenty minutes.
- **Two new events**: **XP Frenzy** (double skill XP) and **Phantom Swarm** (the skies fill with phantoms).
- **Events change the game, not just the chat.** The multipliers the events advertise are now wired into the paths that matter: **Merchant Festival** boosts sell prices, **XP Frenzy** doubles skill XP, **Gold Rush** doubles ore drops, and **Blood Moon / Double Trouble** raise mob damage. The old banner promised modifiers that no code ever read.

### 🔧 A server setting can no longer flip itself off when someone rejoins
- **The cause was an op's client re-uploading its own config on every connect.** The client kept a local mirror of the server's settings and, on join, pushed its copy back for anyone with OP - so the stale local value for Explosion Rebuild won every time an op left and rejoined. Joining now only *asks* for the server's snapshot (a read-only request) and mirrors it locally; nothing is ever written back on connect.
- **It cannot happen silently again.** When a config push does change something, the server logs it *and* tells the pusher and every other online op exactly which keys changed and to what. A setting flipping is now always attributable.

### 🛒 Chest shops: placed like signs, closeable, unbreakable, deletable
- **Sneak-right-click places the shop, like a sign on a wall.** Right-click *or* sneak-right-click a chest with a Buy/Sell Sign and the shop is placed on that chest. Sneak-right-clicking a placed shop chest reopens its settings, and sneak-right-clicking anything that is not a chest hands the click back to vanilla - so a shop sign is never a sign you cannot place.
- **Buy → Sell → Closed, from one button.** The owner GUI's MODE button now cycles all three states instead of only buy/sell, and a **closed** shop stops all trading while keeping its prices, currency, stock and protection - so a shop can be parked without being rebuilt. Closed shops show no particles and, for everyone but the owner, behave as an ordinary chest again (claim checks included) rather than opening a trade window that would refuse every click.
